## 0. Setting and inputs

Interior (young) bonds D ⊂ Λ₁, exterior free bonds J = E_{Ω,*} ∖ D, z = i_D a + i_J A_e. The TeX kernel is K_ξ(a) = 1_{𝒟_ξ}(a) Θ_D^σ(a) p_ξ(Ψ_ξ(z_ξ(a))) |det DΨ_ξ| with p_ξ(z) = exp[−½⟨z, H_ξ z⟩ + v_ξ(g₀C_ξz) − g₀⁻²V_ξ(g₀C_ξz)] and Θ_D^σ(a) = ∏_{b∈D} 1{|a_b|_σ < r}, r = δ₀/g₀. Ψ_ξ is the bondwise boundary change (1.22), the identity off S₁* ⊂ Ω₁ ∩ Λ₁^c, so it changes A_e only: write A′_e = Ψ_ξ(A_e)|_J. Then

  −½⟨z, H_ξ z⟩ = −½⟨a, H_{DD} a⟩ − ⟨a, H_{DJ} A′_e⟩ − ½⟨A′_e, H_{JJ} A′_e⟩,                                    (0.1)

with H_{DD}, H_{DJ}, H_{JJ} the blocks of H_ξ. All three terms are inside K_ξ (not in the outer weight 𝒪_V), by eq:retained-prechange-density.

Stage 2 inputs (Lemma lem:regular-bounds, statement L14; Lemma lem:c2-entry-range L83; Lemma lem:c2-regional-entry L130; proof L158–170):

 (I1) entry range: (H_ξ)_{bb′} = 0 unless |z(b) − z(b′)|₁ ≤ 2, hence unless the initial sites are within ℓ^∞ distance ρ_L = 4L in fine units; each row has at most 164L⁴ nonzero entries;
 (I2) row sums: Σ_{b′∈Ω₁*} ‖(H_ξ)_{bb′}‖ ≤ m_L for every row b ∈ D. This is not a stated lemma but the "Upper bound" paragraph of the proof of lem:regular-bounds ("For a row b ∈ I all entries f_{bb′} have stencils in 𝔓_{z(b)} … hence Σ_{b′}‖f_{bb′}(U₁)‖ ≤ m_L", the sum over all b′, with (H_ξ)_{bb′} = f_{bb′} on Ω₁* × Ω₁* by lem:c2-regional-entry and every stencil within 3L of b₋ by lem:c2-entry-range). Column sums are not used below;
 (I3) two-sided bound on every branch in the support of the outer weight: (γ_L/2) id ≤ H_{ξ,DD} ≤ m_L id, and the same for every principal restriction (stage 2 writes I for the carrier D).

Put γ = γ_L/2 and h₊ = m_L. The constants γ_L, m_L, ρ_L depend on L and the group only; the bounds (I2)–(I3) hold under stage 2's coupling thresholds (eq:convex-background-thresholds, app:thresholds). Norms: |·|_σ, σ ∈ {op, HS}, is the TeX's cutoff norm, the operator norm or the normalised Hilbert–Schmidt norm h, fixed once in stage 1 (stage 1 conventions, L27); h ≤ |·|_σ ≤ c_h h with c_h = √N (stage 2 conventions). Stage 1 and 2 write I for the carrier the TeX calls D (sec:regional-identification); D′ ⊂ D denotes the carrier of a retreated region in §3.1, and (I3) passes to it by principal restriction while (I2) is unchanged. Bond convention (stage 1): the bonds of a set X are the positive fine bonds with at least one endpoint in X. ℓ := LMR₁ (stage 3's ℓ_mac).

## 1. Item 1: sup-norm bound on the mean shift

Definition. m = −H_{DD}⁻¹ H_{DJ} A′_e ∈ 𝔤^D, so that −½⟨a,H_{DD}a⟩ − ⟨a,H_{DJ}A′_e⟩ = −½⟨a − m, H_{DD}(a − m)⟩ + ½⟨m, H_{DD} m⟩.

Lemma 1.1 (entrywise decay of the interior inverse). For b, b′ ∈ D, with d(b,b′) the ℓ^∞ distance of the initial sites in fine units,

  ‖H_{DD}⁻¹(b,b′)‖ ≤ γ⁻¹ (1 − γ/h₊)^{⌈d(b,b′)/ρ_L⌉} ≤ γ⁻¹ e^{−μ_H d(b,b′)},   μ_H := γ/(h₊ρ_L) = γ_L/(8L m_L).

Proof. By (I3), 0 ≤ K := id − h₊⁻¹H_{DD} ≤ (1 − γ/h₊) id, so H_{DD}⁻¹ = h₊⁻¹ Σ_{n≥0} K^n (norm-convergent, ‖K‖ ≤ 1 − γ/h₊ < 1). K has the entry range ρ_L of (I1) (the identity is diagonal), so K^n(b,b′) = 0 when d(b,b′) > nρ_L. Hence H_{DD}⁻¹(b,b′) = h₊⁻¹ Σ_{n ≥ ⌈d/ρ_L⌉} K^n(b,b′) and ‖K^n(b,b′)‖ ≤ ‖K^n‖ ≤ (1 − γ/h₊)^n; the geometric sum gives h₊⁻¹(1 − γ/h₊)^{⌈d/ρ_L⌉}/(γ/h₊). Finally (1 − x)^{s} ≤ e^{−xs} with x = γ/h₊, s ≥ d/ρ_L. ∎

Lemma 1.2 (sup-norm shift bound). Let J₁ := {e ∈ J : H(b′,e) ≠ 0 for some b′ ∈ D}; by (I1), J₁ ⊂ {e ∈ J : dist(e,D) ≤ ρ_L}. Then m depends on A′_e|_{J₁} only (exterior bonds with dist(e,D) > ρ_L have a zero column in H_{DJ}), and for every b ∈ D

  h(m_b) ≤ C_H^{near} max_{e∈J₁} h(A′_e),   C_H^{near} := γ⁻¹ m_L N_lat(μ_H),   N_lat(μ) := Σ_{s≥0} 4(2s+1)⁴ e^{−μs} < ∞.        (1.1)

Proof. m_b = −Σ_{b′∈D} H_{DD}⁻¹(b,b′) Σ_{e∈J₁} H(b′,e) A′_e. Operator norms of entries are those induced by h (stage 2 convention), so h(H(b′,e)A′_e) ≤ ‖H(b′,e)‖ h(A′_e). By Lemma 1.1 and the row-sum bound (I2) for the rows b′ ∈ D,

  h(m_b) ≤ Σ_{b′∈D} γ⁻¹ e^{−μ_H d(b,b′)} Σ_{e∈J₁} ‖H(b′,e)‖ h(A′_e) ≤ γ⁻¹ m_L max_{e∈J₁} h(A′_e) Σ_{b′∈D} e^{−μ_H d(b,b′)},

and Σ_{b′} e^{−μ_H d(b,b′)} ≤ Σ_{s≥0} 4(2s+1)⁴ e^{−μ_H s}: at most (2s+1)⁴ sites lie at ℓ^∞ distance s from the initial site of b, each carries four positive bonds, and projection to the torus only identifies sites. Only row sums over b′ ∈ D are used; no column-sum bound for columns e ∈ J is needed. ∎

Remark 1.3 (norms and the choice of ϑ). The cutoff norm |·|_σ is the operator norm or h (stage 2 conventions), and h ≤ |·|_σ ≤ c_h h with c_h = √N. Hence |m_b|_σ ≤ c_h h(m_b) ≤ c_h C_H^{near} max_{e∈J₁} h(A′_e) ≤ c_h C_H^{near} max_{e∈J₁} |A′_e|_σ. Put

  ϑ := 1/(4 c_h C_H^{near}),   a constant depending on L and the group only (through γ_L, m_L, N); ϑ ≤ 1/16, since c_h ≥ 1, N_lat ≥ 4 (its s = 0 term) and m_L ≥ γ by (I3).

Conclusion of item 1. If |A′_e|_σ < ϑr for every e ∈ J with dist(e,D) ≤ ρ_L (a set containing J₁), then

  sup_{b∈D} |m_b|_σ ≤ r/4.                                                                                           (1.2)

No coupling threshold and no condition on the exterior bonds farther than ρ_L from D are involved: those bonds do not enter m (Lemma 1.2). The decay of H_{DD}⁻¹ (Lemma 1.1) is used only to sum the b′-series; it plays no role in decoupling far exterior bonds, which are decoupled by the entry range (I1) alone.

Remark 1.4 (correction record). The first version of this note (snapshot collar-items-1-2-20261019-r1) stated a shift bound with an additional "far" term carrying a constant C_H′ and a decay e^{−μ_H ℓ₀}, a shell bound (1.2), a normalisation N_H and a coupling threshold. The isolated review of that snapshot found the far term identically zero (zero columns of H_{DJ}), the shell bound missing the shell cardinality, C_H′ misderived, and the final constant in the displayed (1.3) not the one proved. All of these were removed; (1.1)–(1.2) above are the statements the proof delivers, with the norm comparison c_h made explicit.

## 2. Item 2 (repaired): the exterior Gaussian in the history weight, and the payment of a bad collar bond

§7 item 2 asked for two things: the identification of the exterior Gaussian inside the history weight, and a lower bound S ≥ γ for its Schur complement, to pay a bad collar bond by e^{−γϑ²r²/2}. The first is Identity 2.1 below (exact). The second is not available and cannot be made available (2.2), so the payment is taken instead from the physical measure, with every hypothesis checked (2.3). This replaces §6(c) of the pass note.

2.1 Identity (where the exterior Gaussian sits). By (0.1) and completing the square (needs only H_{DD} invertible, (I3), and |det DΨ_ξ| independent of a, stage 1: log|det DΨ_ξ(A′)| depends on A′|_{S₁*} only),

  K_ξ(a) = e^{−½⟨A′_e, S A′_e⟩} · 1_{𝒟_ξ}(a) Θ_D^σ(a) e^{−½⟨a − m, H_{DD}(a − m)⟩ + v_ξ − g₀⁻²V_ξ} |det DΨ_ξ|,   S := H_{JJ} − H_{JD}H_{DD}⁻¹H_{DJ},        (2.1)

using ½⟨m,H_{DD}m⟩ − ½⟨A′_e,H_{JJ}A′_e⟩ = −½⟨A′_e,SA′_e⟩ and H_{JD} = H_{DJ}^*. The factor is written in A′_e = Ψ_ξ(A_e)|_J, which differs from A_e on S₁* only. Off S₁* it is the quadratic form CRE collects in T₁ (1.29); on S₁* the conventions differ (CRE moves first-order boundary corrections into V^{(0)}(S₁), p.252, while the TeX retains p_ξ∘Ψ_ξ without truncation), and nothing below uses this correspondence. In the drafts' convention (stage 3 §7.2: w_ξ = e^{c_ξ}I_{ξ;1}, c_ξ = E_ξ(0) − ½⟨A_e^Ψ,H_ξA_e^Ψ⟩ + Σ_far p_α(A_e^Ψ) + log|det DΨ_ξ|, K_{ξ;1} carrying the II and IJ blocks and the near primitives) the same algebra gives e^{c_ξ}I_{ξ;1} = e^{−½⟨A′_e,SA′_e⟩} × e^{c_ξ + ½⟨A′_e,H_{JJ}A′_e⟩} × ∫ 1_{𝒟}Θ e^{−½⟨a−m,H_{DD}(a−m)⟩} × (near primitives and cutoffs)(a) da, the far primitives and E_ξ(0) staying in c_ξ. So in either convention the Schur factor is an exact factor of w_ξ. It is independent of a, hence cancels in the conditional ratio 𝒥_ξ[f]/𝒥_ξ[1] inside 𝒪_V (eq:retained-outer-kernel) and acts only through the history weight.

2.2 No lower bound for S on 𝔤^J is available. ⟨x,Sx⟩ = min_{y∈𝔤^D}⟨(y,x),H_ξ(y,x)⟩ (H_{DD} > 0 by (I3)), so S ≥ γ on 𝔤^J is equivalent to ⟨(y,x),H_ξ(y,x)⟩ ≥ γ|x|² for all (y,x) ∈ 𝔤^{D∪J}, a lower bound of H_ξ in the J-directions on all of 𝔤^{E_{Ω,*}}. The sources supply no such bound: (i) stage 2's lower bound (proof of lem:regular-bounds, "Local comparison") covers supp B by patches within 2R_c − 1 of I, where Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation (stated for cubes □′ ⊂ Ω₁^{~−1} ∖ R₁ with □′^{~4} ⊂ Ω₁) gives a gauge with phases ≤ μ̂ and the flat gap γ_L applies after the perturbation c_Lμ̂ < γ_L/4; (ii) J contains the free bonds of S₁ = (Ω₁ ∖ Ω₁^{~−1}) ∪ R₁ (CRE p.251 L4): on R₁ the (1.18) fluctuation test fails, which says nothing about the background U₁ (the comparison U₁ ≈ U_{1,□′} holds there too, stage 3 §7.1), but stage 2's small-phase gauge is proved only for cubes of Ω₁^{~−1} ∖ R₁; on Ω₁ ∖ Ω₁^{~−1} the tests χ₁(Ω₁), χ₀′(Ω₁) are in force (CRE (1.11), p.250) but the comparison U₁ ≈ U_{1,□′} is not available there (□′^{~4} ⊄ Ω₁), and no near-flat gauge is supplied; (iii) a bound H_ξ ≥ γ on the regular part D ∪ J₁ alone (a plausible re-run of (i)) gives only the compression S_{J₁J₁} ≥ γ, because S_{J₁,J∖J₁} = H_{J₁,J∖J₁} ≠ 0 (entry range ρ_L). No counterexample is claimed: what is established is that no proof of S ≥ γ on 𝔤^J exists in the sources, and none is attempted. Hence the Gaussian factor (2.1) is not used for any bound; the exterior field on the large-field part is treated as CRE treats it, paid by the cutoff factors that put it there. Nothing in §§2–3 uses S, and items 4–5 (not done here) must not use it either.

2.3 The payment of a bad collar bond from the physical measure.

Definitions. For a region Λ (a union of ℓ-cubes, Λ ⊂ Λ₁) the carrier is D(Λ) := {b ∈ I : b has an endpoint in Λ} ⊂ I and the exterior is J(Λ) := E_{Ω,*} ∖ D(Λ) ⊃ J; the collar is J_col(Λ) := {e ∈ J(Λ) : dist(e, D(Λ)) ≤ ρ_L} (ℓ^∞ distance of initial sites); it contains J₁(Λ) of Lemma 1.2 (H_ξ does not depend on Λ). A collar bond e is bad if |A_e|_σ ≥ ϑr, good otherwise; A_e denotes the fluctuation variable of CRE (1.13)–(1.16) at e whether e is exterior or interior in the original split (for e ∈ I ∖ D(Λ) it is the young variable a_e of the original split, exterior on the labelled terms where it is tested, §3.1). In the construction of §3.1 a labelled term carries the regions Λ₁ ⊃ Λ₁^{(0)} = Fill(Λ₁) ⊃ Λ₁^{(1)} ⊃ … ⊃ Λ₁^{fin}, unions of ℓ-cubes, Λ₁^{(0)} label-independent (Lemma 3.0) and the others determined by the labels (B^{(0)}, B^{(1)}, …) (Lemma 3.1), and the set of bonds tested on it is T(labels) := ⋃_k J_col(Λ₁^{(k)}) ⊂ J(Λ₁^{fin}). The mark of a bond e on a labelled term is

  M_e := 1{e ∈ T(labels)} · 1{|A_e|_σ ≥ ϑr},

a function of the labels and of U (A_e is a function of U, of V = the block average of U, and of Ω₁ through U₁(Ω₁,V)); M_e = 0 on every labelled term on which e is not tested, so M_e is defined on every history and every labelled term; on the support of a term's indicators a tested bond is bad exactly when it belongs to some B^{(k)}, so there M_e = 1{e ∈ ⋃_k B^{(k)}} is a function of the labels alone. Let w_ξ^{lab} be the weight of a labelled term: w_ξ = ∫_{𝔤^I} ρ_ξ(a_old) da_old with ρ_ξ := e^{c_ξ}K_{ξ;1} the integrand of stage 3 §7.2 (the factors already placed in κ_ξ, ζ_ξ, χ_{ξ,e} of stage 1's 𝒪 are not duplicated), and w_ξ^{lab} := ∫_{𝔤^I} ρ_ξ(a_old) 1_lab(a_old, A_e) da_old, where 1_lab is the product of the term's collar indicators (functions of A_e and of a_old|_{I∖D(Λ₁^{fin})}, §3.1); inside this integral the variables a_old|_{I∖D(Λ₁^{fin})} are exterior for the re-split of Lemma 2.5 and a_old|_{D(Λ₁^{fin})} is the young variable, so w_ξ^{lab} = ∫_{𝔤^{I∖D^{fin}}} 1_lab e^{c_ξ^{Λ^{fin}}} (∫_{𝔤^{D^{fin}}} K^{Λ^{fin}}_{ξ;1} da) da_old|_{I∖D^{fin}}. Since the indicators are nonnegative measurable functions summing to one (3.4(c)), Σ_lab w_ξ^{lab} = w_ξ (Tonelli). The marked physical probability is

  p_e^ϑ := Z₀⁻¹ ∫ 𝒪[Σ_lab w_ξ^{lab} M_e](V) dV,

with the history sum inside 𝒪 as in stage 3 §7; by the previous sentence w_ξ^{lab} M_e = ∫_{𝔤^I} ρ_ξ 1_lab M_e da_old, so p_e^ϑ is well defined. For e ∈ J this equals Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[w_ξ Σ_lab 1_lab M_e](V)dV with Σ_lab 1_lab M_e independent of a_old, because T(labels) ∩ J = J_col(Λ₁^{(0)}) ∩ J is label-independent (D^{(k)} ⊂ D^{(0)}); for e ∈ I the mark involves a variable that is young in the original split, and the labelled-term form is the definition.

Lemma 2.3 (collar bonds are regular, eligible and in the chart). Let e ∈ J_col(Λ) for some Λ ⊂ Λ₁ as above. Then: (a) d(e, S₁*) ≥ ℓ − 2 − ρ_L > 0, so Ψ_ξ is the identity at e and A′_e = A_e (stage 1: d(I,S₁*) ≥ LMR₁ − 2, eq:c-collar-distance; Ψ_ξ bondwise with support S₁*); also e ∈ 𝒢_ξ ∖ S₁* by Lemma lem:collar-coverage(i), since ρ_L = 4L ≤ 3R_c. (b) On the branch |A_e|_σ < r (stage 1 Lemma lem:collar-coverage(iii): the (1.25) good test ∏_{b∈𝒢_ξ}1{|A(b)|<r}), so a bad collar bond has ϑr ≤ |A_e|_σ < r and g₀A_e lies in the principal chart (chart condition h(g₀A_e) < π/√N: h ≤ |·|_σ and |g₀A_e|_σ < δ₀, with δ₀ < π/√N for small coupling). (c) The L²M₂R₁-cube □′ of (1.18) containing the initial vertex of e lies within 3R_c + 1 + L²M₂R₁ < ℓ − 1 of Λ₁, hence in Ω₁^{~−1} ∖ R₁′ (lem:collar-coverage(i)), and e ∈ (□′^{~2})*: it is a good, eligible cube in the sense of stage 3 §7.1, and the comparison sup_{b∈(□′^{[2]})*}|U₁(b)U_{1,□′}(b)⁻¹ − 1|_σ ≤ Cε₁e^{−cLM₂R₁} holds for it (CRE p.250 after (1.19) with stage 2's Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation, as quoted in stage 3 §7.1).

Proof. (a)–(c) are the quoted stage 1 statements applied to e, using D(Λ) ⊂ I and dist(e, D(Λ)) ≤ ρ_L. ∎

Lemma 2.4 (a bad collar bond forces a witness at threshold θ_ϑ). Put θ_ϑ := min(ε₀, ε₁/(4B₃N^{3/2}L²), ϑδ₀/(2C_Q)), with C_Q = (9K_L+2)(1+2B₃N^{3/2}L²) the constant of stage 3 §7.1 (k_N³ = N^{3/2} in the seed's notation). Since ϑ ≤ 1, θ_ϑ ≥ ϑθ with θ = min(ε₀, ε₁/(4B₃N^{3/2}L²), δ₀/(2C_Q)) the stage 3 threshold. For g₀ below a threshold depending on L, M₂, the group, the source constants and ϑ: on every labelled term and for every tested bond e with M_e = 1, some plaquette p or some active mean-transport variable w within the neighbourhood N(□′) of the seed (the 6ℓ-neighbourhood of □′, whose side L²M₂R₁ ≤ ℓ/8 by M ≥ 8LM₂; its sites are within 7ℓ of the initial vertex of e, the gauge-label centres within O(L) more, hence within Kℓ, K = 256) satisfies |U(∂p) − 1|_σ ≥ θ_ϑ or |w − 1|_σ ≥ θ_ϑ.

Proof. This is a re-run of the R₁-step of stage 3 §7.1 (the seed proof's display eq:seed-approximate-fluctuation, which is linear in the threshold) with δ₀ replaced by ϑδ₀ ≤ δ₀. By Lemma 2.3(b), |g₀A_e|_σ ≥ ϑδ₀ at the free bond e, and the unitary logarithm inequality gives |Ũ(e)U₁(e)⁻¹ − 1| ≥ (2/π)ϑδ₀ for the gauge-fixed field Ũ. The comparison of Lemma 2.3(c) is < ϑδ₀/10 for small coupling: the seed records that ε₁/δ₀ is bounded by fixed source constants (CRE (2.6), (2.8)), and e^{−cLM₂R₁} → 0 as R₁ → ∞. Hence |Ũ(e)U_{1,□′}(e)⁻¹ − 1| ≥ (2/π − 1/10)ϑδ₀ > ϑδ₀/2. If every plaquette and every active gauge-variable witness in N(□′) were below θ_ϑ, the seed's two-block comparison would give |Ũ(e)U_{1,□′}(e)⁻¹ − 1| ≤ C_Qθ_ϑ ≤ ϑδ₀/2, a contradiction. The seed's smallness hypotheses (θ ≤ η₀, 2B₃k_N³L²θ ≤ η₀, k_Nθ ≤ c₂′, the VAR condition 2k_N²L²θ ≤ a₁, the uniqueness class from 2k_N²L²ε₀, quadratic term at most the linear term) are all upper bounds on the threshold and are preserved by θ_ϑ ≤ θ; the P₀, P₁ cutoffs at ε₀, ε₁ in the neighbourhood and the local determining-data problem are those of the branch, unchanged by the collar labels. The conclusion holds on the original averaging-constraint and earlier-cutoff support, which is the support on which it is used below. ∎

Lemma 2.5 (the history weight of a retreated carrier). Let Λ ⊂ Λ₁ be a union of ℓ-cubes, D(Λ) ⊂ I, J(Λ) ⊃ J; write a ∈ 𝔤^{D(Λ)} for the young variable of the retreated split and A_e^Λ ∈ 𝔤^{J(Λ)} for its exterior variable, so A_e^Λ = (A_e, a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)}) in the original variables, and A^{Λ,Ψ} := Ψ_ξ(A_e^Λ) = (Ψ_ξ(A_e), a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)}), Ψ_ξ being the identity on I ∖ D(Λ) ⊂ I. Keep S₁, R₁, Ψ_ξ, U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁^c,V) and χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁^c,S₁) as built from the original Λ₁ (determined by (Ω₁,R₁), hence recoverable from the labels); call a primitive near if its free-input set meets D(Λ), far otherwise (near relative to D(Λ) implies near relative to I). With E_ξ(B) = E_ξ(0) − ½⟨B,H_ξB⟩ + Σ_α p_α(B) the pre-boundary-change exponent of CRE (1.15) in stage 1's primitives (stage 3 §7.2), put

  c_ξ^Λ := E_ξ(0) − ½⟨A^{Λ,Ψ}, H_{J(Λ)J(Λ)} A^{Λ,Ψ}⟩ + Σ_{α far} p_α(A^{Λ,Ψ}) + log|det DΨ_ξ(A_e)|,
  K^Λ_{ξ;1}(a) := 1_{𝒟^Λ}(a) exp[−½⟨a, H_{D(Λ)D(Λ)} a⟩ − ⟨a, H_{D(Λ)J(Λ)} A^{Λ,Ψ}⟩ + Σ_{α near} p_α(a + A^{Λ,Ψ})],

where 1_{𝒟^Λ}(a) := 1{Ψ_ξ(a + A_e^Λ) ∈ 𝒜_ξ} is the indicator of the young fibre of the retreated split (for Λ = Λ₁ it is 1_{𝒟_ξ}(a) by the definition of 𝒟_ξ). Then, pointwise,

  e^{c_ξ^Λ} K^Λ_{ξ;1}(a) = 1_{𝒟^Λ}(a) |det DΨ_ξ(A_e)| exp E_ξ(Ψ_ξ(a + A_e^Λ)),

and the composed retained cutoffs and ζ multiply both sides. The box factors 1{|a_b|_σ < r} of the bonds b ∈ I ∖ D(Λ) are factors of the retained cutoff (lem:collar-coverage(ii), I ⊂ 𝒢_ξ), now functions of exterior variables. For Λ = Λ₁ these are stage 3 §7.2's c_ξ, K_{ξ;1} and identity. (|det DΨ_ξ(A_e)| = |det DΨ_ξ(A_e^Λ)|, the determinant depending on the S₁*-components only.)

Proof. Ψ_ξ(a + A_e^Λ) = a + A^{Λ,Ψ}, because Ψ_ξ is bondwise with support S₁* and d(I,S₁*) ≥ ℓ − 2 > 0 (eq:c-collar-distance), D(Λ) ⊂ I. Write z = a + A^{Λ,Ψ}. Expanding −½⟨z,H_ξz⟩ into the D(Λ)D(Λ), D(Λ)J(Λ), J(Λ)J(Λ) blocks and splitting Σ_α p_α(z) into near and far primitives, where a far primitive depends on z only through z|_{J(Λ)} = A^{Λ,Ψ} (its free-input set misses D(Λ); Lemma lem:row-locality), gives E_ξ(z) = E_ξ(0) − ½⟨a,H_{D(Λ)D(Λ)}a⟩ − ⟨a,H_{D(Λ)J(Λ)}A^{Λ,Ψ}⟩ − ½⟨A^{Λ,Ψ},H_{J(Λ)J(Λ)}A^{Λ,Ψ}⟩ + Σ_near p_α(z) + Σ_far p_α(A^{Λ,Ψ}); multiplying by |det DΨ_ξ(A_e)| 1_{𝒟^Λ}(a) gives the identity. The density 1_{𝒟^Λ}|det DΨ_ξ| exp E_ξ(Ψ_ξ(a + A_e^Λ)) is the CRE (1.15) density in the transformed variables whatever free bonds are called interior; the split only decides which factors are collected in c_ξ^Λ and which in K^Λ_{ξ;1}. ∎

Proposition 2.6 (normalised payment). On compatible even fine periods (stage 3 §7's standing restriction), for every bond e,

  p_e^ϑ ≤ (μ₀ ⊗ q_g^{⊗𝓘} ⊗ Haar^{⊗T})(W_e^ϑ) ≤ q_ϑ := n_W max(z_P(θ_ϑ), z_G(θ_ϑ)),                                  (2.2)

with W_e^ϑ := {a plaquette or auxiliary gauge-variable witness ≥ θ_ϑ within Kℓ of e}, the stage 3 formulas z_P(θ) = exp[−b_Gθ²/(4g₀²) + 4d_G log g₀⁻¹ + 4 log c_H⁻¹ + 48], z_G(θ) = e^{1/2}(c_Hg₀^{d_G})⁻¹e^{−b_Gθ²/g₀²}, b_G = 1/(2N), n_W = 7(2Kℓ + 7)⁴ (θ_ϑ ≤ ε₀, as z_G requires). Moreover, with π_* = min(p₀(g₀), p₀(g₁)) and θ ≥ c_θg₀π_* (stage 3 §7.3, from CRE (2.6)–(2.9) and δ₀ = (A₁/A₀)ε₀),

  q_ϑ ≤ exp[−c_qϑ²π_*²],   c_q := b_Gc_θ²/8,   for 0 < g₀ < g_*(ϑ)                                                      (2.3)

(c_q is written out so as not to be confused with stage 3's unspecified c_*; for ϑ = 1 it is a value of that constant).

Proof. Fix a history and a labelled term. By Lemma 2.5 (with Λ = Λ₁^{fin} of the term) its weight w_ξ^{lab} is the integral of the CRE (1.15) density in transformed variables, times the term's collar indicators, and the mark M_e is a nonnegative function of the labels and of U. The reversal of stage 3 §7.2 is a change of variables for integrals of (density × nonnegative function of U and the labels), so it applies to w_ξ^{lab} M_e exactly as it applies to w_ξ 1{z ∈ E(B_ξ)} there: undo Ψ_ξ, the constraint elimination (it changes designated bonds only; e is free), the scaling, the linearisation, the chart and the translation by U₁. After the reversal M_e equals 1{e ∈ T(labels)} · 1{|(1/i)log(Ũ(e)U₁(Ω₁,V)(e)⁻¹)|_σ ≥ ϑδ₀} (Ψ_ξ is the identity at e by Lemma 2.3(a); the relevant background is U₁(Ω₁,V), as e ∉ S₁*). By Lemma 2.4, M_e ≤ 1_{W_e^ϑ} on the support of the term's cutoffs, and W_e^ϑ is label-free (independent q_g variables are added for inactive indices, Haar variables for centres and unused sites, as in §7.2). All labelled terms are nonnegative and their partitions (P₀, P₁, Q₁, R₁ and the collar labels, 3.4(c)) sum to one, so Σ_hist Σ_lab (term · M_e) ≤ Σ_hist Σ_lab (term · 1_{W_e^ϑ}) ≤ (μ₀ ⊗ q_g^{⊗𝓘} ⊗ Haar^{⊗T})(W_e^ϑ)·Z₀ (stage 3 §7.2–7.3; only "≤" is needed), which gives the first inequality of (2.2); the second is §7.3's union bound over the n_W witness labels with the single-witness tails z_P, z_G at threshold θ_ϑ. For (2.3): θ_ϑ/g₀ ≥ ϑθ/g₀ ≥ ϑc_θπ_*, so −log z_P(θ_ϑ) ≥ b_Gc_θ²ϑ²π_*²/4 − 4d_G log g₀⁻¹ − C and −log z_G(θ_ϑ) ≥ b_Gc_θ²ϑ²π_*² − d_G log g₀⁻¹ − C; log n_W ≤ C log log g₀⁻² since ℓ ≤ L²M(2 log g₀⁻²)^𝔯 (from CRE (2.5)–(2.6), 3.4 below); and π_*² ≥ A₀²(½ log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀} with p₀ ≥ 5𝔯 ≥ 10 dominates every logarithm for small g₀, leaving the exponent b_Gc_θ²ϑ²π_*²/8. ∎

2.4 What item 2 now delivers. The exact factorisation (2.1); the finding that no lower bound for S on 𝔤^J is available from the sources (2.2), so §7 item 2's second half and §6(c) of the pass note are withdrawn as mechanisms; and in their place the normalised, Z₀-divided payment (2.2)–(2.3) for every bond tested and found bad, with the hypotheses of stage 3 §7 checked for the collar bonds (Lemmas 2.3–2.5). This is the quantity the history average needs (stage 3 §7: E_h D_ξ ≤ C_L S_c n_C sup_z p_z). The Wilson insertion does not enter p_e^ϑ: as in stage 3, the insertion is bounded inside the conditional estimate (|ℬ_ξ[f_ξ]| ≤ C_{resp,χ} g₀ r D_ξ on covered branches, stage 3 §5), and the history average of D_ξ is insertion-free.

## 3. Item 3: the retreat, its termination and its costs

Notation. Cubes are the aligned ℓ-cubes of CRE (1.10), (1.20); Λ₁ is a union of such cubes, D = D(Λ₁), J = J(Λ₁), J_col as in 2.3; ϑ as in Remark 1.3 (the only definition used; ϑ ≤ 1/16). All tests use |·|_σ.

3.1 The collar partition, the retreat and the filling. Connected components of unions of ℓ-cubes are taken with respect to a fixed adjacency of ℓ-cubes (CRE's; the arguments below use only that a component of X ∪ Y is either a component of X not adjacent to Y or a set meeting Y). Fill₁(Λ) denotes one pass of CRE's modification after (1.20) (p.251 L6–14): every connected component of Λ^c contained in a cube of side 100ℓ is replaced by the smallest rectangular parallelepiped of ℓ-cubes containing it; Fill(Λ) is the fixed point of iterating Fill₁ (each pass removes at least one cube from Λ or stops, so the iteration ends after finitely many passes). A region Λ is filled if Fill₁(Λ) = Λ, i.e. every component of Λ^c contained in a cube of side 100ℓ is a rectangular parallelepiped; this is CRE's condition (2.3) (p.255 L28–29, j = 1: 100MR₁ in the L⁻¹-lattice is 100ℓ fine sites; the side-100ℓ version is used so that filled regions satisfy (2.3) literally, while p.251 fills components in cubes of side < 100ℓ). Adjacency: two ℓ-cubes are adjacent when they share a face, so that a rectangular parallelepiped of ℓ-cubes is connected (used in the equivalence "filled ⇔ Fill₁(Λ) = Λ"); CRE p.251 and stage 3 §7.1 do not specify theirs, and the proofs below hold for any symmetric adjacency under which parallelepipeds are connected. Standing hypothesis of this note, additional to stage 1 ("a multiple of LMR₁ and at least 8M") and stage 3 ("compatible even fine periods"): every fine period exceeds 200ℓ. It is coupling-dependent through ℓ = LMR₁(g₀) (on a fixed torus it fails as g₀ → 0), so the caller's statement must carry it; the W18 statement is for periods → ∞ at fixed small g₀, where it holds. Under it a set contained in a cube of side 100ℓ lifts isometrically to ℤ⁴, and its smallest containing parallelepiped of ℓ-cubes is well defined. Whether CRE's Λ₁ is filled is not used: round 0 fills it.

Lemma 3.0 (round 0). Put Λ₁^{(0)} := Fill(Λ₁) and 𝔅₀ := (Λ₁^{(0)})^c ⊃ Λ₁^c (a set of sites; the bad bond sets of the labels are B^{(k)}). (a) Λ₁^{(0)} ⊂ Λ₁ is a filled union of ℓ-cubes, determined by (Ω₁, R₁) like Λ₁, so label-independent; if Λ₁ already satisfies (2.3) then Λ₁^{(0)} = Λ₁. (b) Every point of 𝔅₀ is within 100ℓ of a point of the unfilled bad region Q₁′^{~4} ∪ (B(P₁¹)^c)^{~5} ∪ R₁′^{~1} of CRE (1.10), (1.20), so stage 3 §7.1 applies to 𝔅₀ without change: every z ∈ E(𝔅₀) forces a witness ≥ θ within Kℓ of z, K = 256, with the same n_W, and p_z^{(0)} := Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[w_ξ 1{z ∈ E(𝔅₀)}](V)dV ≤ q on compatible even fine periods.

Proof. (a) is the definition (Fill(Λ) ⊂ Λ, and the output is filled). (b) Stage 3 §7.1's induction ("by induction every current component contains an original bad point, and each fill is performed inside a cube of side less than 100ℓ containing that component … This also holds if small merged components are filled again") is run with CRE's p.251 fill as the base and the passes of Fill₁ as further steps: a Fill₁ pass changes only components contained in a cube of side 100ℓ, each containing an original bad point, and two points of such a cube are within 100ℓ − 1 < 100ℓ of each other, so every added point is within 100ℓ of an original bad point of its component; mergers preserve the presence of an original point. A point of the unfilled region is within 10ℓ of a bad test (stage 3 §7.1), whose witness lies in the seed's neighbourhood N(Q) of the test cube (6ℓ, or P₀'s defining enlargement), within 7ℓ of it; so the witness is within 117ℓ + O(L) + 1 < Kℓ of z. The mark 1{z ∈ E(𝔅₀)} depends on the regions only, so §7.2–7.3 apply verbatim with the same n_W, giving p_z^{(0)} ≤ q. ∎

On a labelled term with current region Λ₁^{(k)} (k = 0: Λ₁^{(0)} = Fill(Λ₁), Lemma 3.0; the bonds of D ∖ D^{(0)} are exterior on every term, with their box factors as exterior good tests, Lemma 2.5) insert, inside the a-independent part of the integrand, 1 = ∏_{e} [1{|A_e|_σ < ϑr} + 1{|A_e|_σ ≥ ϑr}] over the untested bonds e of J_col(Λ₁^{(k)}) (collar bonds have A′_e = A_e, Lemma 2.3(a), so the factors are a-independent, and they remain exterior factors on every later term, since J(Λ) only grows as Λ shrinks), and expand. A term is labelled by its bad set B^{(k)}. If B^{(k)} = ∅ the iteration stops with Λ₁^{fin} = Λ₁^{(k)}. Otherwise put

  Λ₁^{(k+1)} := Fill(Λ₁^{(k)} ∖ ⋃_{e∈B^{(k)}} 𝒬(e)),   𝒬(e) := the union of the ℓ-cubes meeting the closed ℓ^∞-ball of radius ρ_L + 1 around the initial site x_e of e.

Since 2(ρ_L+1) + 1 = 8L + 3 < ℓ (standing condition LMR₁ > 4R_c + 4L²M₂R₁ + 8, stage 1 L33), 𝒬(e) consists of at most 2⁴ = 16 cubes and every point of 𝒬(e) is within ℓ + ρ_L + 1 ≤ 2ℓ of x_e. The bonds of D^{(k)} ∖ D^{(k+1)} become exterior variables of the term; they are the same bond variables as before (the chart (1.13)–(1.16) and U₁ depend on Ω₁ and V, not on Λ₁: CRE (1.12)–(1.16) are defined on Ω₁, and Λ₁ first enters in (1.20)), and their box factors 1{|a_b|_σ < r} become exterior good tests (Lemma 2.5). Convention (Lemma 2.5): S₁, R₁, Ψ_ξ, U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁^c,V) and χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁^c,S₁) are those of the original Λ₁ throughout (consistent, since Ω₁ ∩ Λ₁^c ⊂ Ω₁ ∩ (Λ₁^{(k)})^c); the retreated term is the same integral with a different split of the free bonds into integrated and exterior ones, and Ψ_ξ remains an invertible change of variables with its Jacobian retained (eq:retained-change-domain). The weight w_ξ^{lab} of 2.3 is the integral of ρ_ξ × (the term's indicators) over all of 𝔤^I, the variables of I ∖ D^{fin} being integrated as exterior variables of the re-split.

Lemma 3.1 (termination, final state, counts). (a) The iteration stops after at most |Λ₁|/ℓ⁴ rounds with B^{(k)} ≠ ∅, followed by one terminal round. (b) On every final term, every e ∈ J_col(Λ₁^{fin}) is good: |A_e|_σ < ϑr. (c) Every region Λ₁^{(k)} is filled, and every point of Λ₁^{(0)} ∖ Λ₁^{fin} is within 102ℓ of x_e for some bond e found bad on the term. (d) The bonds with an endpoint within 102ℓ of a given site number at most N_ret := 4(207ℓ)⁴; hence at most N_ret bonds are removed on account of one bad bond e, and for a given bond z at most N_ret bonds e have x_e within 102ℓ + 1 of an endpoint of z (that count is 4(204ℓ+4)(204ℓ+3)³ ≤ N_ret for ℓ ≥ 2).

Proof. (a) A bad e ∈ J_col(Λ₁^{(k)}) has dist(e, D^{(k)}) ≤ ρ_L, so some bond of D^{(k)} has an endpoint in Λ₁^{(k)} within ρ_L + 1 of x_e; the cube containing that endpoint lies in 𝒬(e) ∩ Λ₁^{(k)} and is removed. So each non-terminal round removes at least one cube. (b) Λ₁^{fin} = Λ₁^{(k)} for the terminal round k, whose untested collar bonds were all good; the collar bonds tested earlier were either good (the same variable, still good) or bad, and a bad bond e cannot lie in J_col(Λ₁^{fin}): Λ₁^{fin} ⊂ Λ₁^{(j)} ∖ 𝒬(e) for the round j in which e was found bad (Fill only removes cubes), so every b ∈ D^{fin} has an endpoint in Λ₁^{fin}, disjoint from the closed ball of radius ρ_L + 1 around x_e; if that endpoint is x_b then |x_b − x_e|_∞ ≥ ρ_L + 2, and if it is x_b + e_μ then |x_b − x_e|_∞ ≥ ρ_L + 1; in both cases dist(b, e) > ρ_L. (c) Induction over the rounds and over the passes of Fill, with the invariant: the current region is filled, and every point removed so far lies within 100ℓ of a point of ⋃ 𝒬(e) over the bonds e found bad so far. The invariant holds for Λ₁^{(0)} (filled by Lemma 3.0(a); nothing removed yet). In a round, removing the cubes of 𝒬(B^{(k)}) gives a complement whose components are either components of (Λ₁^{(k)})^c not adjacent to any removed cube, or sets meeting ⋃_{e∈B^{(k)}} 𝒬(e). A component of the first kind contained in a cube of side 100ℓ is a parallelepiped (Λ₁^{(k)} is filled), so a Fill₁ pass does not change it; a pass changes only components of the second kind, each inside a cube of side 100ℓ that contains a point of some 𝒬(e), so every point added lies within 100ℓ of that point. After the pass the components are again either untouched components of the first kind or sets meeting ⋃ 𝒬(e) (a merger involving a filled hull contains a 𝒬-point). Iterating to the fixed point keeps the invariant, and the output is filled by definition. Since every point of 𝒬(e) is within 2ℓ of x_e, every removed point is within 102ℓ of x_e for some bad e. (d) Sites within 102ℓ of a given site number at most (204ℓ + 1)⁴; the bonds with an endpoint among them have their initial site within 102ℓ + 1, at most 4(204ℓ + 3)⁴ ≤ 4(207ℓ)⁴ bonds. Sites within 102ℓ + 1 of one of the two endpoints of z lie in a box of (204ℓ + 4)(204ℓ + 3)³ sites, each the initial site of four bonds, and 204ℓ + 4 ≤ 207ℓ for ℓ ≥ 2. ∎

Remark 3.2 (what the final state gives). On a final term every e ∈ J^{fin} within ρ_L of D^{fin} is good (Lemma 3.1(b)) and A′_e = A_e there (for e ∈ J by Lemma 2.3(a); for e ∈ I ∖ D^{fin} because I ∩ S₁* = ∅), so (1.2) gives sup_{b∈D^{fin}}|m_b|_σ ≤ r/4 with the same C_H^{near} and ϑ: (I1) is unchanged, (I2) is a statement about the rows b ∈ I ⊃ D^{fin} with the sum over all b′, and (I3) passes to D^{fin} by principal restriction. This is the input for items 4–5, with margin 3r/4.

Lemma 3.3 (the regions and the retreated fibre). (i) Λ₁^{fin} ⊂ Λ₁^{(0)} ⊂ Λ₁ ⊂ Ω₁^{~−2} ∩ (R₁′^~)^c is a filled union of ℓ-cubes (Lemmas 3.0(a), 3.1(c)), so it satisfies CRE (2.3) with the adjacency of 3.1. (ii) lem:collar-coverage(i) (every site within ℓ − 1 of Λ₁^{fin} lies in Ω₁^{~−1} ∖ R₁′; every free bond of Ω₁ within 3R_c of D^{fin} lies in 𝒢_ξ ∖ S₁*) and eq:c-collar-distance (d(D^{fin}, S₁*) ≥ ℓ − 2) hold by the inclusions Λ₁^{fin} ⊂ Λ₁, D^{fin} ⊂ I. (iii) |A_e^{fin}(b)|_σ < r for every b ∈ J^{fin} with d(b, D^{fin}) ≤ 3R_c: for b ∈ J by lem:collar-coverage(iii) (d(b,I) ≤ d(b,D^{fin})), for b ∈ I ∖ D^{fin} by the retained box factor 1{|a_b|_σ < r} (Lemma 2.5), nonzero on the term. (iv) On a final term with exterior data A_e^{fin} for which the young fibre {a ∈ 𝔤^{D^{fin}} : Ψ_ξ(a + A_e^{fin}) ∈ 𝒜_ξ} is nonempty, the fibre is the open box {a : |a_b|_σ < r for all b ∈ D^{fin}} (otherwise it is empty and the term vanishes). Ω₁, R₁, S₁, Ψ_ξ and U₁ are those of the original Λ₁ by the convention of 3.1. The history label acquires the finite sequence (B^{(0)}, B^{(1)}, …) of bad sets; its bookkeeping in stage 1's history sum is §7 item 6 and is not done here.

Proof of (iv). The proof of lem:collar-coverage(iv) is re-run with I replaced by D^{fin}; its inputs are checked one by one. Let a₀ be a point of the fibre (nonempty on a term that survives) and a any point of the box; B₀ = Ψ_ξ(a₀ + A_e^{fin}) ∈ 𝒜_ξ and B = a + Ψ_ξ(A_e^{fin}) (Lemma 2.5). The cutoff (1.25) at B factorises as Θ_{D^{fin}}(a) × (a function of Ψ_ξ(A_e^{fin}) and the branch data): the proof of lem:collar-coverage(ii) uses only D^{fin} ⊂ 𝒢_ξ, the bondwise identity Ψ_ξ(a + A_e^{fin}) = a + Ψ_ξ(A_e^{fin}), and that the bonds of the factors χ^{(0)c}(R₁), χ₀′(S₁) have no endpoint in Λ₁ ⊃ Λ₁^{fin}; so (1.25) at B equals (1.25) at B₀ with Θ_{D^{fin}}(a₀) = 1 replaced by Θ_{D^{fin}}(a) = 1, nonzero. Every row equation has a small solution at g₀C_UB: on the rows within 2R_c + 2L of D^{fin} (near rows for I as well, D^{fin} ⊂ I) by Lemma lem:charts, whose polydisc condition |B(b)| < 2 + 4r on E_c holds by (iii) and |a_b| < r (the bonds of E_c are within 2R_c + 4L − 1 ≤ 3R_c of D^{fin}); on the other rows by Lemma lem:row-locality, since E_c misses D^{fin} and B = B₀ off D^{fin}, where the small solution exists because B₀ ∈ 𝒜_ξ. The constraint M(U(B)) = V on every row and the restriction V₀ off Ω₁ follow as in the stage 1 proof (they use the row equations only). The tests of (1.11) at U(B): χ₁(Ω₁) (a function of V) and ζ(Ω₁^c) (eq:c-large-field-factor) do not depend on a, so they take at U(B) their values at U(B₀), which are nonzero; χ₀′(S₁) is nonzero by the first condition; every other cube □′ ⊂ Ω₁ lies in Ω₁^{~−1} ∖ R₁ and its factor is the second factor of (1.19), equal to one by (B3)(ii). Hence B ∈ 𝒜_ξ. Conversely a point of the fibre has nonzero (1.25), hence Θ_{D^{fin}}(a) = 1. ∎

3.4 Costs of the retreat and the comparison.

 (a) Coverage and attachment. Stage 3 defines D_ξ = Σ_{b∈I_C} e^{−μ d(b,J)/2} (§5), with I_C ⊂ I the young bonds in the free-input stencils of the contour traversals, and the covered event A_cov (contour bonds in Ω₁, free inputs in I; §6). For a final term with carrier D^{fin} the corresponding quantities are D_ξ^{fin} := Σ_{b∈I_C∩D^{fin}} e^{−μ d(b,J^{fin})/2} and A_cov^{fin} := {all contour bonds lie in Ω₁ and every free input lies in D^{fin}}; both depend on the labels only through the regions. Put B_ξ := (Λ₁^{fin})^c. Pointwise on the term, with c = μ/2,

  D_ξ^{fin} ≤ Σ_{b∈Γ_C}Σ_z e^{−c d(b,z)}1{z ∈ E(B_ξ)},   1_{(A_cov^{fin})^c} ≤ Σ_{b∈Γ_C}1{b ∈ E(B_ξ)}:

a free bond not in D^{fin} has no endpoint in Λ₁^{fin}, so J^{fin} ⊂ E(B_ξ), and each b ∈ I_C ∩ D^{fin} ⊂ Γ_C contributes e^{−c d(b,J^{fin})} ≤ Σ_{z∈J^{fin}} e^{−c d(b,z)}; a contour bond outside Ω₁ or a free input outside D^{fin} meets B_ξ (stage 3 §7's argument with I replaced by D^{fin}). These two inequalities are combinatorial. The statement that the response of the retreated conditional integral is bounded by C_{resp,χ} g₀ r D_ξ^{fin} is the conditional estimate for the retreated carrier (item 4, with Remark 3.2 and Lemma 3.3 as its inputs) and is not claimed here. For the history average: by Lemma 3.1(c)–(d), on every final term

  1{z ∈ E(B_ξ)} ≤ 1{z ∈ E(𝔅₀)} + Σ_{e : x_e within 102ℓ+1 of an endpoint of z} M_e

(a bond z meets (Λ₁^{fin})^c either because it meets 𝔅₀ = (Λ₁^{(0)})^c or because one of its endpoints lies in Λ₁^{(0)} ∖ Λ₁^{fin}, which is within 102ℓ of x_e for a bond e found bad on the term, M_e = 1). Multiply by ρ_ξ 1_lab, integrate over 𝔤^I, sum over labels and histories, apply 𝒪 and divide by Z₀ (all integrands nonnegative, finitely many labels per history, Tonelli): the first term is label-independent, Σ_lab 1_lab = 1 reduces it to Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[w_ξ 1{z ∈ E(𝔅₀)}]dV = p_z^{(0)} ≤ q (Lemma 3.0(b)); the second gives Σ_{e near z} p_e^ϑ ≤ N_ret q_ϑ (Lemma 3.1(d), Proposition 2.6). Hence, on compatible even fine periods exceeding 200ℓ (3.1),

  Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[Σ_lab w_ξ^{lab} D_ξ^{fin}]dV ≤ C_L S_c n_C (q + N_ret q_ϑ),   Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[Σ_lab w_ξ^{lab} 1_{(A_cov^{fin})^c}]dV ≤ C_L n_C (q + N_ret q_ϑ).

This is stage 3 §7's bound with q replaced by q + N_ret q_ϑ; whatever stage 3's caller does with the term q (it enters |E_bdry| ≤ C_χ n_C(1+g₀r)·q in stage 3's conclusion), it does with q + N_ret q_ϑ, which has the same normalised form. The scalar κ_C of the TeX (eq:extracted-loop-scalar) does not enter the history average: the W18 criterion's ratio κ_C⁻¹ℬ_ξ[f_ξ] is formed by the caller from the response bound and this average, as in stage 3.

 (b) Boundary activities. The bonds that change status are D ∖ D^{(0)} (label-independent, round 0, Lemma 3.0) and, on a final term, D^{(0)} ∖ D^{fin} (young in the original split, exterior on the term): at most N_ret of the latter per bad bond e, each with an endpoint within 102ℓ of x_e (Lemma 3.1(c)–(d)), and p_e^ϑ ≤ q_ϑ. How the connected boundary terms of the retreated conditional integral are localised relative to these bonds, and their bound, are item 5; nothing about them is claimed here. If item 5's cost on a term is a sum over the bonds b ∈ D^{(0)} ∖ D^{fin} of quantities bounded by C e^{−c d(b,Γ_C)} with C, c independent of the labels, the fields and r, then it is linear in the marks, (c) applies, and its history average is at most C N_ret Σ_b e^{−c d(b,Γ_C)} sup_e p_e^ϑ ≤ C N_ret S_c C_L n_C q_ϑ, uniform in the volume; without such a localisation the sum over all testable bonds is proportional to the volume, and if the cost is multiplicative in the bad bonds, (c) does not apply and item 5 must supply its own resummation. The bonds D ∖ D^{(0)} carry no marks; they form the label-independent boundary of Λ₁^{(0)} and are item 5's business in the same way as the original boundary of Λ₁.

 (c) No label entropy for the coverage cost. The collar labels are a partition of unity on each term: Σ_{B^{(k)}} ∏_{e∈B^{(k)}}1{e bad}∏_{e∉B^{(k)}}1{e good} = 1 pointwise, round by round, so by induction from the terminal rounds (termination: Lemma 3.1(a)) Σ_lab 1_lab = 1 pointwise on 𝔤^I × 𝔤^J, and Σ_lab w_ξ^{lab} = ∫_{𝔤^I} ρ_ξ Σ_lab 1_lab da_old = w_ξ (Tonelli; the labels are indicators of finitely many measurable sets). Every term is nonnegative and the cost in (a) is bounded by Σ_e M_e × (a local quantity), linear in the marks; summing the labelled terms back therefore gives Σ_e M_e × (local quantity) under the history average, with no product over labels and no factor depending on the number of tested bonds. This is established for the cost (a) only; for (b) see the last sentence there. (The first version of this note bounded the label sum by ∏_e(1 + q_ϑ); that bound needed a multi-witness factorisation which stage 3 does not supply and was not uniform in the volume. It is not needed.)

 Comparison. r = δ₀/g₀ = A₁(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀} (CRE p.246 L14, p.247: ε₀ = g₀A₀(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀}, δ₀ = (A₁/A₀)ε₀; r is not used in (3.1)–(3.2)). CRE (2.6) (p.255 L45–46, n = 1, m = 0): g₁ ≤ (1+β₀)g₀ and g₀ ≤ (1+β₀)g₁, so for small g₀, ½ log g₀⁻² ≤ log g₁⁻² ≤ 2 log g₀⁻². R₁ is the smallest power of L with R₁ ≥ (log g₁⁻²)^𝔯 (p.246 L25), so R₁ ≤ L(log g₁⁻²)^𝔯 ≤ L(2 log g₀⁻²)^𝔯 and

  ℓ = LMR₁ ≤ L²M(2 log g₀⁻²)^𝔯,   hence   log N_ret ≤ C + 4𝔯 log(2 log g₀⁻²)   and   C ℓ⁴ ≤ C(L²M)⁴2^{4𝔯}(log g₀⁻²)^{4𝔯}.                 (3.1)

With π_* ≥ A₀(½ log g₀⁻²)^{p₀} and p₀ ≥ 5𝔯 (p.246 L14),

  log(1/q_ϑ) ≥ c_qϑ²A₀²2^{−2p₀}(log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀} ≫ C(L²M)⁴2^{4𝔯}(log g₀⁻²)^{4𝔯} ≥ C ℓ⁴,                                            (3.2)

since 2p₀ ≥ 10𝔯 > 4𝔯. Hence for g₀ below a threshold depending on L, M, M₂, the group, the source constants and ϑ, N_ret q_ϑ ≤ e^{−c_qϑ²π_*²/2}; and for each C independent of g₀ there is a threshold, depending also on C, below which e^{Cℓ⁴}q_ϑ ≤ e^{−c_qϑ²π_*²/2} (C a constant of L, M, M₂, the group and the source constants; a per-term cost growing with r, say like r² ∝ (log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀}, is not covered by this comparison and would have to be paid differently). This is CRE's p₀ ≥ 5𝔯 accounting with the drafts' constants.

3.5 (continued in §4 below; the "not established" list of this paragraph is the state after item 3.) What items 2–3 establish and what they do not. Established: the exact exterior factorisation (2.1); the unavailability of a Schur lower bound and the withdrawal of §6(c) (2.2); the normalised payment (2.2)–(2.3) for every tested bad bond, with the stage 3 hypotheses checked for collar bonds (Lemmas 2.3–2.5), on compatible even fine periods; the label-independent round-0 filling with stage 3's witness bound p_z^{(0)} ≤ q for its complement (Lemma 3.0); the retreat with iterated filling, its termination, the no-re-entry property, the attribution of every removed point to a bad bond and the counts (Lemma 3.1); the final shift bound r/4 (Remark 3.2); the regions in CRE's filled normal form (2.3), the collar properties and the box form of the retreated young fibre, with the stated convention for Ψ_ξ, S₁, R₁ (Lemma 3.3); the two combinatorial inequalities and the history-average bound for the coverage and attachment cost with q replaced by q + N_ret q_ϑ (3.4(a)); the absence of label entropy for that cost (3.4(c)); and the comparison (3.2) for coupling-independent constants. Not established here: the conditional estimate for the retreated carrier, i.e. the response bound C_{resp,χ}g₀rD_ξ^{fin} and the selected-majorant lemma for the translated box (item 4); the boundary-activity bound and the form of its cost (item 5); and the bookkeeping of the labels (B^{(k)}) in stage 1's history sum (item 6).

## 4. Item 4: the selected majorant for the translated box

4.1 Setting. Fix a history and a labelled term with final region Λ := Λ₁^{fin} (a union of ℓ-cubes, Lemma 3.1), carrier D := D(Λ) = D^{fin} ⊂ I, exterior J(Λ) = E_{Ω,*} ∖ D and exterior variable A^{Λ,Ψ} = (Ψ_ξ(A_e), a_old|_{I∖D}) (Lemma 2.5). Write H := H_{D(Λ)D(Λ)}, ℓ_Λ := H_{D(Λ)J(Λ)} A^{Λ,Ψ} ∈ 𝔤^D and, for t ∈ [0,1],

  P^Λ_t(a) := Σ_{𝔭 near} 𝔭(a + tA^{Λ,Ψ}),   K^Λ_{ξ;t}(a) := 1_{𝒟^Λ}(a) exp[−½⟨a,Ha⟩ − t⟨a,ℓ_Λ⟩ + P^Λ_t(a)],   I^Λ_t := ∫_{𝔤^D} K^Λ_{ξ;t} da,   dν^Λ_t := K^Λ_{ξ;t} da / I^Λ_t,

the retreated split's version of stage 1's exterior interpolation (eq:native-source-boundary-interpolation): near means near relative to D(Λ) (Lemma 2.5), 𝔭 runs over the near primitives of all three kinds, and for Λ = Λ₁ these are stage 1's P_{ξ,t}, K_{ξ;t}, I_{ξ;t}, ν_{ξ;t}; at t = 1, K^Λ_{ξ;1} is that of Lemma 2.5. On the support of the term's exterior cutoffs (the branch indicator ι_ξ, the collar indicators and the box factors on I ∖ D(Λ), Lemma 2.5) the retreated young fibre is nonempty and is the box: by stage 1 Lemma lem:collar-coverage(iv) the original fibre over the branch data is the box over I, and the box factors on I ∖ D(Λ) place (a, a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)}) in it for every a with |a_b|_σ < r, b ∈ D(Λ), so the fibre of the retreated split over (A^{Λ,Ψ}) is nonempty and Lemma 3.3(iv) gives 1_{𝒟^Λ}(a) = Θ_D(a) := ∏_{b∈D} χ_b(a), χ_b(a) := 1{|a_b|_σ < r}; off that support the term vanishes, and nothing is claimed for it. (This nonemptiness is used for I^Λ_t > 0 in Lemma 4.0(c), for 1_{𝒟^Λ}(B + tm) = Θ^t(B) in (4.2), and for the identity hard cutoff in Lemma 4.4.) The mean shift of the retreated split is m := −H⁻¹ℓ_Λ (Definition §1 with D(Λ), J(Λ)), and by Lemma 1.2 (its proof uses (I1)–(I3) for H_{D(Λ)D(Λ)} only, which hold by principal restriction, §0) m depends on A^{Λ,Ψ}|_{J₁(Λ)} only, J₁(Λ) ⊂ J_col(Λ). The final collar is good (Lemma 3.1(b)), |A^{Λ,Ψ}_e|_σ = |A^Λ_e|_σ < ϑr for every e ∈ J_col(Λ) (Ψ_ξ is the identity there, Lemma 2.3(a); on I ∖ D(Λ) by (eq:c-collar-distance)), so (1.2) gives

  sup_{b∈D(Λ)} |m_b|_σ ≤ r/4,   hence   sup_b t|m_b|_σ ≤ r/4 for every t ∈ [0,1].                                           (4.1)

Completing the square (Definition §1): −½⟨a,Ha⟩ − t⟨a,ℓ_Λ⟩ = −½⟨a − tm, H(a − tm)⟩ + ½t²⟨m,Hm⟩.

Lemma 4.0 (the final term is a branch for the stage 1–2 estimate lemmas). (a) H = H_{D(Λ)D(Λ)} satisfies stage 1's (eq:fixed-kernel-inputs), γ id ≤ H ≤ h₊ id with γ = γ_L/2, h₊ = m_L, and the entry range ρ_L ≤ R: this is (I1), (I3) for the principal restriction (§0). Hence Lemmas lem:walk-series, lem:conditioning, lem:component-blocks and lem:gaussian-payment hold for H with the constants n_*, R_*, C_w, δ_M, A_*, C_d, α_G of stage 1–2 (stage 2 L416–420: these lemmas "use only (eq:fixed-kernel-inputs), the range and the partitions"). (b) Retained inputs near the carrier. Let r^Λ_e := max{|A^{Λ,Ψ}(b)|_σ : b ∈ J(Λ), dist(b, D(Λ)) ≤ 3R_c} (zero if the set is empty), the retreated split's r_{e,ξ} (stage 3 §1). Then r^Λ_e < r: a bond b ∈ J within 3R_c of D(Λ) ⊂ I is within 3R_c of I, so by Lemma lem:collar-coverage(i),(iii) for the original term it lies in 𝒢_ξ ∖ S₁*, Ψ_ξ is the identity at b and |A_e(b)|_σ < r on the branch; a bond b ∈ I ∖ D(Λ) carries a_old(b) with |a_old(b)|_σ < r (its box factor is a factor of the retained cutoff of the term, Lemma 2.5) and Ψ_ξ is the identity on I (eq:c-collar-distance). So s^Λ := 1 + r + r^Λ_e ≤ 1 + 2r. On the collar J_col(Λ) (range ρ_L = 4L ≤ 3R_c) the sharper bound < ϑr of Lemma 3.1(b) holds; it is used only through (4.1). (c) Charts and primitive bounds. A row within 2R_c + 2L of D(Λ) is within 2R_c + 2L of I, so stage 2's applicability paragraph (Lemmas c2-plaquette-deviation, c2-local-gauge, c2-uniform-chart, which need only that the row's two-block set lies within 3R_c of I and the retained inputs there are below r, supplied by (b)) applies to it; hence Lemma lem:c2-primitive-bounds holds for the near primitives of the retreated split under stage 2's threshold item 3, 4C_Cg₀(1+2r) ≤ ϑ_*/2, with s_ξ replaced by s^Λ ≤ 1 + 2r: every near primitive 𝔭 is, at the input a + tA^{Λ,Ψ}, the restriction to real arguments of a function analytic in (a,t) on a complex neighbourhood of the enlarged box {|a_b|_σ ≤ 2r ∀ b ∈ D(Λ)} × [0,1], with |𝔭(a + tA^{Λ,Ψ})| ≤ C_p g₀ (s^Λ)³ for the plaquette and gauge primitives and the smaller bounds C_p g₀²(s^Λ)², C_p g₀ s^Λ for the Haar and Jacobian primitives (g₀ ≤ 1 ≤ s^Λ), and the derivative bounds (eq:convex-primitive-derivatives) with s^Λ; and Lemma lem:charts holds at D(Λ), so the exponent of K^Λ_{ξ;t} is real and finite on the open box and K^Λ_{ξ;t} > 0 there, I^Λ_t > 0. (d) Hosts. The host Y_𝔭 of a primitive (eq:part1-potential-host) and the probe host Y_b of a bond (eq:c-probe-host) are defined from the lifted stencil and M alone; the localisation family of the retreated split is 𝐃₀(Λ) := {Y_𝔭 : 𝔭 near relative to D(Λ)} ∪ {Y_b : b ∈ D(Λ)}, with E(Y) := {b ∈ D(Λ) : b ∩ Y ≠ ∅}, and the free-input set of a primitive assigned to Y lies in Y (Lemma lem:row-locality), its young part in E(Y). The counts of Lemma lem:c2-potential-size — at most N_h primitives per host and d₀(Y) ≤ B_h + 4 — are counts over templates and anchor positions, independent of the carrier. Hence, for Y ∈ 𝐃₀(Λ), t ∈ [0,1] and a in the local box {|a_b|_σ < r ∀ b ∈ E(Y)},

  𝐕^Λ_{ξ,t}(Y,a) := Σ_{𝔭 near, Y_𝔭 = Y} 𝔭(a + tA^{Λ,Ψ})   satisfies   |𝐕^Λ_{ξ,t}(Y,a)| ≤ N_h C_p g₀ (s^Λ)³ ≤ ω_t e^{−κd₀(Y)},   ω_t := C₀ g₀ (1+2r)³,          (4.4)

by (c), d₀(Y) ≤ B_h + 4 and C₀ = N_h C_p e^{κ(B_h+4)} (eq:part1-host-multiplicity); 𝐕^Λ_{ξ,t}(Y,·) := 0 off the local box and on hosts carrying no near primitive. For t = 0 and Λ = Λ₁ this is stage 1's 𝐕_ξ with the bound ω(g₀) = C₀g₀(1+r)³ ≤ ω_t; the factor (1+2r)³ in place of (1+r)³ is the retained input, which enters for t > 0. ∎

4.2 The translated objects. Let B := a − tm be the centred variable, a = B + tm. Put, for b ∈ D(Λ) and Y ∈ 𝐃₀(Λ),

  χ^t_b(B) := χ_b(B + tm) = 1{|B_b + tm_b|_σ < r},   Θ^t(B) := ∏_{b∈D(Λ)} χ^t_b(B),   𝐖_t(Y,B) := 𝐕^Λ_{ξ,t}(Y, B + tm);

χ^t_b is a Borel function of B_b alone with values in {0,1}, 𝐖_t(Y,·) depends on B through B|_{E(Y)} only (m is a fixed vector), and 𝐖_t(Y,B) = 0 unless |B_b + tm_b|_σ < r for all b ∈ E(Y) (the translated local box), where |𝐖_t(Y,B)| ≤ ω_t e^{−κd₀(Y)} by (4.4). Translation invariance of Lebesgue measure gives, for every bounded Borel F on 𝔤^{D(Λ)},

  ∫ F(a) K^Λ_{ξ;t}(a) da = e^{½t²⟨m,Hm⟩} ∫ F(B + tm) Θ^t(B) exp[−½⟨B,HB⟩ + Σ_{Y∈𝐃₀(Λ)} 𝐖_t(Y,B)] dB,                                     (4.2)

since 1_{𝒟^Λ}(B + tm) = Θ^t(B) and, where Θ^t(B) = 1, Σ_Y 𝐖_t(Y,B) = Σ_{𝔭 near} 𝔭(B + tm + tA^{Λ,Ψ}) = P^Λ_t(B + tm) (each near primitive is assigned to exactly one host). The constant e^{½t²⟨m,Hm⟩} is the same in the numerator and the denominator of every conditional expectation,

  ∫ F dν^Λ_t = ∫ F(B + tm) Θ^t e^{−½⟨B,HB⟩ + Σ_Y 𝐖_t(Y,B)} dB / ∫ Θ^t e^{−½⟨B,HB⟩ + Σ_Y 𝐖_t(Y,B)} dB,                                     (4.3)

and is never estimated: its logarithm is at most ½h₊ Σ_b h(m_b)² ≤ h₊|D(Λ)|r²/32 (h ≤ |·|_σ, t ≤ 1), a constant of the order of the carrier volume, which disappears in (4.3). Every use of the translated expansion below is through ratios of the form (4.3) or through quantities normalised by Ξ^{f,t}_Λ (Corollary 4.3).

Lemma 4.1 (translated polymer representation). With 𝔫_Λ := (2π)^{d_G|D(Λ)|/2} det(H)^{−1/2},

  ∫ Θ^t(B) exp[−½⟨B,HB⟩ + Σ_Y 𝐖_t(Y,B)] dB = 𝔫_Λ Ξ^{f,t}_Λ,   Ξ^{f,t}_Λ := Σ_{𝒫 compatible} ∏_{Z∈𝒫} k^{f,t}_Λ(Z),   k^{f,t}_Λ(Z) := Σ_{(𝒟,𝖯) : Z₀ ≠ ∅, Z₀′ ⊆ Z} 𝒜^{f,t}_{(𝒟,𝖯,Z)}[1],

where the selections 𝔰 = (𝒟,𝖯,Z) are those of Definition def:selection for the carrier D(Λ) and the family 𝐃₀(Λ) (𝒟 ⊆ 𝐃₀(Λ) finite, 𝖯 ⊆ D(Λ) ∖ E(Y₀) finite, seed Z₀, E₀ = E(Z₀), enlargement Z₀′, final domain Z ⊇ Z₀′ a union of LM-cubes), and the translated selected integral of a bounded Borel F on 𝔤^{E₀} is (eq:c-selected-integral) with χ_b replaced by χ^t_b and 𝐖 by 𝐖_t:

  𝒜^{f,t}_𝔰[F] = ∏_{Δ∈π₁(Z)∖π₁(Z₀′)} ∫₀¹ds_Δ ∮_{|σ_Δ|=e^{κ₁}} dσ_Δ/(2πi(σ_Δ − s_Δ)²) ∏_{Y∈𝒟} ∫₀¹dt_Y ∫dμ₀(X) ∫dμ_{C_{E₀}}(B) e^{−½⟨Γ_{E₀}(σ)X, C_{E₀}Γ_{E₀}(σ)X⟩} e^{−⟨B,Γ_{E₀}(σ)X⟩} (−1)^{|𝖯|} ∏_{b∈E(Y₀)} χ^t_b(B) ∏_{b∈𝖯} (1 − χ^t_b(B)) ∏_{Y∈𝒟} 𝐖_t(Y,B) e^{t_Y 𝐖_t(Y,B)} F(B),

with C_{E₀}, Γ_{E₀}(σ), μ₀, μ_{C_{E₀}} built from H = H_{D(Λ)D(Λ)}. Consequently Ξ^{f,t}_Λ = e^{−½t²⟨m,Hm⟩} I^Λ_t / 𝔫_Λ > 0. For each selection 𝔰 and each bounded Borel F on 𝔤^{E₀} the translated selected integral 𝒜^{f,t}_𝔰[F] is a well-defined finite complex number. No representation of the numerator of (4.3) for an insertion is asserted in this lemma: the insertion of a function of the young variables requires the probe-host (forced-seed) expansion of stage 1's probe-kernel section for the t-family, which is item 5.

Proof. Stage 1's proof of Lemma lem:polymer is reproduced with χ_b → χ^t_b, 𝐖 → 𝐖_t, I → D(Λ), 𝐃₀ → 𝐃₀(Λ). The properties of the cutoff and of the potential family that proof uses are: (i) Θ = ∏_b χ_b with each χ_b a Borel function of a_b alone with values in [0,1] — in the expansion Θ = ∏_{E(Y₀)} χ_b ∏_{I∖E(Y₀)} (1 − (1 − χ_b)) with the sign (−1)^{|𝖯|}, and in the factorisation over the components of Z, where a bad bond lies in the component containing its incident cubes and a cutoff factor is a function of one bond variable; χ^t_b has these properties. (ii) 𝐖(Y,·) depends on a through a|_{E(Y)}, with E(Y) ⊆ E₀ for Y ∈ 𝒟, and is bounded where ∏_{b∈E(Y)} χ_b ≠ 0 — in the reduction to a|_{E₀} that lets Lemma lem:conditioning produce (eq:fixed-kernel-density), and in the Fubini steps (bounded factors times an absolutely integrable density); 𝐖_t has these properties, with the translated local box in place of the local box, by 4.2. (iii) Everything else — the walk series (lem:walk-series), the conditioning identity and the weakened density (lem:conditioning, eq:fixed-kernel-density), the block structure (lem:component-blocks), the integrability of the absolute density uniformly on the closed polydisc (lem:gaussian-payment(ii)), the Cauchy representation of the σ-derivatives and the vanishing of unseeded components, and the geometry of seeds, enlargements and the coarse cover — involves H, the partitions π₀, π₁ of the torus and σ only, and holds for H = H_{D(Λ)D(Λ)} by Lemma 4.0(a); the host and probe geometry is that of 4.0(d). The expansion of exp(Σ_Y 𝐖_t) into Mayer factors ∫₀¹ 𝐖_t e^{t_Y𝐖_t} dt_Y is the same algebra. Positivity: by (4.2) with F = 1, 𝔫_Λ Ξ^{f,t}_Λ = e^{−½t²⟨m,Hm⟩} I^Λ_t, and I^Λ_t > 0 by Lemma 4.0(c). Well-definedness of 𝒜^{f,t}_𝔰[F]: the integrand is a bounded function (|F| ≤ sup|F|, cutoffs ≤ 1, |𝐖_t e^{t_Y𝐖_t}| ≤ ω_t e^{ω_t} by (4.4)) times the density (eq:fixed-kernel-density), whose modulus is integrable uniformly on the closed polydisc by lem:gaussian-payment(ii) for H (Lemma 4.0(a)); the finitely many s-, σ- and t_Y-integrals are over compact sets. ∎

Lemma 4.2 (translated selected majorant). Let g₀ satisfy stage 2's thresholds (app:thresholds, items 1–5) and in addition

  (4.T)  C₀ g₀ (1+2r)³ ≤ ω_*   (i.e. ω_t ≤ ω_*; implied by 8C₀g₀(1+r)³ ≤ ω_*, a strengthening of threshold item 4 by the factor 8),
  (4.T′)  (9/16) a_bad ≥ max(160(κ + log(8M⁴ + 8) + 1), 64 log(1/η_*)),   a_bad = c_n α_G r²/4   (eq:native-source-bad-budget for (9/16)a_bad).

Both are conditions on g₀ alone of the kind already in stage 2 (g₀(1+r)³ → 0 and a_bad → ∞ as g₀ → 0, r = A₁(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀}), with thresholds depending on L, the group, M, κ, α_G, ω_* and the cutoff parameters A₁, p₀ only (through r(g₀), as stage 2's threshold item 4 does). Then on every labelled term with final region Λ, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs (where (4.1) and r^Λ_e < r hold, Lemmas 3.1(b) and 3.3(iii); the term vanishes elsewhere), for every t ∈ [0,1], every selection 𝔰 = (𝒟,𝖯,Z) for D(Λ) and every bounded Borel F on 𝔤^{E₀},

  |𝒜^{f,t}_𝔰[F]| ≤ sup|F| · e^{−(κ₁−1)N₁(Z∖Z₀′) + N₁(Z) − (9/16)a_bad|𝖯|} ∏_{Y∈𝒟} ω_* e^{1/2} e^{−κd₀(Y)}.                                           (4.5)

The right side depends neither on t, nor on m or the exterior data A^{Λ,Ψ} within that support, nor on the labels; bondwise, the reserve is (1 − t|m_b|_σ/r)² a_bad ≥ (9/16)a_bad.

Proof. Stage 2's proof of Lemma lem:c2-selected-majorant with three substitutions. The weakening step is unchanged: for each cube Δ of Z ∖ Z₀′ the operation G ↦ ∫₀¹ds ∮ G dσ_Δ/(2πi(σ_Δ − s)²) on the inner integral as a function of σ_Δ on the circle |σ_Δ| = e^{κ₁} is bounded by e^{−(κ₁−1)} times the supremum of the inner integral's modulus over the distinguished boundary, by e^{κ₁}(e^{κ₁} − 1)⁻² ≤ e^{−(κ₁−1)} (e^{κ₁} ≥ 3); it needs the continuity of the integrand in σ (the walk series, lem:walk-series for H) and the uniform integrability below. On the distinguished boundary, the inner integrand's modulus is bounded factor by factor: ∫₀¹dt_Y has mass one; χ^t_b ≤ 1; for b ∈ 𝖯 and real B, by the triangle inequality for the norm |·|_σ and (4.1),

  1 − χ^t_b(B) = 1{|B_b + tm_b|_σ ≥ r} ≤ 1{|B_b|_σ ≥ r − t|m_b|_σ} ≤ 1{|B_b|_σ ≥ (1 − t|m_b|_σ/r) r} ≤ 1{|B_b|_σ ≥ 3r/4},

and on {|X|_σ ≥ 3r/4} the group inequality h(X)² ≥ c_n|X|_σ² of lem:gaussian-payment(iii) (c_n = 1/N serves for both cutoff norms, stage 2 conventions) gives h(X)² ≥ (9/16)c_n r², so 1{|B_b|_σ ≥ 3r/4} ≤ exp[(γ₂/2)(h(B_b)² − (9/16)c_n r²)] for every γ₂ > 0 (the exponent is nonnegative on the set); with γ₂ = α_G/2, a_bad = c_nγ₂r²/2 = c_nα_Gr²/4 and ‖B‖² = Σ_b h(B_b)²,

  ∏_{b∈𝖯} (1 − χ^t_b(B)) ≤ e^{−(9/16)a_bad|𝖯|} e^{(α_G/2)Σ_{b∈𝖯}h(B_b)²} ≤ e^{−(9/16)a_bad|𝖯|} e^{(α_G/2)‖B‖²}

(this is stage 1's (iii) at the radius 3r/4 in place of r; bondwise the same argument gives the reserve (1 − t|m_b|_σ/r)²a_bad); for Y ∈ 𝒟, on the support of ∏_{b∈E(Y)}χ^t_b the point B + tm lies in the local box of Y, so |𝐖_t(Y,B)| ≤ ω_t e^{−κd₀(Y)} ≤ ω_* e^{−κd₀(Y)} by (4.4) and (4.T), and |𝐖_t e^{t_Y𝐖_t}| ≤ ω_* e^{ω_*} e^{−κd₀(Y)} ≤ ω_* e^{1/2} e^{−κd₀(Y)}, since ω_* ≤ 1/2 (η_* = 2e^{1/2+5κ}ω_* ≤ 1/32); |F| ≤ sup|F|; and the integral of the modulus of the density (eq:fixed-kernel-density) against e^{(α_G/2)‖B‖²} is at most e^{N₁(Z)} by lem:gaussian-payment(ii) for H (Lemma 4.0(a)), under stage 2's conditions on δ_M and α_G (threshold list, app:thresholds). Multiplying the bounds and applying the weakening bound once per cube of Z ∖ Z₀′ gives (4.5). ∎

Corollary 4.3 (activity bounds and vacuum clusters for the t-family). Under the hypotheses of Lemma 4.2, for every t ∈ [0,1] and every connected final domain Z for D(Λ),

  |k^{f,t}_Λ(Z)| ≤ ε_* e^{−λ_*ℓ₁(Z)},   Σ_{Z′ ≁ Z} |k^{f,t}_Λ(Z′)| e^{(1+6θ)N₁(Z′)} ≤ N₁(Z)/8,

with the constants λ_*, ε_*, θ of stage 2 (eq:c2-kappa-choice)–(eq:native-source-vacuum-KP-budget), and Lemma lem:vacuum-clusters holds for the cluster weights Φ^{v,t}_Λ(𝔠) = (φ^T(𝔠)/m!) ∏_j k^{f,t}_Λ(D_j): the hitting sum Σ_{𝔠 hits 𝒜} |Φ^{v,t}_Λ(𝔠)| e^{2θΣ_jN₁(D_j)} ≤ (1/8)Σ_{D∈𝒜}N₁(D) and the identity (Ξ^{f,t}_Λ)^{[∪𝒜]}/Ξ^{f,t}_Λ = exp(−Σ_{𝔠 hits 𝒜} Φ^{v,t}_Λ(𝔠)), uniformly in t, m, the exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, and the labels.

Proof. Stage 2's proof of Lemma lem:activity-bounds uses, about the activities, only the majorant (eq:fixed-kernel-selected-majorant) — here (4.5) with F = 1 — with ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)} ≤ η_*e^{−κℓ₀(Y)} (ℓ₀ = d₀ + 5 and η_* = 2e^{1/2+5κ}ω_*), and the number a_bad through the conditions it draws from (eq:native-source-bad-budget): a_bad ≥ 160(κ+1) > 1 and 64M⁴e^{−a_bad/4} ≤ 1 (bad-label sum), a_bad/32 ≥ 5κ (component reserve), e^{−a_bad/64} ≤ η_* ≤ t₀ (the case k_j = 0), and C_g e^{−a_bad/64} ≤ 162e^{−5(κ+1)/2} ≤ κ/40 (choice of Y₀). Each of these holds for every number not smaller than one satisfying (eq:native-source-bad-budget); (4.T′) says that (9/16)a_bad satisfies it, so the proof applies verbatim with a_bad replaced by (9/16)a_bad and η_*, t₀, ω_*, κ, δ, α₆, C_g, M, L unchanged (the geometric inputs Lemma c2-domain-geometry, Lemma c2-cover-sum and Lemma c2-scale concern the partitions only). The proof of Lemma lem:vacuum-clusters (stage 2 app:kp, Kotecký–Preiss with the pinned-tree bound) uses only the two displayed bounds and the geometry (stage 2 L420: these proofs "use nothing else about the branch"). ∎

Lemma 4.4 (the conditional estimate at the retreated carrier). Under stage 3's thresholds C_P g₀(1+2r) ≤ γ/2 and r ≥ 1 (stage 3 §3 uses r ≥ 1 in O(g₀s²/r) = O(g₀s); both are conditions on g₀) and the hypotheses of Lemma 4.0, on every labelled term with final region Λ, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, and on the covered event A_cov^{fin} of 3.4(a) (every contour bond in Ω₁, every free input of the contour in D(Λ)),

  |ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ]| ≤ C_{resp,χ} g₀ r D^Λ_ξ,   ℬ^Λ_ξ[f] := ∫ f dν^Λ_1 − ∫ f dν^Λ_0 = ∫₀¹ Cov_{ν^Λ_t}(f, L^Λ_t) dt,   D^Λ_ξ := Σ_{b ∈ I_C ∩ D(Λ)} e^{−(μ/2) d(b, J(Λ))},

with the constant C_{resp,χ} and the rate μ = R⁻¹log(1 + γ/(4M_*)), M_* = m_L + γ/2, of stage 3 §§4–5, independent of g₀, the term, the labels, the volume and t; D^Λ_ξ is the D_ξ^{fin} of 3.4(a), and L^Λ_t := −⟨·,ℓ_Λ⟩ + ∂_tP^Λ_t.

Proof. Stage 3 §§1–5 are applied with (I, J, A_e, H, ℓ, P_t, r_{e,ξ}) replaced by (D(Λ), J(Λ), A^{Λ,Ψ}, H_{D(Λ)D(Λ)}, ℓ_Λ, P^Λ_t, r^Λ_e). The inputs they use, each supplied: γ ≤ H ≤ m_L and the finite range R of H and of the primitive stencils (Lemma 4.0(a); stage 3 §2 takes R = 8L+8 ≥ ρ_L); the primitive bounds on the enlarged box at the inputs a + tA^{Λ,Ψ}, uniformly in t, with s^Λ ≤ 1 + 2r (Lemma 4.0(c)), giving ‖D²_aP^Λ_t‖ ≤ C_Pg₀s^Λ and the Schur row/column bounds of §3; r^Λ_e < r for the score bound h(∇_b∂_tP^Λ_t) ≤ C_Sg₀s^Λ r^Λ_e 1_{K^Λ}(b) and h((ℓ_Λ)_b) ≤ m_L r^Λ_e 1_{K^Λ}(b), K^Λ := {b ∈ D(Λ) : dist(b, J(Λ)) ≤ R} (Lemma 4.0(b) and (I2)); the source gradient bound sup h(∇_b f_ξ) ≤ C_{resp,χ}g₀ 1_{I_C}(b) on the enlarged box, from the rowwise reconstruction and the uniform charts for the rows meeting D(Λ) (Lemma 4.0(c); the links of the contour depend on a|_{D(Λ)} through those rows, the de-younged bonds being constants of the term); the identity ℬ = ∫₀¹Cov dt, which is the differentiation of t ↦ ∫f dν^Λ_t under the integral (the exponent of K^Λ_{ξ;t} is smooth in t with bounded derivative on the box, Lemma 4.0(c)); the extension T_r of §3, the barrier β_n and the Helffer–Sjöstrand identity of §4, which involve only the box {|a_b|_σ < r, b ∈ D(Λ)}, the Hessian bounds and the Schur bounds; and the shell summation of §5 with d(c, J(Λ)) ≤ R on K^Λ. The contour bonds whose free inputs are in D(Λ) are exactly those counted by I_C ∩ D(Λ) on A_cov^{fin}; the others do not depend on the young variable. Nothing in stage 3 §§1–5 uses that the carrier is Λ₁^*. ∎

4.3 What item 4 establishes and what it does not. Established: the retreated split's exterior interpolation K^Λ_{ξ;t} and the bound (4.1) on its mean shift; the exact translation identities (4.2)–(4.3), with the constant e^{½t²⟨m,Hm⟩} cancelling in every conditional expectation and never estimated; the translated polymer representation (Lemma 4.1) for the normalisation I^Λ_t, and the well-definedness of the translated selected integrals 𝒜^{f,t}_𝔰[F] for bounded insertions F on 𝔤^{E₀} (no representation of an inserted numerator is claimed in item 4; that is the probe-host expansion of item 5); the translated selected majorant (4.5), uniform in t ∈ [0,1], in m, in the exterior data within the support of the term's exterior cutoffs and in the labels, with the reserve (1 − t|m_b|_σ/r)²a_bad ≥ (9/16)a_bad, the hosts unchanged and the primitives evaluated at a = B + tm in the original local box (so the chart condition is stage 2's threshold item 3 at s^Λ ≤ 1 + 2r; the enlarged box {|a_b|_σ ≤ 2r} is not needed for the majorant and is available for analyticity, B ↦ B + tm mapping {|B_b|_σ ≤ 7r/4} into it); the activity bounds and the vacuum cluster expansion for the t-family with stage 2's constants (Corollary 4.3), under the two additional coupling thresholds (4.T), (4.T′); and stage 3's conditional estimate at the retreated carrier (Lemma 4.4), which is the response bound C_{resp,χ}g₀rD_ξ^{fin} that 3.4(a) uses. Not established here: anything about the source-containing connected terms attached to J(Λ) — the translated expansion gives the vacuum activities k^{f,t}_Λ, their clusters and a majorant for selected integrals with a bounded insertion F; the insertion of the source through the probe hosts of 𝐃₀(Λ), the t-centred reference, the marked connected terms and their bound of the form κ_C⁻¹|ℬ_ξ[f_ξ]| ≤ C n_C(1+r)² Σ_{e∈J} e^{−μ′dist(e,C)} × (history weights) are item 5 — and the pass note's expectation that they follow from (4.5) with "F the source" is not verified by anything here, since f_ξ(B + tm) is not a function of B|_{E₀} for a selection unrelated to the contour; and the bookkeeping of the labels in stage 1's history sum (item 6). The two thresholds (4.T), (4.T′) must be added to stage 2's threshold item 4 wherever the t-family is used.

## 5. Item 5: the source-containing connected boundary terms

5.0 Setting and target. Throughout §5 fix a history, a labelled term with final region Λ, carrier D := D(Λ), exterior J(Λ), exterior variable A^{Λ,Ψ}, shift m, and the t-family K^Λ_{ξ;t}, ν^Λ_t, 𝐖_t, χ^t_b of §4, with the exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs (where (4.1), r^Λ_e < r and Lemma 4.0 hold; the term vanishes elsewhere, and every bound below is a bound on a factor of the term that is multiplied by those indicators — caveat A2 of review r2), on the covered event A_cov^{fin} of 3.4(a) (every contour bond in Ω₁, every free input of the contour in D(Λ); so I_C ⊆ D(Λ), and f_ξ(a) = W_χ(C; U_ξ(a)) depends on a ∈ 𝔤^{D(Λ)} through a|_{I_C} only, the de-younged bonds I ∖ D(Λ) being constants of the term, Lemma 4.4). Write

  F^Λ(t) := ∫ f_ξ dν^Λ_t,   ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ] = F^Λ(1) − F^Λ(0)   (Lemma 4.4),

and n_C for the number of traversals of C, I_C for the set of free-input bonds of the contour (each within ℓ^∞ distance 2L+1 of a contour bond: stage 1's probe-host construction, L606, "the forward influence set ℛ_b … lies within ℓ^∞ distance 2L+1 of b", applied to the contour bond r with b ∈ 𝓕_r; hence dist(e, I_C) ≥ dist(e, C) − (2L+1) in the distance convention of the geometry paragraph below), J₁(Λ) := {e ∈ J(Λ) : H(b′,e) ≠ 0 for some b′ ∈ D(Λ)} ⊆ J_col(Λ) (Lemma 1.2 at the retreated carrier, 4.1). The target of item 5 (pass note §7 item 5, §4 L46) is a bound on ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ] relative to the reference gas, with the perimeter factor extracted inside the connected terms before absolute values, of the form (reference) × Σ_{e∈J(Λ)} e^{−μ′dist(e,C)} × (polynomial in r) × (history weights). The reference used here is S°_Λ, the reference group gas at the carrier D(Λ) (5.3); its identification with κ_Ce^{E°_ξ} and the comparison with S°_{ξ,C} at the carrier I are stated in 5.5 as what is and is not supplied.

Inputs beyond §§0–4 (used as stated, at their stated hypotheses; their own verification is not item 5's): stage 1 lem:probe-measures, lem:measure-identity, prop:centred-expectation (L603–682) and sec:joint-extraction (def:groups, lem:grouped-source, the reference data, lem:schur, prop:joint-extraction, L684–760); stage 4 signed-defect draft §4.2 and §7.1 (the dummy comparison Σ_{ℓ: Z(ℓ)=Z, 𝖩≠∅} u_*^{q(ℓ)}‖Λ^f_{ξ;ℓ}‖_TV ≤ m(Z) := ε_*e^{−λ_*ℓ₁(Z)}, u_* = min(1/4, ω_*e^{−κs_p}/(8P_M)), proved by the positive host comparison from the selected majorant and stage 2's cover/seed sums), §6.1 (the closed-contraction factorisation of R_ξ(𝔅) and the product inequality |R_ξ(𝔅)| ≤ ∏_W K_χ𝔡_W, K_χ the constant of the irreducible character); attempt file §13 (the reference exclusion ratio q°(𝔅) = S°^{[𝔅]}/S° = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}Φ°(Γ)) by Kotecký–Preiss for the reference group gas), §18.3 and §20.1 (the positive domain/cluster/interval summation giving the footprint and contour pins sup_Δ Σ_{W: Δ∈F(W)} e^{νσ(W)}M(W) ≤ Cε and sup_j Σ_{W: j∈ℋ(W)} e^{νσ(W)}M(W) ≤ Cε for a positive group majorant M(W) with root weights ρ m(Z) and the positive cluster tree weights, ν the support reserve, σ(W) the size, F(W) the footprint), §19.3 (the connected envelope E(W) of a group has at most A_Eσ(W) cubes and is connected under maximum-norm adjacency, so a group whose footprint contains the cube Δ has σ(W) ≥ dist(Δ, C)/(A_ELM)), §19.4 (the positive tree bound for the reference gas: the Kotecký–Preiss theorem with activities |a_W|e^{3ηs(W)/2} and node cost ηs(W)/8, giving the rooted bound Σ_{Γ∋W}m_W(Γ)|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ |a_W|e^{13ηs(W)/8} and the exclusion identity); stage 4 §6.2 (the positive source-containing group summation with the weights e^{4η𝔰(W)} under CρLM ≤ η/8, and the fact that incompatible groups have touching footprints or overlapping intervals); the TeX's tree-graph justification following eq:marginal-pinned-bound (|φ^T| ≤ number of spanning trees of the incompatibility graph, used in stage 2 app:kp); §20.2 (the far event N_b = {dist(Γ_C, E(Λ₁^c)) ≤ b}, b = ⌊LMR₁/16⌋). Stage 4 §4.1 and attempt §§18–20 are drafts whose status is their own; item 5 uses them as inputs and says so in 5.5 (the finite-difference bound of stage 4 §4.1 is not used as an input: Lemma 5.7⁰ proves the form needed here from stage 1 lem:charts and stage 2 lem:c2-uniform-chart).

Geometry. Throughout §5 and §6 the square/torus geometry of the TeX's subsection "Extent Weights and the Square Geometry" is in force, as imposed by stage 4 §6 (L227) and attempt §18.3: C is a coarse square of side s = nL fine bonds, n_C = 4s, on fine periods LK_i with K_i ≥ 3(n+2), together with the period condition of §3.1 (compatible even periods exceeding 200ℓ). The constants C_pin, A_F, A_E and the hull/extent bounds of stage 4 §6.2 and attempt §§18.3, 19.3 are proved under this geometry, and Lemmas 5.3(d), 5.8, Proposition 5.9 and Corollary 5.10 inherit it as a hypothesis. Distance convention (review r3): every distance in §§5–6 is the ℓ^∞ distance in fine units; the distance of a bond is measured from its initial site, that of a cube or host from its nearest point, and the distance to C is the distance to the vertex set of C (so dist(b, C) = 0 for a contour bond b, whose initial site is a vertex, and dist(Δ, C) = 0 for a cube of C(ℋ)); the counts 4(2s+1)⁴ and the conversions by LM + 1, 4M + LM, 2L + 1 below are stated in this convention.

5.1 The decay form of the mean shift. Lemma 1.2 bounds sup_b h(m_b); item 5 needs the dependence on the distance to the exterior.

Lemma 5.1. For every b ∈ D(Λ),

  h(m_b) ≤ C_m ϑ r Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)} e^{−μ_H d(b,e)},   C_m := γ⁻¹ m_L · 4(2ρ_L+1)⁴ e^{μ_Hρ_L},                                                   (5.1)

with μ_H = γ/(h₊ρ_L) of Lemma 1.1 and d the ℓ^∞ distance of initial sites in fine units. Consequently, with N_lat(μ) = Σ_{s≥0}4(2s+1)⁴e^{−μs} of Lemma 1.2,

  Σ_{b∈D(Λ)} h(m_b) e^{−μ dist(b,X)} ≤ C_m ϑ r N_lat(μ_H/2) Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)} e^{−min(μ_H/2, μ) dist(e,X)}   for every set X of bonds or of sites (dist(b, X) the ℓ^∞ distance from the initial site of b; Proposition 5.9 takes X = the vertex set of C) and μ ≥ 0,      (5.2)

in particular Σ_{b∈I_C} h(m_b) ≤ C_m ϑ r N_lat(μ_H/2) Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)} e^{−μ_H dist(e,I_C)/2}.

Proof. m_b = −Σ_{b′∈D(Λ)} H⁻¹(b,b′) Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)} H(b′,e) A^{Λ,Ψ}_e (Definition §1, Lemma 1.2). By (I1), H(b′,e) ≠ 0 forces d(b′,e) ≤ ρ_L, hence d(b,b′) ≥ d(b,e) − ρ_L; by Lemma 1.1, ‖H⁻¹(b,b′)‖ ≤ γ⁻¹e^{−μ_H d(b,b′)} ≤ γ⁻¹e^{μ_Hρ_L}e^{−μ_H d(b,e)} for such b′; the number of b′ with d(b′,e) ≤ ρ_L is at most 4(2ρ_L+1)⁴; each entry has ‖H(b′,e)‖ ≤ Σ_{b″}‖H(b′,b″)‖ ≤ m_L by (I2) for the row b′ ∈ D(Λ); and h(A^{Λ,Ψ}_e) ≤ |A^{Λ,Ψ}_e|_σ < ϑr for e ∈ J₁(Λ) ⊆ J_col(Λ) (Lemma 3.1(b), 4.1). Multiplying gives (5.1). For (5.2): e^{−μ_H d(b,e)} e^{−μ dist(b,X)} ≤ e^{−μ_H d(b,e)/2} e^{−min(μ_H/2,μ)(d(b,e) + dist(b,X))} ≤ e^{−μ_H d(b,e)/2} e^{−min(μ_H/2,μ) dist(e,X)} by the triangle inequality, and Σ_{b∈D(Λ)} e^{−μ_H d(b,e)/2} ≤ N_lat(μ_H/2) by the lattice count of Lemma 1.2 (the sum over all bonds dominates the sum over D(Λ)). The last display is (5.2) with X = I_C and any μ ≥ μ_H/2 (so that min(μ_H/2, μ) = μ_H/2), restricted to b ∈ I_C ⊆ D(Λ), where dist(b, I_C) = 0; the terms with b ∉ I_C are nonnegative and dropped. ∎

Only (I1)–(I3) at the retreated carrier and Lemma 3.1(b) are used; no column-sum bound.

5.2 Thresholds for item 5. In addition to stage 2's thresholds, (4.T) and (4.T′), assume

  (5.T)  (i) u_t := C′_χ g₀ (1+2r) ≤ u_*;   (ii) ω_t = C₀ g₀ (1+2r)³ ≤ ω_*/2;   (iii) ω̄ := C₀ g₀ (1+3r)³ ≤ min(1, ω_* e^{−κ(B_h+4)}/(e^{9/8} N_host));   (iv) ρ_t := u_t/u_* ≤ ρ_pin;   (v) ε̄_t := 2K_χ C_pin ρ_t ≤ 1,

together with stage 4 §7.1's standing choice ω_* ≤ 1/2. Here C′_χ := 2N_trC_link is the constant of Lemma 5.7⁰ (the finite-difference bound for closed contractions: Cauchy estimates on stage 2's row chart with C_link, the traversal multiplicity N_tr, and the factor 2 for the two s-dependent words of the source slot in Lemma 5.7(d); it depends on L, the group and the character only, and plays the role of stage 4 §4.1's C_χ, which is not imported). N_host := 4⁴·20⁴ is the host count of Lemma 5.6 and Proposition 5.9 (the number of hosts Y ∈ 𝐃₀(Λ) containing a given bond, and the number within distance R_st ≤ 5M of a given bond: a host is a box of M-cubes of side at most 4 cubes, eq:part1-potential-host and eq:c-probe-host, determined by its lowest cube, one of at most 20⁴ cubes within 9M of the bond, and by its side lengths, at most 4⁴ choices). ρ_pin is the root-weight smallness required by the positive summation of stage 4 §6.2 and attempt §18.3, §19.4 (the conditions "C_pin′ρLM ≤ η/8", "CρLM ≤ η" there, and (3⁴A_F + LM + 1)C_pinρ ≤ η/16 for the reference gas, Lemma 5.3(d), with their fixed constants; 3⁴ is the neighbour factor for footprint contact of stage 4 §12.4 and attempt §19.3's J_inc = 3⁴A_EQ_E), and C_pin, K_χ are the constants of Lemma 5.8(a) and Lemma 5.7(a). The subscript t in u_t, ρ_t, ε̄_t names the t-family; none of them depends on t. Every condition holds for g₀ small, since g₀(1+r)³ → 0 (r = A₁(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀}), with thresholds depending on L, the group, M, κ, ω_*, η, B_h, A₁, p₀ and the character only. Put s̄ := 1 + 2r + r^Λ_e ≤ 1 + 3r.

5.3 Translated probe measures and the centred expectation for the t-family. The probe hosts Y_b, b ∈ D(Λ), are in 𝐃₀(Λ) (Lemma 4.0(d)); for a union R of LM-cubes put 𝖫_R := {b ∈ D(Λ) : Y_b ⊆ R} (eq:probe-eligible-carrier at the carrier D(Λ)), and for λ ∈ 𝔤^{D(Λ)}_ℂ let L_Y(λ,B) := Σ_{b: Y_b=Y}⟨λ_b, B_b⟩ (the probe in the centred variable). The family 𝐖_t(Y,·) + L_Y(λ,·), Y ∈ 𝐃₀(Λ), has the properties (i)–(iii) of the proof of Lemma 4.1: L_Y(λ,·) depends on B|_{E(Y)} (Y_b = Y gives b ∈ E(Y)), and is bounded on the translated local box of Y (|B_b|_σ < r + t|m_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4 there), and nothing else changes. Hence Lemma 4.1 holds for it, with activities k^{f,t}_{Λ,Z}(λ) entire in λ and k^{f,t}_{Λ,Z}(0) = k^{f,t}_Λ(Z).

Lemma 5.2 (probe measures, majorant and dummy comparison for the t-family). Under the hypotheses of Lemma 4.2 and (5.T), for every t ∈ [0,1] and every connected final domain Z for D(Λ):
(a) There is a finite complex Borel measure ν^{f,t}_{Λ,Z} on 𝔤^{𝖫_Z}, supported in the closed translated box {|B_b + tm_b|_σ ≤ r, b ∈ 𝖫_Z}, with k^{f,t}_{Λ,Z}(λ) = ∫e^{Σ_{b∈𝖫_Z}⟨λ_b,B_b⟩}dν^{f,t}_{Λ,Z}(B) and ν^{f,t}_{Λ,Z}(𝔤^{𝖫_Z}) = k^{f,t}_Λ(Z); explicitly, with the split selections ℓ = (𝒟,𝖩,𝖯,Z) of lem:probe-measures (𝖩 ⊆ 𝖫_Z the selected probes, enlarged seed data Y₀ = ∪_{Y∈𝒟}Y ∪ ∪_{b∈𝖩}Y_b, Z₀, E₀ = E(Z₀), Z₀′) and Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ} the complex measure on 𝔤^{E₀} defined by the right side of the display of Lemma 4.1 with these enlarged data (cutoffs χ^t_b over E(Y₀), the weakened density and the bad set over this seed, Mayer factors for Y ∈ 𝒟 only, no probe factor), ν^{f,t}_{Λ,Z} = Σ_{ℓ: Z(ℓ)=Z, Z₀≠∅} ∫[⊗_{b∈𝖩}(δ_{B_b} − δ_0) ⊗ ⊗_{b∈𝖫_Z∖𝖩}δ_0] dΛ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}(B).
(b) (Probe-enlarged majorant; the variant of (4.5) asked for by review r2, caveat A1.) For every split selection ℓ and every bounded Borel F on 𝔤^{E₀},

  |∫F dΛ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}| ≤ sup|F| · e^{−(κ₁−1)N₁(Z∖Z₀′) + N₁(Z) − (9/16)a_bad|𝖯|} ∏_{Y∈𝒟} ω_* e^{1/2} e^{−κd₀(Y)},                                           (5.3)

uniformly in t, m, the exterior data within the cutoff support and the labels; the probe hosts Y_b, b ∈ 𝖩, carry no factor.
(c) (Dummy comparison for the t-family.) Σ_{ℓ: Z(ℓ)=Z} u_*^{|𝖩|} ‖Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}‖_TV ≤ m(Z) = ε_*e^{−λ_*ℓ₁(Z)}, the sum over all split selections with final domain Z and nonempty seed Z₀ ≠ ∅ (𝖩 = ∅ included; stage 1's activity sum excludes Z₀ = ∅, and the Z₀ = ∅ term has zero measure by the Cauchy argument of lem:polymer).
(d) (Centred expectation.) For bounded Borel g on 𝔤^{D(Λ)}, with 𝔔^t_Λ(𝒜;g) := ∫g(𝖾_𝒜B) d(⊗_{Z∈𝒜}ν̂^{f,t}_{Λ,Z})(B), ν̂^{f,t}_{Λ,Z} := ν^{f,t}_{Λ,Z} − k^{f,t}_Λ(Z)δ_0, q^t_Λ(𝒜) := (Ξ^{f,t}_Λ)^{[∪𝒜]}/Ξ^{f,t}_Λ,

  ∫ g(B) Θ^t e^{−½⟨B,HB⟩+Σ_Y𝐖_t(Y,B)} dB / ∫ Θ^t e^{−½⟨B,HB⟩+Σ_Y𝐖_t(Y,B)} dB = g(0) + Σ_{∅≠𝒜 compatible} q^t_Λ(𝒜) 𝔔^t_Λ(𝒜; g),

and, by (4.3) with F = f_ξ,

  F^Λ(t) = f_ξ(tm) + Σ_{∅≠𝒜 compatible} q^t_Λ(𝒜) 𝔔^t_Λ(𝒜; f_ξ(· + tm)),   𝔔^t_Λ(𝒜; f_ξ(·+tm)) = Σ_{(ℓ_Z)_{Z∈𝒜}: 𝖩_Z≠∅} ∫ Δ_𝖩[f_ξ(·+tm)](B) d⊗_Z Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ_Z}(B),      (5.4)

where 𝖩 = ∪_Z𝖩_Z and Δ_𝖩 g(B) := Σ_{K⊆𝖩}(−1)^{|𝖩∖K|} g(1_K B) is the mixed finite difference of stage 4 §4 (every label outside K set to zero, so the young variable of f_ξ in the K-term is 1_K B + tm: the unselected labels sit at a = tm).

Proof. (a) is the proof of lem:probe-measures with 𝐕_ξ → 𝐖_t, χ_b → χ^t_b, I → D(Λ): the split-label expansion at a host, the exact integration (eq:probe-selected-factor) of the probe Mayer variables, the Laplace transform of δ_{B_b} − δ_0 and the finiteness of each Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ} (image of a density whose modulus is integrable by lem:gaussian-payment(ii) for H, times bounded factors — the Cauchy kernels on the circles |σ_Δ| = e^{κ₁} are bounded by e^{κ₁}(e^{κ₁}−1)⁻², and the integrand is jointly Borel in (X,B,s,σ,t_Y)) use only Lemma 4.1 and its properties (i)–(iii). The support statement: χ^t_b(B), b ∈ E(Y₀) ⊇ 𝖩, forces |B_b + tm_b|_σ < r on the selected probes, and the unselected labels carry δ_0 (B_b = 0, and |tm_b|_σ ≤ r/4 < r). At λ = 0 every term with 𝖩 ≠ ∅ has zero mass (each δ_{B_b} − δ_0 has), so the total mass is k^{f,t}_{Λ,Z}(0) = k^{f,t}_Λ(Z). (b) The proof of Lemma 4.2 is written for a selection 𝔰 = (𝒟,𝖯,Z) and bounds factor by factor; the enlarged seed enters only through E₀ = E(Z₀) in the density (eq:fixed-kernel-density), whose modulus is paid against e^{(α_G/2)‖B‖²} by lem:gaussian-payment(ii) for every seed Z₀ and every Z ⊇ Z₀′ (the lemma is stated for every seed), through the cutoffs χ^t_b over E(Y₀), each ≤ 1, and through the bad bonds 𝖯 ⊆ D(Λ) ∖ E(Y₀), each paying e^{−(9/16)a_bad}e^{(α_G/2)h(B_b)²} as in Lemma 4.2; the Mayer factors are those of Y ∈ 𝒟 only, each bounded by ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)} on the support of its cutoffs; a probe host contributes no factor; the weakening bound e^{−(κ₁−1)} per cube of Z ∖ Z₀′ is unchanged. (c) is stage 4 §7.1's positive host comparison: at a host Y the sum over nonempty label subsets of the positive weights v_ordinary = e^{A_Y} − 1, v_probe = u_* is at most ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)}, and all split selections with the same active-host set have the same enlarged seed, density, bad set and weakening, so the many-to-one sum is dominated hostwise by the undifferentiated majorant (5.3) with F = 1, after which stage 2's seed/enlargement/final-domain sums (eq:native-source-initial-domain-sum, eq:native-source-enlarged-domain-sum, the Y′-sum in the proof of lem:activity-bounds) give m(Z) exactly as in Corollary 4.3 (the proof of Corollary 4.3 shows that these sums hold with (9/16)a_bad in place of a_bad under (4.T′)); the comparison needs the ordinary factor's modulus |∫₀¹𝐖_te^{t_Y𝐖_t}dt_Y| = |e^{𝐖_t} − 1| ≤ e^{|𝐖_t|} − 1 ≤ e^{A_Y} − 1 = v_ordinary with A_Y = (ω_*/2)e^{−κd₀(Y)}, which holds because |𝐖_t(Y,B)| ≤ ω_te^{−κd₀(Y)} ≤ A_Y on the support of the host's cutoffs by (4.4) and (5.T)(ii), and the chain's last step uses ω_* ≤ 1/2 and n_Yu_* ≤ (ω_*/8)e^{−κd₀(Y)} (stage 4 §7.1). The sum includes the split selections with 𝖩 = ∅ (a host is active through its ordinary label or through probe labels; stage 4 §7.1 sums over all nonempty label subsets), so (c) holds with Σ_{ℓ: Z(ℓ)=Z} in place of Σ_{ℓ: Z(ℓ)=Z, 𝖩≠∅}. (d) is the proof of lem:measure-identity and prop:centred-expectation for the translated expansion: both sides of 𝔫_Λ⁻¹Θ^te^{−½⟨B,HB⟩+Σ𝐖_t}dB = Σ_𝒫(𝖾_𝒫)_*⊗_{Z∈𝒫}ν^{f,t}_{Λ,Z} are finite complex measures with compact support and the same Laplace transform (Lemma 4.1 for 𝐖_t + L(λ,·) and (a)), hence equal; expanding ν^{f,t} = ν̂^{f,t} + k^{f,t}δ_0 and dividing by 𝔫_ΛΞ^{f,t}_Λ gives the first display; the second is (4.3) with F = f_ξ (bounded on 𝔤^{D(Λ)}, extended by zero off the enlarged box, which contains the support of every ν^{f,t}: |B_b + tm_b|_σ ≤ r and |tm_b|_σ ≤ r/4 give |B_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4 and |1_KB + tm|_b ≤ 3r/2 ≤ 2r), and the formula for 𝔔^t in terms of Δ_𝖩 is the integration of g against the Dirac tensors in (a), zero-mass part (the terms with 𝖩_Z = ∅ for some Z are removed by the centring). ∎

Lemma 5.3 (joint extraction for the t-family). (a) For a connected final domain Z for D(Λ) the contact set 𝖳_Z := {n : 𝓕_{r_n} ∩ 𝖫_Z ≠ ∅} (eq:part1-contact-set, with the convention 𝓕_r = ∅ off Ω₁) is field-independent and t-independent; if 𝒜 contains an unmarked Z (𝖳_Z = ∅) then 𝔔^t_Λ(𝒜; f_ξ(·+tm)) = 0. The groups W = (𝒜(W), (𝔠_1,…,𝔠_{k(W)}), ℋ(W)) of def:groups for the carrier D(Λ) form one index set for all t ∈ [0,1] and for the reference data; their weights are w^t_Λ(W) := ((−1)^{k(W)}/k(W)!)∏_j Φ^{v,t}_Λ(𝔠_j) with the cluster weights of Corollary 4.3. With A^t_Λ(𝒮) := ∏_{W∈𝒮}w^t_Λ(W) 𝔔^t_Λ(𝒜(𝒮); f_ξ(·+tm)), A^t_Λ(∅) := f_ξ(tm), lem:grouped-source holds: F^Λ(t) = Σ_{𝔅 compatible} A^t_Λ(𝔅), absolutely convergent.
(b) Reference data at the carrier D(Λ): the same Ω₁, cubes, hosts 𝐃₀(Λ), constants and cutoffs, with U°₁ = id, V°₀ = id, A° = 0, Ψ° = id, exterior variable zero on J(Λ) (so m° = 0, ℓ° = 0, and the reference objects are independent of t), H° = (𝖧^{Ω₁}_{id})_{D(Λ)D(Λ)}, f°(a) = W_χ(C; e^{iq_{id}(a)}) with links off Ω₁ equal to id. Lemma 4.0 holds for these data (its (a) by principal restriction of stage 1's reference Hessian, which satisfies (eq:fixed-kernel-inputs) by stage 2 Lemma lem:c2-reference; (b) trivially, r°_e = 0; (c), (d) as stated), and so do Lemma 4.1, Lemma 4.2, Corollary 4.3 and Lemma 5.2 (their proofs use the listed lemmas only).
