Define a^Λ_W := w°_Λ(W)∫χ(P°_W(a))d(⊗_{Z∈𝒜(W)}ν̂^{f,°}_{Λ,Z})(a) as in (eq:part1-reference-group), with P°_W the ordered product of reference links along ℋ(W) at the young variable 𝖾_{𝒜(W)}a. Then lem:schur holds: w°_Λ(W)∫χ(P°_W(a)h)d⊗ν̂^{f,°}_{Λ} = a^Λ_Wχ(h) for h ∈ G, and S°_Λ := Σ_{𝔅 compatible}∏_{W∈𝔅}a^Λ_W = ∫f°K°_Λda/I°_Λ.
(c) With R^t_Λ(𝔅) := Σ_{𝒮⊆𝔅}(−1)^{|𝔅∖𝒮|}(∏_{W∈𝔅∖𝒮}a^Λ_W)A^t_Λ(𝒮) and S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ the reference sum over families compatible with every member of 𝔅,

  F^Λ(t) = S°_Λ f_ξ(tm) + Σ_{∅≠𝔅 compatible} S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ R^t_Λ(𝔅),   hence   ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ] = S°_Λ[f_ξ(m) − f_ξ(0)] + Σ_{∅≠𝔅} S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ [R¹_Λ(𝔅) − R⁰_Λ(𝔅)].      (5.5)

(d) |a^Λ_W| ≤ M_Λ(W), the positive group majorant of Lemma 5.7(d) below ((1/k(W)!)∏_{Z∈𝒜(W)}ρ_tm(Z)∏_j|Φ|⁺_Λ(𝔠_j), defined without reference to the source data). Under (5.T)(iv) the reference group gas with the activities a^Λ_W satisfies the Kotecký–Preiss condition, so that S°_Λ ≠ 0, q°_Λ(𝔅) := S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ/S°_Λ = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}Φ°_Λ(Γ)) (the cluster expansion of the reference group gas, attempt §13 and §19.4, at the carrier D(Λ); a cluster hits 𝔅 when it contains a group incompatible with a member of 𝔅), and |q°_Λ(𝔅)| ≤ ∏_{W∈𝔅}e^{θ°(W)} with θ°(W) := Σ_{Γ hits W}|Φ°_Λ(Γ)| ≤ νσ(W)/16, where σ(W) and ν are the size and the reserve constant of Lemma 5.8; and the rooted bound Σ_{Γ∋W}|Φ°_Λ(Γ)| ≤ |a^Λ_W|e^{13νσ(W)/8} holds. All of (d) holds verbatim for the reference gas at the carrier I (stage 1's reference data, activities a_W, cluster coefficients Φ°_ξ), since its proof uses only the stage 1–2 lemmas at the carrier, which hold at I by stage 1–2 themselves.

Proof. (a) A traversal's link depends on the young variable through a|_{𝓕_{r_n}∩D(Λ)} (lem:row-locality; the de-younged bonds are constants); for unmarked Z the function B ↦ f_ξ(1_KB + tm) does not depend on B|_{𝖫_Z}, so the K-sum over the labels of 𝖩_Z telescopes to zero in (5.4) (equivalently, ν̂^{f,t}_{Λ,Z} has zero mass). The groups are built from roots, cluster lists (tuples of domains), contact sets and hulls, none of which involves the fields or t (stage 1 L721); only w^t_Λ and ν̂^{f,t}_Λ depend on t. The regrouping proof of lem:grouped-source is combinatorial, and its absolute convergence uses |𝔔^t_Λ(𝒜;g)| ≤ sup|g|∏_Z‖ν̂^{f,t}_{Λ,Z}‖ (finite by Lemma 5.2(a)) and the hitting sum Σ_{𝔠 hits 𝒜}|Φ^{v,t}_Λ(𝔠)| ≤ (1/8)Σ_{Z∈𝒜}N₁(Z) of Corollary 4.3. (b) The proof of lem:schur uses the constant gauge R_u: H° = R_uH°R_u⁻¹ holds for the principal restriction because R_u acts bondwise; the domains, weights, cutoffs (Ad-invariant norm), the Gaussian density, the probe term and the reconstruction covariance q_{id}(R_uB) = R_uq_{id}(B) are as in stage 1, and the hosts and partitions are field-independent; Schur's lemma and the Fubini evaluation of the group product are verbatim. (c) is prop:joint-extraction's Boolean inversion and interchange of absolutely convergent sums, with the bound β_W of its proof formed from w^t_Λ, w°_Λ, ν̂^{f,t}_Λ, ν̂^{f,°}_Λ; the t-independence of a^Λ_W and S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ gives the second form. (d) The activity bound: a^Λ_W = w°_Λ(W)∫g°_W d⊗_{Z∈𝒜(W)}ν̂^{f,°}_{Λ,Z} with g°_W(a) := χ(P°_W(a)), a closed Wilson contraction at the reference data; by Lemma 5.2(a),(d) for the reference data (part (b)), ∫g°_W d⊗ν̂° = Σ_{(ℓ_Z)_{Z∈𝒜(W)}: 𝖩_Z≠∅}∫Δ_𝖩g°_W d⊗_ZΛ^{f,°}_{Λ;ℓ_Z}; Lemma 5.7⁰(b) at the reference data gives sup|Δ_𝖩g°_W| ≤ u_t^{|𝖩|} (base point a₀ = 0, increments |B_b|_σ ≤ r ≤ 5r/4 on the support of ν^{f,°}, m° = 0), and Lemma 5.2(c) for the reference data with (u_t/u_*)^{|𝖩_Z|} ≤ ρ_t gives |∫g°_W d⊗ν̂°| ≤ ∏_{Z∈𝒜(W)}ρ_tm(Z); |w°_Λ(W)| ≤ (1/k(W)!)∏_j|Φ^{v,°}_Λ(𝔠_j)| ≤ (1/k(W)!)∏_j|Φ|⁺_Λ(𝔠_j) by Corollary 4.3 for the reference data and the tree-graph inequality (Lemma 5.6(b)). The Kotecký–Preiss condition: by the first display of Lemma 5.8(a), which uses only the majorant M_Λ, sup_ΔΣ_{W: Δ∈F(W)}e^{4νσ(W)}M_Λ(W) + sup_nΣ_{W: n∈ℋ(W)}e^{4νσ(W)}M_Λ(W) ≤ C_pinρ_t; two groups are incompatible only if their footprints touch (contact includes adjacency across a face, edge or corner, not only intersection: stage 4 §12.4) or their intervals overlap (stage 4 §6.2); a footprint has at most A_Fσ(W) cubes (attempt §19.3), so an incompatible W′ has a footprint cube in the set N₁(F(W)) of cubes touching F(W), of at most 3⁴A_Fσ(W) cubes (the neighbour factor 3⁴ of stage 4 §12.4, attempt §19.3's J_inc = 3⁴A_EQ_E), or a traversal in ℋ(W), of at most LMσ(W) traversals (|ℋ(W)|/(LM) ≤ σ(W)); hence Σ_{W′ incompatible with W}|a^Λ_{W′}|e^{3νσ(W′)/2}e^{νσ(W′)/8} ≤ Σ_{Δ∈N₁(F(W))}Σ_{W′: Δ∈F(W′)}(…) + Σ_{n∈ℋ(W)}Σ_{W′: n∈ℋ(W′)}(…) ≤ (3⁴A_F + LM)σ(W)C_pinρ_t ≤ νσ(W)/8 under (5.T)(iv): this is the Kotecký–Preiss hypothesis for the gas with activities |a^Λ_W|e^{3νσ(W)/2} and node cost νσ(W)/8, and attempt §19.4's positive tree bound (the Kotecký–Preiss theorem in that form) gives the absolute convergence of the cluster series, S°_Λ = exp(Σ_ΓΦ°_Λ(Γ)) ≠ 0, the exclusion identity S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ = exp(Σ_{Γ hits no member of 𝔅}Φ°_Λ(Γ)) (the same with the species incompatible with a member of 𝔅 excluded), and the rooted bound Σ_{Γ∋W}m_W(Γ)|Φ°_Λ(Γ)| ≤ |a^Λ_W|e^{13νσ(W)/8}, m_W(Γ) the occurrence multiplicity. Hence θ°(W) ≤ Σ_{W′ = W or incompatible with W}Σ_{Γ∋W′}|Φ°_Λ(Γ)| ≤ Σ_{W′ = W or incompatible with W}|a^Λ_{W′}|e^{13νσ(W′)/8} ≤ (3⁴A_F + LM + 1)σ(W)C_pinρ_t ≤ νσ(W)/16 under (5.T)(iv) (e^{13νσ/8} ≤ e^{4νσ}, and the term W′ = W is bounded by the footprint pin at a cube of F(W)). ∎

5.4 The two terms of (5.5). The first is the shift of the loop at zero fluctuation; the second is the difference of the joint defects, which is where the source-containing connected boundary terms live.

Lemma 5.4 (the shift term). |f_ξ(m) − f_ξ(0)| ≤ C_{resp,χ} g₀ Σ_{b∈I_C} h(m_b) ≤ C_{resp,χ} C_m ϑ N_lat(μ_H/2) g₀ r Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)} e^{−μ_H dist(e,I_C)/2}.

Proof. f_ξ(m) − f_ξ(0) = ∫₀¹ Σ_{b∈I_C}⟨m_b, ∇_bf_ξ(sm)⟩ds; the points sm lie in the box |a_b|_σ ≤ r/4 by (4.1), inside the enlarged box, where sup h(∇_bf_ξ) ≤ C_{resp,χ}g₀1_{I_C}(b) (stage 3's source gradient bound, as used in the proof of Lemma 4.4); then (5.2). ∎

Lemma 5.5 (conditional-Gaussian shell bound). Let b ∈ E₀, S_b := {x ∈ 𝔤 : r − |m_b|_σ ≤ |x|_σ < r + |m_b|_σ}, v ∈ 𝔤^{E₀} real, and 0 ≤ α ≤ α_G (α_G ≤ γ/2, stage 2 conventions; h(x)² = ⟨x,x⟩ is the squared norm of the invariant inner product). Write μ_{C_{E₀}}(dB) = μ_{C′}(dB′) N(μ(B′), c_b)(dB_b) for the disintegration of the centred Gaussian of covariance C_{E₀} = (ι*_{E₀}Hι_{E₀})⁻¹ along the coordinate b (B′ the other coordinates, N(μ,c) the Gaussian on 𝔤 with mean μ and covariance c), where c_b = ((C_{E₀}⁻¹)_{bb})⁻¹ = (H_{bb})⁻¹ and μ(B′) is linear in B′. Then for every B′,

  ∫ 1_{S_b}(B_b) e^{−⟨B_b,v_b⟩ + (α/2)h(B_b)²} N(μ(B′),c_b)(dB_b) ≤ ϖ_b ∫ e^{−⟨B_b,v_b⟩ + (α/2)h(B_b)²} N(μ(B′),c_b)(dB_b),   ϖ_b := (h₊/2π)^{d_G/2} Leb(S_b) ≤ C_V r^{d_G−1} |m_b|_σ,      (5.6)

C_V := (h₊/2π)^{d_G/2} 2 d_G V_σ (5/4)^{d_G−1}, V_σ the volume of the unit |·|_σ-ball of 𝔤 for the Lebesgue measure of the h-inner product. Consequently ϖ_b ≤ C_ϖ r^{d_G} Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)} e^{−μ_H d(b,e)}, C_ϖ := C_V c_h C_m ϑ.

Proof. The conditional law of B_b given B′ under a centred Gaussian of covariance C is Gaussian with covariance ((C⁻¹)_{bb})⁻¹ and mean linear in B′; here C⁻¹ = ι*Hι, whose bb block is H_{bb}. Completing the square: the density of e^{−⟨x,v⟩+(α/2)⟨x,x⟩}N(μ,c_b)(dx) is a constant times exp(−½⟨x,(H_{bb} − α id)x⟩ + ⟨x, H_{bb}μ − v⟩), and H_{bb} − α id ≥ (γ − α_G)id ≥ (γ/2)id > 0 (H_{bb} ≥ γ id as a principal restriction of H, (I3)), so e^{−⟨x,v⟩+(α/2)⟨x,x⟩}N(μ,c_b)(dx) = Z_α N(μ_α, c_α)(dx) with c_α := (H_{bb} − α id)⁻¹, μ_α := c_α(H_{bb}μ − v) and a positive constant Z_α (for α = 0: Z_0 = e^{−⟨μ,v⟩+½⟨v,c_bv⟩}, μ_0 = μ − c_bv); hence the left side of (5.6) equals Z_αN(μ_α,c_α)(S_b) and the right side's integral equals Z_α; the Gaussian density of N(μ_α,c_α) is at most (2π)^{−d_G/2}det(c_α)^{−1/2} = (2π)^{−d_G/2}det(H_{bb} − α id)^{1/2} ≤ (2π)^{−d_G/2}det(H_{bb})^{1/2} ≤ (h₊/2π)^{d_G/2} (H_{bb} ≤ h₊ id by (I3)), whence N(μ_α,c_α)(S_b) ≤ (h₊/2π)^{d_G/2}Leb(S_b). By homogeneity of the norm, Leb({|x|_σ < s}) = V_σs^{d_G}, so Leb(S_b) = V_σ[(r+ε)^{d_G} − (r−ε)^{d_G}] with ε = |m_b|_σ ≤ r/4, and the mean value theorem gives ≤ V_σ·2ε·d_G(5r/4)^{d_G−1}. The last bound is |m_b|_σ ≤ c_hh(m_b) (Remark 1.3) and (5.1). ∎

The point of (5.6) is that the shell is paid by the Gaussian itself, with the tilt e^{−⟨B,v⟩} (which in the density (eq:fixed-kernel-density) is e^{−⟨B,Re Γ_{E₀}(σ)X⟩} after moduli are taken) kept intact: the ratio ϖ_b is of order r^{d_G−1}|m_b| and does not involve the reserve a_bad. The alternative sup-over-B_b thin-shell bound would cost e^{25h₊r²/32} per mark, which e^{−(9/16)a_bad} cannot pay (α_G ≤ γ/2 < h₊), and is not used.

Lemma 5.6 (marked differences). Define, for a bond b ∈ D(Λ) and a host Y ∈ 𝐃₀(Λ), the mark weights

  𝔪_b := 2ϖ_b ≤ 2C_ϖ r^{d_G} Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)} e^{−μ_H d(b,e)}   (one ϖ_b for the bond's own translated cutoff, one for the cutoffs of the hosts containing b, paid by (5.T)(iii)),
  𝔪_Y := 2ω̄e^{ω̄}·e^{κ(B_h+4)}/ω_*·e^{1/8}   if some near primitive assigned to Y has an input in J(Λ),   𝔪_Y := N_hC_pg₀s̄²(1+ω̄)e^{ω̄}·e^{κ(B_h+4)}/ω_*·e^{1/8} Σ_{b∈E(Y)}h(m_b)   otherwise,

and for a union Z of LM-cubes 𝔐_Λ(Z) := Σ_{b∈E(Z)}𝔪_b + Σ_{Y∈𝐃₀(Λ), Y⊆Z}𝔪_Y. Under the hypotheses of Lemma 5.2:
(a) For every split selection ℓ with Z(ℓ) = Z (𝖩 = ∅ allowed) and every bounded Borel F, |∫F d(Λ^{f,1}_{Λ;ℓ} − Λ^{f,0}_{Λ;ℓ})| ≤ sup|F|·𝔐_Λ(Z)·e^{−(κ₁−1)N₁(Z∖Z₀′)+N₁(Z)−(9/16)a_bad|𝖯|}∏_{Y∈𝒟}ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)} (the majorant (5.3) times the sum of the mark weights; the division of a host mark by the host's majorant factor is built into 𝔪_Y); and Σ_{ℓ: Z(ℓ)=Z} u_*^{|𝖩|}‖Λ^{f,1}_{Λ;ℓ} − Λ^{f,0}_{Λ;ℓ}‖_TV ≤ 𝔐_Λ(Z) m(Z).
(b) |k^{f,1}_Λ(Z) − k^{f,0}_Λ(Z)| ≤ 𝔐_Λ(Z) m(Z), and for a cluster 𝔠 = (D_1,…,D_n) with the positive incompatibility-tree majorant |Φ|⁺_Λ(𝔠) := (1/n!)Σ_{T∈Trees_n}∏_{{i,j}∈T}1{D_i ≁ D_j}∏_i m(D_i) (the M_v(𝔠) of attempt §18.3; |Φ|⁺_Λ(𝔠) ≥ max_t|Φ^{v,t}_Λ(𝔠)| by the tree-graph inequality |φ^T(𝔠)| ≤ #{spanning trees of the incompatibility graph of 𝔠}, proved in the TeX in the justification following eq:marginal-pinned-bound and used in stage 2 app:kp, and by |k^{f,t}_Λ(D_i)| ≤ m(D_i), Corollary 4.3), |Φ^{v,1}_Λ(𝔠) − Φ^{v,0}_Λ(𝔠)| ≤ (Σ_i𝔐_Λ(D_i))|Φ|⁺_Λ(𝔠).

Proof. (a) In the display of Lemma 4.1 for the enlarged data, the t-dependent factors are χ^t_b (b ∈ E(Y₀)), 1 − χ^t_b (b ∈ 𝖯) and the Mayer factors M^t_Y := ∫₀¹𝐖_t(Y,B)e^{t_Y𝐖_t(Y,B)}dt_Y (Y ∈ 𝒟), with 𝐖_t(Y,B) = Θ^t_{E(Y)}(B)𝐏_t(Y,B), 𝐏_t(Y,B) := Σ_{𝔭 near, Y_𝔭=Y}𝔭(B + tm + tA^{Λ,Ψ}), Θ^t_{E(Y)} := ∏_{b∈E(Y)}χ^t_b; the density, the Dirac tensor's pushforward (through F) and the s-, σ-integrations are t-independent. Order these factors (bonds first, then hosts) and telescope: ∏a_k − ∏b_k = Σ_k(∏_{i<k}a_i)(a_k − b_k)(∏_{i>k}b_i), a the t = 1 and b the t = 0 factors. Each term has exactly one differenced factor, the mark. In every term each bond of E(Y₀) ∪ 𝖯 carries a factor χ¹_b, χ⁰_b, 1 − χ^t_b or a difference of two of these, so on the support |B_b|_σ < 5r/4 for b ∈ E(Y₀) (|B_b + tm_b|_σ < r, |m_b|_σ ≤ r/4), and every host's primitives are evaluated in the enlarged box (young part of B + tm + tA^{Λ,Ψ} of norm < 3r/2 ≤ 2r), where they are analytic with |𝔭| ≤ C_pg₀s̄³ and ‖D𝔭‖ ≤ C_pg₀s̄² (Lemma 4.0(c), stage 2 lem:c2-primitive-bounds on the chart containing the enlarged box under threshold item 3), so |𝐏_t(Y,B)| ≤ N_hC_pg₀s̄³ ≤ ω̄ for both t. Bond mark at b: |χ¹_b − χ⁰_b| ≤ 1_{S_b}(B_b) and |(1−χ¹_b) − (1−χ⁰_b)| ≤ 1_{S_b}(B_b), since the two indicators differ only where |B_b|_σ is within |m_b|_σ of r. Host mark at Y ∈ 𝒟: in its term the bond factors are at t = 1, and E(Y) ⊆ E(Y₀) ∖ 𝖯 (def:selection: 𝖯 ∩ E(Y₀) = ∅), so Θ¹_{E(Y)} = 1 on the support and

  M¹_Y − M⁰_Y = ∫₀¹[𝐏₁e^{t_Y𝐏₁} − 𝐏₀e^{t_Y𝐏₀}]dt_Y + (1 − Θ⁰_{E(Y)})∫₀¹𝐏₀e^{t_Y𝐏₀}dt_Y;

the smooth part is bounded on the support by 2ω̄e^{ω̄} always, and, when no primitive of Y has an input in J(Λ), by ∫₀¹|∂_t(𝐏_te^{t_Y𝐏_t})|dt ≤ (1+ω̄)e^{ω̄}sup_t|∂_t𝐏_t| with ∂_t𝐏_t(Y,B) = Σ_𝔭D𝔭(B+tm)[m|_{E(Y)}] (no exterior input), |∂_t𝐏_t| ≤ N_hC_pg₀s̄²Σ_{b∈E(Y)}h(m_b); the cutoff part satisfies 0 ≤ 1 − Θ⁰_{E(Y)} ≤ Σ_{b∈E(Y)}(1 − χ⁰_b), and on the support of χ¹_b, (1 − χ⁰_b)χ¹_b ≤ 1_{S_b}(B_b), with |∫₀¹𝐏₀e^{t_Y𝐏₀}dt_Y| ≤ ω̄e^{ω̄}: it is a sum over b ∈ E(Y) of bond marks 1_{S_b} in which the host's factor is replaced by ω̄e^{ω̄}. Now bound each term. Take moduli; every factor other than the mark and the density is bounded by its supremum as in the proof of Lemma 4.2 (cutoffs ≤ 1; bad bonds by e^{−(9/16)a_bad}e^{(α_G/2)h(B_b)²}, from 1{|B_b|_σ ≥ 3r/4} ≤ e^{−(9/16)a_bad}e^{(α_G/2)h(B_b)²}; undifferenced Mayer factors by ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)}, valid at either t since Θ^t_{E(Y)} restricts B + tm to the local box of Y, where (4.4) and (5.T)(ii) give |𝐖_t| ≤ (ω_*/2)e^{−κd₀(Y)}; |F| ≤ sup|F|); the modulus of the density is e^{−½Re⟨Γ(σ)X,CΓ(σ)X⟩}e^{−⟨B,v⟩}dμ_{C_{E₀}}(B)dμ₀(X) with v := Re Γ_{E₀}(σ)X real. At a bond mark b ∈ E(Y₀) (b ∉ 𝖯): integrate over B_b first; by Lemma 5.5 with α = 0 the mark 1_{S_b} is replaced by ϖ_b, leaving ∫e^{−⟨B,v⟩}e^{(α_G/2)Σ_{b′∈𝖯}h(B_{b′})²}dμ_C ≤ ∫e^{−⟨B,v⟩}e^{(α_G/2)‖B‖²}dμ_C, which lem:gaussian-payment(ii) pays with e^{N₁(Z)} together with the X-integral. At a bond mark b ∈ 𝖯: on S_b, |B_b|_σ ≥ r − |m_b|_σ ≥ 3r/4, so 1_{S_b}(B_b) ≤ 1_{S_b}(B_b)e^{−(9/16)a_bad}e^{(α_G/2)h(B_b)²}, the reserve of b is kept, and Lemma 5.5 with α = α_G (the conditional Gaussian in B_b with the tilt and the factor e^{(α_G/2)h(B_b)²} on b) replaces the mark by ϖ_b with all factors e^{(α_G/2)h(B_{b′})²}, b′ ∈ 𝖯, in place, so the bad-bond payment e^{−(9/16)a_bad|𝖯|} is complete and the Gaussian payment is as before. Hence a bond-marked term is bounded by ϖ_b times the majorant (5.3). At a host mark Y, the smooth part is a constant, 2ω̄e^{ω̄} or N_hC_pg₀s̄²(1+ω̄)e^{ω̄}Σ_{E(Y)}h(m_b), in place of the host's majorant factor ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)} ≥ ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κ(B_h+4)}; the quotient (constant)/(ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)}) is at most (constant)·e^{κ(B_h+4)}/ω_* (d₀(Y) ≤ B_h+4, e^{1/2} ≥ 1), which is 𝔪_Y without its factor e^{1/8}, reserved for the dummy comparison below; the cutoff part gives, for each b ∈ E(Y), a bond mark 1_{S_b} (b ∉ 𝖯), paid by ϖ_b as above, with the host's factor ω̄e^{ω̄} in place of ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)}, a quotient ≤ ω̄e^{ω̄}e^{κ(B_h+4)}/ω_* ≤ eω̄e^{κ(B_h+4)}/ω_* ≤ e^{−1/8}/N_host ≤ 1/N_host by (5.T)(iii); the hosts Y ∈ 𝒟 with b ∈ E(Y) number at most N_host (§5.2), so these contribute at most ϖ_b per bond b, which with the bond's own mark ϖ_b gives 𝔪_b = 2ϖ_b; the rest of the proof of Lemma 4.2 (weakening per cube, Gaussian payment) is unchanged. Summing over the marks gives the first assertion with 𝔐_Λ(Z) (every mark bond lies in E(Y₀) ∪ 𝖯 ⊆ E(Z), every marked host in 𝒟 lies in Z₀′ ⊆ Z). For the dummy comparison with a mark, repeat stage 4 §7.1's positive host comparison (Lemma 5.2(c)) with the marked factor: at the bond's own mark (the differenced factor is χ^t_b or 1 − χ^t_b) the hostwise comparison is unchanged (the undifferenced hosts have v_ordinary = e^{A_Y} − 1 at either t, as above) and the mark ϖ_b multiplies the whole; at a bond mark generated by the cutoff part of a host Y ∈ 𝒟, the host Y is active through its ordinary label with its factor replaced by ω̄e^{ω̄}, so the sum over Y's label subsets containing the ordinary label is ω̄e^{ω̄}(1+u_*)^{n_Y} ≤ ω̄e^{ω̄}e^{1/8}, whose quotient by Y's majorant factor ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)} is at most e^{9/8}ω̄e^{κ(B_h+4)}/ω_* ≤ 1/N_host by (5.T)(iii) (this is what the factor e^{9/8} in (iii) is for), and the at most N_host such hosts again contribute at most ϖ_b per bond, so 𝔪_b = 2ϖ_b suffices in the dummy comparison as well; at a host mark Y the ordinary weight v_ordinary of Y is replaced by the mark's constant, and Σ_{∅≠S}∏_{ℓ′∈S}v_{ℓ′} ≤ (constant)·(1+u_*)^{n_Y} ≤ (constant)·e^{1/8} (n_Yu_* ≤ ω_*/8 ≤ 1/16 < 1/8), so the host's contribution is ≤ 𝔪_Y·ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)}, the factor e^{1/8} being the one in 𝔪_Y; the remaining sums give m(Z) as in Lemma 5.2(c), and dropping the constraint that the mark lies in the selection only enlarges the sum. (b) k^{f,t}_Λ(Z) = Σ_{(𝒟,𝖯)}𝒜^{f,t}_{(𝒟,𝖯,Z)}[1] is the 𝖩 = ∅, F = 1 case of (a), summed over the selections, which yields 𝔐_Λ(Z)m(Z) by the same seed/enlargement sums; the cluster bound is the telescoping of the product ∏_ik^{f,t}_Λ(D_i) with |k^{f,t}_Λ(D_i)| ≤ m(D_i) (Corollary 4.3) and the tree-graph inequality for |φ^T(𝔠)|. ∎

The mark weights are small where it matters: 𝔪_b is of order r^{d_G} times the decay from b to J₁(Λ); a host carrying an exterior input lies within the stencil range of J(Λ) (its near primitive has an input in J(Λ) and an input in D(Λ)), and its mark 𝔪_Y ≤ C_{bd}g₀(1+3r)³ with C_{bd} := 2e·e^{κ(B_h+4)}e^{1/8}C₀/ω_* is small in the coupling (g₀(1+r)³ → 0) though not by a threshold; a host without exterior input has 𝔪_Y ≤ C′_{bd}g₀(1+3r)²Σ_{E(Y)}h(m_b) with C′_{bd} := 2e·N_hC_pe^{κ(B_h+4)}e^{1/8}/ω_* ((1+ω̄)e^{ω̄} ≤ 2e for ω̄ ≤ 1), which carries the decay of Lemma 5.1.

Lemma 5.7⁰ (the finite-difference bound for closed contractions). Let π be the irreducible unitary representation with χ = dim(π)⁻¹tr π (π also denotes the Lie-algebra representation, extended complex-linearly to 𝔤_ℂ = 𝔤 ⊕ i𝔤, and π denotes as well the holomorphic extension to the complexified group, with π(e^{iX}) = e^{iπ(X)} for X ∈ 𝔤_ℂ), h_ℂ(x + iy)² := h(x)² + h(y)² the Hermitian extension of h to 𝔤_ℂ, C_π := sup_{0≠x∈𝔤}‖π(x)‖_op/h(x), so that ‖π(x + iy)‖_op ≤ C_π(h(x) + h(y)) ≤ √2C_πh_ℂ(x + iy) (review r3, D4), m_* := 1 + max_n|𝓕_{r_n}| (one more than the largest free-input set of a traversal link; 𝓕_r ⊆ 𝓔_c ∩ E_* for the row c of r, so m_* depends on L only), N_tr := max_b #{traversals n of C : b ∈ 𝓕_{r_n}} (b lies in the two-block set 𝓔_c of boundedly many rows c, and a traversal with input b is the free bond b itself or the designated bond b₀(c) of such a row, each traversed once by the square, so N_tr depends on L only). For a row c within 2R_c + 2L of I, lem:charts and lem:row-locality give the phase q_U(B)|_{𝓔_c} as an analytic function Φ_c of the row phase A = g₀𝖢_UB|_{𝓔_c} on a complex neighbourhood of the closed ball ‖A‖ ≤ ϑ_*/2 (stage 2 lem:c2-primitive-bounds, chart statements: the row solution and the phase are analytic there), with values in 𝔤_ℂ^{𝓔_c} and h_ℂ(Φ_c(A)(b)) ≤ Q_* for every b ∈ 𝓔_c on that ball, Q_* a constant depending on L and the group only; put C_Π := e^{√2C_πQ_*} (so ‖π(e^{iΦ_c(A)(b)})‖_op = ‖e^{iπ(Φ_c(A)(b))}‖_op ≤ e^{‖π(Φ_c(A)(b))‖} ≤ e^{√2C_πh_ℂ(Φ_c(A)(b))} ≤ C_Π on the ball) and C_link := max(C_π, 4m_*C_ΠC_𝖢/ϑ_*), C′_χ := 2N_trC_link; all depend on L, the group and the character only. Under threshold item 3 (stage 2 app:thresholds: 4C_𝖢g₀(1+2r) ≤ ϑ_*/2), on A_cov^{fin} (where every bound of §5 is stated, 5.0) the row c of every traversal n of C has its free-input set 𝓕_{r_n} ⊆ D(Λ), and the row phase A = g₀𝖢_Ua|_{𝓔_c} depends on a|_{𝓕_{r_n}} only (lem:row-locality), so the exterior variable A^{Λ,Ψ} (supported on J(Λ), disjoint from D(Λ)) does not enter a traversal link at all (review r3 D3, review r4 item 2); for every real a in the enlarged box {|a_b|_σ ≤ 2r, b ∈ D(Λ)} the input of the row lies in the row polydisc 𝒰_c = {|B(b)| < 2 + 4r} of stage 2 lem:c2-primitive-bounds (every input bond has |·|_σ ≤ 2r < 2 + 4r), and the chart statements give ‖A‖ ≤ 2C_𝖢g₀(1+2r) ≤ ϑ_*/4 for the row phase. At the reference data (Lemma 5.3(b)) the exterior variable is zero and the same holds. "The enlarged box" below means this.
(a) (Link derivatives.) For a traversal link L_n(a) := U_ξ(a)(r_n) = e^{iq_U(Ψ_ξ(a+A_e))(r_n)}U₁(r_n) (eq:c-reconstructed-field; it depends on a through a|_{𝓕_{r_n}} only, lem:row-locality), every real a in the enlarged box, every k ≤ m_* and every bonds b₁,…,b_k ∈ 𝓕_{r_n} (repetitions allowed) with h-unit directions e_1,…,e_k ∈ 𝔤: ‖∂_{e_1,b_1}⋯∂_{e_k,b_k}π(L_n)(a)‖_op ≤ (C_linkg₀)^k. Consequently ‖∂_sπ(L_n(a + sm))‖ ≤ C_linkg₀Σ_{b∈𝓕_{r_n}}h(m_b) and ‖π(L_n(a + m)) − π(L_n(a))‖ ≤ C_linkg₀Σ_{b∈𝓕_{r_n}}h(m_b) whenever the segment from a to a + m lies in the enlarged box.
(b) (Mixed finite differences, with fixed insertions.) Let g(a) = dim(π)⁻¹tr(X_1(a)⋯X_p(a)), where each factor X_i(a) is either a traversal link π(L_{n_i}(a)) of C or its inverse π(L_{n_i}(a))⁻¹ = π(L_{n_i}(a))^* (the orientation-reversed traversals of the Wilson word; at real points its real-direction derivatives are the adjoints of those of π(L_{n_i}), with the same norms, so (a) applies to it), with every traversal n of C occurring at most once among the link factors (as in the contour word, its inverse and their sub-words; review r4, item 1), or a fixed matrix N_i ∈ End(V_π) not depending on a, and put ‖N‖ := ∏_{i: X_i fixed}‖N_i‖_op (‖N‖ = 1 when every fixed factor is unitary; the closed contractions χ(L_{n_1}(a)⋯L_{n_j}(a)h₀), h₀ ∈ G, with h₀ anywhere in the word, and the words with a fixed unitary insertion such as π(P)⁻¹π(h) for a word P of links at a fixed argument, are of this kind with ‖N‖ = 1). Let 𝖩 be a finite set of bonds of D(Λ), a₀ real with |a₀_b|_σ ≤ r/4 for all b (the base point), and B with |B_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4 for b ∈ 𝖩. Then Δ_𝖩g(B) := Σ_{K⊆𝖩}(−1)^{|𝖩∖K|}g(1_KB + a₀) satisfies

  |Δ_𝖩g(B)| ≤ (N_trC_linkg₀·5r/4)^{|𝖩|}‖N‖ ≤ u_t^{|𝖩|}‖N‖,   and   |Δ_𝖩[∂_{e,b}g](B)| ≤ (N_trC_linkg₀·5r/4)^{|𝖩|}N_trC_linkg₀‖N‖ ≤ u_t^{|𝖩|}(C′_χ/2)g₀‖N‖

for every bond b of D(Λ) and h-unit e (u_t = C′_χg₀(1+2r) = 2N_trC_linkg₀(1+2r) ≥ N_trC_linkg₀·5r/4). Both hold at the reference data (U = id, lem:charts) with the same constants.

Proof. (a) Write π(L_n(a)) = Π_n(g₀𝖢_Ua|_{𝓔_c})π(U₁(r_n)) (on A_cov^{fin}, where no exterior input enters the row, as in the preamble) with Π_n(A) := π(e^{iΦ_c(A)(b₀(c))}) = e^{iπ(Φ_c(A)(b₀(c)))} for the designated bond r_n = b₀(c) (the component of the row phase at the designated bond; the symbol Π avoids a clash with the region Λ, review r4), analytic on the neighbourhood of the closed ball ‖A‖ ≤ ϑ_*/2 (composition of the analytic Φ_c with the entire exponential of the linear map π on 𝔤_ℂ) with ‖Π_n‖ ≤ C_Π there by the bound of the statement; for a free bond r_n, q_U(B)(r_n) = g₀B(r_n) (lem:row-locality), so π(L_n(a)) = e^{ig₀π(a_{r_n})}π(U₁(r_n)) depends on a_{r_n} alone, and its k-fold derivative at a real point is bounded by (C_πg₀)^k directly (Duhamel's formula: the k-th derivative of e^{ig₀π(a)} in the h-unit directions e_1,…,e_k is a sum over the k! orderings of the k insertions ig₀π(e_i), each of norm ≤ C_πg₀, between unitary factors, integrated over a simplex of volume 1/k!). The directional derivative ∂_{e,b} of a ↦ Π_n(g₀𝖢_Ua) is g₀DΠ_n(g₀𝖢_Ua)[𝖢_Ue_b] with ‖𝖢_Ue_b‖ ≤ C_𝖢 (lem:c2-uniform-chart(i); e_b the vector e in the slot b, of Hilbert norm 1), and the k-fold mixed derivative is g₀^kD^kΠ_n(A)[𝖢_Ue_{b_1},…,𝖢_Ue_{b_k}]. At a real a in the enlarged box, ‖A‖ ≤ ϑ_*/4, so the ball of radius ϑ_*/4 about A lies in the closed ball of radius ϑ_*/2; Cauchy's estimate for the analytic Π_n on that ball bounds the homogeneous polynomial D^kΠ_n(A)[h,…,h]/k! by C_Π(4‖h‖/ϑ_*)^k, and polarisation bounds the symmetric k-linear form D^kΠ_n(A)/k! by k^k/k! times the polynomial's norm; hence ‖D^kΠ_n(A)[h_1,…,h_k]‖ ≤ k^kC_Π(4/ϑ_*)^k∏‖h_i‖ ≤ (m_*C_Π4/ϑ_*)^k∏‖h_i‖ for k ≤ m_* (C_Π ≥ 1), giving ‖∂_{e_1,b_1}⋯∂_{e_k,b_k}π(L_n)‖ ≤ (4m_*C_ΠC_𝖢g₀/ϑ_*)^k ≤ (C_linkg₀)^k; π(U₁(r_n)) is a constant unitary. The derivative and difference bounds are the chain rule and the mean value inequality along the segment, ∂_s π(L_n(a + sm)) = Σ_{b∈𝓕_{r_n}}h(m_b)∂_{m_b/h(m_b),b}π(L_n)(a + sm), each term of norm ≤ h(m_b)C_linkg₀ by the case k = 1. (b) Write q := |𝖩| and B^s := (s_bB_b)_{b∈𝖩} + a₀ for s ∈ [0,1]^𝖩. For each b ∈ 𝖩, the difference in the label b alone is G(1) − G(0) = ∫₀¹∂_{s_b}G(s_b)ds_b for the function G of s_b at the other labels fixed, and iterating over the labels (the signs (−1)^{|𝖩∖K|} are exactly the iterated differences) gives Δ_𝖩g(B) = ∫_{[0,1]^𝖩}∂_{s_{b_1}}⋯∂_{s_{b_q}}g(B^s)ds with ∂_{s_b}g(B^s) = ⟨B_b,∇_bg⟩(B^s) = h(B_b)∂_{B_b/h(B_b),b}g(B^s), so |Δ_𝖩g(B)| ≤ ∏_{b∈𝖩}h(B_b)·sup_s|∂^𝖩g(B^s)|, where ∂^𝖩 is the mixed derivative in the q directions B_b/h(B_b) at the labels b ∈ 𝖩; every B^s is real and in the enlarged box (|s_bB_b + a₀_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4 + r/4 ≤ 2r). Product rule inside the normalised trace: the fixed factors N_i do not depend on a, and a link L_{n_i} depends on a_b only if b ∈ 𝓕_{r_{n_i}}, so ∂^𝖩g = Σ_{assignments}dim(π)⁻¹tr(X_1^{(𝖩_1)}⋯X_p^{(𝖩_p)}), where X_i^{(𝖩_i)} := ∂^{𝖩_i}π(L_{n_i}) for a link factor and X_i^{(∅)} := N_i for a fixed factor, the sum over the assignments 𝖩 = ⊔_i𝖩_i of each label b to one of the link factors with b ∈ 𝓕_{r_{n_i}}, of which there are at most N_tr^q; |tr(∏M_i)| ≤ dim(π)∏‖M_i‖_op, the undifferentiated links are unitary at real points, the fixed factors contribute ∏‖N_i‖ = ‖N‖, and each differentiated link has |𝖩_i| ≤ |𝓕_{r_{n_i}}| ≤ m_* − 1 directions, so by (a) sup|∂^𝖩g| ≤ (N_trC_linkg₀)^q‖N‖. With h(B_b) ≤ |B_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4 (h ≤ |·|_σ, as in the proof of Lemma 5.1) this gives |Δ_𝖩g(B)| ≤ (N_trC_linkg₀·5r/4)^q‖N‖ ≤ (N_trC_linkg₀(1+2r))^q‖N‖ ≤ u_t^q‖N‖, since C′_χ = 2N_trC_link ≥ N_trC_link (review r3, D1: no constant c_σ enters). For ∂_{e,b}g the same argument applies to the mixed derivative ∂^{𝖩}∂_{e,b}g of order q + 1 (the extra direction at the label b, possibly repeating a label of 𝖩; each link then carries at most m_* directions, within (a)): |Δ_𝖩[∂_{e,b}g](B)| ≤ (5r/4)^q(N_trC_linkg₀)^{q+1}‖N‖ ≤ u_t^q·N_trC_linkg₀‖N‖ = u_t^q(C′_χ/2)g₀‖N‖. At the reference data lem:charts holds for U = id with the same ϑ_*, C_𝖢, Q_*, so (a) and (b) hold there verbatim. ∎

Lemma 5.7⁰ replaces stage 4 §4.1's finite-difference bound (whose argument it follows: product rule inside the closed character, Cauchy estimates on the row chart, bounded traversal multiplicity) by a proof from stage 1 lem:charts and stage 2 lem:c2-uniform-chart at the carrier D(Λ); the parity gain of stage 4 §4.1 is not used.

Lemma 5.7 (the defect difference as a sum of marked connected terms). Let π be the irreducible unitary representation with χ = dim(π)⁻¹tr π, acting on V_π. For a group W and t ∈ {0,1} let T^t_W(B) be the ordered product of the reconstructed links along the consecutive traversals of ℋ(W) at the young variable 1_{𝒜(W)}B + tm (links off Ω₁ equal to their background value), P^t_W := T^t_W(0) (young variable tm throughout), and

  Υ^t_W := w^t_Λ(W) ∫ π(T^t_W(B)(P^t_W)⁻¹) d(⊗_{Z∈𝒜(W)}ν̂^{f,t}_{Λ,Z})(B) − a^Λ_W·id_{V_π} ∈ End(V_π),

so that dim(π)⁻¹tr(Υ^t_Wπ(h)) = c^t_W(h) − a^Λ_Wχ(h) with c^t_W(h) := w^t_Λ(W)∫χ(T^t_W(B)(P^t_W)⁻¹h)d⊗ν̂^{f,t}_Λ (stage 4 §6.1 at the young variable tm). (a) (Burnside.) There is K_χ depending on π only with ‖Υ‖_op ≤ K_χ sup_{h∈G}|dim(π)⁻¹tr(Υπ(h))| for every Υ ∈ End(V_π). (b) For a compatible family 𝔅 = {W_1,…,W_n} with short intervals in cyclic order, R^t_Λ(𝔅) = dim(π)⁻¹tr(Υ^t_{W_1}U^t_1Υ^t_{W_2}U^t_2⋯Υ^t_{W_n}U^t_n), where U^t_j := π(P^t_{W_j}G^t_j) with G^t_j the ordered product of the links of the traversals strictly between ℋ(W_j) and ℋ(W_{j+1}) (cyclically; G^t_j = id if there are none) at the young variable tm: the t-background word of the interval ℋ(W_j) followed by the gap to the next interval (for a whole-contour group, n = 1, G^t_1 = id and U^t_1 = π(P^t_W)). (c) Hence

  R¹_Λ(𝔅) − R⁰_Λ(𝔅) = Σ_{j=1}^n dim(π)⁻¹tr(Υ¹_{W_1}U¹_1⋯(Υ¹_{W_j} − Υ⁰_{W_j})U⁰_j⋯Υ⁰_{W_n}U⁰_n) + Σ_{j} dim(π)⁻¹tr(⋯Υ¹_{W_j}(U¹_j − U⁰_j)Υ⁰_{W_{j+1}}⋯),

  |R¹_Λ(𝔅) − R⁰_Λ(𝔅)| ≤ K_χ^{|𝔅|}[ Σ_{W₀∈𝔅} 𝔡^{diff}_{W₀} ∏_{W≠W₀}𝔡̄_W + C′_χg₀(Σ_{b∈I_C}h(m_b)) ∏_{W∈𝔅}𝔡̄_W ],                                           (5.7)

with 𝔡̄_W := max_{t∈{0,1}} sup_h|c^t_W(h) − a^Λ_Wχ(h)| and 𝔡^{diff}_W := sup_h|c¹_W(h) − c⁰_W(h)|.
(d) (Marks in 𝔡^{diff}_W.) With M^t_Λ(W) := (1/k(W)!)∏_{Z∈𝒜(W)}ρ_t m(Z) ∏_{j}|Φ|⁺_Λ(𝔠_j) (the positive group majorant with root weight ρ_tm(Z), the same for t = 0 and t = 1 since ρ_t does not depend on t; write M_Λ(W) for it; it is the attempt's M(W) with ρ = ρ_t),

  𝔡^{diff}_W ≤ M_Λ(W) [ Σ_{Z∈𝒜(W)} 𝔐_Λ(Z) + Σ_j Σ_{D∈𝔠_j} 𝔐_Λ(D) + C′_χ g₀ Σ_{b∈I_{ℋ(W)}} h(m_b) ],   I_{ℋ(W)} := free inputs of the traversals of ℋ(W),       (5.8)

where the last term is the source-shift mark (the root measures contribute their ∏_Zρ_tm(Z) through the finite-difference factor u_t^{|𝖩|} exactly as in M_Λ(W); the shift adds the factor C′_χg₀Σh(m_b)).
(e) (Bounds for the undifferenced slots.) 𝔡̄_W ≤ M_Λ(W) + |a^Λ_W| =: 𝔟̄_W.

Proof. (a) Υ ↦ (h ↦ tr(Υπ(h))) is linear and injective on End(V_π) (π(G) spans End(V_π) for an irreducible representation, Burnside's theorem), so the sup over h is a norm on the finite-dimensional End(V_π), equivalent to the operator norm; K_χ is the equivalence constant. (b) is stage 4 §6.1's factorisation at the young variable tm: a traversal in ℋ(W_j) has no contact with a root of another group, so its link uses W_j's root variables (and tm); a traversal outside every interval has its link at tm; in the expansion of the product, a slot W_j taken from the ∫π(T^t_{W_j}(P^t_{W_j})⁻¹)-term contributes π(T^t_{W_j}(B))π(G^t_j) (the links of ℋ(W_j) at the root variables, then the gap at tm) and a slot taken from −a^Λ_{W_j}id contributes −a^Λ_{W_j}π(P^t_{W_j}G^t_j) (the links of ℋ(W_j) and the gap at tm), so the normalised trace of the ordered product is f_ξ(1_{𝒜(𝒮)}B + tm) for the subfamily 𝒮 of slots of the first kind, times ∏_{W∉𝒮}(−a^Λ_W); expanding ∏(X_W − a_Wid) over subsets reproduces the signed sum R^t_Λ(𝔅) of Lemma 5.3(c) by multilinearity of the trace, and the Fubini evaluation of the ∫π(…)-slots against the product measure is the one in lem:schur's proof. (c) Telescoping of the ordered product in its 2n factors; then |tr(X_1⋯X_{2n})| ≤ dim(π)∏‖X_i‖_op, ‖U^t_j‖ = 1, ‖Υ^t_W‖ ≤ K_χ𝔡̄_W, ‖Υ¹_W − Υ⁰_W‖ ≤ K_χ𝔡^{diff}_W by (a), and ‖U¹_j − U⁰_j‖ ≤ Σ_{traversals in ℋ(W_j) and in the gap after it}‖π(link at m) − π(link at 0)‖ ≤ C′_χg₀Σ_{b∈I_C}h(m_b) (the links are unitary and the word is their ordered product, so the difference is at most the sum of the link differences; ‖π(L_n(m)) − π(L_n(0))‖ ≤ C_linkg₀Σ_{b∈𝓕_{r_n}}h(m_b) by Lemma 5.7⁰(a), the segment sm lying in the box of radius r/4; over j the intervals and gaps partition the traversals of C, each traversal's inputs lie in I_C, and a bond is an input of at most N_tr traversals, so the total is at most N_trC_linkg₀Σ_{b∈I_C}h(m_b) ≤ C′_χg₀Σ_{b∈I_C}h(m_b)). (d) c¹_W − c⁰_W is telescoped over its t-dependent slots: the k(W) cluster weights Φ^{v,t}_Λ(𝔠_j), the |𝒜(W)| root measures ν̂^{f,t}_{Λ,Z} (through their split selections ℓ_Z, Lemma 5.2(a)), and the source word g_t(B) := χ(T^t_W(B)(P^t_W)⁻¹h). Cluster slot: |Φ^{v,1} − Φ^{v,0}|(𝔠_j) ≤ Σ_{D∈𝔠_j}𝔐_Λ(D)|Φ|⁺(𝔠_j) (Lemma 5.6(b)), the other clusters at |Φ^{v,t}| ≤ |Φ|⁺, and the root integral |∫Δ_𝖩g_t d⊗Λ^{f,t}_{ℓ_Z}| ≤ ∏_Z u_t^{|𝖩_Z|}‖Λ^{f,t}_{ℓ_Z}‖_TV summed with Lemma 5.2(c) and (u_t/u_*)^{|𝖩_Z|} ≤ ρ_t gives ∏_Zρ_tm(Z). Here sup|Δ_𝖩g_t| ≤ u_t^{|𝖩|} is the first assertion of Lemma 5.7⁰(b) for g_t(B) = dim(π)⁻¹tr(π(T_W(1_KB + tm))N_t) with the fixed unitary factor N_t := π(P^t_W)⁻¹π(h) (‖N_t‖ = 1: P^t_W is the word of ℋ(W) at the fixed argument tm and h a fixed group element) at the base point a₀ = tm (|tm_b|_σ ≤ r/4) with the increments 1_KB, |B_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4 on the support of ν^{f,t}. Root slot: one root Z₀ carries Λ^{f,1}_{ℓ} − Λ^{f,0}_{ℓ} and Lemma 5.6(a) gives Σ_ℓ u_*^{|𝖩|}‖Λ¹_ℓ − Λ⁰_ℓ‖ ≤ 𝔐_Λ(Z₀)m(Z₀), with the same u_t^{|𝖩|} from the finite difference, so the root pays 𝔐_Λ(Z₀)ρ_tm(Z₀). Source slot: all measures and weights at one t, and the insertion is Δ_𝖩[g₁ − g₀](B) = ∫₀¹Δ_𝖩[∂_sg_s](B)ds with g_s(a) := dim(π)⁻¹tr(π(T_W(a))π(P^s_W)⁻¹π(h)), evaluated in the K-term of Δ_𝖩 at a = 1_KB + sm, T_W(a) the word of ℋ(W) at the young variable a and P^s_W = T_W(sm) (the de-younged bonds are constants, so only the inputs in I_{ℋ(W)} ∩ D(Λ) vary; ∂_s below is the derivative of s ↦ g_s(1_KB + sm) in each K-term). Two things depend on s: the argument 1_KB + sm of the link word and the fixed factor N_s := π(P^s_W)⁻¹π(h), whose links sit at the argument sm and not at 1_KB + sm (review r3, D2). Hence ∂_sg_s = Σ_{b∈I_{ℋ(W)}∩D(Λ)}h(m_b)∂_{m_b/h(m_b),b}g̃_s + dim(π)⁻¹tr(π(T_W(1_KB + sm))N′_s), with g̃_s(a) := dim(π)⁻¹tr(π(T_W(a))N_s) (fixed unitary insertion, ‖N_s‖ = 1) and N′_s := ∂_s[π(P^s_W)⁻¹]π(h) = −π(P^s_W)⁻¹(∂_sπ(P^s_W))π(P^s_W)⁻¹π(h), a fixed non-unitary insertion with ‖N′_s‖ ≤ ‖∂_sπ(P^s_W)‖ ≤ Σ_{n∈ℋ(W)}‖∂_sπ(L_n(sm))‖ ≤ Σ_{n∈ℋ(W)}C_linkg₀Σ_{b∈𝓕_{r_n}}h(m_b) ≤ N_trC_linkg₀Σ_{b∈I_{ℋ(W)}}h(m_b) (product rule for the word of unitaries; Lemma 5.7⁰(a) along the segment sm, which lies in the box of radius r/4; each bond is an input of at most N_tr traversals). The mixed difference of order |𝖩| of the first sum is bounded by the second assertion of Lemma 5.7⁰(b) (base point sm, increments 1_KB, ‖N_s‖ = 1): ≤ u_t^{|𝖩|}(C′_χ/2)g₀Σ_{b∈I_{ℋ(W)}}h(m_b); that of the second term by the first assertion with the insertion N′_s: ≤ u_t^{|𝖩|}‖N′_s‖ ≤ u_t^{|𝖩|}N_trC_linkg₀Σ_{b∈I_{ℋ(W)}}h(m_b) = u_t^{|𝖩|}(C′_χ/2)g₀Σ_{b∈I_{ℋ(W)}}h(m_b). Together sup|Δ_𝖩[∂_sg_s]| ≤ u_t^{|𝖩|}C′_χg₀Σ_{b∈I_{ℋ(W)}}h(m_b): the factor 2 in C′_χ = 2N_trC_link pays the two s-dependent words. This gives (5.8); the three sums are over the marks of the three kinds. (e) |c^t_W(h)| ≤ M^t_Λ(W) by the same root and cluster bounds (no mark), and |a^Λ_Wχ(h)| ≤ |a^Λ_W|. ∎

Nothing in Lemma 5.7 uses a parity (odd-root) cancellation: the translated cutoffs χ^t_b are not even in B for t > 0, so stage 4 §4.1's reversal argument is unavailable and only the first-order gain ρ_t = u_t/u_* per root is taken; this is why ρ_t = Cg₀(1+2r) rather than Cg₀²(1+r)⁴ appears.

Lemma 5.8 (pins for the t-family). Let F(W) be the footprint of W (the LM-cubes of its roots, cluster constituents and hull stencils, attempt §19.1), σ(W) := s(W) = n(W) + |ℋ(W)|/(LM) its size (attempt §18.3, stage 4 §6.2; n(W) counts the cubes of the roots and cluster constituents with tuple multiplicity, so σ(W) is at least the number of domain occurrences in W), ν := η the reserve constant of attempt §18.3 and stage 4 §6.2, and C_F := A_E the envelope constant of attempt §19.3 (|E(W)| ≤ A_Eσ(W)). (a) Under (5.T)(iv) the positive summation of stage 4 §6.2 and attempt §18.3, §20.1 applies to M_Λ(W) (it uses only the root weights ρm(Z) with ρ = ρ_t ≤ ρ_pin, the positive incompatibility-tree weights |Φ|⁺_Λ = M_v, and the field-independent domain/cluster/interval geometry, none of which involves the carrier beyond the cubes): with C_pin the constant C of stage 4 §6.2's display,

  sup_Δ Σ_{W: Δ∈F(W)} e^{4νσ(W)} M_Λ(W) + sup_{n} Σ_{W: n∈ℋ(W)} e^{4νσ(W)} M_Λ(W) ≤ C_pin ρ_t.

Consequently, with ε̄_t := 2K_χC_pinρ_t,

  sup_Δ Σ_{W: Δ∈F(W)} e^{2νσ(W)} K_χ e^{θ°(W)} 𝔟̄_W ≤ ε̄_t,   sup_{n} Σ_{W: n∈ℋ(W)} e^{2νσ(W)} K_χ e^{θ°(W)} 𝔟̄_W ≤ ε̄_t,   Σ_W K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄_W ≤ n_C ε̄_t,

the last because every group has a contact with the contour (Lemma 5.3(a)) and so meets Σ_n. The same statements hold for the reference gas at the carrier I (stage 1's reference data, activities a_W) with the same constants: the proof uses only the stage 1–2 lemmas at the carrier, which hold at I by stage 1–2 themselves. (b) (Decay from the footprint to the contour.) If Δ ∈ F(W) then σ(W) ≥ dist(Δ, C)/(C_FLM): the envelope E(W) ⊇ F(W) ∪ C(ℋ(W)) is connected under maximum-norm adjacency of LM-cubes and has at most A_Eσ(W) cubes (attempt §19.3), so its ℓ^∞ diameter in fine units is at most A_ELMσ(W), and it contains Δ and a cube meeting the contour. Hence for every cube Δ,

  Σ_{W: Δ∈F(W)} e^{νσ(W)} K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄_W ≤ ε̄_t e^{−ν dist(Δ,C)/(C_FLM)},   Σ_{W: Δ∈F(W)} σ(W) K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄_W ≤ ν⁻¹ ε̄_t e^{−ν dist(Δ,C)/(C_FLM)}.

Proof. (a) The first display is the cited summation with ρ_t for the root weights; the second follows from 𝔟̄_W ≤ 2M_Λ(W) (Lemma 5.3(d) and Lemma 5.7(e)) and e^{θ°(W)} ≤ e^{νσ(W)/16} ≤ e^{2νσ(W)} (Lemma 5.3(d)). (b) e^{2νσ(W)} ≥ e^{νσ(W)}e^{ν dist(Δ,C)/(C_FLM)} by the geometric fact, and σ ≤ ν⁻¹e^{νσ} (x ≤ e^x). ∎

Proposition 5.9 (the source-containing connected boundary terms). In the square/torus geometry of 5.0, under the hypotheses of Lemmas 4.0–4.4, (4.T), (4.T′) and (5.T), on every labelled term with final region Λ, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, on A_cov^{fin},

  |ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ]| ≤ |S°_Λ| e^{2n_Cε̄_t} [ C_1 g₀ r 𝔖_1(Λ) + C_2 ε̄_t r^{d_G} 𝔖_2(Λ) ],                                                                     (5.9)
  𝔖_1(Λ) := Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)} e^{−μ_H dist(e,I_C)/2},   𝔖_2(Λ) := Σ_{e∈J(Λ)} e^{−μ′dist(e,C)},   μ′ := min(μ_H/4, ν/(2C_FLM)),

with C_1 := C_mϑN_lat(μ_H/2)(C_{resp,χ} + (1+N_tr)C′_χ) and C_2 := ν⁻¹C_dist[4C_ϖN_lat(μ_H/2) + N_hoste^{νR_st/(C_FLM)}(64C_{bd} + 16C′_{bd}C_mϑN_lat(μ_H/2))], C_dist := e^{7ν/C_F}, R_st := 4M (≥ 4L + 3M, since M ≥ 8LM₂ ≥ 8L² ≥ 4L by eq:c-standing-conditions), constants depending on L, the group, M, κ, ω_*, ν, ϑ, the character and the stencil ranges only, in the square/torus geometry of 5.0; g₀ r → 0 and ε̄_t = 2K_χC_pinρ_t → 0 as g₀ → 0.

Proof. Divide (5.5) by S°_Λ (Lemma 5.3(d)): ℬ^Λ/S°_Λ = [f_ξ(m) − f_ξ(0)] + Σ_{𝔅≠∅}q°_Λ(𝔅)[R¹_Λ(𝔅) − R⁰_Λ(𝔅)]. The first term is Lemma 5.4: ≤ C_{resp,χ}C_mϑN_lat(μ_H/2)g₀r𝔖_1(Λ). For the second, insert (5.7) and |q°_Λ(𝔅)| ≤ ∏_We^{θ°(W)}. The background-word part: C′_χg₀(Σ_{I_C}h(m_b))Σ_𝔅∏_{W∈𝔅}K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄_W ≤ C′_χg₀(Σ_{I_C}h(m_b))∏_W(1 + K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄_W) ≤ C′_χg₀(Σ_{I_C}h(m_b))e^{n_Cε̄_t} (families are sets of distinct groups; Lemma 5.8(a)), and Σ_{I_C}h(m_b) ≤ C_mϑN_lat(μ_H/2)r𝔖_1(Λ) by (5.2): together with the first term this is C_mϑN_lat(μ_H/2)(C_{resp,χ} + C′_χ)g₀r𝔖_1(Λ)e^{n_Cε̄_t}, the first part of the C_1 term (the second part is (ii) below). The marked part: Σ_𝔅Σ_{W₀∈𝔅}∏_We^{θ°(W)}K_χ𝔡^{diff}_{W₀}∏_{W≠W₀}K_χ𝔡̄_W ≤ e^{n_Cε̄_t}Σ_{W₀}K_χe^{θ°(W₀)}𝔡^{diff}_{W₀} (sum first over 𝔅 ∖ {W₀}, as above). Insert (5.8). (i) Root and cluster marks. Write 𝔐_Λ(Δ) := Σ_{b∈E(Δ)}𝔪_b + Σ_{Y anchored at Δ}𝔪_Y for an LM-cube Δ (a host is anchored at the LM-cube containing its lowest M-cube; a bond meeting two cubes is counted in both), so that 𝔐_Λ(Z) ≤ Σ_{Δ⊆Z}𝔐_Λ(Δ) for every domain Z. The occurrences of a cube Δ among the roots and cluster constituents of W₀ number mult_{W₀}(Δ) ≤ (number of domain occurrences in W₀) ≤ σ(W₀) (Lemma 5.8), so Σ_{Z∈𝒜(W₀)}𝔐_Λ(Z) + Σ_jΣ_{D∈𝔠_j}𝔐_Λ(D) ≤ Σ_{Δ∈F(W₀)}mult_{W₀}(Δ)𝔐_Λ(Δ) ≤ σ(W₀)Σ_{Δ∈F(W₀)}𝔐_Λ(Δ), and Σ_{W₀}K_χe^{θ°(W₀)}M_Λ(W₀)σ(W₀)Σ_{Δ∈F(W₀)}𝔐_Λ(Δ) ≤ Σ_Δ𝔐_Λ(Δ)Σ_{W₀: Δ∈F(W₀)}σ(W₀)K_χe^{θ°}𝔟̄_{W₀} ≤ ν⁻¹ε̄_tΣ_Δ𝔐_Λ(Δ)e^{−ν dist(Δ,C)/(C_FLM)} by Lemma 5.8(b). Convert cube distances to bond and host distances: for b ∈ E(Δ), dist(Δ,C) ≥ dist(b,C) − LM − 1, and for Y anchored at Δ, dist(Δ,C) ≥ dist(Y,C) − 4M − LM (a host has side at most 4M), so e^{−ν dist(Δ,C)/(C_FLM)} ≤ C_diste^{−ν dist(b,C)/(C_FLM)}, resp. ≤ C_diste^{−ν dist(Y,C)/(C_FLM)}, with C_dist := e^{7ν/C_F} ((2LM + 4M + 1)/(LM) ≤ 7). Now the three kinds of 𝔪-weights. Bond marks: a bond lies in E(Δ) for at most two cubes Δ (no site lies on a cube face), so Σ_ΔΣ_{b∈E(Δ)}𝔪_be^{−ν dist(Δ,C)/(C_FLM)} ≤ 2C_distΣ_{b∈D(Λ)}𝔪_be^{−ν dist(b,C)/(C_FLM)}, and Σ_{b∈D(Λ)}𝔪_be^{−ν dist(b,C)/(C_FLM)} ≤ 2C_ϖr^{d_G}Σ_{b}Σ_{e∈J₁}e^{−μ_Hd(b,e)}e^{−ν dist(b,C)/(C_FLM)} ≤ 2C_ϖr^{d_G}N_lat(μ_H/2)Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ′dist(e,C)} (so the bond term of C_2 is 4C_ϖN_lat(μ_H/2) after the factor C_dist is taken out) by the splitting in the proof of (5.2) with μ′ ≤ min(μ_H/2, ν/(C_FLM))/2. Host marks with an exterior input e ∈ J(Λ): the primitive's stencil has ℓ^∞ diameter at most 4L and contains its input e, and its host lies in the M-cubes meeting the stencil with one further M-layer (eq:part1-potential-host), so every point of the host is within 4L + 3M ≤ 4M = R_st of e (M ≥ 8L² ≥ 4L), dist(Y,C) ≥ dist(e,C) − R_st, and there are at most N_host such hosts per e (§5.2); their total is ≤ N_hoste^{νR_st/(C_FLM)}C_{bd}g₀(1+3r)³Σ_{e∈J(Λ)}e^{−μ′dist(e,C)} (ν/(C_FLM) ≥ μ′). Host marks without exterior input: Σ_Y𝔪_Ye^{−ν dist(Y,C)/(C_FLM)} ≤ C′_{bd}g₀(1+3r)²Σ_{b∈D(Λ)}h(m_b)·#{Y ∈ 𝐃₀(Λ): b ∈ E(Y)}·e^{νR_st/(C_FLM)}e^{−ν dist(b,C)/(C_FLM)} ≤ C′_{bd}N_hoste^{νR_st/(C_FLM)}g₀(1+3r)²C_mϑrN_lat(μ_H/2)Σ_{e∈J₁}e^{−μ′dist(e,C)} by (5.2) (dist(Y,C) ≥ dist(b,C) − 4M = dist(b,C) − R_st for b ∈ E(Y), a host having side at most 4M; at most N_host hosts contain b). (ii) Source-shift marks: Σ_{W₀}K_χe^{θ°}M_Λ(W₀)C′_χg₀Σ_{b∈I_{ℋ(W₀)}}h(m_b) ≤ C′_χg₀Σ_{b∈I_C}h(m_b)Σ_{n∈n(b)}Σ_{W₀: n∈ℋ(W₀)}K_χe^{θ°}𝔟̄_{W₀} ≤ N_trε̄_tC′_χg₀Σ_{b∈I_C}h(m_b) (the first step is "≤", not "=": a bond b ∈ I_{ℋ(W₀)} is an input of at least one traversal n ∈ ℋ(W₀) ∩ n(b), so every term on the left occurs at least once on the right, possibly several times when b is an input of several traversals of ℋ(W₀); review r3, D5) ≤ N_trC′_χg₀C_mϑN_lat(μ_H/2)r𝔖_1(Λ) (n(b) the set of traversals with input b, |n(b)| ≤ N_tr, Lemma 5.7⁰; the contour pin of Lemma 5.8(a) for each n ∈ n(b); ε̄_t ≤ 1 by (5.T)(v); then (5.2)); this is the second part of the C_1 term, C_1 = C_mϑN_lat(μ_H/2)(C_{resp,χ} + (1+N_tr)C′_χ). Collecting, with e^{n_Cε̄_t} ≤ e^{2n_Cε̄_t}, g₀(1+3r)³ ≤ 64r^{d_G} and (1+3r)²r ≤ 16r^{d_G} for r ≥ 1, d_G ≥ 3, gives (5.9) with C_2 = ν⁻¹C_dist[4C_ϖN_lat(μ_H/2) + N_hoste^{νR_st/(C_FLM)}(64C_{bd} + 16C′_{bd}C_mϑN_lat(μ_H/2))]. ∎

Corollary 5.10 (the exterior sum on the far event). J(Λ) = J ∪ (I ∖ D(Λ)) with J = E_{Ω,*} ∖ I ⊆ E(Λ₁^c) (a bond not in I has both endpoints off Λ₁, by the bond convention of §0). On the far event N_b^c of attempt §20.2 (dist(Γ_C, E(Λ₁^c)) > b, b = ⌊LMR₁/16⌋) every e ∈ J has dist(e,C) ≥ b − 1 in the convention of 5.0 (attempt §20.2's distance for N_b may be measured between bond sites rather than to the vertex set of C, which changes a distance by at most 1; review r4), and

  Σ_{e∈J} e^{−μ′dist(e,C)} ≤ Σ_{s≥b−1} #{e : dist(e,C) ≤ s} e^{−μ′s} ≤ 4n_C Σ_{s≥b−1}(2s+1)⁴e^{−μ′s} ≤ n_C N_lat(μ′/2) e^{−μ′(b−1)/2}

(at most 4(2s+1)⁴ bonds within ℓ^∞ distance s of each of the n_C vertices of C). Hence on A_cov^{fin} ∩ N_b^c,

  |ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ]| ≤ |S°_Λ| e^{2n_Cε̄_t} [ (C_1′g₀r + C_2ε̄_t r^{d_G}) ( n_CN_lat(μ′/2)e^{−μ′(b−1)/2} + 𝔇_Λ ) ],   𝔇_Λ := Σ_{e∈I∖D(Λ)} e^{−μ′dist(e,C)} ≤ |I ∖ D(Λ)|,          (5.10)

using 𝔖_1(Λ) ≤ e^{μ_H(2L+1)/2}𝔖_2(Λ) (dist(e,I_C) ≥ dist(e,C) − (2L+1), μ_H/2 ≥ μ′), with C_1′ := C_1e^{μ_H(2L+1)/2}. The first summand is volume-independent and, since b = ⌊ℓ_mac/16⌋ with ℓ_mac = LMR₁ growing as a fixed positive power of log g₁⁻² (stage 3 §7, the scale integer R₁; g₁ ≥ g₀/(1+β₀), so log g₁⁻² ≥ log g₀⁻² − 2log(1+β₀)), beats every polynomial in r: r^{d_G}e^{−μ′(b−1)/2} ≤ e^{−μ′(b−1)/4} as soon as b − 1 ≥ (4d_G/μ′)log r, which holds for g₀ small, since b − 1 ≥ ℓ_mac/16 − 2 grows as a power of log g₀⁻² while log r = p₀ log log g₀⁻² + log A₁. The second summand 𝔇_Λ is the term's de-younged bonds weighted by their distance to the contour; it is a quantity of the labelled term, bounded by the number |I ∖ D(Λ)| of de-younged bonds, and its average over the labels is item 6's bookkeeping (3.4(a)–(b): a de-younged bond z of D^{(0)} ∖ D^{fin} is not itself marked; it is attributed to a marked bond e (a collar-test failure M_e) with x_e within 102ℓ+1 of an endpoint of z, and 3.4(a) pays the marked bonds attributed to z by Σ_e p^ϑ_e ≤ N_retq_ϑ, which is the term N_retq_ϑ of (3.4); the round-0 bonds D ∖ D^{(0)} carry no marks and are paid by p_z^{(0)} ≤ q, Lemma 3.0(b); review r3, D6).

5.5 What item 5 establishes and what it does not. Established, on every labelled term with final region Λ, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, on A_cov^{fin}: the translated probe measures, the probe-enlarged majorant (5.3), the dummy comparison and the centred expectation for the t-family (Lemma 5.2; caveat A1 of review r2 discharged); the joint extraction for the t-family with the t-independent reference data at the carrier D(Λ), the Schur identity and the decomposition (5.5) of ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ] into the shift term and the defect differences (Lemma 5.3); the shift term (Lemma 5.4) and the decay form of the mean shift (Lemma 5.1); the conditional-Gaussian shell bound (Lemma 5.5), which pays a translated-cutoff mark by r^{d_G−1}|m_b| without touching the reserve; the marked differences of root measures, activities and cluster weights (Lemma 5.6); the telescoping of R¹_Λ(𝔅) − R⁰_Λ(𝔅) into closed connected terms each carrying one mark — a translated cutoff (bond), a potential with an exterior input or with the shift (host), or the shift of the source word — with the perimeter factor S°_Λ extracted before absolute values (Lemma 5.7); the pins and the summation (Lemma 5.8, Proposition 5.9), giving the bound (5.9), of the pass note's form with the polynomial r^{d_G} in place of the heuristic (1+r)² and the family-sum factor e^{2n_Cε̄_t} ((7) below); and the splitting (5.10) on the far event, where the exterior part is volume-independent and beats every power of r. The marked terms are the source-containing connected boundary terms: each is a closed contraction (Lemma 5.7(b)) connected to J(Λ) through its mark, and each mark pays its distance to the contour by the footprint pin (Lemma 5.8(b)) or by the decay of m (Lemma 5.1). Not established here: (1) the identification of S°_Λ with κ_Ce^{E°_ξ} — stage 1's closing remark (after lem:covered) assigns the identification of S°_{ξ,C} at the carrier I with the flat coefficient κ_Ce^{E°_ξ} to a planned section sec:one-step-insertion, which stage 1's label audit lists among the unwritten stage 3–4 targets and which no draft or the TeX contains yet (the TeX's existing identification theorem is prop:regional-square-extraction, eq:regional-square-extraction: S°_{D,C_n,T} = κ_Ce^{E°_{D,C_n,T}} for the reference scalar eq:regional-square-scalar at a general young carrier D, with |E°| ≤ Cτ²[Σ_je^{−cd_j} + 4ne^{−cι_T} + (n+1)e^{−cn}], d_j = dist(K_j, D^c) − R₀; its application needs the identity S°_Λ = S°_{D(Λ),C,T}, i.e. closure P4 Step 1 with I replaced by D(Λ), which is not checked here); the reference data at D(Λ) differ from those at I not only in the groups whose roots or clusters meet I ∖ D(Λ) but also in H° (the principal restriction to D(Λ)), the eligible labels 𝖫_Z, the host set 𝐃₀(Λ), the seed sets E₀ and their covariances C_{E₀}, so the activities of the groups near I ∖ D(Λ) change as well, and no bound on the ratio S°_Λ/S°_{ξ,C} is established or claimed here (review r3); the direct identification of S°_Λ at the retreated carrier through prop:regional-square-extraction with D = D(Λ) (item 7, planned, not yet approved) is the route by which (5.9) is to be read as κ_C⁻¹|ℬ_ξ| ≤ …; (2) the history average of 𝔇_Λ (item 6); (3) the complementary events: A_cov^{fin,c} is paid by 3.4(a) (its bound in terms of q + N_retq_ϑ), and N_b is stage 4 §2's far-history exclusion, whose probability is paid there (not reproduced here); (4) the inputs listed in 5.0 are used as stated (the finite-difference bound is not among them: Lemma 5.7⁰ proves it from stage 1 lem:charts and stage 2 lem:c2-uniform-chart at the carrier D(Λ), at the base point tm, with increments ≤ 5r/4 and with one more derivative order): stage 4 §4.2/§7.1's dummy comparison, §6.1's factorisation, §6.2's positive group summation (C_pin, ρ_pin), attempt §13's and §19.4's reference-gas cluster expansion and rooted bound, §18.3/§20.1's positive summation and pins, §19.3's envelope count, and the TeX's tree-graph inequality; their own verification is stage 4's, the attempt file's and the TeX's, and a failure of any of them propagates to (5.9) (ε̄_t = 2K_χC_pinρ_t is the pin bound of those sections with ρ_t for the root weights; it is not re-derived here); (5) the thresholds (5.T)(i)–(v) and ω_* ≤ 1/2 (accepted by the user 2026-10-19 together with (4.T), (4.T′) and the period hypothesis of §3.1: all are smallness conditions on g₀ with constants depending on L, M, M₂, the group, the character, κ, ω_*, η, B_h, A₁, p₀, ϑ and the stencil ranges, none on n, the periods or the lattice spacing except through g₀; ω_* ≤ 1/2 is a normalisation of the host budget; the period hypothesis is independent of n and holds once n ≥ 67MR₁ under the TeX's K_i ≥ 3(n+2)) must join (4.T), (4.T′) in stage 2's threshold item 4 [done 2026-10-19 by the user's decision: stage 2 draft app:thresholds item 4 now lists (R.T)(i)–(ii) and (5.T)(iii)–(v), which imply (4.T), (4.T′), (5.T)(i)–(ii), with ω_* ≤ 1/2 fixed in the order of choice; stage 2 check record item 14]; (6) the parity gain of stage 4 §4.1 is not available for t > 0 (5.4), so the smallness per root is ρ_t = Cg₀(1+2r)/u_*, first order, which suffices for (5.9) but is weaker than the physical-endpoint ρ_ph of attempt §20.1 [superseded 2026-10-19 by §5R: Lemma 5R.3 gives the second-order root weight ρ_ph (§5R's quantity, not attempt §20.1's) for the undifferenced slots at t ∈ {0,1} and for a^Λ_W, by evenness at t = 0, the Cauchy parameter for roots with ordinary hosts, and the large-field payment of the translated-cutoff marks at t = 1]; (7) the factor e^{2n_Cε̄_t} in (5.9) is the cost of summing over the undifferenced groups of a family, ∏_W(1 + K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄_W) ≤ e^{n_Cε̄_t}, produced by the same mechanism as the factor e^{C_χn_Cρ(…)} − 1 in stage 4 §12's defect bound (an exponential family series with a one-group weight summed over n_C contour anchors), though stage 4's factor carries the small factor of its subtracted slots and ours is a multiplicative factor ≥ 1; at fixed n_C it is 1 + O(n_Cg₀r), but it is not a constant uniformly in n_C, and a bound of the pass note's literal form C n_C(…) uniformly in n_C would need the undifferenced slots resummed against the reference exclusions, for which the joint marked expansion of the boundary operand sketched in attempt §20.4 would be the setting; this is not done here. The user (2026-10-19) does not accept the form e^{2n_Cε̄_t} as final, for the following reason, checked against the TeX: every n-dependence of the TeX is either exponentially suppressed or a perimeter rate per coarse segment — κ_C = e^{−δm P(C)}z_c⁴ has the rate δm, built from one-mark components of weight τ² (dχ_id = 0, TeX L401), and the tolerated error E° has the rate Cτ² per segment (TeX L1023, L1115), τ = C[g₀(1+r)³ + e^{−cr²}] — while e^{2n_Cε̄_t} is a perimeter rate 2Lε̄_t per coarse segment (2n_Cε̄_t over the 4n segments, n_C = 4nL; corrected from 8Lε̄_t, review of §5R) with ε̄_t = 2K_χC_pinC′_χg₀(1+2r)/u_* = O(g₀r), first order, larger than δm by a factor of order 1/τ; for n_C ≳ (log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀}/(g₀r) the bound (5.10) no longer puts the boundary term below κ_C, and in the TeX's limit n → ∞ at fixed g₀ the perimeter coefficient would not be distinguishable from the boundary error. The pass note's linear form has rate zero. Two repairs are known: the second-order (parity) gain for the t-family, ρ_t → O(g₀²(1+r)⁴), which brings the rate to O(τ²), the order the TeX accepts for E° (it needs an argument replacing the evenness of the cutoffs, lost for t > 0, (6) above); or the resummation of the undifferenced slots against the reference exclusions (attempt §20.4). Neither is done here [the first is done in §5R (approved 2026-10-19): Proposition 5R.4 gives (5.9R)–(6.4R) with e^{n_Cε̄_ph}, ε̄_ph ≤ 32K_χC_pinC_{ph}g₀²(1+r)⁴ = O(τ²), perimeter rate Lε̄_ph per coarse segment; the second is not done]; (8) the labels enter (5.9)–(5.10) additively through 𝔇_Λ and 𝔖_2(Λ) and multiplicatively through |S°_Λ| (Λ = Λ₁^{fin} is label-dependent); the multiplicative dependence is not averaged over the labels but divided out: item 6 bounds |ℬ^Λ|/|S°_Λ| and uses |S°_Λ| ≤ 1 (Proposition 6.4), and the identification of S°_Λ with κ_Ce^{E°_Λ} at the retreated carrier ((1) above) is what makes the quotient the pass note's κ_C⁻¹|ℬ|; (9) the square/torus geometry of 5.0 is a standing hypothesis of C_pin, A_F, A_E and the hull bounds.

## 5R. Rate repair: second-order bounds for the undifferenced slots (calculation authorised by the user 2026-10-19; isolated review r1 2026-10-19, see 5R.5(6))

5R.0 Target and mechanisms. Proposition 5.9 carries the family-sum factor e^{n_Cε̄_t} (written e^{2n_Cε̄_t} in (5.9)), produced by ∏_W(1 + K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔡̄_W) ≤ exp(Σ_WK_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔡̄_W) with the first-order bound 𝔡̄_W ≤ 2M_Λ(W) of Lemma 5.7(e), whose root weight ρ_tm(Z) has ρ_t = u_t/u_* = O(g₀r) (5.5(6)–(7)). This section proves the second-order bound 𝔡̄_W ≤ 2M^{ph}_Λ(W), root weight ρ_phm(Z) with ρ_ph = O(g₀²(1+r)⁴), for the undifferenced slots at t ∈ {0,1} and for the reference activities, root by root inside the selected integrals, by three mechanisms: (α) a root with at least two selected labels carries (u_t/u_*)^{|𝖩_Z|} ≤ ρ_t² from the finite-difference bound as it stands; (β) a root with a single selected label whose split selection contains an ordinary host (|𝖩_Z| = 1, 𝒟_Z ≠ ∅; a root with two or more labels is in (α) whatever its hosts) carries the factor 2ω_t/ω_* of its own Mayer factors, through a complex parameter on the selected ordinary primitives (stage 4 §4.1's ζ), at every t and without parity; (γ) a single-label root without ordinary host (𝒟_Z = ∅, |𝖩_Z| = 1) has an even measure at t = 0 and at the reference data, so the term of the source linear in its label integrates to zero and the remainder carries one more factor u_t; at t = 1 its measure is the t = 0 measure plus marked differences of its translated cutoffs, and each mark is paid by the large-field reserve (each mark costs e^{−(9/16)a_bad} against the majorant with the reserve (9/128)a_bad, and the whole marked difference of the measure costs 2C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}, Lemma 5R.2(b)), not by ϖ_b — so the marks of the undifferenced slots carry neither exterior decay nor label dependence, and the exponent of the family sum stays a constant times n_C. The differenced slots (𝔡^{diff}_W, (5.8)) and the marked part of Proposition 5.9 are unchanged. No statement of §§1–6 is altered (5.5(6)–(7) and 6.3(3) carry pointers to this section): the results are Lemmas 5R.1–5R.3 and Proposition 5R.4, which restates (5.9), (5.10), (6.3), (6.4) with e^{n_Cε̄_ph} in place of e^{2n_Cε̄_t}.

Thresholds. In addition to stage 2's thresholds, (4.T), (4.T′), (5.T) and ω_* ≤ 1/2 assume

  (R.T)  (i) (9/128)a_bad ≥ max(160(κ + log(8M⁴+8) + 1), 64 log(1/η_*))   ((4.T′) for a_bad/8);   (ii) ρ_t + u_t/2 + 2ω_t/ω_* + 2C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad} ≤ 1;   (iii) 2C_M ≤ (1+2r)³,  log g₀⁻² ≥ 1,  (9c_nα_G/64)A₁² log g₀⁻² ≥ 1,

where C_M := 8(4M+1)⁴ ≥ max_b|E(Y_b)| (a probe host is a box of M-cubes of side at most 4 cubes, stage 1 eq:c-probe-host, so it has at most (4M+1)⁴ sites, each an endpoint of eight bonds). Lemmas 5R.1–5R.3 and the bounds of Proposition 5R.4 use (i)–(ii) only; (iii) is used only for the explicit form of the rate (the display ρ_ph ≤ C_{ph}g₀²(1+2r)⁴ below and the rate bound of Proposition 5R.4; review r2, G1). All three are conditions on g₀ alone of the kind of (4.T′) and (5.T): a_bad = c_nα_Gr²/4 → ∞, r → ∞ and g₀(1+r)³ → 0 as g₀ → 0, with thresholds depending on L, M, the group, the character, κ, ω_*, α_G, η_*, A₁ and p₀. Put

  ρ_ph := ρ_t[ρ_t + u_t/2 + 2ω_t/ω_* + 2C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}],   ε̄_ph := 2K_χC_pinρ_ph,   M^{ph}_Λ(W) := (1/k(W)!)∏_{Z∈𝒜(W)}ρ_phm(Z)∏_j|Φ|⁺_Λ(𝔠_j),

so that ρ_ph ≤ ρ_t ≤ ρ_pin by (R.T)(ii) and (5.T)(iv), M^{ph}_Λ(W) ≤ M_Λ(W), and, with ρ_t = C′_χg₀(1+2r)/u_*, u_t = C′_χg₀(1+2r), ω_t = C₀g₀(1+2r)³ and a_bad = c_nα_Gr²/4,

  ρ_ph ≤ C_{ph}g₀²(1+2r)⁴,   C_{ph} := (C′_χ/u_*)(C′_χ/u_* + C′_χ/2 + 2C₀/ω_* + 1),   hence   ε̄_ph ≤ 32K_χC_pinC_{ph}g₀²(1+r)⁴

under (R.T)(iii): the last bracket term needs 2C_Me^{−9c_nα_Gr²/128} ≤ g₀(1+2r)³, which holds once 2C_M ≤ (1+2r)³ and e^{−9c_nα_Gr²/128} ≤ g₀, the latter because r = A₁(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀} with p₀ ≥ 1 and log g₀⁻² ≥ 1 gives, with c := 9c_nα_G/128, e^{−cr²} ≤ e^{−cA₁²(log g₀⁻²)²} = g₀^{2cA₁²log g₀⁻²} ≤ g₀ as soon as 2cA₁² log g₀⁻² ≥ 1, which is the third condition of (R.T)(iii). The first three bracket terms are at most their coefficients times g₀(1+2r)³ without any condition. In the TeX's τ = C[g₀(1+r)³ + e^{−cr²}] (L411), τ² ≥ C²g₀²(1+r)⁶ ≥ C²g₀²(1+r)⁴, so ε̄_ph ≤ (32K_χC_pinC_{ph}/C²)τ²: the family-sum rate becomes second order, of the order the TeX accepts for E° (5.5(7)).

5R.1 Higher derivatives and the two-term Taylor remainder.

Lemma 5R.1. (a) Lemma 5.7⁰(a) holds for every k ≤ 2m_* with 2C_link in place of C_link: ‖∂_{e_1,b_1}⋯∂_{e_k,b_k}π(L_n)(a)‖_op ≤ (2C_linkg₀)^k for real a in the enlarged box, b_i ∈ 𝓕_{r_n} (repetitions allowed), h-unit e_i. (b) Let g be as in Lemma 5.7⁰(b) (link factors, each traversal at most once, fixed insertions N), a₀ real with |a₀_b|_σ ≤ r/4, 𝖩 a finite set of bonds of D(Λ), and φ(B) := g(B + a₀) for B ∈ 𝔤^𝖩 with |B_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4 (B extended by zero off 𝖩). For b ∈ 𝖩 define, on C² functions Ψ of B (D_b makes sense on every function; L_b and R_b need two derivatives, and all three are applied to the analytic φ only), (D_bΨ)(B) := Ψ(B) − Ψ(B|_{B_b→0}), (L_bΨ)(B) := ⟨B_b,∇_bΨ(B|_{B_b→0})⟩ = h(B_b)∂_{B_b/h(B_b),b}Ψ(B|_{B_b→0}), (R_bΨ)(B) := ∫₀¹(1−s)∂²_s[Ψ(B|_{B_b→sB_b})]ds; operators at distinct bonds commute, and D_b = L_b + R_b on C² functions. Then Δ_𝖩g(B) = (∏_{b∈𝖩}D_bφ)(B), and for every partition 𝖩 = 𝖩′ ⊔ {b_1,…,b_p} (p ≥ 0)

  |(∏_{b∈𝖩′}D_b∏_{i=1}^pR_{b_i}φ)(B)| ≤ 2^{−p}u_t^{|𝖩′|+2p}‖N‖,   ∏_{b∈𝖩}D_bφ − ∏_{b∈𝖩′}D_b∏_{i=1}^pR_{b_i}φ = Σ_{i=1}^p ∏_{b∈𝖩′}D_b(∏_{i′<i}D_{b_{i′}})L_{b_i}(∏_{i′>i}R_{b_{i′}})φ,

where the i-th term of the sum is, at fixed values of the coordinates other than B_{b_i}, a linear function of B_{b_i}: B_{b_i} ↦ ⟨B_{b_i}, c_i(B|_{𝖩∖b_i})⟩. Both (a) and (b) hold at the reference data (U = id, base point a₀ = 0, the link factors π(L_n) at the flat phase) with the same constants, as Lemma 5.7⁰ does: the proofs below use only the charts and the Cauchy estimates of Lemma 5.7⁰, which hold there.

Proof. (a) The proof of Lemma 5.7⁰(a) with m_* replaced by 2m_*: Cauchy's estimate and polarisation give ‖D^kΠ_n(A)[h_1,…,h_k]‖ ≤ k^kC_Π(4/ϑ_*)^k∏‖h_i‖ for every k, and k^k ≤ (2m_*)^k for k ≤ 2m_*, so ‖D^kΠ_n(A)[h_1,…,h_k]‖ ≤ (2m_*)^kC_Π(4/ϑ_*)^k∏‖h_i‖ and ‖∂_{e_1,b_1}⋯∂_{e_k,b_k}π(L_n)‖ ≤ (8m_*C_ΠC_𝖢g₀/ϑ_*)^k ≤ (2C_linkg₀)^k; for a free bond the Duhamel bound (C_πg₀)^k ≤ (2C_linkg₀)^k holds for every k. (b) D_b = L_b + R_b is Taylor's formula with integral remainder for s ↦ Ψ(B|_{B_b→sB_b}) on [0,1]. Expanding ∏_{b∈𝖩}D_b ("value minus value with the b-th coordinate zeroed") gives Σ_{K⊆𝖩}(−1)^{|𝖩∖K|}φ(1_KB) = Δ_𝖩g(B). The telescoping identity ∏_i(L_{b_i} + R_{b_i}) − ∏_iR_{b_i} = Σ_i(∏_{i′<i}(L+R)_{b_{i′}})L_{b_i}(∏_{i′>i}R_{b_{i′}}) with (L+R)_b = D_b, and the commuting factor ∏_{𝖩′}D_b, give the second display. L_{b_i}Ψ is linear in B_{b_i} with a coefficient not depending on B_{b_i}; the operators at bonds b ≠ b_i are linear and act on the other coordinates, so they preserve this form. The bound: D_bΨ(B) = ∫₀¹∂_{s_b}Ψ(B|_{B_b→s_bB_b})ds_b with ∂_{s_b} = h(B_b)∂_{B_b/h(B_b),b}, and R_{b_i} contributes ∫₀¹(1−s_i)ds_i·h(B_{b_i})²∂²_{e_i,b_i}, e_i := B_{b_i}/h(B_{b_i}); iterating, ∏_{𝖩′}D_b∏_iR_{b_i}φ(B) is an integral over [0,1]^{|𝖩′|+p} with weights ∏_i(1−s_i) of ∏_{𝖩′}h(B_b)∏_ih(B_{b_i})²·(∂^{𝖩′}∂²_{e_1,b_1}⋯∂²_{e_p,b_p}g)(a₀ + B^s), every evaluation point real and in the enlarged box (|s_bB_b + a₀_b|_σ ≤ 3r/2 ≤ 2r). The mixed derivative of order Q := |𝖩′| + 2p is bounded as in the proof of Lemma 5.7⁰(b): product rule inside the normalised trace, at most N_tr^Q assignments of the Q directions to link factors whose input set contains the direction's bond, fixed factors contributing ‖N‖, undifferentiated links unitary, and each differentiated link carrying at most two directions per bond of its input set, hence at most 2|𝓕_{r_{n_i}}| ≤ 2m_* − 2 directions, bounded by (a); so sup|∂^{…}g| ≤ (2N_trC_linkg₀)^Q‖N‖. With h(B_b) ≤ |B_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4, ∫₀¹(1−s)ds = 1/2 and (5r/4)·2N_trC_linkg₀ ≤ 2N_trC_linkg₀(1+2r) = u_t, the bound is 2^{−p}u_t^Q‖N‖. ∎

5R.2 Evenness, marks paid by the large-field reserve, and the Cauchy parameter. For a split selection ℓ = (𝒟,𝖩,𝖯,Z) for the carrier D(Λ) (Lemma 5.2(a)) and x ∈ {1/8, 1} put

  𝔐^{(x)}(ℓ) := e^{−(κ₁−1)N₁(Z∖Z₀′) + N₁(Z) − x(9/16)a_bad|𝖯|}∏_{Y∈𝒟}(e^{A_Y} − 1),   A_Y := (ω_*/2)e^{−κd₀(Y)},

the right side of (5.3) with the host factor e^{A_Y} − 1 ≤ ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)} (as in the proof of Lemma 5.2(c)) and the reserve exponent multiplied by x; 𝔐^{(1)} ≤ 𝔐^{(1/8)}. (𝔐^{(x)}(ℓ) is a majorant of a single selection and is not the mark weight 𝔐_Λ(Z) of §5.)

Lemma 5R.2. Let ℓ be a split selection for D(Λ), Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ} its measure on 𝔤^{E₀} (t ∈ [0,1]) and Λ^{f,°}_{Λ;ℓ} the reference measure (Lemma 5.3(b)); the hypotheses are those of Lemma 5.2.
(a) (Evenness.) If 𝒟 = ∅ then Λ^{f,0}_{Λ;ℓ} and Λ^{f,°}_{Λ;ℓ} are invariant under B ↦ −B on 𝔤^{E₀}: ∫F(−B)dΛ(B) = ∫F(B)dΛ(B) for bounded Borel F. In particular ∫Ψ dΛ = 0 for every bounded Borel Ψ with Ψ(−B) = −Ψ(B), e.g. Ψ(B) = ⟨B_b, c⟩1{|B_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4} for a bond b ∈ E(Y₀) and a fixed c ∈ 𝔤 (the truncation is even and equals 1 on the support of the measure, where |B_b|_σ < r by the cutoff χ⁰_b; the untruncated linear function is not bounded).
(b) (Marked difference, large-field payment.) If 𝒟 = ∅ and |𝖩| = 1, then under (R.T)(i), for bounded Borel F, |∫F d(Λ^{f,1}_{Λ;ℓ} − Λ^{f,0}_{Λ;ℓ})| ≤ sup|F|·2C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ).
(c) (Cauchy parameter.) For ζ ∈ ℂ let Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}(ζ) be defined by the display of Lemma 4.1 for the enlarged data with each Mayer factor 𝐖_t(Y,B)e^{t_Y𝐖_t(Y,B)}, Y ∈ 𝒟, replaced by ζ𝐖_t(Y,B)e^{t_Yζ𝐖_t(Y,B)} (every near primitive assigned to a host of 𝒟 multiplied by ζ; density, cutoffs, probes unchanged), so that Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}(1) = Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}; put R_Υ := ω_*/(2ω_t) ≥ 1 ((5.T)(ii)). For bounded Borel F the function ζ ↦ ∫F dΛ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}(ζ) is entire; it vanishes at ζ = 0 if 𝒟 ≠ ∅; and |∫F dΛ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}(ζ)| ≤ sup|F|·𝔐^{(1)}(ℓ) for |ζ| ≤ R_Υ. Consequently ‖Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}‖_TV ≤ 𝔐^{(1)}(ℓ), and if 𝒟 ≠ ∅ then |∫F dΛ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}| ≤ (2ω_t/ω_*)·sup_{|ζ|≤R_Υ}|∫F dΛ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}(ζ)| ≤ (2ω_t/ω_*)sup|F|𝔐^{(1)}(ℓ). The same holds at the reference data.
(d) (Weakened dummy comparison.) Under (R.T)(i), for every connected final domain Z for D(Λ) and x ∈ {1/8, 1}, Σ_{ℓ: Z(ℓ)=Z}u_*^{|𝖩|}𝔐^{(x)}(ℓ) ≤ m(Z), the sum over all split selections with final domain Z and Z₀ ≠ ∅ (𝖩 = ∅ included).

Proof. (a) With 𝒟 = ∅ the display of Lemma 4.1 for the enlarged data has, inside the s- and σ-integrations (which do not involve (B,X)), the integrand ∏_{b∈E(Y₀)}χ⁰_b(B)∏_{b∈𝖯}(1 − χ⁰_b(B))F(B) against the density (eq:fixed-kernel-density) e^{−½⟨Γ_{E₀}(σ)X,C_{E₀}Γ_{E₀}(σ)X⟩}e^{−⟨B,Γ_{E₀}(σ)X⟩}dμ_{C_{E₀}}(B)dμ₀(X). Substitute (B,X) → (−B,−X): μ_{C_{E₀}} and μ₀ are centred Gaussians, hence invariant; the quadratic form in X and the bilinear form ⟨B,Γ_{E₀}(σ)X⟩ are invariant under the joint sign change; χ⁰_b(−B) = 1{|−B_b|_σ < r} = χ⁰_b(B) and likewise 1 − χ⁰_b (|·|_σ is a norm); there is no Mayer factor. Hence ∫F(−B)dΛ⁰ = ∫F dΛ⁰. At the reference data the display is the same with H°, m° = 0 and the cutoffs χ_b(a) = 1{|a_b|_σ < r} (Lemma 5.3(b)). For odd Ψ, ∫Ψ dΛ = ∫Ψ(−B)dΛ = −∫Ψ dΛ. (b) With 𝒟 = ∅ and 𝖩 = {b₀}, the t-dependent factors of the display are χ^t_b, b ∈ E(Y₀) = E(Y_{b₀}) (at most C_M bonds), and 1 − χ^t_b, b ∈ 𝖯 (proof of Lemma 5.6(a)); order them and telescope, ∏a_k − ∏c_k = Σ_k(∏_{i<k}a_i)(a_k − c_k)(∏_{i>k}c_i), a the t = 1 and c the t = 0 factors: at most C_M + |𝖯| terms, each with exactly one differenced factor (the mark at a bond b), of modulus |χ¹_b − χ⁰_b| ≤ 1_{S_b}(B_b) ≤ 1{|B_b|_σ ≥ r − |m_b|_σ} ≤ 1{|B_b|_σ ≥ 3r/4} ≤ e^{−(9/16)a_bad}e^{(α_G/2)h(B_b)²} ((4.1) and the proof of Lemma 4.2). Bound the modulus of each term factor by factor as in the proof of Lemma 4.2: undifferenced cutoffs ≤ 1; undifferenced bad factors 1 − χ^t_{b′} ≤ e^{−(9/16)a_bad}e^{(α_G/2)h(B_{b′})²}; the mark as displayed; |F| ≤ sup|F|; the product of the exponential factors is e^{−(9/16)a_badn}e^{(α_G/2)‖B‖²} with n := |𝖯| + 1{b ∉ 𝖯} ≥ max(|𝖯|,1), and the density's modulus against e^{(α_G/2)‖B‖²} integrates to at most e^{N₁(Z)} (lem:gaussian-payment(ii) for H), the weakening giving e^{−(κ₁−1)N₁(Z∖Z₀′)}. Since (9/16)a_badn ≥ (9/128)a_bad|𝖯| + (63/128)a_bad·max(|𝖯|,1), each term is at most sup|F|·e^{−(63/128)a_bad max(|𝖯|,1)}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ), and the number of terms is at most C_M + |𝖯| ≤ C_M(1 + |𝖯|). For |𝖯| = 0 this gives C_Me^{−(63/128)a_bad} ≤ C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}; for |𝖯| = k ≥ 1, C_M(1+k)e^{−(63/128)a_badk} = C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}·(1+k)e^{−((63k−36)/128)a_bad} ≤ C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}·(1+k)e^{−(27/128)a_badk} ≤ 2C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}, because 63k − 36 ≥ 27k for k ≥ 1 and (1+k)e^{−ck} ≤ 2e^{−c} ≤ 2 for c := (27/128)a_bad ≥ 1 (implied by (R.T)(i)). (c) For fixed (X,B,s,σ,(t_Y)) the integrand is ∏_{Y∈𝒟}ζ𝐖_te^{t_Yζ𝐖_t} times a ζ-independent factor, entire in ζ; 𝐖_t(Y,B) vanishes off the translated local box of Y and has |𝐖_t(Y,B)| ≤ ω_te^{−κd₀(Y)} ≤ ω_t on it (4.2, (4.4)), so on |ζ| ≤ R the integrand's modulus is at most (Rω_te^{Rω_t})^{|𝒟|}sup|F| times the density's modulus, which is integrable (lem:gaussian-payment(ii)); the s-, σ-, t_Y-integrals are over compact sets. Dominated convergence gives continuity in ζ and, with Morera's theorem (or differentiation under the integral sign), analyticity on every disc: the function is entire. At ζ = 0 every Mayer factor vanishes and 𝒟 ≠ ∅, so the integral is zero. For |ζ| ≤ R_Υ, integrate t_Y first: ∫₀¹ζ𝐖_te^{t_Yζ𝐖_t}dt_Y = e^{ζ𝐖_t} − 1, of modulus ≤ e^{|ζ𝐖_t|} − 1 ≤ e^{R_Υω_te^{−κd₀(Y)}} − 1 = e^{A_Y} − 1 on the support of Θ^t_{E(Y)}; the other factors are bounded as in the proofs of Lemma 4.2 and Lemma 5.2(b) (cutoffs ≤ 1, bad bonds by e^{−(9/16)a_bad}e^{(α_G/2)h²}, Gaussian payment e^{N₁(Z)}, weakening), giving sup|F|𝔐^{(1)}(ℓ). The TV bound is the case ζ = 1 (for 𝒟 = ∅ there is no ζ, and the bound is the factor-by-factor bound directly). Schwarz's lemma for Φ(ζ) := ∫F dΛ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ}(ζ) on |ζ| ≤ R_Υ with Φ(0) = 0 and |Φ| ≤ M := sup|F|𝔐^{(1)}(ℓ) gives |Φ(ζ)| ≤ M|ζ|/R_Υ, and ζ = 1 lies in the disc since R_Υ ≥ 1. At the reference data (4.4) holds by Lemma 4.0(c) for these data (Lemma 5.3(b)). (d) The proof of Lemma 5.2(c): the hostwise comparison bounds the sum over the nonempty label subsets of a host of the weights e^{A_Y} − 1 (ordinary) and u_* (probe) by ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)}, so Σ_ℓu_*^{|𝖩|}𝔐^{(x)}(ℓ) is at most the sum over the ordinary selections 𝔰 = (𝒟,𝖯,Z) of the majorant (4.5) with x(9/16)a_bad in place of (9/16)a_bad; stage 2's seed, enlargement and final-domain sums bound this by m(Z) exactly as in Corollary 4.3, whose proof applies to every number ≥ 1 in the role of a_bad that satisfies (eq:native-source-bad-budget), which (9/128)a_bad does by (R.T)(i) (and (9/16)a_bad by (4.T′)). ∎

5R.3 The second-order group bound.

Lemma 5R.3. Under the hypotheses of Lemma 5.2, (R.T) and the setting of 5.0 (on A_cov^{fin}), for every group W (Lemma 5.3(a)), every h ∈ G and t ∈ {0,1}: |c^t_W(h)| ≤ M^{ph}_Λ(W) and |a^Λ_W| ≤ M^{ph}_Λ(W). Hence 𝔡̄_W ≤ 2M^{ph}_Λ(W) =: 𝔟̄^{ph}_W (Lemma 5.7(e) improved), and Lemma 5.8 holds with (M^{ph}_Λ, ρ_ph, ε̄_ph) in place of (M_Λ, ρ_t, ε̄_t):

  sup_ΔΣ_{W: Δ∈F(W)}e^{2νσ(W)}K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄^{ph}_W ≤ ε̄_ph,   sup_nΣ_{W: n∈ℋ(W)}e^{2νσ(W)}K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄^{ph}_W ≤ ε̄_ph,   Σ_WK_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄^{ph}_W ≤ n_Cε̄_ph,

and (b) of Lemma 5.8 likewise with ε̄_ph. The differenced bound (5.8), its majorant M_Λ(W) and θ°(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16 are unchanged.

Proof. Fix W, h and t ∈ {0,1}. By Lemma 5.2(a),(d) and Lemma 5.3(a), c^t_W(h) = w^t_Λ(W)Σ_{(ℓ_Z)_{Z∈𝒜(W)}: 𝖩_Z≠∅}∫Δ_𝖩g_t d⊗_ZΛ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ_Z}, with g_t(a) = dim(π)⁻¹tr(π(T_W(a))N_t), N_t := π(P^t_W)⁻¹π(h) fixed unitary, base point a₀ = tm, 𝖩 = ⊔_Z𝖩_Z, and |w^t_Λ(W)| ≤ (1/k(W)!)∏_j|Φ|⁺_Λ(𝔠_j) (Corollary 4.3 and Lemma 5.6(b), as in the proof of Lemma 5.3(d)). The integrand Δ_𝖩g_t depends on B through the labels B_𝖩 only, and the labels of the root Z are its 𝖩_Z ⊆ E₀(ℓ_Z). Fix a tuple (ℓ_Z) and sort its roots into three classes: (α) |𝖩_Z| ≥ 2; (β) |𝖩_Z| = 1 and 𝒟_Z ≠ ∅; (γ) |𝖩_Z| = 1 and 𝒟_Z = ∅, with single label b_Z.

Step 1 (splitting the class-(γ) measures; t = 1 only). For each class-(γ) root write Λ^{f,1}_{Λ;ℓ_Z} = Λ^{f,0}_{Λ;ℓ_Z} + δΛ_{ℓ_Z} and expand the product measure: ⊗_ZΛ^{f,1}_{ℓ_Z} = Σ_{𝒮⊆(γ)}[⊗_{Z∈𝒮}δΛ_{ℓ_Z}] ⊗ [⊗_{Z∈(γ)∖𝒮}Λ^{f,0}_{ℓ_Z}] ⊗ [⊗_{Z∉(γ)}Λ^{f,1}_{ℓ_Z}]. At t = 0 only 𝒮 = ∅ occurs. Fix 𝒮; the roots of (γ) ∖ 𝒮 are the parity roots, Z_1,…,Z_p with labels b_1,…,b_p, and 𝖩′ := 𝖩 ∖ {b_1,…,b_p}.

Step 2 (parity). By Lemma 5R.1(b) with φ_t := g_t(· + tm) (|tm_b|_σ ≤ r/4 by (4.1); |B_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4 on the supports of the measures, Lemma 5.2(a); every function of the labels below — φ_t, the L- and R-terms, g^{(p)} — is multiplied by the even truncation ∏_{b∈𝖩}1{|B_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4}, which equals 1 on the supports of the measures and makes it bounded Borel on 𝔤^{E₀}, so that Lemma 5R.2 applies), Δ_𝖩g_t = g^{(p)} + Σ_{i=1}^p(term linear in B_{b_i}), g^{(p)} := ∏_{𝖩′}D_b∏_iR_{b_i}φ_t. The i-th linear term depends on the variables of the root Z_i through B_{b_i} alone (𝖩_{Z_i} = {b_i}) and is odd under B_{E₀(Z_i)} → −B_{E₀(Z_i)} at fixed values of the other roots' variables; the measures are finite and the integrand is bounded Borel, so by Fubini and Lemma 5R.2(a) (Λ^{f,0}_{ℓ_{Z_i}} is even, 𝒟_{Z_i} = ∅) its integral against the product measure of Step 1 vanishes. Hence ∫Δ_𝖩g_t d(product for 𝒮) = ∫g^{(p)}d(product for 𝒮), and sup|g^{(p)}| ≤ 2^{−p}u_t^{|𝖩′|+2p} (‖N_t‖ = 1).

Step 3 (Cauchy parameter). Let Φ((ζ_Z)_{Z∈(β)}) := ∫g^{(p)}d[⊗_{Z∈(β)}Λ^{f,t}_{Λ;ℓ_Z}(ζ_Z) ⊗ (the other factors of the product for 𝒮)]. Φ is analytic in each ζ_Z separately on the open polydisc {|ζ_Z| < R_Υ}: at fixed values of the other roots' variables, the integral over the variables of the root Z is, for every bounded Borel integrand, entire in ζ_Z with the uniform bound sup|integrand|·𝔐^{(1)}(ℓ_Z) on |ζ_Z| ≤ R_Υ (Lemma 5R.2(c), a single-root statement), and this uniform bound carries analyticity through the integrals over the other roots (finite measures, Fubini; dominated convergence and Morera's theorem for the outer integral). Analyticity on the open polydisc with a bound on the closed one is all Schwarz's lemma below needs. Φ vanishes whenever some ζ_Z = 0, and on the polydisc {|ζ_Z| ≤ R_Υ ∀Z ∈ (β)} satisfies |Φ| ≤ M_Φ := sup|g^{(p)}|·∏_{Z∈(β)}𝔐^{(1)}(ℓ_Z)·∏_{Z∈𝒮}‖δΛ_{ℓ_Z}‖_TV·∏_{Z∈(α)∪((γ)∖𝒮)}‖Λ_{ℓ_Z}‖_TV (the TV norms of the t = 0 measures of the parity roots, of the t = 1 measures of class (α)). Schwarz's lemma one variable at a time, from the inside out: write (β) = {Z_1,…,Z_q}; for each fixed (ζ_{Z_1},…,ζ_{Z_{q−1}}) in the polydisc, ζ_{Z_q} ↦ Φ(ζ_{Z_1},…,ζ_{Z_{q−1}},ζ_{Z_q}) vanishes at 0 and is bounded by M_Φ on |ζ_{Z_q}| ≤ R_Υ, so |Φ(ζ_{Z_1},…,ζ_{Z_{q−1}},1)| ≤ M_Φ/R_Υ on the polydisc of the remaining q − 1 variables; the function (ζ_{Z_1},…,ζ_{Z_{q−1}}) ↦ Φ(…,1) is again entire in each variable, vanishes when any variable is 0 and is bounded by M_Φ/R_Υ, so the same step in ζ_{Z_{q−1}} gives M_Φ/R_Υ² at ζ_{Z_{q−1}} = 1, and after q steps |Φ(1,…,1)| ≤ R_Υ^{−q}M_Φ = (2ω_t/ω_*)^{|(β)|}M_Φ.

Step 4 (collecting). With ‖Λ_{ℓ_Z}‖_TV ≤ 𝔐^{(1)}(ℓ_Z) ≤ 𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) (Lemma 5R.2(c)) and ‖δΛ_{ℓ_Z}‖_TV ≤ 2C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) (Lemma 5R.2(b)), the summand for ((ℓ_Z), 𝒮) is at most

  ∏_{Z∈(α)}u_t^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) · ∏_{Z∈(β)}u_t(2ω_t/ω_*)𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) · ∏_{Z∈(γ)∖𝒮}(u_t²/2)𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) · ∏_{Z∈𝒮}u_t·2C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z).

Summing over 𝒮 ⊆ (γ), a class-(γ) root contributes [u_t²/2 + 2C_Mu_te^{−(9/32)a_bad}]𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) = ρ_t[u_t/2 + 2C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad}]u_*^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) ≤ ρ_phu_*^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z); a class-(β) root ρ_t(2ω_t/ω_*)u_*^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) ≤ ρ_phu_*^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z); a class-(α) root ρ_t^{|𝖩_Z|}u_*^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) ≤ ρ_t²u_*^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) ≤ ρ_phu_*^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) (ρ_t ≤ 1 by (5.T)(i)). Summing over the tuples, Σ_{(ℓ_Z)}∏_Zρ_phu_*^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z) = ∏_Z[ρ_phΣ_{ℓ_Z: Z(ℓ_Z)=Z}u_*^{|𝖩_Z|}𝔐^{(1/8)}(ℓ_Z)] ≤ ∏_Zρ_phm(Z) by Lemma 5R.2(d) (the sum over all split selections dominates the sum over those with 𝖩_Z ≠ ∅). Multiplying by |w^t_Λ(W)| gives |c^t_W(h)| ≤ M^{ph}_Λ(W).

The reference activity a^Λ_W = w°_Λ(W)∫g°_W d⊗_Zν̂^{f,°}_{Λ,Z} (Lemma 5.3(b),(d)) is treated by the same four steps at the reference data, where Lemma 5R.2 holds (Lemma 5.3(b)): base point 0, increments |B_b|_σ ≤ r ≤ 5r/4, 𝒮 = ∅ throughout (the reference cutoffs are even, so every class-(γ) root is a parity root), g°_W a closed contraction with ‖N‖ = 1, and |w°_Λ(W)| ≤ (1/k(W)!)∏_j|Φ|⁺_Λ(𝔠_j).

Lemma 5.7(e) improved: 𝔡̄_W = max_t sup_h|c^t_W(h) − a^Λ_Wχ(h)| ≤ 2M^{ph}_Λ(W). The pins: Lemma 5.8(a)'s first display is the positive summation with a root weight ρm(Z), and the inherited summation bound (stage 4 §6.2, attempt §18.3; 5.5(4)) holds in the form "sum ≤ C_pinρ for every ρ ≤ ρ_pin" (its smallness conditions on ρ are monotone, so every ρ ≤ ρ_pin satisfies them, and the bound is linear in ρ at each such ρ — monotonicity alone would only give C_pinρ_t); applied at ρ = ρ_ph ≤ ρ_pin it gives C_pinρ_ph. Equivalently, M^{ph}_Λ(W) = (ρ_ph/ρ_t)^{|𝒜(W)|}M_Λ(W) with |𝒜(W)| ≥ 1, so the first-order pin at ρ_t gives C_pinρ_t(ρ_ph/ρ_t) = C_pinρ_ph as well; the second display uses 𝔟̄_W ≤ 2M_Λ(W) and e^{θ°(W)} ≤ e^{2νσ(W)}, now with 𝔟̄^{ph}_W = 2M^{ph}_Λ(W) and the unchanged θ°(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16 (Lemma 5.3(d), which uses the first-order M_Λ, |a^Λ_W| ≤ M_Λ(W) and (5.T)(iv), all still in force); Lemma 5.8(b) is the geometric fact applied to the new pin. ∎

5R.4 The repaired bounds.

Proposition 5R.4. Under the hypotheses of Proposition 5.9 and (R.T), on every labelled term with final region Λ, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, on A_cov^{fin},

  |ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ]| ≤ |S°_Λ| e^{n_Cε̄_ph} [ C_1 g₀ r 𝔖_1(Λ) + C_2 ε̄_t r^{d_G} 𝔖_2(Λ) ],                                                                     (5.9R)

with C_1, C_2, μ′, 𝔖_1, 𝔖_2 of Proposition 5.9; on A_cov^{fin} ∩ N_b^c, (5.10) holds with e^{n_Cε̄_ph} in place of e^{2n_Cε̄_t} (5.10R); and Proposition 6.4's (6.3), (6.4) and its closing form hold with e^{n_Cε̄_ph} in place of e^{2n_Cε̄_t} ((6.3R), (6.4R)). In the square geometry of 5.0 (n_C = 4nL, 4n coarse segments) the family-sum factor has, under (R.T)(iii), the perimeter rate Lε̄_ph ≤ 32LK_χC_pinC_{ph}g₀²(1+r)⁴ per coarse segment, second order in g₀(1+r)², of the order τ² that the TeX accepts for E° (5.5(7)); the first-order constant ε̄_t survives only as the prefactor C_2ε̄_t of 𝔖_2(Λ) (through Corollary 5.10 it multiplies n_CN_lat(μ′/2)e^{−μ′(b−1)/2} + 𝔇_Λ) and no longer appears in an exponent.

Proof. The proof of Proposition 5.9 with one change. In (5.7) the undifferenced slots carry 𝔡̄_W, bounded by Lemma 5R.3 instead of Lemma 5.7(e); so the two family sums ∏_{W}(1 + K_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔡̄_W) — in the background-word part, and over 𝔅 ∖ {W₀} in the marked part — are at most exp(Σ_WK_χe^{θ°(W)}𝔟̄^{ph}_W) ≤ e^{n_Cε̄_ph} (Lemma 5R.3's contour pin summed over the n_C traversals, every group having a contact with the contour). The differenced slot 𝔡^{diff}_{W₀} keeps (5.8) with the first-order M_Λ(W₀) and the pins of Lemma 5.8 with ε̄_t, so (i) and (ii) of that proof and the constants C_1, C_2 are unchanged, and the closing step e^{n_Cε̄_t} ≤ e^{2n_Cε̄_t} is dropped. Corollary 5.10 and Proposition 6.4 use (5.9) only through its right side. The rate: n_Cε̄_ph = 4n·Lε̄_ph, and ε̄_ph ≤ 32K_χC_pinC_{ph}g₀²(1+r)⁴ by 5R.0 under (R.T)(iii). ∎

5R.5 What the rate repair establishes and what it does not. Established, on every labelled term, on A_cov^{fin}, under (R.T) in addition to the earlier thresholds: the higher-order link derivative bound and the two-term Taylor expansion of the mixed finite difference with its odd linear terms (Lemma 5R.1); the evenness of the t = 0 and reference selected measures without ordinary hosts, the large-field payment of the translated-cutoff marks of a single-label probe root (2C_Me^{−(9/32)a_bad} relative to the weakened majorant 𝔐^{(1/8)}), the Cauchy-parameter bound (2ω_t/ω_*) for every selected integral with an ordinary host, and the dummy comparison with the reserve (9/128)a_bad (Lemma 5R.2); the second-order bound |c^t_W(h)|, |a^Λ_W| ≤ M^{ph}_Λ(W) with ρ_ph = O(g₀²(1+r)⁴), the improved pins and the family sum e^{n_Cε̄_ph} (Lemma 5R.3); and the restated bounds (5.9R), (5.10R), (6.3R), (6.4R) (Proposition 5R.4). The mechanisms are stage 4 §4.1's (parity of the kernel, the parameter ζ on the ordinary primitives), re-proved here from the display of Lemma 4.1, the factor bounds of Lemma 4.2 and Schwarz's lemma, and not imported. Not established or not changed: (1) the differenced slots and the marked part keep the first-order prefactor ε̄_t (5.8), harmless because it multiplies the exterior sum and not n_C; (2) the family-sum factor remains e^{n_Cε̄_ph} ≥ 1 and is not uniform in n_C — like the TeX's e^{E°} (L1115), it grows with the perimeter at a second-order rate; the pass note's literal linear form would still need the resummation of attempt §20.4 (5.5(7)), not done; (3) the thresholds (R.T)(i)–(ii) were written, with (4.T), (4.T′), (5.T)(i)–(v) and ω_* ≤ 1/2, into stage 2's app:thresholds on 2026-10-19 by the user's decision, in the reduced form (R.T)(i), (R.T)(ii), (5.T)(iii)–(v) — (R.T)(i) implies (4.T′), (R.T)(ii) implies (5.T)(i)–(ii) and (4.T) — with the collar constants C′_χ, K_χ, u_*, ρ_pin, C_pin, η, N_host, C_M named in the order of choice (stage 2 check record item 14; the stage 2 draft's only edit since its fifth referee round); after review r2 (G1) the explicit-rate conditions (R.T)(iii) were added as item 4(vi) and the subsection repaired after its own isolated review (`stage2-thresholds-20261019-r1`: transcription, implications and smallness confirmed; its secondary points repaired or left to the user, stage 2 check record item 15); (R.T)(i) is (4.T′) with a_bad/8, and both are smallness conditions on g₀ of the accepted kind (5.5(5)); (4) 5.5(1)–(5), (8), (9) and 6.3(1)–(2), (4)–(5) stand as written; 5.5(6)–(7) and 6.3(3) are superseded by this section as recorded there; (5) the identification of S°_Λ (item 7) is untouched; (6) review r1 (`collar-rate-20261019-r1`, 2026-10-19, 215 lines): no blocking error, every numbered statement and every constant confirmed; gaps G1 (the order of the iterated Schwarz lemma in Step 3), G2 (the label functions must be truncated to be bounded Borel; the example of Lemma 5R.2(a)), G3 (the heading asserted approval), G4 (notation 𝔐^{(x)} vs 𝔐_Λ(Z), M vs M_Φ, ρ_ph vs attempt §20.1) and four wording points (the cost "per mark" in 5R.0; "nothing altered"; "multiplies the far decay" in 5R.4; 5.5(7)'s 8Lε̄_t, which is 2Lε̄_t per coarse segment) — all repaired in this text; review r2 of the repaired text (`collar-rate-20261019-r2`, 2026-10-19, 256 lines): no blocking error, every constant rechecked, non-blocking gaps G1 (the explicit rate needs the three small-coupling conditions now stated as (R.T)(iii)), G2 (the pin transfer rests on the inherited bound being C_pinρ for every ρ ≤ ρ_pin, not on monotonicity), G3 (Lemma 5R.1 at the reference data), G4 (analyticity of Φ on the open polydisc), G5 (wording of (β); C² functions in 5R.1(b)) — all repaired in this text; the inherited inputs of 5.0 and 5.5(4) (the pin sums of stage 4 §6.2 and attempt §18.3 for every ρ ≤ ρ_pin, stage 4 §7.1's host comparison, the reference-gas expansion of attempt §§13, 19.4, the TeX tree-graph inequality, the square geometry) remain inherited and are not verified here; (7) ρ_ph of this section is not the quantity of the same name in attempt §20.1 (built from stage 4's u, v, u_*); both are O(g₀²(1+r)⁴), and nothing of §20.1 is imported.

## 6. Item 6: the modified stage 1 bookkeeping

6.0 The labelled history sum. Stage 1's history sum is the sum over the branch data ξ = (Ω₁,R₁,V,V₀,A_e) inside the outer operation 𝒪, each term carrying the weight w_ξ = e^{c_ξ}I_{ξ;1} = ∫_{𝔤^I}ρ_ξ da_old and the conditional Wilson expectation F_ξ(1) = 𝒥^a_ξ[f_ξ]/𝒥^a_ξ[1] (lem:collar, eq:c-collar-identity), and the leading term, the defects and the exterior response are read off (eq:c-one-step-branch-identity).
