After §3 every history term is the finite sum of its labelled terms (lab = (B^{(0)},B^{(1)},…), Lemma 3.1(a)), with Σ_lab 1_lab = 1 pointwise (3.4(c)); a labelled term has the final region Λ := Λ₁^{fin}(lab), carrier D(Λ), exterior J(Λ) = J ∪ (I ∖ D(Λ)), and the objects of Lemma 2.5 and §§4–5. Write 𝒥^{a,lab}_ξ[f] := ∫_{𝔤^I}ρ_ξ 1_lab f da_old for bounded Borel f on 𝔤^I; then 𝒥^a_ξ[f] = Σ_lab𝒥^{a,lab}_ξ[f] (Tonelli for f ≥ 0, then by linearity), and the extra resummation index is lab.

Lemma 6.1 (the labelled branch identity). On every history and every labelled term, with the convention that e^{c^Λ_ξ}I^Λ_1F^Λ(1) and e^{c^Λ_ξ}I^Λ_1 denote the integrals ∫_{𝔤^{D(Λ)}}ρ_ξ(a, a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)})f_ξ(a)da and ∫_{𝔤^{D(Λ)}}ρ_ξ(a, a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)})da over the retreated fibre (both vanish where the fibre is empty, i.e. where |a_{old,b}|_σ ≥ r for some b ∈ I ∖ D(Λ), since ρ_ξ carries the box factor 1_{𝒟_ξ}; c^Λ_ξ and F^Λ(1) themselves are used only where the fibre is nonempty),

  𝒥^{a,lab}_ξ[f_ξ] = ∫_{𝔤^{I∖D(Λ)}} 1_lab e^{c^Λ_ξ} I^Λ_1 F^Λ(1) da_old|_{I∖D(Λ)},   w^{lab}_ξ = ∫_{𝔤^{I∖D(Λ)}} 1_lab e^{c^Λ_ξ} I^Λ_1 da_old|_{I∖D(Λ)},                        (6.1)

and, on A_cov^{fin} (an event depending on the labels through the region only), on the support of 1_lab (which carries the collar condition |A_e|_σ < ϑr on J_col(Λ) required by §4.1 and Lemma 4.2; every use of (6.2) below is multiplied by 1_lab), where the fibre is nonempty, under the hypotheses of Lemma 4.2 and (5.T) (through Lemma 5.3, whose absolute convergence and reference gas rest on the inputs listed in 5.0 and 5.5(4)),

  F^Λ(1) = S°_Λ W_χ(C;U₁) + Σ_{∅≠𝔅 compatible} S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ R⁰_Λ(𝔅) + ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ],                                                                        (6.2)

the retreated carrier's version of (eq:c-one-step-branch-identity), with S°_Λ, S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ, R⁰_Λ(𝔅) of Lemma 5.3 and ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ] of Lemma 4.4, bounded by Proposition 5.9.

Proof. By Lemma 2.5, pointwise e^{c^Λ_ξ}K^Λ_{ξ;1}(a) = 1_{𝒟^Λ}(a)|det DΨ_ξ(A_e)|exp E_ξ(Ψ_ξ(a + A^Λ_e)) = ρ_ξ(a_old) at a_old = (a, a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)}) (stage 3 §7.2's identity for ρ_ξ = e^{c_ξ}K_{ξ;1}, the same density in the two splits), and f_ξ, 1_lab are functions of the same variables (1_lab of A_e and a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)} only); integrate over a ∈ 𝔤^{D(Λ)} first (Fubini; ρ_ξ ≥ 0, f_ξ and 1_lab bounded): ∫K^Λ_{ξ;1}f_ξ da = I^Λ_1 F^Λ(1) by the definition of ν^Λ_1 in §4.1 where the fibre is nonempty (both sides vanish otherwise), and with f = 1 the weight. For (6.2): F^Λ(1) = F^Λ(0) + ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ] (Lemma 4.4), F^Λ(0) = S°_Λf_ξ(0) + Σ_𝔅S°^{[𝔅]}_ΛR⁰_Λ(𝔅) (Lemma 5.3(c) at t = 0), and f_ξ(0) = W_χ(C;U₁) on A_cov^{fin}: at a = 0 the free vector is (0 on D(Λ), A^{Λ,Ψ} on J(Λ)), every contour bond is a bond of Ω₁ and its free inputs lie in D(Λ), so lem:covered's argument with I replaced by D(Λ) gives U_ξ(0) = U₁ on the contour (the de-younged bonds, though nonzero, are not inputs of the contour on A_cov^{fin}). ∎

6.1 The species and their locality. The species of the labelled term are the final domains Z for the carrier D(Λ) with their activities k^{f,t}_Λ(Z) and probe measures ν^{f,t}_{Λ,Z} (Lemma 4.1, Lemma 5.2), the groups W with their weights w^t_Λ(W) and reference activities a^Λ_W (Lemma 5.3), and the marked-polymer objects built from them (the pins of Lemma 5.8). They depend on the labels through D(Λ) only. Let 𝒩_lab be the set of LM-cubes meeting a bond of I ∖ D(Λ) (no site lies on a cube face, so a bond meets exactly the one or two LM-cubes containing its endpoints, and |𝒩_lab| ≤ 2|I ∖ D(Λ)|).

Lemma 6.2 (locality of the species). Let Z be a union of LM-cubes containing no cube of 𝒩_lab, equivalently: every bond of I with an endpoint in Z lies in D(Λ). Then for every selection and split selection with final domain Z, the selected integrals at the carrier D(Λ) and at the carrier I coincide at t = 0: k^{f,0}_Λ(Z) = k^f_ξ(Z), ν^{f,0}_{Λ,Z} = ν^f_{ξ,Z}, and likewise for the reference data (k^{f,°}_Λ(Z) = k^{f,°}_ξ(Z), ν̂^{f,°}_{Λ,Z} = ν̂^{f,°}_{ξ,Z}). Consequently, for a group W none of whose roots and cluster constituents contains a cube of 𝒩_lab, w⁰_Λ(W) = w_ξ(W), a^Λ_W = a_W, and on A_cov^{fin} the pairings 𝔔⁰_Λ(𝒜(𝒮); f_ξ) = 𝔔_ξ(𝒜(𝒮); f_ξ) for families 𝒮 of such groups, so A⁰_Λ(𝒮) = A_ξ(𝒮) and R⁰_Λ(𝒮) = R_ξ(𝒮). The species that differ between the two carriers are those with a root or constituent containing a cube of 𝒩_lab.

Proof. Fix a selection 𝔰 = (𝒟,𝖯,Z) (or a split selection) at the carrier I with final domain Z; it is a selection at the carrier D(Λ) and conversely: the hosts Y ∈ 𝒟 lie in Z (Y ⊆ Z₀ ⊆ Z₀′ ⊆ Z), their primitives have free-input sets in Y, whose young part lies in E(Y) = {b ∈ carrier : b ∩ Y ≠ ∅}, the same set for both carriers by hypothesis, so a primitive assigned to Y is near relative to I exactly when it is near relative to D(Λ), and 𝐃₀(Λ)-hosts inside Z are the 𝐃₀-hosts inside Z (probe hosts Y_b ⊆ Z have b ∈ E(Y_b), the same set); the bad bonds 𝖯 meet Z (their incident cubes are in Z₀ ⊆ Z, def:selection), so 𝖯 ⊆ D(Λ) ∖ E(Y₀) and 𝖯 ⊆ I ∖ E(Y₀) are the same condition; the seed, E₀, Z₀′ are formed from 𝒟, 𝖯 alone. The integrand of (eq:c-selected-integral) at t = 0: the potentials 𝐖₀(Y,B) = Σ_{𝔭: Y_𝔭=Y}𝔭(B) (exterior input zero in both splits) and the cutoffs are the same functions of B|_{E₀}; C_{E₀} = (ι*_{E₀}Hι_{E₀})⁻¹ involves the entries of H between bonds of E₀, which are in both carriers; Γ_{E₀}(σ) = ι*_{E₀}HΠ_{carrier∖E₀}S_{Z₀}(σ) involves, for a row e ∈ E₀, the entries H_{eb′} with d(e₋,b′₋) ≤ R, so b′ ⊂ int D ⊂ Z for the component D of e (proof of lem:component-blocks), and the walk series S_{Z₀}(σ)_{b′b} with σ_Δ = 0 off Z, whose nonvanishing walks have D(ω) ⊂ Z (every cube meeting D(ω) is in Z, by the weight (eq:fixed-kernel-walk-weakening)); the blocks (𝖠_H^r)_{b₀b₁}, (𝖯_H)_{b_jb_{j+1}} of a walk are entries of powers of id − H/s_H restricted to the carrier, and each depends only on the carrier's H-coefficients within distance (n+1)R of its first bond, which lie in D(ω) ⊂ Z by the interior margin of D_b (sec:fixed-first-kernel: D_b contains the bonds and coefficient stencils of every block starting at b with an interior margin); hence every bond entering S_{Z₀}(σ) and Γ_{E₀}(σ) is a bond of I meeting Z, in D(Λ) by hypothesis, and every coefficient is an entry of H between two such bonds, the same for both carriers (principal restrictions of the one Hessian H_ξ), so the two carriers give the same Γ_{E₀}(σ) and the same C_{E₀}; the X-integral is over 𝔤^{carrier} with the standard Gaussian, but Γ_{E₀}(σ)X involves X only on bonds of Z (the columns of Γ outside ⊔J_D vanish, lem:component-blocks), so the remaining coordinates integrate to one in both cases. The Dirac tensors of a split selection are the same. Hence 𝒜^{f,0}_{Λ;𝔰}[F] = 𝒜^f_{ξ;𝔰}[F] for every F, giving the activities and measures; the reference case is the same argument with H°. The group statements follow: w⁰_Λ(W) is a product of activities of constituents, a^Λ_W an integral of the reference source word (the same function, the reference links depending on the root variables only) against the same measures, and 𝔔 involves f_ξ(𝖾_𝒜B), which on A_cov^{fin} depends on B|_{I_C} with I_C ⊆ D(Λ) and is the same function for both carriers. ∎

Lemma 6.3 (the reference gas at the retreated carrier). S°_Λ = S°_{ξ,C}·exp(η_lab) with

  |η_lab| ≤ Σ_{Γ containing a group W with F(W) ∩ 𝒩_lab ≠ ∅}(|Φ°_Λ(Γ)| + |Φ°_ξ(Γ)|) ≤ Σ_{Δ∈𝒩_lab} Σ_{Γ hits Δ}(|Φ°_Λ(Γ)| + |Φ°_ξ(Γ)|) ≤ 2|I ∖ D(Λ)|·ε°_g,

where ε°_g := sup_Δ Σ_{Γ hits Δ}(|Φ°_Λ(Γ)| + |Φ°_ξ(Γ)|) ≤ 2C_pinρ_t: a cluster Γ hits the cube Δ when one of its groups W has Δ ∈ F(W); the rooted bound of Lemma 5.3(d), Σ_{Γ∋W}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ |a_W|e^{13νσ(W)/8}, gives Σ_{Γ hits Δ}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ Σ_{W: Δ∈F(W)}|a_W|e^{13νσ(W)/8} ≤ Σ_{W: Δ∈F(W)}e^{4νσ(W)}M_Λ(W) ≤ C_pinρ_t by |a_W| ≤ M_Λ(W) and the first display of Lemma 5.8(a), at the carrier D(Λ) and, by the closing remarks of Lemma 5.3(d) and Lemma 5.8(a), at the carrier I. Moreover |S°_Λ| ≤ 1.

Proof. log S°_Λ − log S°_{ξ,C} = Σ_Γ[Φ°_Λ(Γ) − Φ°_ξ(Γ)] (Lemma 5.3(d) at both carriers; the reference group gas at I is stage 1's, with the same Kotecký–Preiss expansion); a cluster all of whose groups have roots and constituents containing no cube of 𝒩_lab has Φ°_Λ(Γ) = Φ°_ξ(Γ) by Lemma 6.2 (the Ursell coefficient depends on the incompatibility graph only, the group activities coincide), and the groups present for one carrier and not the other contain a cube of 𝒩_lab as well (a root or constituent met by a de-younged bond). |S°_Λ| ≤ 1: S°_Λ = ∫f°K°_Λda/I°_Λ with K°_Λ > 0 on the box (Lemma 4.0(c) for the reference data) and |f°| ≤ 1. ∎

6.2 The accounting. Three quantities of stage 3 and stage 4 acquire the index lab: the history weight (w_ξ = Σ_lab w^{lab}_ξ, (6.1)), the exterior-response/uncovered term E_bdry, and the defect term E_def. Define

  E^{lab}_bdry := Z₀⁻¹ ∫ 𝒪[ Σ_lab ∫_{𝔤^{I∖D(Λ)}} 1_lab e^{c^Λ_ξ} I^Λ_1 ( ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ] 1_{A_cov^{fin}} + F^Λ(1) 1_{(A_cov^{fin})^c} ) da_old ](V) dV,

the labelled version of stage 3's E_bdry (the exterior response on covered terms, the whole conditional expectation on uncovered terms, |F^Λ(1)| ≤ 1; the integrands are read with the convention of Lemma 6.1, as ∫_{𝔤^{D(Λ)}}ρ_ξ(…)da, so they are defined and vanish where the fibre is empty), and E^{lab}_def the same with Σ_𝔅S°^{[𝔅]}_ΛR⁰_Λ(𝔅)1_{A_cov^{fin}} in place of the bracket; the leading term is Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[Σ_lab ∫1_lab e^{c^Λ}I^Λ_1 S°_Λ 1_{A_cov^{fin}} da_old · W_χ(C;U₁)]dV. By (6.1)–(6.2) these three sum to the labelled Wilson numerator, exactly.

Proposition 6.4 (the boundary accounting with labels). On compatible even fine periods exceeding 200ℓ, under the hypotheses of Proposition 5.9, for g₀ below the thresholds of Lemma 2.4, of Proposition 2.6 (g_*(ϑ)) and of (2.3), with q, q_ϑ, N_ret, C_L of §§2–3 and ε̄_t, C_1, C_2, μ′ of §5 (the labelled integrands S°_Λ, F^Λ(t), ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ] are Borel in the branch data: the labels take finitely many values, and on each labelled term these are ratios and differences of the Borel integrals of stage 1's measurability paragraph):

  Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[Σ_lab ∫_{𝔤^{I∖D(Λ)}} 1_lab e^{c^Λ_ξ} I^Λ_1 1_{A_cov^{fin}} |ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ]|/|S°_Λ| da_old]dV ≤ e^{2n_Cε̄_t}(C_1g₀r + C_2ε̄_t r^{d_G})(1 + e^{μ_H(2L+1)/2}) N_lat(μ′) n_C (q + N_ret q_ϑ),                     (6.3)
  Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[Σ_lab w^{lab}_ξ 1_{(A_cov^{fin})^c}]dV ≤ C_L n_C (q + N_ret q_ϑ)   (3.4(a)),

hence, with |S°_Λ| ≤ 1 (Lemma 6.3),

  |E^{lab}_bdry| ≤ n_C (q + N_ret q_ϑ) [ e^{2n_Cε̄_t}(C_1g₀r + C_2ε̄_t r^{d_G})(1 + e^{μ_H(2L+1)/2})N_lat(μ′) + C_L ],                                              (6.4)

and for g₀ below a further threshold depending on L, M, M₂, the group, the source constants, ϑ and the character, r^{d_G}N_ret(q + N_retq_ϑ) ≤ e^{−c_qϑ²π_*²/2}, so that |E^{lab}_bdry| ≤ C n_C e^{2n_Cε̄_t} e^{−c_qϑ²π_*²/2}: stage 3's form |E_bdry| ≤ C n_C(1+g₀r)e^{−c_*π_*²} with the exponent c_qϑ²/2 in place of c_* and the family-sum factor e^{2n_Cε̄_t} of (5.9) (5.5(7); it is not a history weight).

Proof. (6.3): on A_cov^{fin}, (5.9) gives |ℬ^Λ|/|S°_Λ| ≤ e^{2n_Cε̄_t}[C_1g₀r𝔖_1(Λ) + C_2ε̄_t r^{d_G}𝔖_2(Λ)] with 𝔖_1(Λ) ≤ e^{μ_H(2L+1)/2}𝔖_2(Λ) (Corollary 5.10's remark), and 𝔖_2(Λ) = Σ_{e∈J(Λ)}e^{−μ′dist(e,C)} ≤ Σ_e e^{−μ′dist(e,C)}1{e ∈ E(B^{fin}_ξ)}, B^{fin}_ξ := (Λ₁^{fin})^c, because a bond not in D(Λ) has no endpoint in Λ₁^{fin} (3.4(a)). The prefactor is a constant (independent of the history, the labels and the fields: (5.9) is uniform in the exterior data within the term's support), and 𝔖_2(Λ), S°_Λ and A_cov^{fin} depend on the labels through Λ only, so the integral over a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)} of 1_lab e^{c^Λ_ξ}I^Λ_1 1_{A_cov^{fin}}|ℬ^Λ|/|S°_Λ| is at most (prefactor)·𝔖_2(Λ)·w^{lab}_ξ1_{A_cov^{fin}}. By 3.4(a)'s inequality 1{e ∈ E(B^{fin}_ξ)} ≤ 1{e ∈ E(𝔅₀)} + Σ_{e′: x_{e′} within 102ℓ+1 of an endpoint of e}M_{e′} (Lemma 3.1(c)–(d)); multiply by w^{lab}_ξ ≥ 0, sum over the labels and histories, apply 𝒪, divide by Z₀ (Tonelli, finitely many labels per history): the first term gives p_e^{(0)} ≤ q (Lemma 3.0(b)), the second Σ_{e′}p^ϑ_{e′} ≤ N_retq_ϑ (Lemma 3.1(d), Proposition 2.6). Then Σ_e e^{−μ′dist(e,C)} ≤ n_CN_lat(μ′) (at most 4n_C(2s+1)⁴ bonds at ℓ^∞ distance ≤ s from the contour's initial sites, as in Corollary 5.10). The second display is 3.4(a). (6.4) adds the two with |ℬ^Λ| ≤ |S°_Λ|·(…) and |F^Λ(1)| ≤ 1. The comparison: log(r^{d_G}N_ret) ≤ d_G(log A₁ + p₀ log log g₀⁻²) + C + 4𝔯 log(2 log g₀⁻²) by (3.1) and r = A₁(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀}, while log(1/q_ϑ) ≥ c_qϑ²A₀²2^{−2p₀}(log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀} by (3.2), and q ≤ q_ϑ (proof of Lemma 2.4: θ_ϑ ≤ θ for ϑ ≤ 1, and z_P, z_G are decreasing in θ at fixed n_W; alternatively, (2.3) at ϑ = 1 gives q ≤ e^{−c_qπ_*²} ≤ e^{−c_qϑ²π_*²} for g₀ < g_*(1)), so q + N_retq_ϑ ≤ (1 + N_ret)q_ϑ; every logarithm is dominated for small g₀, leaving e^{−c_qϑ²π_*²/2}. The polynomial prefactor r^{d_G} is thus paid by the same log comparison (log r = O(log log g₀⁻²)); it is a prefactor, not the cost in the exponent growing with r that 3.4's comparison remark set aside. ∎

6.3 What item 6 establishes and what it does not. Established: the exact labelled branch identity (6.1)–(6.2), which is stage 1's one-step branch identity per labelled term at the retreated carrier, with the label-independent leading Wilson value W_χ(C;U₁) on A_cov^{fin}; the locality of the species at t = 0 (Lemma 6.2): every activity, probe measure, group weight and reference activity of a labelled term at t = 0, and on A_cov^{fin} every defect R⁰_Λ(𝔅), coincides with stage 1's unless its domains meet the de-younged bonds, so the extra resummation index touches only the species whose domains contain a cube of 𝒩_lab, i.e. a cube met by a de-younged bond (such domains may extend far from I ∖ D(Λ); what is local is the marking, not the domain) (the t > 0 species, which enter ℬ^Λ only, depend on m and the exterior data and are bounded, not identified, by §5), and the reference gas changes by exp(η_lab) with |η_lab| ≤ 2ε°_g|I ∖ D(Λ)| (Lemma 6.3); the definition of E^{lab}_bdry and its bound (6.3)–(6.4), of stage 3's normalised form with q → q + N_retq_ϑ, the polynomial factor r^{d_G} paid by the π_*² comparison, and the family-sum factor e^{2n_Cε̄_t} of (5.9), uniform in the volume (every term of (6.3) is a one-point witness probability times a lattice sum). Not established here: (1) the identification of the labelled leading coefficient S°_Λ with κ_Ce^{E°_Λ} — stage 1's closing remark (after lem:covered) assigns the identification of S°_{ξ,C} with the flat coefficient κ_Ce^{E°_ξ} to a planned section sec:one-step-insertion, listed in stage 1's label audit among the unwritten stage 3–4 targets and present in no draft and not in the TeX; the TeX's existing identification theorem is prop:regional-square-extraction (S°_{D,C_n,T} = κ_Ce^{E°_{D,C_n,T}} for a general young carrier D, eq:regional-square-scalar, with the boundary distances d_j = dist(K_j, D^c) − R₀ in the error), and the TeX's eq:retained-leading-source already keeps e^{E°_ξ} inside the history expectation as a label-dependent factor; applying it at D = D(Λ) needs the identity S°_Λ = S°_{D(Λ),C,T} (closure P4 Step 1 with I → D(Λ): reference data at D(Λ) with zero exterior variable, Lemma 5.3(a), the cutoff Θ_{D(Λ)} by Lemma 3.3, the contour stencils in D(Λ) on A_cov^{fin}) and the check that its collar and range hypotheses (R₀) admit the two-threshold collar at Λ₁^{fin}; neither is done here; the planned section's application to the labelled term, and the fate of the regional factor e^{E°_Λ} (label-dependent) in the labelled leading term Σ_lab w^{lab}_ξS°_Λ, are the TeX's business when the drafts are edited; Lemma 6.3 gives S°_Λ = S°_{ξ,C}e^{η_lab} with η_lab marked by the de-younged bonds, but |I ∖ D(Λ)| counts the round-0 bonds D ∖ D^{(0)} (Lemma 3.0; zero when Λ₁ is filled in the side-100ℓ sense) and up to N_ret bonds per failed collar test, numbers controlled in probability only (p_z^{(0)} ≤ q, p_e^ϑ ≤ q_ϑ per bond), so e^{η_lab} − 1 is not small on every history, and averaging it over the labels would need exponential moments of |I ∖ D(Λ)| under the history measure, which §§2–3 do not supply (they give one-point probabilities only); with the tools of this note the leading term is therefore to be identified at Λ₁^{fin} directly rather than compared to the carrier I; (2) E^{lab}_def: stage 4's per-branch defect bound at the carrier D(Λ), i.e. stage 4 read with I → D(Λ), S° → S°_Λ, R_ξ → R⁰_Λ, whose inputs (stage 1–2 lemmas, the pins) hold at D(Λ) by Lemma 4.0, Corollary 4.3, Lemma 5.3(b) and Lemma 5.8, and whose species are stage 1's away from 𝒩_lab (Lemma 6.2) — the reading itself is to be checked when the drafts are edited and is not claimed here; (3) the factor e^{2n_Cε̄_t}: ε̄_t = 2K_χC_pinρ_t → 0 as g₀ → 0 independently of n_C (5.2), but, as in stage 4 §12, n_Cε̄_t must be bounded for the bound to be useful, which holds at fixed n_C as g₀ → 0 and is a statement about the caller's loop (5.5(7)) [superseded by §5R: (6.3R), (6.4R) carry e^{n_Cε̄_ph} with the second-order ε̄_ph, Proposition 5R.4]; (4) the TeX's marked-polymer induction (the species p and the pinned recursion eq:marginal-pinned-recursion) is unchanged in form; its species are the groups at the carrier of the term, and Lemma 6.2 is what makes the induction's inputs label-independent away from the de-younged bonds — writing the index into the TeX's induction is a draft edit, not done here; (5) everything in §6 inherits the status of §5 and of §5's imported inputs listed in 5.5(4) (stage 4 §6.1, §6.2, §7.1, §12.4; attempt §§13, 18.3, 19.3, 19.4, 20.1; the TeX's tree-graph justification; the square/torus geometry of 5.0), and a failure of any of them propagates to (6.2)–(6.4) and Lemma 6.3.

## 7. Item 7: identification of the labelled leading coefficient (calculation authorised by the user 2026-10-19; isolated review r1 2026-10-19, see 7.3(7))

7.0 Target, inputs and thresholds. Item 7 of the pass note (§7) and 6.3(1): identify the labelled leading coefficient S°_Λ of (6.2) with κ_Ce^{E°_Λ}, κ_C := e^{−δmP(C)}z_c⁴ (the TeX's e^{−δm^σP(C_n)}(z^σ_c(g₀,π/2))⁴ in the cutoff prescription σ fixed throughout), on A_cov^{fin}, with a bounded E°_Λ, and record what the bounds (6.3R), (6.4R) and the leading term of (6.1)–(6.2) become relative to κ_C. The objects: S°_Λ = Σ_𝔅∏_{W∈𝔅}a^Λ_W = ∫f°K°_Λda/I°_Λ (Lemma 5.3(b), Lemma 6.3), the reference data at D(Λ) being H° = (𝖧^{Ω₁}_{id})_{D(Λ)D(Λ)}, exterior variable zero on J(Λ), m° = ℓ° = 0, f°(a) = W_χ(C; e^{iq_id(a)}), so that, by the definition of K^Λ_{ξ;t} (§3, the display before Lemma 3.3) at t = 0 and these data,

  K°_Λ(a) = 1_{𝒟^Λ}(a) exp[−½⟨a,H°a⟩ + Σ_{𝔭 near rel. D(Λ)}𝔭(a)],   a ∈ 𝔤^{D(Λ)} (exterior variable zero),   I°_Λ = ∫K°_Λda,   S°_Λ = ∫f°K°_Λda / I°_Λ,                    (7.1)

the near primitives being those of all three kinds (action, Haar, constraint Jacobian) whose free-input set meets D(Λ) (§3 after (3.0)). Inputs, used at their stated status: (I1) the TeX's regional square scalar eq:regional-square-scalar (L994–1002), S°_{D,C_n,T} = ∫Θ^σ_D(B)e^{P_{D,T}(B)}χ(Hol_{U_{g₀,T}(i_{D,T}B)}C_n)dμ_{C_{D,T}}(B) / ∫Θ^σ_D(B)e^{P_{D,T}(B)}dμ_{C_{D,T}}(B), C_{D,T} = (i*_{D,T}H_Ti_{D,T})⁻¹, defined for every set D of free bonds such that every free-input stencil of the contour lies in D ("covered"), with the original primitive labels retained including those crossing the boundary of D; (I2) prop:regional-square-extraction (L1011–1027): under coverage, K_μ ≥ 3(n+2), the collar conditions eq:regional-collar-sizes (L960–964: M ≥ 8LM₂, LMR₁ > 4R₀ + 4L²M₂R₁ + 8, R₀ = 4(1+R_*+R_H+L), enforceable by choosing M with LM − 4L²M₂ > 4R₀ + 8 before the coupling) and the common small-coupling thresholds of the flat and regional expansions (L1034–1039, L1062), S°_{D,C_n,T} = κ_Ce^{E°_{D,C_n,T}} with |E°_{D,C_n,T}| ≤ Cτ²[Σ_{j=0}^{4n−1}e^{−cd_j} + 4ne^{−cι_T} + (n+1)e^{−cn}], d_j = max(0, d_T(K_j,D^c) − R₀), ι_T = max(0, N_min/4 − R₀), K_j the union of the free-input stencils of the fine traversals of coarse segment j, complements in the torus free carrier, d_T the periodic fine-bond metric (L807), C, c independent of D, n and the periods; (I3) the TeX's identification paragraph L957–980: "the comparison holds for every D_− ⊆ D ⊆ D_+" (D_± the free bonds with both/at least one endpoint in Λ₁), "assigning the complementary free variables to the exterior integral", with its facts at identity data — every stencil meeting D ⊆ D_+ stays in the regular region and the Hessian on D is H_{D,T} = i*_{D,T}H_Ti_{D,T} including the diagonal terms of crossing plaquettes (L966, L1032); the constraint linearisation gives the flat phase eq:flat-phase so that the reconstructed links at identity data are e^{iq_id} (L968); at zero exterior free field the conditional cutoff is Θ^σ_D with r = δ₀/g₀ (L972–974); the primitives of the conditional reference scalar are those of eq:regional-periodic-weight with "terms whose free support misses D" vanishing at i_{D,T}B (L1032); (I4) this note: Lemma 3.3(iv) (1_{𝒟^Λ} = Θ_{D(Λ)} on the term's support), Lemma 5.3(b) (the reference data at D(Λ)), 6.1's A_cov^{fin} (every contour bond in Ω₁ with its free-input set 𝓕_r ⊆ D(Λ)), 3.4(a) (a bond not in D(Λ) has no endpoint in Λ₁^{fin}, so lies in E(B^{fin}_ξ), B^{fin}_ξ := (Λ₁^{fin})^c), Proposition 6.4 and §5R. Thresholds, in addition to stage 2's items 1–5 (with the collar item 4), (4.T), (4.T′), (5.T), (R.T) and the period hypothesis of 3.1:

  (7.T)  (i) the TeX's collar conditions eq:regional-collar-sizes: M ≥ 8LM₂ (already in stage 2's order of choice) and LMR₁ > 4R₀ + 4L²M₂R₁ + 8 with R₀ = 4(1+R_*+R_H+L) (a condition on M before the coupling, of the kind of stage 2 item 1, which has R_c in place of R₀; both are imposed), with aligned nested partitions and all periods divisible by LMR₁ (stage 1's standing conditions) and the contour a coarse translate of the counterclockwise (e₁,e₂) square of side nL (5.0, the TeX's square);   (ii) the TeX's common small-coupling thresholds of the flat and regional expansions (g₀C_𝖢(2+4r) < ϑ_*/2, g₀(2+4r) < a_L, and the polymer-load inequalities of L1062), conditions on g₀ alone of the accepted kind;   (iii) K_μ ≥ 3(n+2) for every coarse period (5.0), and, for the n-uniform form of Corollary 7.2(c) only, 4ne^{−cι_T} ≤ 1 (a condition on the torus: N_min ≥ 4R₀ + (4/c)log(4n)).

7.1 The identification at the retreated carrier.

Lemma 7.1. Under (7.T), on every labelled term with final region Λ, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, on A_cov^{fin}: S°_Λ = S°_{D(Λ),C,T}, the TeX's regional square scalar (I1) at D = D(Λ).

Proof. Both sides are ratios of two integrals over 𝔤^{D(Λ)} with the same denominator structure; it suffices to identify the four factors of the integrands, numerator and denominator alike, the Gaussian normaliser of μ_{C_{D(Λ),T}} being common to numerator and denominator on the TeX's side and absent on both on the note's side. First, D(Λ) qualifies: D(Λ) ⊆ I = D_+(Λ₁) (stage 1's carrier consists of all free bonds with an endpoint in Λ₁, §0's bond convention; the retreat only removes bonds), so every stencil meeting D(Λ) stays in the regular region and every bond of D(Λ) has an endpoint in a good cube of Λ₁, which is all the TeX's facts (I3) use (L966, L972: the lower inclusion D_− ⊆ D of the TeX's comparison statement is not used in the proof of these facts — they are stated for D ⊆ D_+ with the collar; this reading of L966–976 is for the reviewer to confirm); the de-younged bonds I ∖ D(Λ) are "complementary free variables assigned to the exterior integral", which is what Lemma 5.3(b)'s zero exterior variable on J(Λ) does; and D(Λ) is covered on A_cov^{fin}, since every contour bond lies in Ω₁ with 𝓕_r ⊆ D(Λ), and 𝓕_r is the TeX's stencil Λ_r (L1209: Λ_b = {b} for a free bond, Λ_{b₀(c)} = 𝒮_c ∩ E_* for a designated bond; 𝓕_r ⊆ 𝓔_c ∩ E_* for the row c of r, 5.0; what (I2) needs is Λ_r ⊆ D(Λ), so the inclusion used is Λ_r ⊆ 𝓕_r) — this reading is flagged in 7.3(2). (F1) Precision: H° = (𝖧^{Ω₁}_{id})_{D(Λ)D(Λ)} is the Hessian at identity of stage 1's regular-region action (CRE's constrained quadratic form, 𝖧^{Ω₁}_{U₁} = 𝖢*Δ₁𝖢, stage 1 lem:primitive-sum) restricted to D(Λ) × D(Λ); stage 2's lem:c2-reference (L410–422) states that the entries of H° with a label in I are f_{bb′}(0), the entries of the flat Hessian of eq:flat-covariance on the branch's torus, so the principal restriction to D(Λ) ⊆ I is the flat torus precision on D(Λ) including the diagonal contributions of the plaquettes crossing the boundary of D(Λ) — the same statement as (I3) fact 1 at D = D(Λ) (L966, L1032), which holds because every stencil meeting D(Λ) lies in the regular region, where the action at identity is the flat quadratic form; the identification of stage 2's flat Hessian with the TeX's H_T is stage 2's (it is the same operator eq:flat-covariance on the same torus). So exp(−½⟨a,H°a⟩)da and dμ_{C_{D(Λ),T}}(B) agree up to the common normaliser, with a = B. (F2) Cutoff: at the reference data the cutoff of K°_Λ is the product of the bond tests ∏_{b∈D(Λ)}1{|a_b|_σ < r} by the reference-data convention — Lemma 5.3(b) ("the same cutoffs", the reference data not being a branch), Lemma 4.0(c) for these data (K°_Λ > 0 on the open box, proof of Lemma 6.3), stage 2's K°_{ξ;0} = 1_{𝒟_I}exp(…) on the open box at the carrier I; on a branch the corresponding statement is Lemma 3.3(iv), which is not what is used here. The TeX's Θ^σ_D is the same product of strict bond tests at the same threshold r = δ₀/g₀ in the same norm |·|_σ (I3), the note's r being V2's cutoff radius (§0). (F3) Primitives: Σ_{𝔭 near rel. D(Λ)}𝔭(a) at zero exterior variable is the sum of stage 1's local primitives at identity background whose free-input set meets D(Λ), evaluated at a = i_{D(Λ),T}B; the TeX's P_{D(Λ),T}(i_{D(Λ),T}B) is the sum of the primitives of eq:regional-periodic-weight with labels meeting D(Λ) (crossing labels retained), the others vanishing at i_{D(Λ),T}B (L1032). The two sums agree term by term once the primitive functions agree, which is stage 1's definition (eq:c-potential-definition: the local terms of the action, Haar and constraint Jacobian at identity background, the same expansion as the TeX's; L1032 asserts this agreement for the TeX's own conditional kernel) — the far primitives of the note contribute their value at zero exterior variable, a constant common to numerator and denominator of (7.1), so whether they are counted is immaterial. (F4) Wilson factor: f°(a) = W_χ(C; e^{iq_id(a)}) is χ of the holonomy along C of the links e^{iq_id(a)_r}; by (I3) fact 2 the TeX's reconstructed links at identity data are U_{g₀,T}(i_{D(Λ),T}B) = e^{iq_id} evaluated at the same free field, and on A_cov^{fin} the link of each contour bond r depends on the free field through 𝓕_r ⊆ D(Λ) only, so the exterior variable (zero) does not enter and f°(a) = χ(Hol_{U_{g₀,T}(i_{D(Λ),T}B)}C). There is no fifth factor: at t = 0 and m° = ℓ° = 0 the kernel K^Λ_{ξ;0} has no linear term and no shift, and the TeX's facts 3 and 5 (boundary change, cross term) concern the identification of the TeX's conditional CRE scalar with S°_{D,C,T}, which is the TeX's own statement (I2, "the collar hypotheses identify this integral with the conditional CRE scalar") and is not needed to compare the two explicit integrals (7.1) and (I1). Numerator and denominator agree factor by factor, so the ratios agree. ∎

Corollary 7.2. Under (7.T), on every labelled term, on A_cov^{fin}, with E°_Λ := E°_{D(Λ),C,T} (the TeX's error at D = D(Λ), its branch that of (I2)) and d_j(Λ) := max(0, d_T(K_j, D(Λ)^c) − R₀):

  (a) S°_Λ = κ_Ce^{E°_Λ},   |E°_Λ| ≤ Cτ²[Σ_{j=0}^{4n−1}e^{−cd_j(Λ)} + 4ne^{−cι_T} + (n+1)e^{−cn}] ≤ Cτ²(9n+1),                                                              (7.2)

E°_Λ depends on the labels through D(Λ) only (a simple function on the finitely many labels of a history), κ_C ≠ 0 (TeX L1298: finite perimeter exponent, z_c the exponential of a convergent sum; also S°_Λ ≠ 0 by (I2)), and |κ_C|e^{−Cτ²(9n+1)} ≤ |S°_Λ| ≤ |κ_C|e^{Cτ²(9n+1)} (so S°_Λ ≠ 0 on A_cov^{fin}, consistent with Lemma 6.3's |S°_Λ| ≤ 1);
  (b) the leading term of (6.1)–(6.2) is κ_CΣ_lab e^{E°_Λ}∫_{𝔤^{I∖D(Λ)}}1_lab e^{c^Λ_ξ}I^Λ_1 1_{A_cov^{fin}} W_χ(C;U₁) da_old (e^{E°_Λ} and 1_{A_cov^{fin}} depend on the labels only and leave the a_old-integral), and on A_cov^{fin}, by (5.9R),

  |κ_C|⁻¹|ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ]| ≤ e^{|E°_Λ|} e^{n_Cε̄_ph} [C_1g₀r𝔖_1(Λ) + C_2ε̄_t r^{d_G}𝔖_2(Λ)] ≤ e^{Cτ²(9n+1)} e^{n_Cε̄_ph} [C_1g₀r𝔖_1(Λ) + C_2ε̄_t r^{d_G}𝔖_2(Λ)];                                   (7.3)

  (c) (refined split) |E°_Λ| ≤ Cτ²(2 + 1/c) + Cτ²Σ_je^{−cd_j(Λ)} under 4ne^{−cι_T} ≤ 1 ((7.T)(iii); for n ≥ 1 this is N_min ≥ 4R₀ + (4/c)log(4n)), since (n+1)e^{−cn} ≤ 1 + sup_{x≥0}xe^{−cx} = 1 + 1/(ec) ≤ 1 + 1/c: the only part of the TeX's error that grows with n at fixed τ is the boundary sum Σ_je^{−cd_j(Λ)} ≤ 4n, each of whose terms is at most 1 and exponentially small in d_T(K_j,D(Λ)^c) − R₀ (the sum is zero only when D(Λ)^c = ∅, by the convention e^{−∞} = 0); it is the quantity averaged in Lemma 7.3.

Proof. (a) Lemma 7.1 and (I2) at D = D(Λ), whose hypotheses are (7.T) and coverage (A_cov^{fin}); the crude bound uses e^{−cd_j} ≤ 1, e^{−cι_T} ≤ 1 and (n+1)e^{−cn} ≤ n+1 (as in the TeX's B_C, L1280); D(Λ) is a function of the labels. (b) The leading term of (6.2) is S°_ΛW_χ(C;U₁) integrated as in (6.1); substitute (a). The bound: (5.9R) is |ℬ^Λ| ≤ |S°_Λ|e^{n_Cε̄_ph}[…] and |S°_Λ| = |κ_C|e^{Re E°_Λ} ≤ |κ_C|e^{|E°_Λ|}. (c) The stated elementary inequality. ∎

7.2 The label average of the boundary distances.

Lemma 7.3. In the setting of Proposition 6.4 (square geometry of 5.0, compatible even fine periods exceeding 200ℓ, the hypotheses of §§2–3), for every c > 0,

  Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[Σ_lab w^{lab}_ξ 1_{A_cov^{fin}} Σ_{j=0}^{4n−1}e^{−cd_j(Λ)}]dV ≤ e^{c(R₀+ϱ_K)} N_lat(c) n_C (q + N_retq_ϑ),   ϱ_K := max_j max_{k∈K_j} min_{r∈C_j}d_T(k,r) ≤ 2L+1,                                      (7.4)

with N_lat(c) = Σ_{s≥0}4(2s+1)⁴e^{−cs} of Lemma 1.2, C_j the contour bonds of coarse segment j, q and q_ϑ of (2.3) and Lemma 2.4, N_ret of Lemma 3.1 — uniform in the volume, and of the same form as (6.3): a lattice sum times one-point witness probabilities.

Proof. Pointwise on a term: d_j(Λ) ≥ d_T(K_j, D(Λ)^c) − R₀, so e^{−cd_j(Λ)} ≤ e^{cR₀}e^{−cd_T(K_j,D(Λ)^c)} ≤ e^{cR₀}Σ_{b∉D(Λ)}e^{−cd_T(K_j,b)} (the maximum over b ∈ D(Λ)^c of e^{−cd_T(K_j,b)} is at most the sum; if D(Λ)^c = ∅ the left side is zero by the TeX's convention e^{−∞} = 0). For k ∈ K_j and b, the triangle inequality for d_T gives d_T(k,b) ≥ min_{r∈C_j}d_T(r,b) − ϱ_K, hence e^{−cd_T(K_j,b)} ≤ e^{cϱ_K}max_{r∈C_j}e^{−cd_T(r,b)} ≤ e^{cϱ_K}Σ_{r∈C_j}e^{−cd_T(r,b)}, and Σ_j Σ_{r∈C_j} = Σ_{r∈C} because the segments partition the contour bonds. A bond b ∉ D(Λ) has no endpoint in Λ₁^{fin} (for b ∈ I ∖ D(Λ) by 3.4(a); for a free bond b ∉ I because I = D_+(Λ₁) is the set of all free bonds with an endpoint in Λ₁ ⊇ Λ₁^{fin}, §0's bond convention — the equality, not only D(Λ) ⊆ I, is used here), so 1{b ∉ D(Λ)} ≤ 1{b ∈ E(B^{fin}_ξ)} ≤ 1{b ∈ E(𝔅₀)} + Σ_{e′: x_{e′} within 102ℓ+1 of an endpoint of b}M_{e′} (3.4(a), Lemma 3.1(c)–(d)). Altogether Σ_je^{−cd_j(Λ)} ≤ e^{c(R₀+ϱ_K)}Σ_{r∈C}Σ_b e^{−cd_T(r,b)}[1{b ∈ E(𝔅₀)} + Σ_{e′ near b}M_{e′}], every term nonnegative and label-independent except through the marks. Multiply by w^{lab}_ξ1_{A_cov^{fin}} ≤ w^{lab}_ξ, sum over the labels and histories, apply 𝒪 and divide by Z₀ (Tonelli; finitely many labels per history; Σ_lab w^{lab}_ξ = w_ξ, 3.4(c)): for each fixed b the first term gives p^{(0)}_b ≤ q (Lemma 3.0(b)) and the second Σ_{e′ near b}p^ϑ_{e′} ≤ N_retq_ϑ (Lemma 3.1(d), Proposition 2.6), exactly as in the proof of Proposition 6.4. Finally Σ_b e^{−cd_T(r,b)} ≤ Σ_{s≥0}4(2s+1)⁴e^{−cs} = N_lat(c) for each contour bond r (d_T(r,b) ≥ ‖x_r − x_b‖_∞, and at most 4(2s+1)⁴ bonds have initial site within ℓ^∞ distance s of x_r), and Σ_{r∈C}1 = n_C. The bound on ϱ_K: for a free contour bond r, Λ_r = {r} and d_T(r,r) = 0; for a designated r = b₀(c), the stencil bonds and r itself have both endpoints in the two endpoint blocks of c (L1209: Λ_{b₀(c)} = 𝒮_c ∩ E_* with 𝒮_c the bonds with both endpoints in the two endpoint blocks of c; r = b₀(c) is a bond of the row c, 5.0), two adjacent blocks of side L, any two sites of which are within ℓ^∞ distance 2L − 1 of each other, so d_T(k,r) ≤ 2L − 1 + 1 = 2L ≤ 2L+1. ∎

Corollary 7.4 (bounds relative to κ_C). Under (7.T) and the hypotheses of Proposition 5R.4 and Lemma 7.3:
  (a) (crude, the TeX's B_C form) Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[Σ_lab∫1_lab e^{c^Λ_ξ}I^Λ_1 1_{A_cov^{fin}} |κ_C|⁻¹|ℬ^Λ_ξ[f_ξ]| da_old]dV ≤ e^{Cτ²(9n+1)} × (the right side of (6.3R)) = e^{Cτ²(9n+1)}e^{n_Cε̄_ph}(C_1g₀r + C_2ε̄_t r^{d_G})(1 + e^{μ_H(2L+1)/2})N_lat(μ′)n_C(q + N_retq_ϑ), by (7.3) and Proposition 6.4's proof with |ℬ^Λ|/|S°_Λ| replaced by |κ_C|⁻¹|ℬ^Λ| ≤ e^{Cτ²(9n+1)}|ℬ^Λ|/|S°_Λ|; by the comparison of Proposition 6.4 (r^{d_G}N_ret(q + N_retq_ϑ) ≤ e^{−c_qϑ²π_*²/2} for g₀ below a further threshold; N_ret and r^{d_G} themselves diverge as g₀ → 0) this is at most C n_C e^{n_Cε̄_ph}e^{Cτ²(9n+1)}e^{−c_qϑ²π_*²/2}, and e^{Cτ²(9n+1)} is the factor the TeX itself carries in B_C = |κ_C|exp(Cτ²(9n+1)) (L1280).
  (b) (first moment of the leading error) Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[Σ_lab w^{lab}_ξ 1_{A_cov^{fin}} |E°_Λ|]dV ≤ Cτ²(2 + 1/c) + Cτ²e^{c(R₀+ϱ_K)}N_lat(c)n_C(q + N_retq_ϑ) under (7.T)(iii), by Corollary 7.2(c), Lemma 7.3 and the normalisation Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[Σ_lab w^{lab}_ξ1_{A_cov^{fin}}]dV ≤ Z₀⁻¹∫𝒪[w_ξ]dV = 1 (Σ_lab w^{lab}_ξ = w_ξ, 3.4(c); Z₀ = ∫𝒪[w_ξ]dV, 6.0): the labelled leading error has, in label average, a part O(τ²) uniform in n and a part linear in n_C paid by the one-point witness probabilities (small for g₀ below the threshold of Proposition 6.4's comparison, at fixed n_C); the first moment is not uniform in n. What is not obtained: a bound on the average of e^{E°_Λ} − 1 or of e^{|E°_Λ|} uniform in n, which would need exponential moments of the boundary sum under the history measure — the same limitation as 6.3(1)'s e^{η_lab}; the crude (a) is what is available for the exponential, and it is the TeX's own form.

7.3 What item 7 establishes and what it does not. Established: the identity S°_Λ = S°_{D(Λ),C,T} on A_cov^{fin} (Lemma 7.1), by a factorwise comparison of the two explicit integrals (precision, cutoff, primitives, Wilson factor), using the TeX's facts (I3) at D = D(Λ) ⊆ D_+(Λ₁) — so stage 1's deferred identification (stage 1 after lem:covered, L770; 6.3(1)) is, at the retreated carrier, reduced to these four factor identities; the identification S°_Λ = κ_Ce^{E°_Λ} with the TeX's error bound at D(Λ) and the crude bound Cτ²(9n+1) (Corollary 7.2), E°_Λ a function of D(Λ); the form of the labelled leading term and of (6.3R) relative to κ_C (Corollaries 7.2(b), 7.4(a)); the label average of the boundary sum Σ_je^{−cd_j(Λ)}, which is a one-point witness quantity of the form of (6.3), linear in n_C (Lemma 7.3), and the resulting first-moment bound on E°_Λ: a deterministic O(τ²) part uniform in n plus the witness term (Corollary 7.4(b)). Not established here: (1) prop:regional-square-extraction and the facts of L957–980 are used at their stated status; their proofs are the TeX's and are not re-verified; in particular (F3) rests on the sum-level agreement of stage 1's exponent (CRE (1.14)–(1.16), lem:primitive-sum) with the TeX's P_{D,T} at identity data, which L1032 asserts for the TeX's own kernel — not checked line by line here, and the TeX's eq:regional-periodic-weight and eq:perimeter-literal-local-terms were not re-read for it; (2) the inclusion Λ_r ⊆ 𝓕_r (the TeX's coverage stencil of L1209 inside the note's free-input set of the traversal link), which is what makes A_cov^{fin} imply the TeX's coverage, is asserted from stage 1's 𝓕_{b₀(c)} = 𝓔_c ∩ E_* (lem:covered's proof) and L1209's Λ_{b₀(c)} = 𝒮_c ∩ E_*, i.e. from 𝒮_c ⊆ 𝓔_c (the bonds with both endpoints in the two endpoint blocks of c among the bonds of the two-block set 𝓔_c of lem:two-block), which was not re-read here; if it failed, A_cov^{fin} would have to be intersected with {Λ_r ⊆ D(Λ) for every contour bond r}, with the same probabilistic bounds (3.4(a) uses only that a stencil bond lies within 2L+1 of its contour bond) and with Lemmas 5.3, 6.1 and Proposition 5R.4 still using 𝓕_r ⊆ D(Λ); (3) the TeX's comparison statement (L957) is for D_− ⊆ D ⊆ D_+; D(Λ) ⊆ D_+ but D_− ⊆ D(Λ) fails whenever a bond with both endpoints in Λ₁ is de-younged; Lemma 7.1 uses only D(Λ) ⊆ D_+ with the collar, which is what the proofs of the facts use on my reading of L966–976 — flagged for the reviewer; (4) uniformity in n of the exponential e^{E°_Λ} in label average (Corollary 7.4(b), last sentence); (5) E^{lab}_def (6.3(2)) and the writing of §§1–7 into the drafts and the TeX are untouched; the TeX's eq:retained-leading-source (L1262–1270) already keeps e^{E°_ξ} inside the history expectation as a label-dependent factor, which is the form Corollary 7.2(b) gives at the retreated carrier; (6) (7.T)(i) adds the TeX's R₀-collar condition to stage 2's R_c-condition (item 1); both are conditions on M before the coupling and are compatible (choose M after both); whether R₀ ≤ R_c or conversely is not checked; (7) further inputs used as stated and not re-read here: the reference-data cutoff convention behind (F2) (Lemma 5.3(b), Lemma 4.0(c), stage 2's K°_{ξ;0}); I = D_+(Λ₁) (§0's bond convention) in Lemma 7.3; the geometry of L1209 behind ϱ_K; the square orientation and M ≥ 8LM₂ of (7.T)(i); the normalisation Z₀ = ∫𝒪[w_ξ]dV in Corollary 7.4(b); the TeX's eq:regional-periodic-weight, eq:flat-phase, eq:perimeter-literal-local-terms, eq:marginal-fixed-forest-bound (τ), stage 1's lem:two-block (𝓔_c), eq:regular-stencil-extension-support, and this note's Lemma 1.2 and Lemma 4.0(c). Review r1 (`collar-item-7-20261019-r1`, 2026-10-19, 227 lines): no blocking error; Lemma 7.1's conclusion follows from the cited inputs subject to (2) and to L1032's status; Corollary 7.2 and Lemma 7.3 correct as inequalities; the lower inclusion D_− ⊆ D confirmed unused in L957–980 and L1029–1130; seven non-blocking points, all repaired in this text: "vanishes" in 7.2(c), "uniform in n" for the first moment, "O(q_ϑ)" in 7.4(a), the citation of Lemma 3.3(iv) for the reference cutoff (F2), "term by term" in (F3), I = D_+(Λ₁) stated as an equality, "7.5(2)" → "7.3(2)"; plus κ_C ≠ 0, the omitted hypotheses of (I2) in (7.T)(i), the normalisation in 7.4(b) and the ϱ_K geometry.

## 8. TeX form of Sections 0-7 and 5R

The block below is the TeX translation of the reviewed text above, spliced on 19 October 2026 into WILSON_FIRST_RG_STEP_V3.tex as the last numbered section (`sec:one-step-insertion`, immediately before `\appendix`). It omits the review records and process prose and keeps the mathematical scope remarks as `remark` environments; the translation is being reviewed subsection by subsection (record in the status line at the head of this file; the TeX in V3 is authoritative where it differs from this block, which is not re-mirrored). Cross-references to the stage 3/4 drafts and to the attempt appear as `\draftref{...}`; `app:exterior-chessboard` was a forward reference to the planned appendix on the exterior response, spliced into V3 later on 19 October 2026 (stage 3 draft, Section 10); the `\draftref{stage 3, ...}` references of this block were replaced in V3 (not in this block) by `\ref` to the appendix labels at 22:16. The section-number map: draft section k = `sec:c5-k`, Lemma k.j = `lem:c5-k-j`, display (k.j) = `eq:c5-k-j`, section 5R = `sec:c5-5R`.

```tex
\section{The Insertion at the Retreated Carrier}
\label{sec:one-step-insertion}

This section bounds the terms of the branch identity \eqref{eq:c-one-step-branch-identity} relative to the flat coefficient $\kappa_C$ of Section~\ref{sec:remaining-physical-comparison}. It does not work at the carrier $I$ of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} directly. A second cutoff test is placed on the collar of $\Lambda_1$; on the branches where it fails, $\Lambda_1$ and $I$ are retreated to a labelled carrier $D(\Lambda)\subseteq I$, the branch identity is written for each labelled term, and the labelled coefficient $S^\circ_\Lambda$ is identified with $\kappa_Ce^{E^\circ_\Lambda}$ at the retreated carrier by Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-square-extraction}. The bonds removed by the retreat are paid by the physical measure, through one-point witness probabilities of the kind of Appendix~\ref{app:exterior-chessboard}; no lower bound on the exterior Schur complement is used. The inputs are Sections~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}, \ref{sec:regional-identification} and~\ref{sec:remaining-physical-comparison}, Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates}, the exterior-response appendix and the two drafts on the signed defects and on the regional scalar, which are cited in the form \draftref{stage 3, \S7.2}, \draftref{stage 4, \S4.1}, \draftref{stage 4C, \S2} until they are inserted; the archived seed subsection of the exterior response is cited as \draftref{attempt, \S19.4}. CRE is \cite{BalabanCRE}, with its equation and page numbers.

\subsection{Setting and inputs}
\label{sec:c5-0}

Interior (young) bonds $D\subset\Lambda_1$, exterior free bonds $J=E_{\Omega,*}\setminus D$, $z=i_Da+i_JA_{\mathrm e}$. The kernel of Section~\ref{sec:remaining-physical-comparison} is $K_\xi(a)=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D_\xi}(a)\Theta^\sigma_D(a)p_\xi(\Psi_\xi(z_\xi(a)))|\det D\Psi_\xi|$ with $p_\xi(z)=\exp[-\tfrac12\langle z,H_\xi z\rangle+v_\xi(g_0C_\xi z)-g_0^{-2}V_\xi(g_0C_\xi z)]$ and $\Theta^\sigma_D(a)=\prod_{b\in D}\mathbf 1\{|a_b|_\sigma<r\}$, $r=\delta_0/g_0$. $\Psi_\xi$ is the bondwise boundary change CRE (1.22), the identity off $S_1^*\subset\Omega_1\cap\Lambda_1^c$, so it changes $A_{\mathrm e}$ only: write $A'_{\mathrm e}=\Psi_\xi(A_{\mathrm e})|_J$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-0-1}
    -\tfrac12\langle z,H_\xi z\rangle=-\tfrac12\langle a,H_{DD}a\rangle-\langle a,H_{DJ}A'_{\mathrm e}\rangle-\tfrac12\langle A'_{\mathrm e},H_{JJ}A'_{\mathrm e}\rangle\;,
\end{equation}
with $H_{DD}$, $H_{DJ}$, $H_{JJ}$ the blocks of $H_\xi$. All three terms are inside $K_\xi$ (not in the outer weight $\mathcal O_V$), by \eqref{eq:retained-prechange-density}.

Inputs from Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} (Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-bounds}, Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-entry-range}, Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-regional-entry} and the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-bounds}):
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(I1)] entry range: $(H_\xi)_{bb'}=0$ unless $|z(b)-z(b')|_1\leq2$, hence unless the initial sites are within $\ell^\infty$ distance $\rho_L=4L$ in fine units; each row has at most $164L^4$ nonzero entries;
\item[(I2)] row sums: $\sum_{b'\in\Omega_1^*}\|(H_\xi)_{bb'}\|\leq m_L$ for every row $b\in D$. This is the upper-bound paragraph of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-bounds} (for a row $b\in I$ all entries $f_{bb'}$ have stencils in $\mathfrak P_{z(b)}$, hence $\sum_{b'}\|f_{bb'}(U_1)\|\leq m_L$, the sum over all $b'$, with $(H_\xi)_{bb'}=f_{bb'}$ on $\Omega_1^*\times\Omega_1^*$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-regional-entry} and every stencil within $3L$ of $b_-$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-entry-range}). Column sums are not used;
\item[(I3)] two-sided bound on every branch in the support of the outer weight: $(\gamma_L/2)\id\leq H_{\xi,DD}\leq m_L\id$, and the same for every principal restriction (Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} writes $I$ for the carrier $D$).
\end{enumerate}
Put $\gamma=\gamma_L/2$ and $h_+=m_L$. The constants $\gamma_L$, $m_L$, $\rho_L$ depend on $L$ and the group only; the bounds (I2)--(I3) hold under the coupling thresholds of Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} (\eqref{eq:convex-background-thresholds}, Section~\ref{app:thresholds}). Norms: $|\cdot|_\sigma$, $\sigma\in\{\mathrm{op},\mathrm{HS}\}$, is the cutoff norm of \eqref{eq:literal-cutoff}, the operator norm or the normalised Hilbert--Schmidt norm $h$, fixed once (the conventions of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}); $h\leq|\cdot|_\sigma\leq c_{\mathrm h}h$ with $c_{\mathrm h}=\sqrt N$. Sections~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} and Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} write $I$ for the carrier that Section~\ref{sec:regional-identification} calls $D$; $D'\subset D$ denotes the carrier of a retreated region in Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-1}, and (I3) passes to it by principal restriction while (I2) is unchanged. Bond convention (Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}): the bonds of a set $X$ are the positive fine bonds with at least one endpoint in $X$. $\ell:=LMR_1$ (the macroscopic scale $\ell_{\mathrm{mac}}$ of \draftref{stage 3}).

\subsection{The mean shift}
\label{sec:c5-1}

Put $m=-H_{DD}^{-1}H_{DJ}A'_{\mathrm e}\in\mathfrak g^D$, so that $-\tfrac12\langle a,H_{DD}a\rangle-\langle a,H_{DJ}A'_{\mathrm e}\rangle=-\tfrac12\langle a-m,H_{DD}(a-m)\rangle+\tfrac12\langle m,H_{DD}m\rangle$.

\begin{lemma}[entrywise decay of the interior inverse]\label{lem:c5-1-1}
For $b,b'\in D$, with $d(b,b')$ the $\ell^\infty$ distance of the initial sites in fine units,
\begin{equation*}
    \|H_{DD}^{-1}(b,b')\|\leq\gamma^{-1}(1-\gamma/h_+)^{\lceil d(b,b')/\rho_L\rceil}\leq\gamma^{-1}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,b')}\;,\qquad \mu_H:=\frac{\gamma}{h_+\rho_L}=\frac{\gamma_L}{8Lm_L}\;.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By (I3), $0\leq K:=\id-h_+^{-1}H_{DD}\leq(1-\gamma/h_+)\id$, so $H_{DD}^{-1}=h_+^{-1}\sum_{n\geq0}K^n$ (norm-convergent, $\|K\|\leq1-\gamma/h_+<1$). $K$ has the entry range $\rho_L$ of (I1) (the identity is diagonal), so $K^n(b,b')=0$ when $d(b,b')>n\rho_L$. Hence $H_{DD}^{-1}(b,b')=h_+^{-1}\sum_{n\geq\lceil d/\rho_L\rceil}K^n(b,b')$ and $\|K^n(b,b')\|\leq\|K^n\|\leq(1-\gamma/h_+)^n$; the geometric sum gives $h_+^{-1}(1-\gamma/h_+)^{\lceil d/\rho_L\rceil}/(\gamma/h_+)$. Finally $(1-x)^s\leq e^{-xs}$ with $x=\gamma/h_+$, $s\geq d/\rho_L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[sup-norm shift bound]\label{lem:c5-1-2}
Let $J_1:=\{e\in J\, | \,H(b',e)\neq0\text{ for some }b'\in D\}$; by (I1), $J_1\subset\{e\in J\, | \,\operatorname{dist}(e,D)\leq\rho_L\}$. Then $m$ depends on $A'_{\mathrm e}|_{J_1}$ only (exterior bonds with $\operatorname{dist}(e,D)>\rho_L$ have a zero column in $H_{DJ}$), and for every $b\in D$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-1-1}
    h(m_b)\leq C_H^{\mathrm{near}}\max_{e\in J_1}h(A'_{\mathrm e}(e))\;,\qquad C_H^{\mathrm{near}}:=\gamma^{-1}m_LN_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H)\;,\qquad N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu):=\sum_{s\geq0}4(2s+1)^4e^{-\mu s}<\infty\;.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$m_b=-\sum_{b'\in D}H_{DD}^{-1}(b,b')\sum_{e\in J_1}H(b',e)A'_{\mathrm e}(e)$. Operator norms of entries are those induced by $h$ (the convention of Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates}), so $h(H(b',e)A'_{\mathrm e}(e))\leq\|H(b',e)\|h(A'_{\mathrm e}(e))$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-1} and the row-sum bound (I2) for the rows $b'\in D$,
\begin{equation*}
    h(m_b)\leq\sum_{b'\in D}\gamma^{-1}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,b')}\sum_{e\in J_1}\|H(b',e)\|h(A'_{\mathrm e}(e))\leq\gamma^{-1}m_L\max_{e\in J_1}h(A'_{\mathrm e}(e))\sum_{b'\in D}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,b')}\;,
\end{equation*}
and $\sum_{b'}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,b')}\leq\sum_{s\geq0}4(2s+1)^4e^{-\mu_Hs}$: at most $(2s+1)^4$ sites lie at $\ell^\infty$ distance $s$ from the initial site of $b$, each carries four positive bonds, and projection to the torus only identifies sites. Only row sums over $b'\in D$ are used; no column-sum bound for columns $e\in J$ is needed.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[norms and the choice of $\vartheta$]\label{rem:c5-1-3}
The cutoff norm $|\cdot|_\sigma$ is the operator norm or $h$, and $h\leq|\cdot|_\sigma\leq c_{\mathrm h}h$ with $c_{\mathrm h}=\sqrt N$. Hence $|m_b|_\sigma\leq c_{\mathrm h}h(m_b)\leq c_{\mathrm h}C_H^{\mathrm{near}}\max_{e\in J_1}h(A'_{\mathrm e}(e))\leq c_{\mathrm h}C_H^{\mathrm{near}}\max_{e\in J_1}|A'_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma$. Put
\begin{equation*}
    \vartheta:=\frac1{4c_{\mathrm h}C_H^{\mathrm{near}}}\;,
\end{equation*}
a constant depending on $L$ and the group only (through $\gamma_L$, $m_L$, $N$); $\vartheta\leq1/16$, since $c_{\mathrm h}\geq1$, $N_{\mathrm{lat}}\geq4$ (its $s=0$ term) and $m_L\geq\gamma$ by (I3). Consequently, if $|A'_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma<\vartheta r$ for every $e\in J$ with $\operatorname{dist}(e,D)\leq\rho_L$ (a set containing $J_1$), then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-1-2}
    \sup_{b\in D}|m_b|_\sigma\leq r/4\;.
\end{equation}
No coupling threshold and no condition on the exterior bonds farther than $\rho_L$ from $D$ are involved: those bonds do not enter $m$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-2}). The decay of $H_{DD}^{-1}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-1}) is used only to sum the $b'$-series; it plays no role in decoupling far exterior bonds, which are decoupled by the entry range (I1) alone.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The exterior Gaussian in the history weight, and the payment of a bad collar bond}
\label{sec:c5-2}

Two things are needed from the exterior Gaussian: the identification of its place inside the history weight, which is the exact identity \eqref{eq:c5-2-1}, and a payment for a collar bond on which the fluctuation is large. A lower bound $S\geq\gamma$ for the Schur complement $S$ of \eqref{eq:c5-2-1}, which would pay such a bond by $e^{-\gamma\vartheta^2r^2/2}$, is not available (Remark~\ref{rem:c5-2-2}), so the payment is taken from the physical measure instead, with every hypothesis checked (Section~\ref{sec:c5-2-3}).

\subsubsection*{Where the exterior Gaussian sits}
\label{sec:c5-2-1}
By \eqref{eq:c5-0-1} and completing the square (which needs only $H_{DD}$ invertible, (I3), and $|\det D\Psi_\xi|$ independent of $a$: $\log|\det D\Psi_\xi(A')|$ depends on $A'|_{S_1^*}$ only, Section~\ref{sec:source-phase-primitives}),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-2-1}
    K_\xi(a)=e^{-\frac12\langle A'_{\mathrm e},SA'_{\mathrm e}\rangle}\cdot\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D_\xi}(a)\Theta^\sigma_D(a)e^{-\frac12\langle a-m,H_{DD}(a-m)\rangle+v_\xi-g_0^{-2}V_\xi}|\det D\Psi_\xi|\;,\qquad S:=H_{JJ}-H_{JD}H_{DD}^{-1}H_{DJ}\;,
\end{equation}
using $\tfrac12\langle m,H_{DD}m\rangle-\tfrac12\langle A'_{\mathrm e},H_{JJ}A'_{\mathrm e}\rangle=-\tfrac12\langle A'_{\mathrm e},SA'_{\mathrm e}\rangle$ and $H_{JD}=H_{DJ}^*$. The factor is written in $A'_{\mathrm e}=\Psi_\xi(A_{\mathrm e})|_J$, which differs from $A_{\mathrm e}$ on $S_1^*$ only. Off $S_1^*$ it is the quadratic form CRE collects in $T_1$, CRE (1.29); on $S_1^*$ the conventions differ (CRE moves first-order boundary corrections into $V^{(0)}(S_1)$, p.252, while \eqref{eq:retained-prechange-density} retains $p_\xi\circ\Psi_\xi$ without truncation), and nothing below uses this correspondence. In the convention of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} ($\mathsf w_\xi=e^{c_\xi}I_{\xi;1}$, with $c_\xi$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:collar} carrying $\mathscr E_\xi(0)$, the exterior quadratic form, the far primitives and $\log|\det D\Psi_\xi|$, and $K_{\xi;1}$ carrying the $II$ and $IJ$ blocks and the near primitives) the same algebra gives $e^{c_\xi}I_{\xi;1}=e^{-\frac12\langle A'_{\mathrm e},SA'_{\mathrm e}\rangle}\times e^{c_\xi+\frac12\langle A'_{\mathrm e},H_{JJ}A'_{\mathrm e}\rangle}\times\int\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D}\Theta\,e^{-\frac12\langle a-m,H_{DD}(a-m)\rangle}\times(\text{near primitives and cutoffs})(a)\,\dd a$, the far primitives and $\mathscr E_\xi(0)$ staying in $c_\xi$. So in either convention the Schur factor is an exact factor of $\mathsf w_\xi$. It is independent of $a$, hence cancels in the conditional ratio $\mathcal J_\xi[f]/\mathcal J_\xi[1]$ inside $\mathcal O_V$ (\eqref{eq:retained-outer-kernel}) and acts only through the history weight.

\begin{remark}[no lower bound for $S$ on $\mathfrak g^J$ is used]\label{rem:c5-2-2}
$\langle x,Sx\rangle=\min_{y\in\mathfrak g^D}\langle(y,x),H_\xi(y,x)\rangle$ ($H_{DD}>0$ by (I3)), so $S\geq\gamma$ on $\mathfrak g^J$ is equivalent to $\langle(y,x),H_\xi(y,x)\rangle\geq\gamma|x|^2$ for all $(y,x)\in\mathfrak g^{D\cup J}$, a lower bound of $H_\xi$ in the $J$-directions on all of $\mathfrak g^{E_{\Omega,*}}$. No such bound is available: (i) the lower bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:regular-bounds} (its proof, local comparison) covers $\operatorname{supp}B$ by patches within $2R_{\mathrm c}-1$ of $I$, where Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-plaquette-deviation} (stated for cubes $\square'\subset\Omega_1^{\sim-1}\setminus R_1$ with $\square'^{\sim4}\subset\Omega_1$) gives a gauge with phases $\leq\widehat\mu$ and the flat gap $\gamma_L$ applies after the perturbation $c_L\widehat\mu<\gamma_L/4$; (ii) $J$ contains the free bonds of $S_1=(\Omega_1\setminus\Omega_1^{\sim-1})\cup R_1$ (CRE p.251 L4): on $R_1$ the CRE (1.18) fluctuation test fails, which says nothing about the background $U_1$ (the comparison $U_1\approx U_{1,\square'}$ holds there too, \draftref{stage 3, \S7.1}), but the small-phase gauge of Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-plaquette-deviation} is proved only for cubes of $\Omega_1^{\sim-1}\setminus R_1$; on $\Omega_1\setminus\Omega_1^{\sim-1}$ the tests $\chi_1(\Omega_1)$, $\chi_0'(\Omega_1)$ are in force (CRE (1.11), p.250) but the comparison $U_1\approx U_{1,\square'}$ is not available there ($\square'^{\sim4}\not\subset\Omega_1$), and no near-flat gauge is supplied; (iii) a bound $H_\xi\geq\gamma$ on the regular part $D\cup J_1$ alone gives only the compression $S_{J_1J_1}\geq\gamma$, because $S_{J_1,J\setminus J_1}=H_{J_1,J\setminus J_1}\neq0$ (entry range $\rho_L$). Hence the Gaussian factor \eqref{eq:c5-2-1} is not used for any bound; the exterior field on the large-field part is treated as CRE treats it, paid by the cutoff factors that put it there. Nothing in this section uses $S$.
\end{remark}

\subsubsection*{The payment of a bad collar bond from the physical measure}
\label{sec:c5-2-3}
For a region $\Lambda$ (a union of $\ell$-cubes, $\Lambda\subset\Lambda_1$) the carrier is $D(\Lambda):=\{b\in I\, | \,b\text{ has an endpoint in }\Lambda\}\subset I$ and the exterior is $J(\Lambda):=E_{\Omega,*}\setminus D(\Lambda)\supset J$; the collar is $J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda):=\{e\in J(\Lambda)\, | \,\operatorname{dist}(e,D(\Lambda))\leq\rho_L\}$ ($\ell^\infty$ distance of initial sites); it contains $J_1(\Lambda)$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-2} ($H_\xi$ does not depend on $\Lambda$). A collar bond $e$ is bad if $|A_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma\geq\vartheta r$, good otherwise; $A_{\mathrm e}(e)$ denotes the fluctuation variable of CRE (1.13)--(1.16) at $e$ whether $e$ is exterior or interior in the original split (for $e\in I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ it is the young variable $a_e$ of the original split, exterior on the labelled terms where it is tested, Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-1}). In the construction of Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-1} a labelled term carries the regions $\Lambda_1\supset\Lambda_1^{(0)}=\operatorname{Fill}(\Lambda_1)\supset\Lambda_1^{(1)}\supset\dots\supset\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$, unions of $\ell$-cubes, $\Lambda_1^{(0)}$ label-independent (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}) and the others determined by the labels ($B^{(0)},B^{(1)},\dots$) (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}), and the set of bonds tested on it is $T(\mathrm{labels}):=\bigcup_kJ_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda_1^{(k)})\subset J(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})$. The mark of a bond $e$ on a labelled term is
\begin{equation*}
    M_e:=\mathbf 1\{e\in T(\mathrm{labels})\}\cdot\mathbf 1\{|A_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma\geq\vartheta r\}\;,
\end{equation*}
a function of the labels and of $U$ ($A_{\mathrm e}$ is a function of $U$, of $V$, the block average of $U$, and of $\Omega_1$ through $U_1(\Omega_1,V)$); $M_e=0$ on every labelled term on which $e$ is not tested, so $M_e$ is defined on every history and every labelled term; on the support of a term's indicators a tested bond is bad exactly when it belongs to some $B^{(k)}$, so there $M_e=\mathbf 1\{e\in\bigcup_kB^{(k)}\}$ is a function of the labels alone. Let $\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi$ be the weight of a labelled term: $\mathsf w_\xi=\int_{\mathfrak g^I}\rho_\xi(a_{\mathrm{old}})\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}$ with $\rho_\xi:=e^{c_\xi}K_{\xi;1}$ the integrand of Lemma~\ref{lem:collar} (the factors already placed in $\kappa_\xi$, $\zeta_\xi$, $\chi_{\xi,\mathrm e}$ of $\mathcal O$ are not duplicated), and $\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi:=\int_{\mathfrak g^I}\rho_\xi(a_{\mathrm{old}})\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}(a_{\mathrm{old}},A_{\mathrm e})\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}$, where $\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}$ is the product of the term's collar indicators (functions of $A_{\mathrm e}$ and of $a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})}$, Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-1}); inside this integral the variables $a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})}$ are exterior for the re-split of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5} and $a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{D(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})}$ is the young variable, so $\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi=\int_{\mathfrak g^{I\setminus D^{\mathrm{fin}}}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{c^{\Lambda^{\mathrm{fin}}}_\xi}\bigl(\int_{\mathfrak g^{D^{\mathrm{fin}}}}K^{\Lambda^{\mathrm{fin}}}_{\xi;1}\,\dd a\bigr)\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D^{\mathrm{fin}}}$. Since the indicators are nonnegative measurable functions summing to one (Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(c)), $\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi=\mathsf w_\xi$ (Tonelli). The marked physical probability is
\begin{equation*}
    p_e^\vartheta:=\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O\Bigl[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi M_e\Bigr](V)\,\dd V\;,
\end{equation*}
with the history sum inside $\mathcal O$ as in \draftref{stage 3, \S7}; by the previous sentence $\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi M_e=\int_{\mathfrak g^I}\rho_\xi\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}M_e\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}$, so $p_e^\vartheta$ is well defined. For $e\in J$ this equals $\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O[\mathsf w_\xi\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}M_e](V)\,\dd V$ with $\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}M_e$ independent of $a_{\mathrm{old}}$, because $T(\mathrm{labels})\cap J=J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda_1^{(0)})\cap J$ is label-independent ($D^{(k)}\subset D^{(0)}$); for $e\in I$ the mark involves a variable that is young in the original split, and the labelled-term form is the definition.

\begin{lemma}[collar bonds are regular, eligible and in the chart]\label{lem:c5-2-3}
Let $e\in J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda)$ for some $\Lambda\subset\Lambda_1$ as above. Then: (a) $d(e,S_1^*)\geq\ell-2-\rho_L>0$, so $\Psi_\xi$ is the identity at $e$ and $A'_{\mathrm e}(e)=A_{\mathrm e}(e)$ ($d(I,S_1^*)\geq LMR_1-2$, \eqref{eq:c-collar-distance}; $\Psi_\xi$ bondwise with support $S_1^*$); also $e\in\mathcal G_\xi\setminus S_1^*$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(i), since $\rho_L=4L\leq3R_{\mathrm c}$. (b) On the branch $|A_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma<r$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(iii): the CRE (1.25) good test $\prod_{b\in\mathcal G_\xi}\mathbf 1\{|A(b)|<r\}$), so a bad collar bond has $\vartheta r\leq|A_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma<r$ and $g_0A_{\mathrm e}(e)$ lies in the principal chart (chart condition $h(g_0A_{\mathrm e}(e))<\pi/\sqrt N$: $h\leq|\cdot|_\sigma$ and $|g_0A_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma<\delta_0$, with $\delta_0<\pi/\sqrt N$ for small coupling). (c) The $L^2M_2R_1$-cube $\square'$ of CRE (1.18) containing the initial vertex of $e$ lies within $3R_{\mathrm c}+1+L^2M_2R_1<\ell-1$ of $\Lambda_1$, hence in $\Omega_1^{\sim-1}\setminus R_1'$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(i)), and $e\in(\square'^{\sim2})^*$: it is a good, eligible cube in the sense of \draftref{stage 3, \S7.1}, and the comparison $\sup_{b\in(\square'^{[2]})^*}|U_1(b)U_{1,\square'}(b)^{-1}-\id|_\sigma\leq C\varepsilon_1e^{-cLM_2R_1}$ holds for it (CRE p.250 after (1.19) with Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-plaquette-deviation}).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a)--(c) are the quoted statements of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} applied to $e$, using $D(\Lambda)\subset I$ and $\operatorname{dist}(e,D(\Lambda))\leq\rho_L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[a bad collar bond forces a witness at threshold $\theta_\vartheta$]\label{lem:c5-2-4}
Put $\theta_\vartheta:=\min(\varepsilon_0,\varepsilon_1/(4B_3N^{3/2}L^2),\vartheta\delta_0/(2C_Q))$, with $C_Q=(9K_L+2)(1+2B_3N^{3/2}L^2)$ the constant of \draftref{stage 3, \S7.1} ($k_N^3=N^{3/2}$ in the notation of the seed). Since $\vartheta\leq1$, $\theta_\vartheta\geq\vartheta\theta$ with $\theta=\min(\varepsilon_0,\varepsilon_1/(4B_3N^{3/2}L^2),\delta_0/(2C_Q))$ the threshold of \draftref{stage 3}. For $g_0$ below a threshold depending on $L$, $M_2$, the group, the source constants and $\vartheta$: on every labelled term and for every tested bond $e$ with $M_e=1$, some plaquette $p$ or some active mean-transport variable $w$ within the neighbourhood $N(\square')$ of the seed (the $6\ell$-neighbourhood of $\square'$, whose side $L^2M_2R_1\leq\ell/8$ by $M\geq8LM_2$; its sites are within $7\ell$ of the initial vertex of $e$, the gauge-label centres within $O(L)$ more, hence within $K\ell$, $K=256$) satisfies $|U(\partial p)-\id|_\sigma\geq\theta_\vartheta$ or $|w-\id|_\sigma\geq\theta_\vartheta$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
This is a re-run of the $R_1$-step of \draftref{stage 3, \S7.1} (the seed's approximate-fluctuation display, which is linear in the threshold) with $\delta_0$ replaced by $\vartheta\delta_0\leq\delta_0$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-3}(b), $|g_0A_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma\geq\vartheta\delta_0$ at the free bond $e$, and the unitary logarithm inequality gives $|\widetilde U(e)U_1(e)^{-1}-\id|\geq(2/\pi)\vartheta\delta_0$ for the gauge-fixed field $\widetilde U$. The comparison of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-3}(c) is $<\vartheta\delta_0/10$ for small coupling: $\varepsilon_1/\delta_0$ is bounded by fixed source constants (CRE (2.6), (2.8)), and $e^{-cLM_2R_1}\to0$ as $R_1\to\infty$. Hence $|\widetilde U(e)U_{1,\square'}(e)^{-1}-\id|\geq(2/\pi-1/10)\vartheta\delta_0>\vartheta\delta_0/2$. If every plaquette and every active gauge-variable witness in $N(\square')$ were below $\theta_\vartheta$, the two-block comparison of the seed would give $|\widetilde U(e)U_{1,\square'}(e)^{-1}-\id|\leq C_Q\theta_\vartheta\leq\vartheta\delta_0/2$, a contradiction. The smallness hypotheses of the seed ($\theta\leq\eta_0$, $2B_3k_N^3L^2\theta\leq\eta_0$, $k_N\theta\leq c_2'$, the VAR condition $2k_N^2L^2\theta\leq a_1$, the uniqueness class from $2k_N^2L^2\varepsilon_0$, quadratic term at most the linear term) are all upper bounds on the threshold and are preserved by $\theta_\vartheta\leq\theta$; the $P_0$, $P_1$ cutoffs at $\varepsilon_0$, $\varepsilon_1$ in the neighbourhood and the local determining-data problem are those of the branch, unchanged by the collar labels. The conclusion holds on the original averaging-constraint and earlier-cutoff support, which is the support on which it is used below.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the history weight of a retreated carrier]\label{lem:c5-2-5}
Let $\Lambda\subset\Lambda_1$ be a union of $\ell$-cubes, $D(\Lambda)\subset I$, $J(\Lambda)\supset J$; write $a\in\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}$ for the young variable of the retreated split and $A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}\in\mathfrak g^{J(\Lambda)}$ for its exterior variable, so $A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}=(A_{\mathrm e},a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)})$ in the original variables, and $A^{\Lambda,\Psi}:=\Psi_\xi(A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e})=(\Psi_\xi(A_{\mathrm e}),a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)})$, $\Psi_\xi$ being the identity on $I\setminus D(\Lambda)\subset I$. Keep $S_1$, $R_1$, $\Psi_\xi$, $U_1(\Omega_1\cap\Lambda_1^c,V)$ and $\chi(\Omega_1\cap\Lambda_1^c,S_1)$ as built from the original $\Lambda_1$ (determined by $(\Omega_1,R_1)$, hence recoverable from the labels); call a primitive near if its free-input set meets $D(\Lambda)$, far otherwise (near relative to $D(\Lambda)$ implies near relative to $I$). With $\mathscr E_\xi(B)=\mathscr E_\xi(0)-\tfrac12\langle B,H_\xi B\rangle+\sum_\alpha\mathfrak p_\alpha(B)$ the pre-boundary-change exponent of CRE (1.15) in the primitives of Section~\ref{sec:source-phase-primitives} (\eqref{eq:native-collar-full-exponent}), put
\begin{align*}
    c^\Lambda_\xi&:=\mathscr E_\xi(0)-\tfrac12\langle A^{\Lambda,\Psi},H_{J(\Lambda)J(\Lambda)}A^{\Lambda,\Psi}\rangle+\sum_{\alpha\ \mathrm{far}}\mathfrak p_\alpha(A^{\Lambda,\Psi})+\log|\det D\Psi_\xi(A_{\mathrm e})|\;,\\
    K^\Lambda_{\xi;1}(a)&:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(a)\exp\Bigl[-\tfrac12\langle a,H_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}a\rangle-\langle a,H_{D(\Lambda)J(\Lambda)}A^{\Lambda,\Psi}\rangle+\sum_{\alpha\ \mathrm{near}}\mathfrak p_\alpha(a+A^{\Lambda,\Psi})\Bigr]\;,
\end{align*}
where $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(a):=\mathbf 1\{\Psi_\xi(a+A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e})\in\mathcal A_\xi\}$ is the indicator of the young fibre of the retreated split (for $\Lambda=\Lambda_1$ it is $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D_\xi}(a)$ by the definition \eqref{eq:c-new-variable-domain} of $\mathcal D_\xi$). Then, pointwise,
\begin{equation*}
    e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}K^\Lambda_{\xi;1}(a)=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(a)|\det D\Psi_\xi(A_{\mathrm e})|\exp\mathscr E_\xi(\Psi_\xi(a+A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}))\;,
\end{equation*}
and the composed retained cutoffs and $\zeta$ multiply both sides. The box factors $\mathbf 1\{|a_b|_\sigma<r\}$ of the bonds $b\in I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ are factors of the retained cutoff (Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(ii), $I\subset\mathcal G_\xi$), now functions of exterior variables. For $\Lambda=\Lambda_1$ these are the $c_\xi$, $K_{\xi;1}$ and identity of Lemma~\ref{lem:collar}. ($|\det D\Psi_\xi(A_{\mathrm e})|=|\det D\Psi_\xi(A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e})|$, the determinant depending on the $S_1^*$-components only.)
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$\Psi_\xi(a+A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e})=a+A^{\Lambda,\Psi}$, because $\Psi_\xi$ is bondwise with support $S_1^*$ and $d(I,S_1^*)\geq\ell-2>0$ (\eqref{eq:c-collar-distance}), $D(\Lambda)\subset I$. Write $z=a+A^{\Lambda,\Psi}$. Expanding $-\tfrac12\langle z,H_\xi z\rangle$ into the $D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)$, $D(\Lambda)J(\Lambda)$, $J(\Lambda)J(\Lambda)$ blocks and splitting $\sum_\alpha\mathfrak p_\alpha(z)$ into near and far primitives, where a far primitive depends on $z$ only through $z|_{J(\Lambda)}=A^{\Lambda,\Psi}$ (its free-input set misses $D(\Lambda)$; Lemma~\ref{lem:row-locality}), gives $\mathscr E_\xi(z)=\mathscr E_\xi(0)-\tfrac12\langle a,H_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}a\rangle-\langle a,H_{D(\Lambda)J(\Lambda)}A^{\Lambda,\Psi}\rangle-\tfrac12\langle A^{\Lambda,\Psi},H_{J(\Lambda)J(\Lambda)}A^{\Lambda,\Psi}\rangle+\sum_{\mathrm{near}}\mathfrak p_\alpha(z)+\sum_{\mathrm{far}}\mathfrak p_\alpha(A^{\Lambda,\Psi})$; multiplying by $|\det D\Psi_\xi(A_{\mathrm e})|\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(a)$ gives the identity. The density $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}|\det D\Psi_\xi|\exp\mathscr E_\xi(\Psi_\xi(a+A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}))$ is the CRE (1.15) density in the transformed variables whatever free bonds are called interior; the split only decides which factors are collected in $c^\Lambda_\xi$ and which in $K^\Lambda_{\xi;1}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[normalised payment]\label{prop:c5-2-6}
On compatible even fine periods (the standing restriction of \draftref{stage 3, \S7}), for every bond $e$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-2-2}
    p_e^\vartheta\leq(\mu_0\otimes q_g^{\otimes\mathcal I}\otimes\mathrm{Haar}^{\otimes T})(W_e^\vartheta)\leq q_\vartheta:=n_W\max(z_P(\theta_\vartheta),z_G(\theta_\vartheta))\;,
\end{equation}
with $W_e^\vartheta:=\{\text{a plaquette or auxiliary gauge-variable witness }\geq\theta_\vartheta\text{ within }K\ell\text{ of }e\}$, the formulas of \draftref{stage 3} $z_P(\theta)=\exp[-b_G\theta^2/(4g_0^2)+4d_G\log g_0^{-1}+4\log c_H^{-1}+48]$, $z_G(\theta)=e^{1/2}(c_Hg_0^{d_G})^{-1}e^{-b_G\theta^2/g_0^2}$, $b_G=1/(2N)$, $n_W=7(2K\ell+7)^4$ ($\theta_\vartheta\leq\varepsilon_0$, as $z_G$ requires). Moreover, with $\pi_*=\min(p_0(g_0),p_0(g_1))$ and $\theta\geq c_\theta g_0\pi_*$ (\draftref{stage 3, \S7.3}, from CRE (2.6)--(2.9) and $\delta_0=(A_1/A_0)\varepsilon_0$),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-2-3}
    q_\vartheta\leq\exp[-c_q\vartheta^2\pi_*^2]\;,\qquad c_q:=b_Gc_\theta^2/8\;,\qquad\text{for }0<g_0<g_*(\vartheta)\;.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix a history and a labelled term. By Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5} (with $\Lambda=\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$ of the term) its weight $\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi$ is the integral of the CRE (1.15) density in transformed variables, times the term's collar indicators, and the mark $M_e$ is a nonnegative function of the labels and of $U$. The reversal of \draftref{stage 3, \S7.2} is a change of variables for integrals of (density $\times$ nonnegative function of $U$ and the labels), so it applies to $\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi M_e$ exactly as it applies to $\mathsf w_\xi\mathbf 1\{z\in E(B_\xi)\}$ there: undo $\Psi_\xi$, the constraint elimination (it changes designated bonds only; $e$ is free), the scaling, the linearisation, the chart and the translation by $U_1$. After the reversal $M_e$ equals $\mathbf 1\{e\in T(\mathrm{labels})\}\cdot\mathbf 1\{|\tfrac1i\log(\widetilde U(e)U_1(\Omega_1,V)(e)^{-1})|_\sigma\geq\vartheta\delta_0\}$ ($\Psi_\xi$ is the identity at $e$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-3}(a); the relevant background is $U_1(\Omega_1,V)$, as $e\notin S_1^*$). By Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-4}, $M_e\leq\mathbf 1_{W_e^\vartheta}$ on the support of the term's cutoffs, and $W_e^\vartheta$ is label-free (independent $q_g$ variables are added for inactive indices, Haar variables for centres and unused sites, as in \draftref{stage 3, \S7.2}). All labelled terms are nonnegative and their partitions ($P_0$, $P_1$, $Q_1$, $R_1$ and the collar labels, Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(c)) sum to one, so $\sum_{\mathrm{hist}}\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}(\text{term}\cdot M_e)\leq\sum_{\mathrm{hist}}\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}(\text{term}\cdot\mathbf 1_{W_e^\vartheta})\leq(\mu_0\otimes q_g^{\otimes\mathcal I}\otimes\mathrm{Haar}^{\otimes T})(W_e^\vartheta)\cdot\mathcal Z_0$ (\draftref{stage 3, \S\S7.2--7.3}; only ``$\leq$'' is needed), which gives the first inequality of \eqref{eq:c5-2-2}; the second is the union bound of \draftref{stage 3, \S7.3} over the $n_W$ witness labels with the single-witness tails $z_P$, $z_G$ at threshold $\theta_\vartheta$. For \eqref{eq:c5-2-3}: $\theta_\vartheta/g_0\geq\vartheta\theta/g_0\geq\vartheta c_\theta\pi_*$, so $-\log z_P(\theta_\vartheta)\geq b_Gc_\theta^2\vartheta^2\pi_*^2/4-4d_G\log g_0^{-1}-C$ and $-\log z_G(\theta_\vartheta)\geq b_Gc_\theta^2\vartheta^2\pi_*^2-d_G\log g_0^{-1}-C$; $\log n_W\leq C\log\log g_0^{-2}$ since $\ell\leq L^2M(2\log g_0^{-2})^{\mathfrak r}$ (from CRE (2.5)--(2.6), Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}); and $\pi_*^2\geq A_0^2(\tfrac12\log g_0^{-2})^{2p_0}$ with $p_0\geq5\mathfrak r\geq10$ dominates every logarithm for small $g_0$, leaving the exponent $b_Gc_\theta^2\vartheta^2\pi_*^2/8$.
\end{proof}

The Wilson insertion does not enter $p_e^\vartheta$: as in Appendix~\ref{app:exterior-chessboard}, the insertion is bounded inside the conditional estimate ($|\mathcal B_\xi[f_\xi]|\leq C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}g_0rD_\xi$ on covered branches, \draftref{stage 3, \S5}), and the history average of $D_\xi$ is insertion-free.

\subsection{The retreat, its termination and its costs}
\label{sec:c5-3}

Cubes are the aligned $\ell$-cubes of CRE (1.10), (1.20); $\Lambda_1$ is a union of such cubes, $D=D(\Lambda_1)$, $J=J(\Lambda_1)$, $J_{\mathrm{col}}$ as in Section~\ref{sec:c5-2-3}; $\vartheta$ as in Remark~\ref{rem:c5-1-3} ($\vartheta\leq1/16$). All tests use $|\cdot|_\sigma$.

\subsubsection*{The collar partition, the retreat and the filling}
\label{sec:c5-3-1}
Connected components of unions of $\ell$-cubes are taken with respect to a fixed adjacency of $\ell$-cubes (CRE's; the arguments below use only that a component of $X\cup Y$ is either a component of $X$ not adjacent to $Y$ or a set meeting $Y$). $\operatorname{Fill}_1(\Lambda)$ denotes one pass of CRE's modification after (1.20) (p.251 L6--14): every connected component of $\Lambda^c$ contained in a cube of side $100\ell$ is replaced by the smallest rectangular parallelepiped of $\ell$-cubes containing it; $\operatorname{Fill}(\Lambda)$ is the fixed point of iterating $\operatorname{Fill}_1$ (each pass removes at least one cube from $\Lambda$ or stops, so the iteration ends after finitely many passes). A region $\Lambda$ is filled if $\operatorname{Fill}_1(\Lambda)=\Lambda$, i.e. every component of $\Lambda^c$ contained in a cube of side $100\ell$ is a rectangular parallelepiped; this is CRE's condition (2.3) (p.255 L28--29, $j=1$: $100MR_1$ in the $L^{-1}$-lattice is $100\ell$ fine sites; the side-$100\ell$ version is used so that filled regions satisfy (2.3) literally, while p.251 fills components in cubes of side $<100\ell$). Adjacency: two $\ell$-cubes are adjacent when they share a face, so that a rectangular parallelepiped of $\ell$-cubes is connected (used in the equivalence ``filled $\Leftrightarrow\operatorname{Fill}_1(\Lambda)=\Lambda$''); CRE p.251 does not specify its adjacency, and the proofs below hold for any symmetric adjacency under which parallelepipeds are connected. Standing hypothesis of this section, additional to \eqref{eq:c-standing-conditions} (every fine period a multiple of $LMR_1$ and at least $8M$) and to the compatible even fine periods of Appendix~\ref{app:exterior-chessboard}: every fine period exceeds $200\ell$. It is coupling-dependent through $\ell=LMR_1(g_0)$ (on a fixed torus it fails as $g_0\to0$), so every statement using it is for periods tending to infinity at fixed small $g_0$. Under it a set contained in a cube of side $100\ell$ lifts isometrically to $\mathbb Z^4$, and its smallest containing parallelepiped of $\ell$-cubes is well defined. Whether CRE's $\Lambda_1$ is filled is not used: round $0$ fills it.

\begin{lemma}[round $0$]\label{lem:c5-3-0}
Put $\Lambda_1^{(0)}:=\operatorname{Fill}(\Lambda_1)$ and $\mathfrak B_0:=(\Lambda_1^{(0)})^c\supset\Lambda_1^c$ (a set of sites; the bad bond sets of the labels are $B^{(k)}$). (a) $\Lambda_1^{(0)}\subset\Lambda_1$ is a filled union of $\ell$-cubes, determined by $(\Omega_1,R_1)$ like $\Lambda_1$, so label-independent; if $\Lambda_1$ already satisfies (2.3) then $\Lambda_1^{(0)}=\Lambda_1$. (b) Every point of $\mathfrak B_0$ is within $100\ell$ of a point of the unfilled bad region $Q_1'^{\sim4}\cup(B(P_1^1)^c)^{\sim5}\cup R_1'^{\sim1}$ of CRE (1.10), (1.20), so the witness argument of \draftref{stage 3, \S7.1} applies to $\mathfrak B_0$ without change: every $z\in E(\mathfrak B_0)$ forces a witness $\geq\theta$ within $K\ell$ of $z$, $K=256$, with the same $n_W$, and $p_z^{(0)}:=\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O[\mathsf w_\xi\mathbf 1\{z\in E(\mathfrak B_0)\}](V)\,\dd V\leq q$ on compatible even fine periods.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) is the definition ($\operatorname{Fill}(\Lambda)\subset\Lambda$, and the output is filled). (b) The induction of \draftref{stage 3, \S7.1} (every current component contains an original bad point, and each fill is performed inside a cube of side less than $100\ell$ containing that component; this also holds if small merged components are filled again) is run with CRE's p.251 fill as the base and the passes of $\operatorname{Fill}_1$ as further steps: a $\operatorname{Fill}_1$ pass changes only components contained in a cube of side $100\ell$, each containing an original bad point, and two points of such a cube are within $100\ell-1<100\ell$ of each other, so every added point is within $100\ell$ of an original bad point of its component; mergers preserve the presence of an original point. A point of the unfilled region is within $10\ell$ of a bad test (\draftref{stage 3, \S7.1}), whose witness lies in the neighbourhood $N(Q)$ of the test cube ($6\ell$, or the defining enlargement of $P_0$), within $7\ell$ of it; so the witness is within $117\ell+O(L)+1<K\ell$ of $z$. The mark $\mathbf 1\{z\in E(\mathfrak B_0)\}$ depends on the regions only, so \draftref{stage 3, \S\S7.2--7.3} apply verbatim with the same $n_W$, giving $p_z^{(0)}\leq q$.
\end{proof}

On a labelled term with current region $\Lambda_1^{(k)}$ ($k=0$: $\Lambda_1^{(0)}=\operatorname{Fill}(\Lambda_1)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}; the bonds of $D\setminus D^{(0)}$ are exterior on every term, with their box factors as exterior good tests, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}) insert, inside the $a$-independent part of the integrand, $1=\prod_e[\mathbf 1\{|A_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma<\vartheta r\}+\mathbf 1\{|A_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma\geq\vartheta r\}]$ over the untested bonds $e$ of $J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda_1^{(k)})$ (collar bonds have $A'_{\mathrm e}(e)=A_{\mathrm e}(e)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-3}(a), so the factors are $a$-independent, and they remain exterior factors on every later term, since $J(\Lambda)$ only grows as $\Lambda$ shrinks), and expand. A term is labelled by its bad set $B^{(k)}$. If $B^{(k)}=\varnothing$ the iteration stops with $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}=\Lambda_1^{(k)}$. Otherwise put
\begin{equation*}
    \Lambda_1^{(k+1)}:=\operatorname{Fill}\Bigl(\Lambda_1^{(k)}\setminus\bigcup_{e\in B^{(k)}}\mathcal Q(e)\Bigr)\;,\qquad\mathcal Q(e):=\text{the union of the $\ell$-cubes meeting the closed $\ell^\infty$-ball of radius $\rho_L+1$ around the initial site $x_e$ of $e$.}
\end{equation*}
Since $2(\rho_L+1)+1=8L+3<\ell$ (the standing condition $LMR_1>4R_{\mathrm c}+4L^2M_2R_1+8$ of \eqref{eq:c-standing-conditions}), $\mathcal Q(e)$ consists of at most $2^4=16$ cubes and every point of $\mathcal Q(e)$ is within $\ell+\rho_L+1\leq2\ell$ of $x_e$. The bonds of $D^{(k)}\setminus D^{(k+1)}$ become exterior variables of the term; they are the same bond variables as before (the chart CRE (1.13)--(1.16) and $U_1$ depend on $\Omega_1$ and $V$, not on $\Lambda_1$: CRE (1.12)--(1.16) are defined on $\Omega_1$, and $\Lambda_1$ first enters in (1.20)), and their box factors $\mathbf 1\{|a_b|_\sigma<r\}$ become exterior good tests (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}). Convention (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}): $S_1$, $R_1$, $\Psi_\xi$, $U_1(\Omega_1\cap\Lambda_1^c,V)$ and $\chi(\Omega_1\cap\Lambda_1^c,S_1)$ are those of the original $\Lambda_1$ throughout (consistent, since $\Omega_1\cap\Lambda_1^c\subset\Omega_1\cap(\Lambda_1^{(k)})^c$); the retreated term is the same integral with a different split of the free bonds into integrated and exterior ones, and $\Psi_\xi$ remains an invertible change of variables with its Jacobian retained (\eqref{eq:retained-change-domain}). The weight $\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi$ of Section~\ref{sec:c5-2-3} is the integral of $\rho_\xi\times$(the term's indicators) over all of $\mathfrak g^I$, the variables of $I\setminus D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ being integrated as exterior variables of the re-split.

\begin{lemma}[termination, final state, counts]\label{lem:c5-3-1}
(a) The iteration stops after at most $|\Lambda_1|/\ell^4$ rounds with $B^{(k)}\neq\varnothing$, followed by one terminal round. (b) On every final term, every $e\in J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})$ is good: $|A_{\mathrm e}(e)|_\sigma<\vartheta r$. (c) Every region $\Lambda_1^{(k)}$ is filled, and every point of $\Lambda_1^{(0)}\setminus\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$ is within $102\ell$ of $x_e$ for some bond $e$ found bad on the term. (d) The bonds with an endpoint within $102\ell$ of a given site number at most $N_{\mathrm{ret}}:=4(207\ell)^4$; hence at most $N_{\mathrm{ret}}$ bonds are removed on account of one bad bond $e$, and for a given bond $z$ at most $N_{\mathrm{ret}}$ bonds $e$ have $x_e$ within $102\ell+1$ of an endpoint of $z$ (that count is $4(204\ell+4)(204\ell+3)^3\leq N_{\mathrm{ret}}$ for $\ell\geq2$).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) A bad $e\in J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda_1^{(k)})$ has $\operatorname{dist}(e,D^{(k)})\leq\rho_L$, so some bond of $D^{(k)}$ has an endpoint in $\Lambda_1^{(k)}$ within $\rho_L+1$ of $x_e$; the cube containing that endpoint lies in $\mathcal Q(e)\cap\Lambda_1^{(k)}$ and is removed. So each non-terminal round removes at least one cube.
```
