(b) $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}=\Lambda_1^{(k)}$ for the terminal round $k$, whose untested collar bonds were all good; the collar bonds tested earlier were either good (the same variable, still good) or bad, and a bad bond $e$ cannot lie in $J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})$: $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset\Lambda_1^{(j)}\setminus\mathcal Q(e)$ for the round $j$ in which $e$ was found bad ($\operatorname{Fill}$ only removes cubes), so every $b\in D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ has an endpoint in $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$, disjoint from the closed ball of radius $\rho_L+1$ around $x_e$; if that endpoint is $x_b$ then $|x_b-x_e|_\infty\geq\rho_L+2$, and if it is $x_b+e_\mu$ then $|x_b-x_e|_\infty\geq\rho_L+1$; in both cases $\operatorname{dist}(b,e)>\rho_L$. (c) Induction over the rounds and over the passes of $\operatorname{Fill}$, with the invariant: the current region is filled, and every point removed so far lies within $100\ell$ of a point of $\bigcup\mathcal Q(e)$ over the bonds $e$ found bad so far. The invariant holds for $\Lambda_1^{(0)}$ (filled by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}(a); nothing removed yet). In a round, removing the cubes of $\mathcal Q(B^{(k)})$ gives a complement whose components are either components of $(\Lambda_1^{(k)})^c$ not adjacent to any removed cube, or sets meeting $\bigcup_{e\in B^{(k)}}\mathcal Q(e)$. A component of the first kind contained in a cube of side $100\ell$ is a parallelepiped ($\Lambda_1^{(k)}$ is filled), so a $\operatorname{Fill}_1$ pass does not change it; a pass changes only components of the second kind, each inside a cube of side $100\ell$ that contains a point of some $\mathcal Q(e)$, so every point added lies within $100\ell$ of that point. After the pass the components are again either untouched components of the first kind or sets meeting $\bigcup\mathcal Q(e)$ (a merger involving a filled hull contains a $\mathcal Q$-point). Iterating to the fixed point keeps the invariant, and the output is filled by definition. Since every point of $\mathcal Q(e)$ is within $2\ell$ of $x_e$, every removed point is within $102\ell$ of $x_e$ for some bad $e$. (d) Sites within $102\ell$ of a given site number at most $(204\ell+1)^4$; the bonds with an endpoint among them have their initial site within $102\ell+1$, at most $4(204\ell+3)^4\leq4(207\ell)^4$ bonds. Sites within $102\ell+1$ of one of the two endpoints of $z$ lie in a box of $(204\ell+4)(204\ell+3)^3$ sites, each the initial site of four bonds, and $204\ell+4\leq207\ell$ for $\ell\geq2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[what the final state gives]\label{rem:c5-3-2}
On a final term every $e\in J^{\mathrm{fin}}$ within $\rho_L$ of $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ is good (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(b)) and $A'_{\mathrm e}(e)=A_{\mathrm e}(e)$ there (for $e\in J$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-3}(a); for $e\in I\setminus D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ because $I\cap S_1^*=\varnothing$), so \eqref{eq:c5-1-2} gives $\sup_{b\in D^{\mathrm{fin}}}|m_b|_\sigma\leq r/4$ with the same $C_H^{\mathrm{near}}$ and $\vartheta$: (I1) is unchanged, (I2) is a statement about the rows $b\in I\supset D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ with the sum over all $b'$, and (I3) passes to $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ by principal restriction. This is the input for Sections~\ref{sec:c5-4} and~\ref{sec:c5-5}, with margin $3r/4$.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[the regions and the retreated fibre]\label{lem:c5-3-3}
(i) $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset\Lambda_1^{(0)}\subset\Lambda_1\subset\Omega_1^{\sim-2}\cap(R_1'^\sim)^c$ is a filled union of $\ell$-cubes (Lemmas~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}(a), \ref{lem:c5-3-1}(c)), so it satisfies CRE (2.3) with the adjacency of Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-1}. (ii) Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(i) (every site within $\ell-1$ of $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$ lies in $\Omega_1^{\sim-1}\setminus R_1'$; every free bond of $\Omega_1$ within $3R_{\mathrm c}$ of $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ lies in $\mathcal G_\xi\setminus S_1^*$) and \eqref{eq:c-collar-distance} ($d(D^{\mathrm{fin}},S_1^*)\geq\ell-2$) hold by the inclusions $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset\Lambda_1$, $D^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset I$. (iii) $|A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm e}(b)|_\sigma<r$ for every $b\in J^{\mathrm{fin}}$ with $d(b,D^{\mathrm{fin}})\leq3R_{\mathrm c}$: for $b\in J$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(iii) ($d(b,I)\leq d(b,D^{\mathrm{fin}})$), for $b\in I\setminus D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ by the retained box factor $\mathbf 1\{|a_b|_\sigma<r\}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}), nonzero on the term. (iv) On a final term with exterior data $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm e}$ for which the young fibre $\{a\in\mathfrak g^{D^{\mathrm{fin}}}\, | \,\Psi_\xi(a+A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm e})\in\mathcal A_\xi\}$ is nonempty, the fibre is the open box $\{a\, | \,|a_b|_\sigma<r\text{ for all }b\in D^{\mathrm{fin}}\}$ (otherwise it is empty and the term vanishes). $\Omega_1$, $R_1$, $S_1$, $\Psi_\xi$ and $U_1$ are those of the original $\Lambda_1$ by the convention of Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-1}. The history label acquires the finite sequence $(B^{(0)},B^{(1)},\dots)$ of bad sets; its bookkeeping in the history sum of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} is Section~\ref{sec:c5-6}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}[Proof of (iv)]
The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(iv) is re-run with $I$ replaced by $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$; its inputs are checked one by one. Let $a_0$ be a point of the fibre (nonempty on a term that survives) and $a$ any point of the box; $B_0=\Psi_\xi(a_0+A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm e})\in\mathcal A_\xi$ and $B=a+\Psi_\xi(A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm e})$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}). The cutoff CRE (1.25) at $B$ factorises as $\Theta_{D^{\mathrm{fin}}}(a)\times$(a function of $\Psi_\xi(A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm e})$ and the branch data): the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(ii) uses only $D^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset\mathcal G_\xi$, the bondwise identity $\Psi_\xi(a+A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm e})=a+\Psi_\xi(A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm e})$, and that the bonds of the factors $\chi^{(0)c}(R_1)$, $\chi_0'(S_1)$ have no endpoint in $\Lambda_1\supset\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$; so (1.25) at $B$ equals (1.25) at $B_0$ with $\Theta_{D^{\mathrm{fin}}}(a_0)=1$ replaced by $\Theta_{D^{\mathrm{fin}}}(a)=1$, nonzero. Every row equation has a small solution at $g_0\mathsf C_UB$: on the rows within $2R_{\mathrm c}+2L$ of $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ (near rows for $I$ as well, $D^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset I$) by Lemma~\ref{lem:charts}, whose polydisc condition $|B(b)|<2+4r$ on $\mathcal E_c$ holds by (iii) and $|a_b|<r$ (the bonds of $\mathcal E_c$ are within $2R_{\mathrm c}+4L-1\leq3R_{\mathrm c}$ of $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$); on the other rows by Lemma~\ref{lem:row-locality}, since $\mathcal E_c$ misses $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ and $B=B_0$ off $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$, where the small solution exists because $B_0\in\mathcal A_\xi$. The constraint $\mathsf M(U(B))=V$ on every row and the restriction $V_0$ off $\Omega_1$ follow as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(iv) (they use the row equations only). The tests of CRE (1.11) at $U(B)$: $\chi_1(\Omega_1)$ (a function of $V$) and $\zeta(\Omega_1^c)$ (\eqref{eq:c-large-field-factor}) do not depend on $a$, so they take at $U(B)$ their values at $U(B_0)$, which are nonzero; $\chi_0'(S_1)$ is nonzero by the first condition; every other cube $\square'\subset\Omega_1$ lies in $\Omega_1^{\sim-1}\setminus R_1$ and its factor is the second factor of (1.19), equal to one by (B3)(ii). Hence $B\in\mathcal A_\xi$. Conversely a point of the fibre has nonzero (1.25), hence $\Theta_{D^{\mathrm{fin}}}(a)=1$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{Costs of the retreat and the comparison}
\label{sec:c5-3-4}
(a) Coverage and attachment. Appendix~\ref{app:exterior-chessboard} defines $D_\xi=\sum_{b\in I_C}e^{-\mu d(b,J)/2}$ (\draftref{stage 3, \S5}), with $I_C\subset I$ the young bonds in the free-input stencils of the contour traversals, and the covered event $A_{\mathrm{cov}}$ (contour bonds in $\Omega_1$, free inputs in $I$; \draftref{stage 3, \S6}). For a final term with carrier $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ the corresponding quantities are $D^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi:=\sum_{b\in I_C\cap D^{\mathrm{fin}}}e^{-\mu d(b,J^{\mathrm{fin}})/2}$ and $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}:=\{\text{all contour bonds lie in }\Omega_1\text{ and every free input lies in }D^{\mathrm{fin}}\}$; both depend on the labels only through the regions. Put $B_\xi:=(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})^c$. Pointwise on the term, with $c=\mu/2$,
\begin{equation*}
    D^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi\leq\sum_{b\in\Gamma_C}\sum_ze^{-cd(b,z)}\mathbf 1\{z\in E(B_\xi)\}\;,\qquad\mathbf 1_{(A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}})^c}\leq\sum_{b\in\Gamma_C}\mathbf 1\{b\in E(B_\xi)\}:
\end{equation*}
a free bond not in $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ has no endpoint in $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$, so $J^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset E(B_\xi)$, and each $b\in I_C\cap D^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset\Gamma_C$ contributes $e^{-cd(b,J^{\mathrm{fin}})}\leq\sum_{z\in J^{\mathrm{fin}}}e^{-cd(b,z)}$; a contour bond outside $\Omega_1$ or a free input outside $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ meets $B_\xi$ (the argument of \draftref{stage 3, \S7} with $I$ replaced by $D^{\mathrm{fin}}$). These two inequalities are combinatorial. The statement that the response of the retreated conditional integral is bounded by $C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}g_0rD^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi$ is the conditional estimate for the retreated carrier (Section~\ref{sec:c5-4}, with Remark~\ref{rem:c5-3-2} and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-3} as its inputs). For the history average: by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(c)--(d), on every final term
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-3-mark-inequality}
    \mathbf 1\{z\in E(B_\xi)\}\leq\mathbf 1\{z\in E(\mathfrak B_0)\}+\sum_{e\, : \,x_e\text{ within }102\ell+1\text{ of an endpoint of }z}M_e
\end{equation}
(a bond $z$ meets $(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})^c$ either because it meets $\mathfrak B_0=(\Lambda_1^{(0)})^c$ or because one of its endpoints lies in $\Lambda_1^{(0)}\setminus\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$, which is within $102\ell$ of $x_e$ for a bond $e$ found bad on the term, $M_e=1$). Multiply by $\rho_\xi\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}$, integrate over $\mathfrak g^I$, sum over labels and histories, apply $\mathcal O$ and divide by $\mathcal Z_0$ (all integrands nonnegative, finitely many labels per history, Tonelli): the first term is label-independent, $\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}=1$ reduces it to $\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O[\mathsf w_\xi\mathbf 1\{z\in E(\mathfrak B_0)\}]\,\dd V=p_z^{(0)}\leq q$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}(b)); the second gives $\sum_{e\text{ near }z}p_e^\vartheta\leq N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(d), Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-2-6}). Hence, on compatible even fine periods exceeding $200\ell$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-3-coverage-average}
    \mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O\Bigl[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi D^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi\Bigr]\dd V\leq C_LS_cn_C(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)\;,\qquad\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O\Bigl[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi\mathbf 1_{(A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}})^c}\Bigr]\dd V\leq C_Ln_C(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)\;.
\end{equation}
This is the bound of \draftref{stage 3, \S7} with $q$ replaced by $q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta$, which has the same normalised form. The scalar $\kappa_C$ of \eqref{eq:extracted-loop-scalar} does not enter the history average: the ratio $\kappa_C^{-1}\mathcal B_\xi[f_\xi]$ is formed in Section~\ref{sec:c5-7} from the response bound and this average.

(b) Boundary activities. The bonds that change status are $D\setminus D^{(0)}$ (label-independent, round $0$, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}) and, on a final term, $D^{(0)}\setminus D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ (young in the original split, exterior on the term): at most $N_{\mathrm{ret}}$ of the latter per bad bond $e$, each with an endpoint within $102\ell$ of $x_e$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(c)--(d)), and $p_e^\vartheta\leq q_\vartheta$. How the connected boundary terms of the retreated conditional integral are localised relative to these bonds, and their bound, are Section~\ref{sec:c5-5}. If that cost on a term is a sum over the bonds $b\in D^{(0)}\setminus D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ of quantities bounded by $Ce^{-cd(b,\Gamma_C)}$ with $C$, $c$ independent of the labels, the fields and $r$, then it is linear in the marks, (c) applies, and its history average is at most $CN_{\mathrm{ret}}\sum_be^{-cd(b,\Gamma_C)}\sup_ep_e^\vartheta\leq CN_{\mathrm{ret}}S_cC_Ln_Cq_\vartheta$, uniform in the volume; without such a localisation the sum over all testable bonds is proportional to the volume, and if the cost is multiplicative in the bad bonds, (c) does not apply. The bonds $D\setminus D^{(0)}$ carry no marks; they form the label-independent boundary of $\Lambda_1^{(0)}$ and are treated in Section~\ref{sec:c5-5} in the same way as the original boundary of $\Lambda_1$.

(c) No label entropy for the coverage cost. The collar labels are a partition of unity on each term: $\sum_{B^{(k)}}\prod_{e\in B^{(k)}}\mathbf 1\{e\text{ bad}\}\prod_{e\notin B^{(k)}}\mathbf 1\{e\text{ good}\}=1$ pointwise, round by round, so by induction from the terminal rounds (termination: Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(a)) $\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}=1$ pointwise on $\mathfrak g^I\times\mathfrak g^J$, and $\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi=\int_{\mathfrak g^I}\rho_\xi\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}=\mathsf w_\xi$ (Tonelli; the labels are indicators of finitely many measurable sets). Every term is nonnegative and the cost in (a) is bounded by $\sum_eM_e\times$(a local quantity), linear in the marks; summing the labelled terms back therefore gives $\sum_eM_e\times$(local quantity) under the history average, with no product over labels and no factor depending on the number of tested bonds. This is established for the cost (a) only; for (b) see the last sentence there.

Comparison. $r=\delta_0/g_0=A_1(\log g_0^{-2})^{p_0}$ (CRE p.246 L14, p.247: $\varepsilon_0=g_0A_0(\log g_0^{-2})^{p_0}$, $\delta_0=(A_1/A_0)\varepsilon_0$; $r$ is not used in \eqref{eq:c5-3-1}--\eqref{eq:c5-3-2}). CRE (2.6) (p.255 L45--46, $n=1$, $m=0$): $g_1\leq(1+\beta_0)g_0$ and $g_0\leq(1+\beta_0)g_1$, so for small $g_0$, $\tfrac12\log g_0^{-2}\leq\log g_1^{-2}\leq2\log g_0^{-2}$. $R_1$ is the smallest power of $L$ with $R_1\geq(\log g_1^{-2})^{\mathfrak r}$ (p.246 L25), so $R_1\leq L(\log g_1^{-2})^{\mathfrak r}\leq L(2\log g_0^{-2})^{\mathfrak r}$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-3-1}
    \ell=LMR_1\leq L^2M(2\log g_0^{-2})^{\mathfrak r}\;,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\log N_{\mathrm{ret}}\leq C+4\mathfrak r\log(2\log g_0^{-2})\qquad\text{and}\qquad C\ell^4\leq C(L^2M)^42^{4\mathfrak r}(\log g_0^{-2})^{4\mathfrak r}\;.
\end{equation}
With $\pi_*\geq A_0(\tfrac12\log g_0^{-2})^{p_0}$ and $p_0\geq5\mathfrak r$ (p.246 L14),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-3-2}
    \log(1/q_\vartheta)\geq c_q\vartheta^2A_0^22^{-2p_0}(\log g_0^{-2})^{2p_0}\gg C(L^2M)^42^{4\mathfrak r}(\log g_0^{-2})^{4\mathfrak r}\geq C\ell^4\;,
\end{equation}
since $2p_0\geq10\mathfrak r>4\mathfrak r$. Hence for $g_0$ below a threshold depending on $L$, $M$, $M_2$, the group, the source constants and $\vartheta$, $N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta\leq e^{-c_q\vartheta^2\pi_*^2/2}$; and for each $C$ independent of $g_0$ there is a threshold, depending also on $C$, below which $e^{C\ell^4}q_\vartheta\leq e^{-c_q\vartheta^2\pi_*^2/2}$ ($C$ a constant of $L$, $M$, $M_2$, the group and the source constants; a per-term cost growing with $r$, say like $r^2\propto(\log g_0^{-2})^{2p_0}$, is not covered by this comparison and would have to be paid differently). This is CRE's $p_0\geq5\mathfrak r$ accounting with the present constants.

\subsection{The selected majorant for the translated box}
\label{sec:c5-4}

Fix a history and a labelled term with final region $\Lambda:=\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$ (a union of $\ell$-cubes, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}), carrier $D:=D(\Lambda)=D^{\mathrm{fin}}\subset I$, exterior $J(\Lambda)=E_{\Omega,*}\setminus D$ and exterior variable $A^{\Lambda,\Psi}=(\Psi_\xi(A_{\mathrm e}),a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D})$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}). Write $H:=H_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}$, $\ell_\Lambda:=H_{D(\Lambda)J(\Lambda)}A^{\Lambda,\Psi}\in\mathfrak g^D$ and, for $t\in[0,1]$,
\begin{equation*}
    P^\Lambda_t(a):=\sum_{\mathfrak p\text{ near}}\mathfrak p(a+tA^{\Lambda,\Psi})\;,\qquad K^\Lambda_{\xi;t}(a):=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(a)\exp\Bigl[-\tfrac12\langle a,Ha\rangle-t\langle a,\ell_\Lambda\rangle+P^\Lambda_t(a)\Bigr]\;,\qquad I^\Lambda_t:=\int_{\mathfrak g^D}K^\Lambda_{\xi;t}\,\dd a\;,\qquad\dd\nu^\Lambda_t:=K^\Lambda_{\xi;t}\,\dd a/I^\Lambda_t\;,
\end{equation*}
the retreated split's version of the exterior interpolation \eqref{eq:native-source-boundary-interpolation}: near means near relative to $D(\Lambda)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}), $\mathfrak p$ runs over the near primitives of all three kinds, and for $\Lambda=\Lambda_1$ these are the $P_{\xi,t}$, $K_{\xi;t}$, $I_{\xi;t}$, $\nu_{\xi;t}$ of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}; at $t=1$, $K^\Lambda_{\xi;1}$ is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}. On the support of the term's exterior cutoffs (the branch indicator $\iota_\xi$, the collar indicators and the box factors on $I\setminus D(\Lambda)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}) the retreated young fibre is nonempty and is the box: by Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(iv) the original fibre over the branch data is the box over $I$, and the box factors on $I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ place $(a,a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)})$ in it for every $a$ with $|a_b|_\sigma<r$, $b\in D(\Lambda)$, so the fibre of the retreated split over $A^{\Lambda,\Psi}$ is nonempty and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-3}(iv) gives $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(a)=\Theta_D(a):=\prod_{b\in D}\chi_b(a)$, $\chi_b(a):=\mathbf 1\{|a_b|_\sigma<r\}$; off that support the term vanishes, and nothing is claimed for it. (This nonemptiness is used for $I^\Lambda_t>0$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c), for $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(B+tm)=\Theta^t(B)$ in \eqref{eq:c5-4-2}, and for the identity hard cutoff in Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-4}.) The mean shift of the retreated split is $m:=-H^{-1}\ell_\Lambda$ (Section~\ref{sec:c5-1} with $D(\Lambda)$, $J(\Lambda)$), and by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-2} (its proof uses (I1)--(I3) for $H_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}$ only, which hold by principal restriction, Section~\ref{sec:c5-0}) $m$ depends on $A^{\Lambda,\Psi}|_{J_1(\Lambda)}$ only, $J_1(\Lambda)\subset J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda)$. The final collar is good (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(b)), $|A^{\Lambda,\Psi}_{\mathrm e}|_\sigma=|A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}|_\sigma<\vartheta r$ for every $e\in J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda)$ ($\Psi_\xi$ is the identity there, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-3}(a); on $I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ by \eqref{eq:c-collar-distance}), so \eqref{eq:c5-1-1} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-4-1}
    \sup_{b\in D(\Lambda)}|m_b|_\sigma\leq r/4\;,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\sup_bt|m_b|_\sigma\leq r/4\quad\text{for every }t\in[0,1].
\end{equation}
Completing the square (Section~\ref{sec:c5-1}): $-\tfrac12\langle a,Ha\rangle-t\langle a,\ell_\Lambda\rangle=-\tfrac12\langle a-tm,H(a-tm)\rangle+\tfrac12t^2\langle m,Hm\rangle$.

\begin{lemma}[the final term is a branch for the estimate lemmas]\label{lem:c5-4-0}
(a) $H=H_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}$ satisfies \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-inputs}, $\gamma\,\id\leq H\leq h_+\id$ with $\gamma=\gamma_L/2$, $h_+=m_L$, and the entry range $\rho_L\leq R$: this is (I1), (I3) for the principal restriction (Section~\ref{sec:c5-0}). Hence Lemmas~\ref{lem:walk-series}, \ref{lem:conditioning}, \ref{lem:component-blocks} and~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment} hold for $H$ with the constants $n_*$, $R_*$, $C_w$, $\delta_M$, $A_*$, $C_d$, $\alpha_G$ of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} and Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} (these lemmas use only \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-inputs}, the range and the partitions, Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-reference}). (b) Retained inputs near the carrier. Let $r^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}:=\max\{|A^{\Lambda,\Psi}(b)|_\sigma\, : \,b\in J(\Lambda),\ \operatorname{dist}(b,D(\Lambda))\leq3R_{\mathrm c}\}$ (zero if the set is empty), the retreated split's $r_{\mathrm e,\xi}$ (\draftref{stage 3, \S1}). Then $r^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}<r$: a bond $b\in J$ within $3R_{\mathrm c}$ of $D(\Lambda)\subset I$ is within $3R_{\mathrm c}$ of $I$, so by Lemma~\ref{lem:collar-coverage}(i),(iii) for the original term it lies in $\mathcal G_\xi\setminus S_1^*$, $\Psi_\xi$ is the identity at $b$ and $|A_{\mathrm e}(b)|_\sigma<r$ on the branch; a bond $b\in I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ carries $a_{\mathrm{old}}(b)$ with $|a_{\mathrm{old}}(b)|_\sigma<r$ (its box factor is a factor of the retained cutoff of the term, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}) and $\Psi_\xi$ is the identity on $I$ (\eqref{eq:c-collar-distance}). So $s^\Lambda:=1+r+r^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}\leq1+2r$. On the collar $J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda)$ (range $\rho_L=4L\leq3R_{\mathrm c}$) the sharper bound $<\vartheta r$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(b) holds; it is used only through \eqref{eq:c5-4-1}. (c) Charts and primitive bounds. A row within $2R_{\mathrm c}+2L$ of $D(\Lambda)$ is within $2R_{\mathrm c}+2L$ of $I$, so the applicability paragraph of Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} (Lemmas~\ref{lem:c2-plaquette-deviation}, \ref{lem:c2-local-gauge}, \ref{lem:c2-uniform-chart}, which need only that the row's two-block set lies within $3R_{\mathrm c}$ of $I$ and the retained inputs there are below $r$, supplied by (b)) applies to it; hence Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-primitive-bounds} holds for the near primitives of the retreated split under threshold item 3 of Appendix~\ref{app:thresholds}, $4C_Cg_0(1+2r)\leq\vartheta_*/2$, with $s_\xi$ replaced by $s^\Lambda\leq1+2r$: every near primitive $\mathfrak p$ is, at the input $a+tA^{\Lambda,\Psi}$, the restriction to real arguments of a function analytic in $(a,t)$ on a complex neighbourhood of the enlarged box $\{|a_b|_\sigma\leq2r\ \forall b\in D(\Lambda)\}\times[0,1]$, with $|\mathfrak p(a+tA^{\Lambda,\Psi})|\leq C_pg_0(s^\Lambda)^3$ for the plaquette and gauge primitives and the smaller bounds $C_pg_0^2(s^\Lambda)^2$, $C_pg_0s^\Lambda$ for the Haar and Jacobian primitives ($g_0\leq1\leq s^\Lambda$), and the derivative bounds \eqref{eq:convex-primitive-derivatives} with $s^\Lambda$; and Lemma~\ref{lem:charts} holds at $D(\Lambda)$, so the exponent of $K^\Lambda_{\xi;t}$ is real and finite on the open box and $K^\Lambda_{\xi;t}>0$ there, $I^\Lambda_t>0$. (d) Hosts. The host $Y_{\mathfrak p}$ of a primitive \eqref{eq:part1-potential-host} and the probe host $Y_b$ of a bond \eqref{eq:c-probe-host} are defined from the lifted stencil and $M$ alone; the localisation family of the retreated split is $\mathbf D_0(\Lambda):=\{Y_{\mathfrak p}\, : \,\mathfrak p\text{ near relative to }D(\Lambda)\}\cup\{Y_b\, : \,b\in D(\Lambda)\}$, with $E(Y):=\{b\in D(\Lambda)\, : \,b\cap Y\neq\varnothing\}$, and the free-input set of a primitive assigned to $Y$ lies in $Y$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:row-locality}), its young part in $E(Y)$. The counts of Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-potential-size}, at most $N_h$ primitives per host and $d_0(Y)\leq B_h+4$, are counts over templates and anchor positions, independent of the carrier. Hence, for $Y\in\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$, $t\in[0,1]$ and $a$ in the local box $\{|a_b|_\sigma<r\ \forall b\in E(Y)\}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-4-4}
    \mathbf V^\Lambda_{\xi,t}(Y,a):=\sum_{\mathfrak p\text{ near},\,Y_{\mathfrak p}=Y}\mathfrak p(a+tA^{\Lambda,\Psi})\quad\text{satisfies}\quad|\mathbf V^\Lambda_{\xi,t}(Y,a)|\leq N_hC_pg_0(s^\Lambda)^3\leq\omega_te^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\;,\qquad\omega_t:=C_0g_0(1+2r)^3\;,
\end{equation}
by (c), $d_0(Y)\leq B_h+4$ and $C_0=N_hC_pe^{\kappa(B_h+4)}$ (\eqref{eq:part1-host-multiplicity}); $\mathbf V^\Lambda_{\xi,t}(Y,\cdot):=0$ off the local box and on hosts carrying no near primitive. For $t=0$ and $\Lambda=\Lambda_1$ this is the $\mathbf V_\xi$ of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} with the bound $\omega(g_0)=C_0g_0(1+r)^3\leq\omega_t$; the factor $(1+2r)^3$ in place of $(1+r)^3$ is the retained input, which enters for $t>0$.
\end{lemma}

\subsubsection*{The translated objects}
\label{sec:c5-4-2}
Let $B:=a-tm$ be the centred variable, $a=B+tm$. Put, for $b\in D(\Lambda)$ and $Y\in\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$,
\begin{equation*}
    \chi^t_b(B):=\chi_b(B+tm)=\mathbf 1\{|B_b+tm_b|_\sigma<r\}\;,\qquad\Theta^t(B):=\prod_{b\in D(\Lambda)}\chi^t_b(B)\;,\qquad\mathbf W_t(Y,B):=\mathbf V^\Lambda_{\xi,t}(Y,B+tm);
\end{equation*}
$\chi^t_b$ is a Borel function of $B_b$ alone with values in $\{0,1\}$, $\mathbf W_t(Y,\cdot)$ depends on $B$ through $B|_{E(Y)}$ only ($m$ is a fixed vector), and $\mathbf W_t(Y,B)=0$ unless $|B_b+tm_b|_\sigma<r$ for all $b\in E(Y)$ (the translated local box), where $|\mathbf W_t(Y,B)|\leq\omega_te^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$ by \eqref{eq:c5-4-4}. Translation invariance of Lebesgue measure gives, for every bounded Borel $F$ on $\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-4-2}
    \int F(a)K^\Lambda_{\xi;t}(a)\,\dd a=e^{\frac12t^2\langle m,Hm\rangle}\int F(B+tm)\,\Theta^t(B)\exp\Bigl[-\tfrac12\langle B,HB\rangle+\sum_{Y\in\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)}\mathbf W_t(Y,B)\Bigr]\dd B\;,
\end{equation}
since $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(B+tm)=\Theta^t(B)$ and, where $\Theta^t(B)=1$, $\sum_Y\mathbf W_t(Y,B)=\sum_{\mathfrak p\text{ near}}\mathfrak p(B+tm+tA^{\Lambda,\Psi})=P^\Lambda_t(B+tm)$ (each near primitive is assigned to exactly one host). The constant $e^{\frac12t^2\langle m,Hm\rangle}$ is the same in the numerator and the denominator of every conditional expectation,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-4-3}
    \int F\,\dd\nu^\Lambda_t=\frac{\int F(B+tm)\,\Theta^te^{-\frac12\langle B,HB\rangle+\sum_Y\mathbf W_t(Y,B)}\,\dd B}{\int\Theta^te^{-\frac12\langle B,HB\rangle+\sum_Y\mathbf W_t(Y,B)}\,\dd B}\;,
\end{equation}
and is never estimated: its logarithm is at most $\tfrac12h_+\sum_bh(m_b)^2\leq h_+|D(\Lambda)|r^2/32$ ($h\leq|\cdot|_\sigma$, $t\leq1$), a constant of the order of the carrier volume, which disappears in \eqref{eq:c5-4-3}. Every use of the translated expansion below is through ratios of the form \eqref{eq:c5-4-3} or through quantities normalised by $\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3}).

\begin{lemma}[translated polymer representation]\label{lem:c5-4-1}
With $\mathfrak n_\Lambda:=(2\pi)^{d_G|D(\Lambda)|/2}\det(H)^{-1/2}$,
\begin{equation*}
    \int\Theta^t(B)\exp\Bigl[-\tfrac12\langle B,HB\rangle+\sum_Y\mathbf W_t(Y,B)\Bigr]\dd B=\mathfrak n_\Lambda\,\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda\;,\qquad\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda:=\sum_{\mathcal P\text{ compatible}}\prod_{Z\in\mathcal P}k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z)\;,\qquad k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z):=\sum_{(\mathcal D,\mathsf P)\, : \,Z_0\neq\varnothing,\ Z_0'\subseteq Z}\mathcal A^{f,t}_{(\mathcal D,\mathsf P,Z)}[1]\;,
\end{equation*}
where the selections $\mathfrak s=(\mathcal D,\mathsf P,Z)$ are those of Definition~\ref{def:selection} for the carrier $D(\Lambda)$ and the family $\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$ ($\mathcal D\subseteq\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$ finite, $\mathsf P\subseteq D(\Lambda)\setminus E(Y_0)$ finite, seed $Z_0$, $E_0=E(Z_0)$, enlargement $Z_0'$, final domain $Z\supseteq Z_0'$ a union of $LM$-cubes), and the translated selected integral of a bounded Borel $F$ on $\mathfrak g^{E_0}$ is \eqref{eq:c-selected-integral} with $\chi_b$ replaced by $\chi^t_b$ and $\mathbf W$ by $\mathbf W_t$:
\begin{multline*}
    \mathcal A^{f,t}_{\mathfrak s}[F]=\prod_{\Delta\in\pi_1(Z)\setminus\pi_1(Z_0')}\int_0^1\dd s_\Delta\oint_{|\sigma_\Delta|=e^{\kappa_1}}\frac{\dd\sigma_\Delta}{2\pi i(\sigma_\Delta-s_\Delta)^2}\prod_{Y\in\mathcal D}\int_0^1\dd t_Y\int\dd\mu_0(X)\int\dd\mu_{C_{E_0}}(B)\,e^{-\frac12\langle\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X,C_{E_0}\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X\rangle}e^{-\langle B,\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X\rangle}\\
    \times(-1)^{|\mathsf P|}\prod_{b\in E(Y_0)}\chi^t_b(B)\prod_{b\in\mathsf P}(1-\chi^t_b(B))\prod_{Y\in\mathcal D}\mathbf W_t(Y,B)e^{t_Y\mathbf W_t(Y,B)}\,F(B)\;,
\end{multline*}
with $C_{E_0}$, $\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)$, $\mu_0$, $\mu_{C_{E_0}}$ built from $H=H_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}$. Consequently $\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda=e^{-\frac12t^2\langle m,Hm\rangle}I^\Lambda_t/\mathfrak n_\Lambda>0$. For each selection $\mathfrak s$ and each bounded Borel $F$ on $\mathfrak g^{E_0}$ the translated selected integral $\mathcal A^{f,t}_{\mathfrak s}[F]$ is a well-defined finite complex number. No representation of the numerator of \eqref{eq:c5-4-3} for an insertion is asserted in this lemma: the insertion of a function of the young variables requires the probe-host (forced-seed) expansion of Section~\ref{sec:probe-kernels} for the $t$-family, Section~\ref{sec:c5-5}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:polymer} is reproduced with $\chi_b\to\chi^t_b$, $\mathbf W\to\mathbf W_t$, $I\to D(\Lambda)$, $\mathbf D_0\to\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$. The properties of the cutoff and of the potential family that proof uses are: (i) $\Theta=\prod_b\chi_b$ with each $\chi_b$ a Borel function of $a_b$ alone with values in $[0,1]$: in the expansion $\Theta=\prod_{E(Y_0)}\chi_b\prod_{I\setminus E(Y_0)}(1-(1-\chi_b))$ with the sign $(-1)^{|\mathsf P|}$, and in the factorisation over the components of $Z$, where a bad bond lies in the component containing its incident cubes and a cutoff factor is a function of one bond variable; $\chi^t_b$ has these properties. (ii) $\mathbf W(Y,\cdot)$ depends on $a$ through $a|_{E(Y)}$, with $E(Y)\subseteq E_0$ for $Y\in\mathcal D$, and is bounded where $\prod_{b\in E(Y)}\chi_b\neq0$: in the reduction to $a|_{E_0}$ that lets Lemma~\ref{lem:conditioning} produce \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-density}, and in the Fubini steps (bounded factors times an absolutely integrable density); $\mathbf W_t$ has these properties, with the translated local box in place of the local box, by Section~\ref{sec:c5-4-2}. (iii) Everything else, the walk series (Lemma~\ref{lem:walk-series}), the conditioning identity and the weakened density (Lemma~\ref{lem:conditioning}, \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-density}), the block structure (Lemma~\ref{lem:component-blocks}), the integrability of the absolute density uniformly on the closed polydisc (Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment}(ii)), the Cauchy representation of the $\sigma$-derivatives and the vanishing of unseeded components, and the geometry of seeds, enlargements and the coarse cover, involves $H$, the partitions $\pi_0$, $\pi_1$ of the torus and $\sigma$ only, and holds for $H=H_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(a); the host and probe geometry is that of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(d). The expansion of $\exp(\sum_Y\mathbf W_t)$ into Mayer factors $\int_0^1\mathbf W_te^{t_Y\mathbf W_t}\,\dd t_Y$ is the same algebra. Positivity: by \eqref{eq:c5-4-2} with $F=1$, $\mathfrak n_\Lambda\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda=e^{-\frac12t^2\langle m,Hm\rangle}I^\Lambda_t$, and $I^\Lambda_t>0$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c). Well-definedness of $\mathcal A^{f,t}_{\mathfrak s}[F]$: the integrand is a bounded function ($|F|\leq\sup|F|$, cutoffs $\leq1$, $|\mathbf W_te^{t_Y\mathbf W_t}|\leq\omega_te^{\omega_t}$ by \eqref{eq:c5-4-4}) times the density \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-density}, whose modulus is integrable uniformly on the closed polydisc by Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment}(ii) for $H$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(a)); the finitely many $s$-, $\sigma$- and $t_Y$-integrals are over compact sets.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[translated selected majorant]\label{lem:c5-4-2}
Let $g_0$ satisfy the thresholds of Appendix~\ref{app:thresholds}, items 1--5, and in addition
\begin{align}
    &C_0g_0(1+2r)^3\leq\omega_*\quad\text{(i.e. $\omega_t\leq\omega_*$; implied by $8C_0g_0(1+r)^3\leq\omega_*$, a strengthening of threshold item 4 by the factor 8)},\label{eq:c5-4-T}\\
    &\tfrac{9}{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}\geq\max\bigl(160(\kappa+\log(8M^4+8)+1),\,64\log(1/\eta_*)\bigr)\;,\qquad a_{\mathrm{bad}}=c_n\alpha_Gr^2/4\quad\text{(\eqref{eq:native-source-bad-budget} for $\tfrac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$)}.\label{eq:c5-4-Tp}
\end{align}
Both are conditions on $g_0$ alone of the kind already in Appendix~\ref{app:thresholds} ($g_0(1+r)^3\to0$ and $a_{\mathrm{bad}}\to\infty$ as $g_0\to0$, $r=A_1(\log g_0^{-2})^{p_0}$), with thresholds depending on $L$, the group, $M$, $\kappa$, $\alpha_G$, $\omega_*$ and the cutoff parameters $A_1$, $p_0$ only (through $r(g_0)$, as threshold item 4 does). Then on every labelled term with final region $\Lambda$, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs (where \eqref{eq:c5-4-1} and $r^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}<r$ hold, Lemmas~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(b) and~\ref{lem:c5-3-3}(iii); the term vanishes elsewhere), for every $t\in[0,1]$, every selection $\mathfrak s=(\mathcal D,\mathsf P,Z)$ for $D(\Lambda)$ and every bounded Borel $F$ on $\mathfrak g^{E_0}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-4-5}
    |\mathcal A^{f,t}_{\mathfrak s}[F]|\leq\sup|F|\cdot e^{-(\kappa_1-1)N_1(Z\setminus Z_0')+N_1(Z)-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}|\mathsf P|}\prod_{Y\in\mathcal D}\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\;.
\end{equation}
The right side depends neither on $t$, nor on $m$ or the exterior data $A^{\Lambda,\Psi}$ within that support, nor on the labels; bondwise, the reserve is $(1-t|m_b|_\sigma/r)^2a_{\mathrm{bad}}\geq\tfrac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-selected-majorant} with three substitutions. The weakening step is unchanged: for each cube $\Delta$ of $Z\setminus Z_0'$ the operation $G\mapsto\int_0^1\dd s\oint G\,\dd\sigma_\Delta/(2\pi i(\sigma_\Delta-s)^2)$ on the inner integral as a function of $\sigma_\Delta$ on the circle $|\sigma_\Delta|=e^{\kappa_1}$ is bounded by $e^{-(\kappa_1-1)}$ times the supremum of the inner integral's modulus over the distinguished boundary, by $e^{\kappa_1}(e^{\kappa_1}-1)^{-2}\leq e^{-(\kappa_1-1)}$ ($e^{\kappa_1}\geq3$); it needs the continuity of the integrand in $\sigma$ (the walk series, Lemma~\ref{lem:walk-series} for $H$) and the uniform integrability below. On the distinguished boundary, the inner integrand's modulus is bounded factor by factor: $\int_0^1\dd t_Y$ has mass one; $\chi^t_b\leq1$; for $b\in\mathsf P$ and real $B$, by the triangle inequality for the norm $|\cdot|_\sigma$ and \eqref{eq:c5-4-1},
\begin{equation*}
    1-\chi^t_b(B)=\mathbf 1\{|B_b+tm_b|_\sigma\geq r\}\leq\mathbf 1\{|B_b|_\sigma\geq r-t|m_b|_\sigma\}\leq\mathbf 1\{|B_b|_\sigma\geq(1-t|m_b|_\sigma/r)r\}\leq\mathbf 1\{|B_b|_\sigma\geq3r/4\}\;,
\end{equation*}
and on $\{|X|_\sigma\geq3r/4\}$ the group inequality $h(X)^2\geq c_n|X|_\sigma^2$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment}(iii) ($c_n=1/N$ serves for both cutoff norms, the conventions of Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates}) gives $h(X)^2\geq\tfrac9{16}c_nr^2$, so $\mathbf 1\{|B_b|_\sigma\geq3r/4\}\leq\exp[(\gamma_2/2)(h(B_b)^2-\tfrac9{16}c_nr^2)]$ for every $\gamma_2>0$ (the exponent is nonnegative on the set); with $\gamma_2=\alpha_G/2$, $a_{\mathrm{bad}}=c_n\gamma_2r^2/2=c_n\alpha_Gr^2/4$ and $\|B\|^2=\sum_bh(B_b)^2$,
\begin{equation*}
    \prod_{b\in\mathsf P}(1-\chi^t_b(B))\leq e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}|\mathsf P|}e^{(\alpha_G/2)\sum_{b\in\mathsf P}h(B_b)^2}\leq e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}|\mathsf P|}e^{(\alpha_G/2)\|B\|^2}
\end{equation*}
(this is step (iii) of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} at the radius $3r/4$ in place of $r$; bondwise the same argument gives the reserve $(1-t|m_b|_\sigma/r)^2a_{\mathrm{bad}}$); for $Y\in\mathcal D$, on the support of $\prod_{b\in E(Y)}\chi^t_b$ the point $B+tm$ lies in the local box of $Y$, so $|\mathbf W_t(Y,B)|\leq\omega_te^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\leq\omega_*e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$ by \eqref{eq:c5-4-4} and \eqref{eq:c5-4-T}, and $|\mathbf W_te^{t_Y\mathbf W_t}|\leq\omega_*e^{\omega_*}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\leq\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$, since $\omega_*\leq1/2$ ($\eta_*=2e^{1/2+5\kappa}\omega_*\leq1/32$); $|F|\leq\sup|F|$; and the integral of the modulus of the density \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-density} against $e^{(\alpha_G/2)\|B\|^2}$ is at most $e^{N_1(Z)}$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment}(ii) for $H$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(a)), under the conditions on $\delta_M$ and $\alpha_G$ of Appendix~\ref{app:thresholds}. Multiplying the bounds and applying the weakening bound once per cube of $Z\setminus Z_0'$ gives \eqref{eq:c5-4-5}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[activity bounds and vacuum clusters for the $t$-family]\label{cor:c5-4-3}
Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2}, for every $t\in[0,1]$ and every connected final domain $Z$ for $D(\Lambda)$,
\begin{equation*}
    |k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z)|\leq\varepsilon_*e^{-\lambda_*\ell_1(Z)}\;,\qquad\sum_{Z'\not\sim Z}|k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z')|e^{(1+6\theta)N_1(Z')}\leq N_1(Z)/8\;,
\end{equation*}
with the constants $\lambda_*$, $\varepsilon_*$, $\theta$ of \eqref{eq:c2-kappa-choice}--\eqref{eq:native-source-vacuum-KP-budget}, and Lemma~\ref{lem:vacuum-clusters} holds for the cluster weights $\Phi^{v,t}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c)=(\varphi^T(\mathfrak c)/m!)\prod_jk^{f,t}_\Lambda(D_j)$: the hitting sum $\sum_{\mathfrak c\text{ hits }\mathcal A}|\Phi^{v,t}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c)|e^{2\theta\sum_jN_1(D_j)}\leq\tfrac18\sum_{D\in\mathcal A}N_1(D)$ and the identity $(\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda)^{[\cup\mathcal A]}/\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda=\exp(-\sum_{\mathfrak c\text{ hits }\mathcal A}\Phi^{v,t}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c))$, uniformly in $t$, $m$, the exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, and the labels.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:activity-bounds} uses, about the activities, only the majorant \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-selected-majorant}, here \eqref{eq:c5-4-5} with $F=1$, with $\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\leq\eta_*e^{-\kappa\ell_0(Y)}$ ($\ell_0=d_0+5$ and $\eta_*=2e^{1/2+5\kappa}\omega_*$), and the number $a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ through the conditions it draws from \eqref{eq:native-source-bad-budget}: $a_{\mathrm{bad}}\geq160(\kappa+1)>1$ and $64M^4e^{-a_{\mathrm{bad}}/4}\leq1$ (bad-label sum), $a_{\mathrm{bad}}/32\geq5\kappa$ (component reserve), $e^{-a_{\mathrm{bad}}/64}\leq\eta_*\leq t_0$ (the case $k_j=0$), and $C_ge^{-a_{\mathrm{bad}}/64}\leq162e^{-5(\kappa+1)/2}\leq\kappa/40$ (choice of $Y_0$). Each of these holds for every number not smaller than one satisfying \eqref{eq:native-source-bad-budget}; \eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp} says that $\tfrac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ satisfies it, so the proof applies verbatim with $a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ replaced by $\tfrac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ and $\eta_*$, $t_0$, $\omega_*$, $\kappa$, $\delta$, $\alpha_6$, $C_g$, $M$, $L$ unchanged (the geometric inputs Lemmas~\ref{lem:c2-domain-geometry}, \ref{lem:c2-cover-sum} and~\ref{lem:c2-scale} concern the partitions only). The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:vacuum-clusters} (Appendix~\ref{app:kp}, Kotecký--Preiss with the pinned-tree bound) uses only the two displayed bounds and the geometry, and nothing else about the branch (Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-reference}).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the conditional estimate at the retreated carrier]\label{lem:c5-4-4}
Under the thresholds $C_Pg_0(1+2r)\leq\gamma/2$ and $r\geq1$ of Appendix~\ref{app:exterior-chessboard} (\draftref{stage 3, \S3} uses $r\geq1$ in $O(g_0s^2/r)=O(g_0s)$; both are conditions on $g_0$) and the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}, on every labelled term with final region $\Lambda$, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, and on the covered event $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ of Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a) (every contour bond in $\Omega_1$, every free input of the contour in $D(\Lambda)$),
\begin{equation*}
    |\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]|\leq C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}g_0rD^\Lambda_\xi\;,\qquad\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f]:=\int f\,\dd\nu^\Lambda_1-\int f\,\dd\nu^\Lambda_0=\int_0^1\operatorname{Cov}_{\nu^\Lambda_t}(f,L^\Lambda_t)\,\dd t\;,\qquad D^\Lambda_\xi:=\sum_{b\in I_C\cap D(\Lambda)}e^{-(\mu/2)d(b,J(\Lambda))}\;,
\end{equation*}
with the constant $C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}$ and the rate $\mu=R^{-1}\log(1+\gamma/(4M_*))$, $M_*=m_L+\gamma/2$, of Appendix~\ref{app:exterior-chessboard} (\draftref{stage 3, \S\S4--5}), independent of $g_0$, the term, the labels, the volume and $t$; $D^\Lambda_\xi$ is the $D^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi$ of Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a), and $L^\Lambda_t:=-\langle\cdot,\ell_\Lambda\rangle+\partial_tP^\Lambda_t$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\draftref{stage 3, \S\S1--5} are applied with $(I,J,A_{\mathrm e},H,\ell,P_t,r_{\mathrm e,\xi})$ replaced by $(D(\Lambda),J(\Lambda),A^{\Lambda,\Psi},H_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)},\ell_\Lambda,P^\Lambda_t,r^\Lambda_{\mathrm e})$. The inputs they use, each supplied: $\gamma\leq H\leq m_L$ and the finite range $R$ of $H$ and of the primitive stencils (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(a); \draftref{stage 3, \S2} takes $R=8L+8\geq\rho_L$); the primitive bounds on the enlarged box at the inputs $a+tA^{\Lambda,\Psi}$, uniformly in $t$, with $s^\Lambda\leq1+2r$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c)), giving $\|D^2_aP^\Lambda_t\|\leq C_Pg_0s^\Lambda$ and the Schur row/column bounds of \draftref{stage 3, \S3}; $r^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}<r$ for the score bound $h(\nabla_b\partial_tP^\Lambda_t)\leq C_Sg_0s^\Lambda r^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}\mathbf 1_{K^\Lambda}(b)$ and $h((\ell_\Lambda)_b)\leq m_Lr^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}\mathbf 1_{K^\Lambda}(b)$, $K^\Lambda:=\{b\in D(\Lambda)\, : \,\operatorname{dist}(b,J(\Lambda))\leq R\}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(b) and (I2)); the source gradient bound $\sup h(\nabla_bf_\xi)\leq C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}g_0\mathbf 1_{I_C}(b)$ on the enlarged box, from the rowwise reconstruction and the uniform charts for the rows meeting $D(\Lambda)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c); the links of the contour depend on $a|_{D(\Lambda)}$ through those rows, the de-younged bonds being constants of the term); the identity $\mathcal B=\int_0^1\operatorname{Cov}\,\dd t$, which is the differentiation of $t\mapsto\int f\,\dd\nu^\Lambda_t$ under the integral (the exponent of $K^\Lambda_{\xi;t}$ is smooth in $t$ with bounded derivative on the box, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c)); the extension $T_r$ of \draftref{stage 3, \S3}, the barrier $\beta_n$ and the Helffer--Sj\"ostrand identity of \draftref{stage 3, \S4}, which involve only the box $\{|a_b|_\sigma<r,\ b\in D(\Lambda)\}$, the Hessian bounds and the Schur bounds; and the shell summation of \draftref{stage 3, \S5} with $d(c,J(\Lambda))\leq R$ on $K^\Lambda$. The contour bonds whose free inputs are in $D(\Lambda)$ are exactly those counted by $I_C\cap D(\Lambda)$ on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$; the others do not depend on the young variable. Nothing in \draftref{stage 3, \S\S1--5} uses that the carrier is $\Lambda_1^*$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[scope of this subsection]\label{rem:c5-4-5}
The translated expansion gives the vacuum activities $k^{f,t}_\Lambda$, their clusters and a majorant for selected integrals with a bounded insertion $F$ on $\mathfrak g^{E_0}$; the hosts are unchanged and the primitives are evaluated at $a=B+tm$ in the original local box, so the chart condition is threshold item 3 at $s^\Lambda\leq1+2r$, and the enlarged box $\{|a_b|_\sigma\leq2r\}$, not needed for the majorant, is available for analyticity, $B\mapsto B+tm$ mapping $\{|B_b|_\sigma\leq7r/4\}$ into it. Nothing here concerns the source-containing connected terms attached to $J(\Lambda)$: $f_\xi(B+tm)$ is not a function of $B|_{E_0}$ for a selection unrelated to the contour, so \eqref{eq:c5-4-5} with $F$ the source is not available, and the insertion of the source through the probe hosts of $\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$, the $t$-centred reference and the marked connected terms are Section~\ref{sec:c5-5}; the bookkeeping of the labels in the history sum is Section~\ref{sec:c5-6}. The two thresholds \eqref{eq:c5-4-T}, \eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp} are added to threshold item 4 wherever the $t$-family is used.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The source-containing connected boundary terms}
\label{sec:c5-5}

\subsubsection*{Setting and target}
\label{sec:c5-5-0}
Throughout this subsection fix a history, a labelled term with final region $\Lambda$, carrier $D:=D(\Lambda)$, exterior $J(\Lambda)$, exterior variable $A^{\Lambda,\Psi}$, shift $m$, and the $t$-family $K^\Lambda_{\xi;t}$, $\nu^\Lambda_t$, $\mathbf W_t$, $\chi^t_b$ of Section~\ref{sec:c5-4}, with the exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs (where \eqref{eq:c5-4-1}, $r^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}<r$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0} hold; the term vanishes elsewhere, and every bound below is a bound on a factor of the term that is multiplied by those indicators), on the covered event $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ of Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a) (every contour bond in $\Omega_1$, every free input of the contour in $D(\Lambda)$; so $I_C\subseteq D(\Lambda)$, and $f_\xi(a)=W_\chi(C;U_\xi(a))$ depends on $a\in\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}$ through $a|_{I_C}$ only, the de-younged bonds $I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ being constants of the term, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-4}). Write
\begin{equation*}
    F^\Lambda(t):=\int f_\xi\,\dd\nu^\Lambda_t\;,\qquad\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]=F^\Lambda(1)-F^\Lambda(0)\quad\text{(Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-4})},
\end{equation*}
and $n_C$ for the number of traversals of $C$, $I_C$ for the set of free-input bonds of the contour (each within $\ell^\infty$ distance $2L+1$ of a contour bond: the forward influence set $\mathcal R_b$ of Section~\ref{sec:probe-kernels} lies within $\ell^\infty$ distance $2L+1$ of $b$, applied to the contour bond $r$ with $b\in\mathcal F_r$; hence $\operatorname{dist}(e,I_C)\geq\operatorname{dist}(e,C)-(2L+1)$ in the distance convention below), $J_1(\Lambda):=\{e\in J(\Lambda)\, : \,H(b',e)\neq0\text{ for some }b'\in D(\Lambda)\}\subseteq J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-2} at the retreated carrier). The target is a bound on $\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]$ relative to the reference gas, with the perimeter factor extracted inside the connected terms before absolute values, of the form (reference)$\times\sum_{e\in J(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}\times$(polynomial in $r$)$\times$(history weights). The reference used here is $S^\circ_\Lambda$, the reference group gas at the carrier $D(\Lambda)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}); its identification with $\kappa_Ce^{E^\circ_\Lambda}$ is Section~\ref{sec:c5-7}.

Inputs beyond Sections~\ref{sec:c5-0}--\ref{sec:c5-4}, used as stated at their stated hypotheses: Lemmas~\ref{lem:probe-measures}, \ref{lem:measure-identity}, Proposition~\ref{prop:centred-expectation} and Section~\ref{sec:joint-extraction} (Definition~\ref{def:groups}, Lemma~\ref{lem:grouped-source}, the reference data, Lemma~\ref{lem:schur}, Proposition~\ref{prop:joint-extraction}); \draftref{stage 4, \S4.2, \S7.1} (the dummy comparison $\sum_{\ell\, : \,Z(\ell)=Z,\,\mathsf J\neq\varnothing}u_*^{q(\ell)}\|\Lambda^f_{\xi;\ell}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq m(Z):=\varepsilon_*e^{-\lambda_*\ell_1(Z)}$, $u_*=\min(1/4,\omega_*e^{-\kappa s_p}/(8P_M))$, proved by the positive host comparison from the selected majorant and the cover/seed sums of Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates}), \draftref{stage 4, \S6.1} (the closed-contraction factorisation of $R_\xi(\mathfrak B)$ and the product inequality $|R_\xi(\mathfrak B)|\leq\prod_WK_\chi\mathfrak d_W$, $K_\chi$ the constant of the irreducible character); \draftref{attempt, \S13} (the reference exclusion ratio $q^\circ(\mathfrak B)=S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}/S^\circ=\exp(-\sum_{\Gamma\text{ hits }\mathfrak B}\Phi^\circ(\Gamma))$ by Kotecký--Preiss for the reference group gas), \draftref{attempt, \S18.3, \S20.1} (the positive domain/cluster/interval summation giving the footprint and contour pins $\sup_\Delta\sum_{W\, : \,\Delta\in F(W)}e^{\nu\sigma(W)}M(W)\leq C\varepsilon$ and $\sup_j\sum_{W\, : \,j\in\mathcal H(W)}e^{\nu\sigma(W)}M(W)\leq C\varepsilon$ for a positive group majorant $M(W)$ with root weights $\rho\,m(Z)$ and the positive cluster tree weights, $\nu$ the support reserve, $\sigma(W)$ the size, $F(W)$ the footprint), \draftref{attempt, \S19.3} (the connected envelope $E(W)$ of a group has at most $A_E\sigma(W)$ cubes and is connected under maximum-norm adjacency, so a group whose footprint contains the cube $\Delta$ has $\sigma(W)\geq\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)/(A_ELM)$), \draftref{attempt, \S19.4} (the positive tree bound for the reference gas: the Kotecký--Preiss theorem with activities $|a_W|e^{3\eta s(W)/2}$ and node cost $\eta s(W)/8$, giving the rooted bound $\sum_{\Gamma\ni W}m_W(\Gamma)|\Phi^\circ(\Gamma)|\leq|a_W|e^{13\eta s(W)/8}$ and the exclusion identity); \draftref{stage 4, \S6.2} (the positive source-containing group summation with the weights $e^{4\eta\mathfrak s(W)}$ under $C\rho LM\leq\eta/8$, and the fact that incompatible groups have touching footprints or overlapping intervals); the tree-graph justification following \eqref{eq:marginal-pinned-bound} ($|\varphi^T|\leq$ number of spanning trees of the incompatibility graph, used in Appendix~\ref{app:kp}); \draftref{attempt, \S20.2} (the far event $N_b=\{\operatorname{dist}(\Gamma_C,E(\Lambda_1^c))\leq b\}$, $b=\lfloor LMR_1/16\rfloor$). The finite-difference bound of \draftref{stage 4, \S4.1} is not used as an input: Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z} proves the form needed here from Lemmas~\ref{lem:charts} and~\ref{lem:c2-uniform-chart}.

Geometry. Throughout Sections~\ref{sec:c5-5} and~\ref{sec:c5-6} the square/torus geometry of Section~\ref{sec:extent-weights} is in force, as imposed by \draftref{stage 4, \S6} and \draftref{attempt, \S18.3}: $C$ is a coarse square of side $s=nL$ fine bonds, $n_C=4s$, on fine periods $LK_i$ with $K_i\geq3(n+2)$, together with the period condition of Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-1} (compatible even periods exceeding $200\ell$). The constants $C_{\mathrm{pin}}$, $A_F$, $A_E$ and the hull/extent bounds of \draftref{stage 4, \S6.2} and \draftref{attempt, \S\S18.3, 19.3} are proved under this geometry, and Lemmas~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d), \ref{lem:c5-5-8}, Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5-9} and Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-5-10} inherit it as a hypothesis. Distance convention: every distance in Sections~\ref{sec:c5-5}--\ref{sec:c5-6} is the $\ell^\infty$ distance in fine units; the distance of a bond is measured from its initial site, that of a cube or host from its nearest point, and the distance to $C$ is the distance to the vertex set of $C$ (so $\operatorname{dist}(b,C)=0$ for a contour bond $b$, whose initial site is a vertex, and $\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)=0$ for a cube of $C(\mathcal H)$); the counts $4(2s+1)^4$ and the conversions by $LM+1$, $4M+LM$, $2L+1$ below are stated in this convention.

\subsubsection*{The decay form of the mean shift}
Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-2} bounds $\sup_bh(m_b)$; the dependence on the distance to the exterior is needed.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:c5-5-1}
For every $b\in D(\Lambda)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-1}
    h(m_b)\leq C_m\vartheta r\sum_{e\in J_1(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,e)}\;,\qquad C_m:=\gamma^{-1}m_L\cdot4(2\rho_L+1)^4e^{\mu_H\rho_L}\;,
\end{equation}
with $\mu_H=\gamma/(h_+\rho_L)$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-1} and $d$ the $\ell^\infty$ distance of initial sites in fine units. Consequently, with $N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu)=\sum_{s\geq0}4(2s+1)^4e^{-\mu s}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-2},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-2}
    \sum_{b\in D(\Lambda)}h(m_b)e^{-\mu\operatorname{dist}(b,X)}\leq C_m\vartheta rN_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)\sum_{e\in J_1(\Lambda)}e^{-\min(\mu_H/2,\mu)\operatorname{dist}(e,X)}
\end{equation}
for every set $X$ of bonds or of sites ($\operatorname{dist}(b,X)$ the $\ell^\infty$ distance from the initial site of $b$; Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5-9} takes $X$ the vertex set of $C$) and $\mu\geq0$; in particular $\sum_{b\in I_C}h(m_b)\leq C_m\vartheta rN_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)\sum_{e\in J_1(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu_H\operatorname{dist}(e,I_C)/2}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$m_b=-\sum_{b'\in D(\Lambda)}H^{-1}(b,b')\sum_{e\in J_1(\Lambda)}H(b',e)A^{\Lambda,\Psi}_e$ (Section~\ref{sec:c5-1}, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-2}). By (I1), $H(b',e)\neq0$ forces $d(b',e)\leq\rho_L$, hence $d(b,b')\geq d(b,e)-\rho_L$; by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-1}, $\|H^{-1}(b,b')\|\leq\gamma^{-1}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,b')}\leq\gamma^{-1}e^{\mu_H\rho_L}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,e)}$ for such $b'$; the number of $b'$ with $d(b',e)\leq\rho_L$ is at most $4(2\rho_L+1)^4$; each entry has $\|H(b',e)\|\leq\sum_{b''}\|H(b',b'')\|\leq m_L$ by (I2) for the row $b'\in D(\Lambda)$; and $h(A^{\Lambda,\Psi}_e)\leq|A^{\Lambda,\Psi}_e|_\sigma<\vartheta r$ for $e\in J_1(\Lambda)\subseteq J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(b)). Multiplying gives \eqref{eq:c5-5-1}. For \eqref{eq:c5-5-2}: $e^{-\mu_Hd(b,e)}e^{-\mu\operatorname{dist}(b,X)}\leq e^{-\mu_Hd(b,e)/2}e^{-\min(\mu_H/2,\mu)(d(b,e)+\operatorname{dist}(b,X))}\leq e^{-\mu_Hd(b,e)/2}e^{-\min(\mu_H/2,\mu)\operatorname{dist}(e,X)}$ by the triangle inequality, and $\sum_{b\in D(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,e)/2}\leq N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)$ by the lattice count of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-2} (the sum over all bonds dominates the sum over $D(\Lambda)$). The last display is \eqref{eq:c5-5-2} with $X=I_C$ and any $\mu\geq\mu_H/2$ (so that $\min(\mu_H/2,\mu)=\mu_H/2$), restricted to $b\in I_C\subseteq D(\Lambda)$, where $\operatorname{dist}(b,I_C)=0$; the terms with $b\notin I_C$ are nonnegative and dropped. Only (I1)--(I3) at the retreated carrier and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(b) are used; no column-sum bound.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{Thresholds}
\label{sec:c5-5-2}
In addition to the thresholds of Appendix~\ref{app:thresholds}, \eqref{eq:c5-4-T} and \eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp}, assume
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-T}
    \begin{aligned}
        &\text{(i) }u_t:=C'_\chi g_0(1+2r)\leq u_*;\qquad\text{(ii) }\omega_t=C_0g_0(1+2r)^3\leq\omega_*/2;\qquad\text{(iii) }\bar\omega:=C_0g_0(1+3r)^3\leq\min\bigl(1,\,\omega_*e^{-\kappa(B_h+4)}/(e^{9/8}N_{\mathrm{host}})\bigr);\\
        &\text{(iv) }\rho_t:=u_t/u_*\leq\rho_{\mathrm{pin}};\qquad\text{(v) }\bar\varepsilon_t:=2K_\chi C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t\leq1,
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
together with the standing choice $\omega_*\leq1/2$ of \draftref{stage 4, \S7.1}. Here $C'_\chi:=2N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}$ is the constant of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z} (the finite-difference bound for closed contractions: Cauchy estimates on the row chart with $C_{\mathrm{link}}$, the traversal multiplicity $N_{\mathrm{tr}}$, and the factor 2 for the two $s$-dependent words of the source slot in Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7}(d); it depends on $L$, the group and the character only). $N_{\mathrm{host}}:=4^4\cdot20^4$ is the host count of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-6} and Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5-9} (the number of hosts $Y\in\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$ containing a given bond, and the number within distance $R_{\mathrm{st}}\leq5M$ of a given bond: a host is a box of $M$-cubes of side at most 4 cubes, \eqref{eq:part1-potential-host} and \eqref{eq:c-probe-host}, determined by its lowest cube, one of at most $20^4$ cubes within $9M$ of the bond, and by its side lengths, at most $4^4$ choices). $\rho_{\mathrm{pin}}$ is the root-weight smallness required by the positive summation of \draftref{stage 4, \S6.2} and \draftref{attempt, \S18.3, \S19.4} (the conditions $C'_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho LM\leq\eta/8$, $C\rho LM\leq\eta$ there, and $(3^4A_F+LM+1)C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho\leq\eta/16$ for the reference gas, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d), with their fixed constants; $3^4$ is the neighbour factor for footprint contact of \draftref{stage 4, \S12.4} and \draftref{attempt, \S19.3}, $J_{\mathrm{inc}}=3^4A_EQ_E$), and $C_{\mathrm{pin}}$, $K_\chi$ are the constants of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(a) and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7}(a). The subscript $t$ in $u_t$, $\rho_t$, $\bar\varepsilon_t$ names the $t$-family; none of them depends on $t$. Every condition holds for $g_0$ small, since $g_0(1+r)^3\to0$ ($r=A_1(\log g_0^{-2})^{p_0}$), with thresholds depending on $L$, the group, $M$, $\kappa$, $\omega_*$, $\eta$, $B_h$, $A_1$, $p_0$ and the character only. Put $\bar s:=1+2r+r^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}\leq1+3r$.

\subsubsection*{Translated probe measures and the centred expectation for the $t$-family}
The probe hosts $Y_b$, $b\in D(\Lambda)$, are in $\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(d)); for a union $R$ of $LM$-cubes put $\mathsf L_R:=\{b\in D(\Lambda)\, : \,Y_b\subseteq R\}$ (\eqref{eq:probe-eligible-carrier} at the carrier $D(\Lambda)$), and for $\lambda\in\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}_{\mathbb C}$ let $L_Y(\lambda,B):=\sum_{b\, : \,Y_b=Y}\langle\lambda_b,B_b\rangle$ (the probe in the centred variable). The family $\mathbf W_t(Y,\cdot)+L_Y(\lambda,\cdot)$, $Y\in\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$, has the properties (i)--(iii) of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1}: $L_Y(\lambda,\cdot)$ depends on $B|_{E(Y)}$ ($Y_b=Y$ gives $b\in E(Y)$), and is bounded on the translated local box of $Y$ ($|B_b|_\sigma<r+t|m_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4$ there), and nothing else changes. Hence Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1} holds for it, with activities $k^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}(\lambda)$ entire in $\lambda$ and $k^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}(0)=k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z)$.

\begin{lemma}[probe measures, majorant and dummy comparison for the $t$-family]\label{lem:c5-5-2}
Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2} and \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}, for every $t\in[0,1]$ and every connected final domain $Z$ for $D(\Lambda)$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
    \item There is a finite complex Borel measure $\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}$ on $\mathfrak g^{\mathsf L_Z}$, supported in the closed translated box $\{|B_b+tm_b|_\sigma\leq r,\ b\in\mathsf L_Z\}$, with $k^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}(\lambda)=\int e^{\sum_{b\in\mathsf L_Z}\langle\lambda_b,B_b\rangle}\,\dd\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}(B)$ and $\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}(\mathfrak g^{\mathsf L_Z})=k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z)$; explicitly, with the split selections $\ell=(\mathcal D,\mathsf J,\mathsf P,Z)$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:probe-measures} ($\mathsf J\subseteq\mathsf L_Z$ the selected probes, enlarged seed data $Y_0=\bigcup_{Y\in\mathcal D}Y\cup\bigcup_{b\in\mathsf J}Y_b$, $Z_0$, $E_0=E(Z_0)$, $Z_0'$) and $\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}$ the complex measure on $\mathfrak g^{E_0}$ defined by the right side of the display of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1} with these enlarged data (cutoffs $\chi^t_b$ over $E(Y_0)$, the weakened density and the bad set over this seed, Mayer factors for $Y\in\mathcal D$ only, no probe factor), $\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}=\sum_{\ell\, : \,Z(\ell)=Z,\,Z_0\neq\varnothing}\int\bigl[\bigotimes_{b\in\mathsf J}(\delta_{B_b}-\delta_0)\otimes\bigotimes_{b\in\mathsf L_Z\setminus\mathsf J}\delta_0\bigr]\dd\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}(B)$.
    \item (Probe-enlarged majorant.) For every split selection $\ell$ and every bounded Borel $F$ on $\mathfrak g^{E_0}$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-3}
        \Bigl|\int F\,\dd\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}\Bigr|\leq\sup|F|\cdot e^{-(\kappa_1-1)N_1(Z\setminus Z_0')+N_1(Z)-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}|\mathsf P|}\prod_{Y\in\mathcal D}\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\;,
    \end{equation}
    uniformly in $t$, $m$, the exterior data within the cutoff support and the labels; the probe hosts $Y_b$, $b\in\mathsf J$, carry no factor.
    \item (Dummy comparison for the $t$-family.) $\sum_{\ell\, : \,Z(\ell)=Z}u_*^{|\mathsf J|}\|\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq m(Z)=\varepsilon_*e^{-\lambda_*\ell_1(Z)}$, the sum over all split selections with final domain $Z$ and nonempty seed $Z_0\neq\varnothing$ ($\mathsf J=\varnothing$ included; the activity sum of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} excludes $Z_0=\varnothing$, and the $Z_0=\varnothing$ term has zero measure by the Cauchy argument of Lemma~\ref{lem:polymer}).
    \item (Centred expectation.) For bounded Borel $g$ on $\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}$, with $\mathfrak Q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A;g):=\int g(\mathsf e_{\mathcal A}B)\,\dd\bigl(\bigotimes_{Z\in\mathcal A}\hat\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}\bigr)(B)$, $\hat\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}:=\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}-k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z)\delta_0$, $q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A):=(\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda)^{[\cup\mathcal A]}/\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \frac{\int g(B)\,\Theta^te^{-\frac12\langle B,HB\rangle+\sum_Y\mathbf W_t(Y,B)}\,\dd B}{\int\Theta^te^{-\frac12\langle B,HB\rangle+\sum_Y\mathbf W_t(Y,B)}\,\dd B}=g(0)+\sum_{\varnothing\neq\mathcal A\text{ compatible}}q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A)\,\mathfrak Q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A;g)\;,
    \end{equation*}
    and, by \eqref{eq:c5-4-3} with $F=f_\xi$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-4}
        F^\Lambda(t)=f_\xi(tm)+\sum_{\varnothing\neq\mathcal A\text{ compatible}}q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A)\,\mathfrak Q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A;f_\xi(\cdot+tm))\;,\qquad\mathfrak Q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A;f_\xi(\cdot+tm))=\sum_{(\ell_Z)_{Z\in\mathcal A}\, : \,\mathsf J_Z\neq\varnothing}\int\Delta_{\mathsf J}[f_\xi(\cdot+tm)](B)\,\dd\bigotimes_Z\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell_Z}(B)\;,
    \end{equation}
    where $\mathsf J=\bigcup_Z\mathsf J_Z$ and $\Delta_{\mathsf J}g(B):=\sum_{K\subseteq\mathsf J}(-1)^{|\mathsf J\setminus K|}g(\mathbf 1_KB)$ is the mixed finite difference of \draftref{stage 4, \S4} (every label outside $K$ set to zero, so the young variable of $f_\xi$ in the $K$-term is $\mathbf 1_KB+tm$: the unselected labels sit at $a=tm$).
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) is the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:probe-measures} with $\mathbf V_\xi\to\mathbf W_t$, $\chi_b\to\chi^t_b$, $I\to D(\Lambda)$: the split-label expansion at a host, the exact integration \eqref{eq:probe-selected-factor} of the probe Mayer variables, the Laplace transform of $\delta_{B_b}-\delta_0$ and the finiteness of each $\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}$ (image of a density whose modulus is integrable by Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment}(ii) for $H$, times bounded factors: the Cauchy kernels on the circles $|\sigma_\Delta|=e^{\kappa_1}$ are bounded by $e^{\kappa_1}(e^{\kappa_1}-1)^{-2}$, and the integrand is jointly Borel in $(X,B,s,\sigma,t_Y)$) use only Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1} and its properties (i)--(iii). The support statement: $\chi^t_b(B)$, $b\in E(Y_0)\supseteq\mathsf J$, forces $|B_b+tm_b|_\sigma<r$ on the selected probes, and the unselected labels carry $\delta_0$ ($B_b=0$, and $|tm_b|_\sigma\leq r/4<r$). At $\lambda=0$ every term with $\mathsf J\neq\varnothing$ has zero mass (each $\delta_{B_b}-\delta_0$ has), so the total mass is $k^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}(0)=k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z)$. (b) The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2} is written for a selection $\mathfrak s=(\mathcal D,\mathsf P,Z)$ and bounds factor by factor; the enlarged seed enters only through $E_0=E(Z_0)$ in the density \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-density}, whose modulus is paid against $e^{(\alpha_G/2)\|B\|^2}$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment}(ii) for every seed $Z_0$ and every $Z\supseteq Z_0'$ (the lemma is stated for every seed), through the cutoffs $\chi^t_b$ over $E(Y_0)$, each $\leq1$, and through the bad bonds $\mathsf P\subseteq D(\Lambda)\setminus E(Y_0)$, each paying $e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{(\alpha_G/2)h(B_b)^2}$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2}; the Mayer factors are those of $Y\in\mathcal D$ only, each bounded by $\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$ on the support of its cutoffs; a probe host contributes no factor; the weakening bound $e^{-(\kappa_1-1)}$ per cube of $Z\setminus Z_0'$ is unchanged. (c) is the positive host comparison of \draftref{stage 4, \S7.1}: at a host $Y$ the sum over nonempty label subsets of the positive weights $v_{\mathrm{ordinary}}=e^{A_Y}-1$, $v_{\mathrm{probe}}=u_*$ is at most $\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$, and all split selections with the same active-host set have the same enlarged seed, density, bad set and weakening, so the many-to-one sum is dominated hostwise by the undifferentiated majorant \eqref{eq:c5-5-3} with $F=1$, after which the seed/enlargement/final-domain sums \eqref{eq:native-source-initial-domain-sum}, \eqref{eq:native-source-enlarged-domain-sum} and the $Y'$-sum in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:activity-bounds} give $m(Z)$ exactly as in Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3} (whose proof shows that these sums hold with $\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ in place of $a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ under \eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp}); the comparison needs the ordinary factor's modulus $|\int_0^1\mathbf W_te^{t_Y\mathbf W_t}\,\dd t_Y|=|e^{\mathbf W_t}-1|\leq e^{|\mathbf W_t|}-1\leq e^{A_Y}-1=v_{\mathrm{ordinary}}$ with $A_Y=(\omega_*/2)e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$, which holds because $|\mathbf W_t(Y,B)|\leq\omega_te^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\leq A_Y$ on the support of the host's cutoffs by \eqref{eq:c5-4-4} and \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(ii), and the chain's last step uses $\omega_*\leq1/2$ and $n_Yu_*\leq(\omega_*/8)e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$ (\draftref{stage 4, \S7.1}). The sum includes the split selections with $\mathsf J=\varnothing$ (a host is active through its ordinary label or through probe labels; \draftref{stage 4, \S7.1} sums over all nonempty label subsets), so (c) holds with $\sum_{\ell\, : \,Z(\ell)=Z}$ in place of $\sum_{\ell\, : \,Z(\ell)=Z,\,\mathsf J\neq\varnothing}$. (d) is the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:measure-identity} and Proposition~\ref{prop:centred-expectation} for the translated expansion: both sides of $\mathfrak n_\Lambda^{-1}\Theta^te^{-\frac12\langle B,HB\rangle+\sum\mathbf W_t}\,\dd B=\sum_{\mathcal P}(\mathsf e_{\mathcal P})_*\bigotimes_{Z\in\mathcal P}\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}$ are finite complex measures with compact support and the same Laplace transform (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1} for $\mathbf W_t+L(\lambda,\cdot)$ and (a)), hence equal; expanding $\nu^{f,t}=\hat\nu^{f,t}+k^{f,t}\delta_0$ and dividing by $\mathfrak n_\Lambda\Xi^{f,t}_\Lambda$ gives the first display; the second is \eqref{eq:c5-4-3} with $F=f_\xi$ (bounded on $\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}$, extended by zero off the enlarged box, which contains the support of every $\nu^{f,t}$: $|B_b+tm_b|_\sigma\leq r$ and $|tm_b|_\sigma\leq r/4$ give $|B_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4$ and $|(\mathbf 1_KB+tm)_b|_\sigma\leq3r/2\leq2r$), and the formula for $\mathfrak Q^t$ in terms of $\Delta_{\mathsf J}$ is the integration of $g$ against the Dirac tensors in (a), zero-mass part (the terms with $\mathsf J_Z=\varnothing$ for some $Z$ are removed by the centring).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[joint extraction for the $t$-family]\label{lem:c5-5-3}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
    \item For a connected final domain $Z$ for $D(\Lambda)$ the contact set $\mathsf T_Z:=\{n\, : \,\mathcal F_{r_n}\cap\mathsf L_Z\neq\varnothing\}$ (\eqref{eq:part1-contact-set}, with the convention $\mathcal F_r=\varnothing$ off $\Omega_1$) is field-independent and $t$-independent; if $\mathcal A$ contains an unmarked $Z$ ($\mathsf T_Z=\varnothing$) then $\mathfrak Q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A;f_\xi(\cdot+tm))=0$. The groups $W=(\mathcal A(W),(\mathfrak c_1,\dots,\mathfrak c_{k(W)}),\mathcal H(W))$ of Definition~\ref{def:groups} for the carrier $D(\Lambda)$ form one index set for all $t\in[0,1]$ and for the reference data; their weights are $w^t_\Lambda(W):=((-1)^{k(W)}/k(W)!)\prod_j\Phi^{v,t}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c_j)$ with the cluster weights of Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3}. With $A^t_\Lambda(\mathcal S):=\prod_{W\in\mathcal S}w^t_\Lambda(W)\,\mathfrak Q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A(\mathcal S);f_\xi(\cdot+tm))$, $A^t_\Lambda(\varnothing):=f_\xi(tm)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:grouped-source} holds: $F^\Lambda(t)=\sum_{\mathfrak B\text{ compatible}}A^t_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)$, absolutely convergent.
    \item Reference data at the carrier $D(\Lambda)$: the same $\Omega_1$, cubes, hosts $\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$, constants and cutoffs, with $U^\circ_1=\id$, $V^\circ_0=\id$, $A^\circ=0$, $\Psi^\circ=\id$, exterior variable zero on $J(\Lambda)$ (so $m^\circ=0$, $\ell^\circ=0$, and the reference objects are independent of $t$), $H^\circ=(\mathsf H^{\Omega_1}_{\id})_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}$, $f^\circ(a)=W_\chi(C;e^{iq_{\id}(a)})$ with links off $\Omega_1$ equal to $\id$. Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0} holds for these data (its (a) by principal restriction of the reference Hessian of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}, which satisfies \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-inputs} by Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-reference}; (b) trivially, $r^\circ_{\mathrm e}=0$; (c), (d) as stated), and so do Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1}, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2}, Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3} and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2} (their proofs use the listed lemmas only). Define $a^\Lambda_W:=w^\circ_\Lambda(W)\int\chi(P^\circ_W(a))\,\dd\bigl(\bigotimes_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\hat\nu^{f,\circ}_{\Lambda,Z}\bigr)(a)$ as in \eqref{eq:part1-reference-group}, with $P^\circ_W$ the ordered product of reference links along $\mathcal H(W)$ at the young variable $\mathsf e_{\mathcal A(W)}a$. Then Lemma~\ref{lem:schur} holds: $w^\circ_\Lambda(W)\int\chi(P^\circ_W(a)h)\,\dd\bigotimes\hat\nu^{f,\circ}_\Lambda=a^\Lambda_W\chi(h)$ for $h\in G$, and $S^\circ_\Lambda:=\sum_{\mathfrak B\text{ compatible}}\prod_{W\in\mathfrak B}a^\Lambda_W=\int f^\circ K^\circ_\Lambda\,\dd a/I^\circ_\Lambda$.
    \item With $R^t_\Lambda(\mathfrak B):=\sum_{\mathcal S\subseteq\mathfrak B}(-1)^{|\mathfrak B\setminus\mathcal S|}\bigl(\prod_{W\in\mathfrak B\setminus\mathcal S}a^\Lambda_W\bigr)A^t_\Lambda(\mathcal S)$ and $S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda$ the reference sum over families compatible with every member of $\mathfrak B$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-5}
        F^\Lambda(t)=S^\circ_\Lambda f_\xi(tm)+\sum_{\varnothing\neq\mathfrak B\text{ compatible}}S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda R^t_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)\;,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]=S^\circ_\Lambda[f_\xi(m)-f_\xi(0)]+\sum_{\varnothing\neq\mathfrak B}S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda[R^1_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)-R^0_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)]\;.
    \end{equation}
    \item $|a^\Lambda_W|\leq M_\Lambda(W)$, the positive group majorant of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7}(d) below ($(1/k(W)!)\prod_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\rho_tm(Z)\prod_j|\Phi|^+_\Lambda(\mathfrak c_j)$, defined without reference to the source data). Under \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(iv) the reference group gas with the activities $a^\Lambda_W$ satisfies the Kotecký--Preiss condition, so that $S^\circ_\Lambda\neq0$, $q^\circ_\Lambda(\mathfrak B):=S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda/S^\circ_\Lambda=\exp(-\sum_{\Gamma\text{ hits }\mathfrak B}\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma))$ (the cluster expansion of the reference group gas, \draftref{attempt, \S13, \S19.4}, at the carrier $D(\Lambda)$; a cluster hits $\mathfrak B$ when it contains a group incompatible with a member of $\mathfrak B$), and $|q^\circ_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)|\leq\prod_{W\in\mathfrak B}e^{\theta^\circ(W)}$ with $\theta^\circ(W):=\sum_{\Gamma\text{ hits }W}|\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma)|\leq\nu\sigma(W)/16$, where $\sigma(W)$ and $\nu$ are the size and the reserve constant of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}; and the rooted bound $\sum_{\Gamma\ni W}|\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma)|\leq|a^\Lambda_W|e^{13\nu\sigma(W)/8}$ holds. All of (d) holds verbatim for the reference gas at the carrier $I$ (the reference data of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}, activities $a_W$, cluster coefficients $\Phi^\circ_\xi$), since its proof uses only the lemmas of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} and Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} at the carrier, which hold at $I$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) A traversal's link depends on the young variable through $a|_{\mathcal F_{r_n}\cap D(\Lambda)}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:row-locality}; the de-younged bonds are constants); for unmarked $Z$ the function $B\mapsto f_\xi(\mathbf 1_KB+tm)$ does not depend on $B|_{\mathsf L_Z}$, so the $K$-sum over the labels of $\mathsf J_Z$ telescopes to zero in \eqref{eq:c5-5-4} (equivalently, $\hat\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}$ has zero mass). The groups are built from roots, cluster lists (tuples of domains), contact sets and hulls, none of which involves the fields or $t$; only $w^t_\Lambda$ and $\hat\nu^{f,t}_\Lambda$ depend on $t$. The regrouping proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:grouped-source} is combinatorial, and its absolute convergence uses $|\mathfrak Q^t_\Lambda(\mathcal A;g)|\leq\sup|g|\prod_Z\|\hat\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}\|$ (finite by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(a)) and the hitting sum $\sum_{\mathfrak c\text{ hits }\mathcal A}|\Phi^{v,t}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c)|\leq\frac18\sum_{Z\in\mathcal A}N_1(Z)$ of Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3}. (b) The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:schur} uses the constant gauge $R_u$: $H^\circ=R_uH^\circ R_u^{-1}$ holds for the principal restriction because $R_u$ acts bondwise; the domains, weights, cutoffs (Ad-invariant norm), the Gaussian density, the probe term and the reconstruction covariance $q_{\id}(R_uB)=R_uq_{\id}(B)$ are as in Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}, and the hosts and partitions are field-independent; Schur's lemma and the Fubini evaluation of the group product are verbatim. (c) is the Boolean inversion and interchange of absolutely convergent sums of Proposition~\ref{prop:joint-extraction}, with the bound $\beta_W$ of its proof formed from $w^t_\Lambda$, $w^\circ_\Lambda$, $\hat\nu^{f,t}_\Lambda$, $\hat\nu^{f,\circ}_\Lambda$; the $t$-independence of $a^\Lambda_W$ and $S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda$ gives the second form. (d) The activity bound: $a^\Lambda_W=w^\circ_\Lambda(W)\int g^\circ_W\,\dd\bigotimes_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\hat\nu^{f,\circ}_{\Lambda,Z}$ with $g^\circ_W(a):=\chi(P^\circ_W(a))$, a closed Wilson contraction at the reference data; by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(a),(d) for the reference data (part (b)), $\int g^\circ_W\,\dd\bigotimes\hat\nu^\circ=\sum_{(\ell_Z)_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\, : \,\mathsf J_Z\neq\varnothing}\int\Delta_{\mathsf J}g^\circ_W\,\dd\bigotimes_Z\Lambda^{f,\circ}_{\Lambda;\ell_Z}$; Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}(b) at the reference data gives $\sup|\Delta_{\mathsf J}g^\circ_W|\leq u_t^{|\mathsf J|}$ (base point $a_0=0$, increments $|B_b|_\sigma\leq r\leq5r/4$ on the support of $\nu^{f,\circ}$, $m^\circ=0$), and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(c) for the reference data with $(u_t/u_*)^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\leq\rho_t$ gives $|\int g^\circ_W\,\dd\bigotimes\hat\nu^\circ|\leq\prod_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\rho_tm(Z)$; $|w^\circ_\Lambda(W)|\leq(1/k(W)!)\prod_j|\Phi^{v,\circ}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c_j)|\leq(1/k(W)!)\prod_j|\Phi|^+_\Lambda(\mathfrak c_j)$ by Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3} for the reference data and the tree-graph inequality (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-6}(b)). The Kotecký--Preiss condition: by the first display of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(a), which uses only the majorant $M_\Lambda$, $\sup_\Delta\sum_{W\, : \,\Delta\in F(W)}e^{4\nu\sigma(W)}M_\Lambda(W)+\sup_n\sum_{W\, : \,n\in\mathcal H(W)}e^{4\nu\sigma(W)}M_\Lambda(W)\leq C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t$; two groups are incompatible only if their footprints touch (contact includes adjacency across a face, edge or corner, not only intersection: \draftref{stage 4, \S12.4}) or their intervals overlap (\draftref{stage 4, \S6.2}); a footprint has at most $A_F\sigma(W)$ cubes (\draftref{attempt, \S19.3}), so an incompatible $W'$ has a footprint cube in the set $N_1(F(W))$ of cubes touching $F(W)$, of at most $3^4A_F\sigma(W)$ cubes (the neighbour factor $3^4$), or a traversal in $\mathcal H(W)$, of at most $LM\sigma(W)$ traversals ($|\mathcal H(W)|/(LM)\leq\sigma(W)$); hence $\sum_{W'\text{ incompatible with }W}|a^\Lambda_{W'}|e^{3\nu\sigma(W')/2}e^{\nu\sigma(W')/8}\leq\sum_{\Delta\in N_1(F(W))}\sum_{W'\, : \,\Delta\in F(W')}(\dots)+\sum_{n\in\mathcal H(W)}\sum_{W'\, : \,n\in\mathcal H(W')}(\dots)\leq(3^4A_F+LM)\sigma(W)C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t\leq\nu\sigma(W)/8$ under \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(iv): this is the Kotecký--Preiss hypothesis for the gas with activities $|a^\Lambda_W|e^{3\nu\sigma(W)/2}$ and node cost $\nu\sigma(W)/8$, and the positive tree bound of \draftref{attempt, \S19.4} (the Kotecký--Preiss theorem in that form) gives the absolute convergence of the cluster series, $S^\circ_\Lambda=\exp(\sum_\Gamma\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma))\neq0$, the exclusion identity $S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda=\exp(\sum_{\Gamma\text{ hits no member of }\mathfrak B}\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma))$ (the same with the species incompatible with a member of $\mathfrak B$ excluded), and the rooted bound $\sum_{\Gamma\ni W}m_W(\Gamma)|\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma)|\leq|a^\Lambda_W|e^{13\nu\sigma(W)/8}$, $m_W(\Gamma)$ the occurrence multiplicity. Hence $\theta^\circ(W)\leq\sum_{W'=W\text{ or incompatible with }W}\sum_{\Gamma\ni W'}|\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma)|\leq\sum_{W'=W\text{ or incompatible with }W}|a^\Lambda_{W'}|e^{13\nu\sigma(W')/8}\leq(3^4A_F+LM+1)\sigma(W)C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t\leq\nu\sigma(W)/16$ under \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(iv) ($e^{13\nu\sigma/8}\leq e^{4\nu\sigma}$, and the term $W'=W$ is bounded by the footprint pin at a cube of $F(W)$).
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{The two terms of \eqref{eq:c5-5-5}}
The first is the shift of the loop at zero fluctuation; the second is the difference of the joint defects, which is where the source-containing connected boundary terms live.

\begin{lemma}[the shift term]\label{lem:c5-5-4}
$|f_\xi(m)-f_\xi(0)|\leq C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}g_0\sum_{b\in I_C}h(m_b)\leq C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}C_m\vartheta N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)g_0r\sum_{e\in J_1(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu_H\operatorname{dist}(e,I_C)/2}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$f_\xi(m)-f_\xi(0)=\int_0^1\sum_{b\in I_C}\langle m_b,\nabla_bf_\xi(sm)\rangle\,\dd s$; the points $sm$ lie in the box $|a_b|_\sigma\leq r/4$ by \eqref{eq:c5-4-1}, inside the enlarged box, where $\sup h(\nabla_bf_\xi)\leq C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}g_0\mathbf 1_{I_C}(b)$ (the source gradient bound, as used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-4}); then \eqref{eq:c5-5-2}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[conditional-Gaussian shell bound]\label{lem:c5-5-5}
Let $b\in E_0$, $S_b:=\{x\in\mathfrak g\, : \,r-|m_b|_\sigma\leq|x|_\sigma<r+|m_b|_\sigma\}$, $v\in\mathfrak g^{E_0}$ real, and $0\leq\alpha\leq\alpha_G$ ($\alpha_G\leq\gamma/2$; $h(x)^2=\langle x,x\rangle$ is the squared norm of the invariant inner product). Write $\mu_{C_{E_0}}(\dd B)=\mu_{C'}(\dd B')\,N(\mu(B'),c_b)(\dd B_b)$ for the disintegration of the centred Gaussian of covariance $C_{E_0}=(\iota^*_{E_0}H\iota_{E_0})^{-1}$ along the coordinate $b$ ($B'$ the other coordinates, $N(\mu,c)$ the Gaussian on $\mathfrak g$ with mean $\mu$ and covariance $c$), where $c_b=((C_{E_0}^{-1})_{bb})^{-1}=(H_{bb})^{-1}$ and $\mu(B')$ is linear in $B'$. Then for every $B'$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-6}
    \int\mathbf 1_{S_b}(B_b)e^{-\langle B_b,v_b\rangle+(\alpha/2)h(B_b)^2}\,N(\mu(B'),c_b)(\dd B_b)\leq\varpi_b\int e^{-\langle B_b,v_b\rangle+(\alpha/2)h(B_b)^2}\,N(\mu(B'),c_b)(\dd B_b)\;,\qquad\varpi_b:=(h_+/2\pi)^{d_G/2}\operatorname{Leb}(S_b)\leq C_Vr^{d_G-1}|m_b|_\sigma\;,
\end{equation}
$C_V:=(h_+/2\pi)^{d_G/2}\,2d_GV_\sigma(5/4)^{d_G-1}$, $V_\sigma$ the volume of the unit $|\cdot|_\sigma$-ball of $\mathfrak g$ for the Lebesgue measure of the $h$-inner product. Consequently $\varpi_b\leq C_\varpi r^{d_G}\sum_{e\in J_1(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,e)}$, $C_\varpi:=C_Vc_hC_m\vartheta$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The conditional law of $B_b$ given $B'$ under a centred Gaussian of covariance $C$ is Gaussian with covariance $((C^{-1})_{bb})^{-1}$ and mean linear in $B'$; here $C^{-1}=\iota^*H\iota$, whose $bb$ block is $H_{bb}$. Completing the square: the density of $e^{-\langle x,v\rangle+(\alpha/2)\langle x,x\rangle}N(\mu,c_b)(\dd x)$ is a constant times $\exp(-\tfrac12\langle x,(H_{bb}-\alpha\,\id)x\rangle+\langle x,H_{bb}\mu-v\rangle)$, and $H_{bb}-\alpha\,\id\geq(\gamma-\alpha_G)\id\geq(\gamma/2)\id>0$ ($H_{bb}\geq\gamma\,\id$ as a principal restriction of $H$, (I3)), so $e^{-\langle x,v\rangle+(\alpha/2)\langle x,x\rangle}N(\mu,c_b)(\dd x)=Z_\alpha N(\mu_\alpha,c_\alpha)(\dd x)$ with $c_\alpha:=(H_{bb}-\alpha\,\id)^{-1}$, $\mu_\alpha:=c_\alpha(H_{bb}\mu-v)$ and a positive constant $Z_\alpha$ (for $\alpha=0$: $Z_0=e^{-\langle\mu,v\rangle+\frac12\langle v,c_bv\rangle}$, $\mu_0=\mu-c_bv$); hence the left side of \eqref{eq:c5-5-6} equals $Z_\alpha N(\mu_\alpha,c_\alpha)(S_b)$ and the right side's integral equals $Z_\alpha$; the Gaussian density of $N(\mu_\alpha,c_\alpha)$ is at most $(2\pi)^{-d_G/2}\det(c_\alpha)^{-1/2}=(2\pi)^{-d_G/2}\det(H_{bb}-\alpha\,\id)^{1/2}\leq(2\pi)^{-d_G/2}\det(H_{bb})^{1/2}\leq(h_+/2\pi)^{d_G/2}$ ($H_{bb}\leq h_+\id$ by (I3)), whence $N(\mu_\alpha,c_\alpha)(S_b)\leq(h_+/2\pi)^{d_G/2}\operatorname{Leb}(S_b)$.
