By homogeneity of the norm, $\operatorname{Leb}(\{|x|_\sigma<s\})=V_\sigma s^{d_G}$, so $\operatorname{Leb}(S_b)=V_\sigma[(r+\epsilon)^{d_G}-(r-\epsilon)^{d_G}]$ with $\epsilon=|m_b|_\sigma\leq r/4$, and the mean value theorem gives $\leq V_\sigma\cdot2\epsilon\cdot d_G(5r/4)^{d_G-1}$. The last bound is $|m_b|_\sigma\leq c_hh(m_b)$ (Remark~\ref{rem:c5-1-3}) and \eqref{eq:c5-5-1}.
\end{proof}

The point of \eqref{eq:c5-5-6} is that the shell is paid by the Gaussian itself, with the tilt $e^{-\langle B,v\rangle}$ (which in the density \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-density} is $e^{-\langle B,\operatorname{Re}\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X\rangle}$ after moduli are taken) kept intact: the ratio $\varpi_b$ is of order $r^{d_G-1}|m_b|$ and does not involve the reserve $a_{\mathrm{bad}}$. The alternative sup-over-$B_b$ thin-shell bound would cost $e^{25h_+r^2/32}$ per mark, which $e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}$ cannot pay ($\alpha_G\leq\gamma/2<h_+$), and is not used.

\begin{lemma}[marked differences]\label{lem:c5-5-6}
Define, for a bond $b\in D(\Lambda)$ and a host $Y\in\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$, the mark weights
\begin{align*}
    \mathfrak m_b&:=2\varpi_b\leq2C_\varpi r^{d_G}\sum_{e\in J_1(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,e)}\quad\text{(one $\varpi_b$ for the bond's own translated cutoff, one for the cutoffs of the hosts containing $b$, paid by \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(iii))},\\
    \mathfrak m_Y&:=2\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}\cdot e^{\kappa(B_h+4)}/\omega_*\cdot e^{1/8}\quad\text{if some near primitive assigned to $Y$ has an input in $J(\Lambda)$},\\
    \mathfrak m_Y&:=N_hC_pg_0\bar s^2(1+\bar\omega)e^{\bar\omega}\cdot e^{\kappa(B_h+4)}/\omega_*\cdot e^{1/8}\sum_{b\in E(Y)}h(m_b)\quad\text{otherwise},
\end{align*}
and for a union $Z$ of $LM$-cubes $\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z):=\sum_{b\in E(Z)}\mathfrak m_b+\sum_{Y\in\mathbf D_0(\Lambda),\,Y\subseteq Z}\mathfrak m_Y$. Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
    \item For every split selection $\ell$ with $Z(\ell)=Z$ ($\mathsf J=\varnothing$ allowed) and every bounded Borel $F$, $|\int F\,\dd(\Lambda^{f,1}_{\Lambda;\ell}-\Lambda^{f,0}_{\Lambda;\ell})|\leq\sup|F|\cdot\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z)\cdot e^{-(\kappa_1-1)N_1(Z\setminus Z_0')+N_1(Z)-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}|\mathsf P|}\prod_{Y\in\mathcal D}\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$ (the majorant \eqref{eq:c5-5-3} times the sum of the mark weights; the division of a host mark by the host's majorant factor is built into $\mathfrak m_Y$); and $\sum_{\ell\, : \,Z(\ell)=Z}u_*^{|\mathsf J|}\|\Lambda^{f,1}_{\Lambda;\ell}-\Lambda^{f,0}_{\Lambda;\ell}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z)m(Z)$.
    \item $|k^{f,1}_\Lambda(Z)-k^{f,0}_\Lambda(Z)|\leq\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z)m(Z)$, and for a cluster $\mathfrak c=(D_1,\dots,D_n)$ with the positive incompatibility-tree majorant $|\Phi|^+_\Lambda(\mathfrak c):=(1/n!)\sum_{T\in\mathrm{Trees}_n}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in T}\mathbf 1\{D_i\not\sim D_j\}\prod_im(D_i)$ (the $M_v(\mathfrak c)$ of \draftref{attempt, \S18.3}; $|\Phi|^+_\Lambda(\mathfrak c)\geq\max_t|\Phi^{v,t}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c)|$ by the tree-graph inequality $|\varphi^T(\mathfrak c)|\leq\#\{$spanning trees of the incompatibility graph of $\mathfrak c\}$, proved in the justification following \eqref{eq:marginal-pinned-bound} and used in Appendix~\ref{app:kp}, and by $|k^{f,t}_\Lambda(D_i)|\leq m(D_i)$, Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3}), $|\Phi^{v,1}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c)-\Phi^{v,0}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c)|\leq\bigl(\sum_i\mathfrak M_\Lambda(D_i)\bigr)|\Phi|^+_\Lambda(\mathfrak c)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) In the display of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1} for the enlarged data, the $t$-dependent factors are $\chi^t_b$ ($b\in E(Y_0)$), $1-\chi^t_b$ ($b\in\mathsf P$) and the Mayer factors $M^t_Y:=\int_0^1\mathbf W_t(Y,B)e^{t_Y\mathbf W_t(Y,B)}\,\dd t_Y$ ($Y\in\mathcal D$), with $\mathbf W_t(Y,B)=\Theta^t_{E(Y)}(B)\mathbf P_t(Y,B)$, $\mathbf P_t(Y,B):=\sum_{\mathfrak p\text{ near},\,Y_{\mathfrak p}=Y}\mathfrak p(B+tm+tA^{\Lambda,\Psi})$, $\Theta^t_{E(Y)}:=\prod_{b\in E(Y)}\chi^t_b$; the density, the Dirac tensor's pushforward (through $F$) and the $s$-, $\sigma$-integrations are $t$-independent. Order these factors (bonds first, then hosts) and telescope: $\prod a_k-\prod b_k=\sum_k(\prod_{i<k}a_i)(a_k-b_k)(\prod_{i>k}b_i)$, $a$ the $t=1$ and $b$ the $t=0$ factors. Each term has exactly one differenced factor, the mark. In every term each bond of $E(Y_0)\cup\mathsf P$ carries a factor $\chi^1_b$, $\chi^0_b$, $1-\chi^t_b$ or a difference of two of these, so on the support $|B_b|_\sigma<5r/4$ for $b\in E(Y_0)$ ($|B_b+tm_b|_\sigma<r$, $|m_b|_\sigma\leq r/4$), and every host's primitives are evaluated in the enlarged box (young part of $B+tm+tA^{\Lambda,\Psi}$ of norm $<3r/2\leq2r$), where they are analytic with $|\mathfrak p|\leq C_pg_0\bar s^3$ and $\|D\mathfrak p\|\leq C_pg_0\bar s^2$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c), Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-primitive-bounds} on the chart containing the enlarged box under threshold item 3), so $|\mathbf P_t(Y,B)|\leq N_hC_pg_0\bar s^3\leq\bar\omega$ for both $t$. Bond mark at $b$: $|\chi^1_b-\chi^0_b|\leq\mathbf 1_{S_b}(B_b)$ and $|(1-\chi^1_b)-(1-\chi^0_b)|\leq\mathbf 1_{S_b}(B_b)$, since the two indicators differ only where $|B_b|_\sigma$ is within $|m_b|_\sigma$ of $r$. Host mark at $Y\in\mathcal D$: in its term the bond factors are at $t=1$, and $E(Y)\subseteq E(Y_0)\setminus\mathsf P$ (Definition~\ref{def:selection}: $\mathsf P\cap E(Y_0)=\varnothing$), so $\Theta^1_{E(Y)}=1$ on the support and
\begin{equation*}
    M^1_Y-M^0_Y=\int_0^1\bigl[\mathbf P_1e^{t_Y\mathbf P_1}-\mathbf P_0e^{t_Y\mathbf P_0}\bigr]\dd t_Y+(1-\Theta^0_{E(Y)})\int_0^1\mathbf P_0e^{t_Y\mathbf P_0}\,\dd t_Y;
\end{equation*}
the smooth part is bounded on the support by $2\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}$ always, and, when no primitive of $Y$ has an input in $J(\Lambda)$, by $\int_0^1|\partial_t(\mathbf P_te^{t_Y\mathbf P_t})|\,\dd t\leq(1+\bar\omega)e^{\bar\omega}\sup_t|\partial_t\mathbf P_t|$ with $\partial_t\mathbf P_t(Y,B)=\sum_{\mathfrak p}D\mathfrak p(B+tm)[m|_{E(Y)}]$ (no exterior input), $|\partial_t\mathbf P_t|\leq N_hC_pg_0\bar s^2\sum_{b\in E(Y)}h(m_b)$; the cutoff part satisfies $0\leq1-\Theta^0_{E(Y)}\leq\sum_{b\in E(Y)}(1-\chi^0_b)$, and on the support of $\chi^1_b$, $(1-\chi^0_b)\chi^1_b\leq\mathbf 1_{S_b}(B_b)$, with $|\int_0^1\mathbf P_0e^{t_Y\mathbf P_0}\,\dd t_Y|\leq\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}$: it is a sum over $b\in E(Y)$ of bond marks $\mathbf 1_{S_b}$ in which the host's factor is replaced by $\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}$. Now bound each term. Take moduli; every factor other than the mark and the density is bounded by its supremum as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2} (cutoffs $\leq1$; bad bonds by $e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{(\alpha_G/2)h(B_b)^2}$, from $\mathbf 1\{|B_b|_\sigma\geq3r/4\}\leq e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{(\alpha_G/2)h(B_b)^2}$; undifferenced Mayer factors by $\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$, valid at either $t$ since $\Theta^t_{E(Y)}$ restricts $B+tm$ to the local box of $Y$, where \eqref{eq:c5-4-4} and \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(ii) give $|\mathbf W_t|\leq(\omega_*/2)e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$; $|F|\leq\sup|F|$); the modulus of the density is $e^{-\frac12\operatorname{Re}\langle\Gamma(\sigma)X,C\Gamma(\sigma)X\rangle}e^{-\langle B,v\rangle}\,\dd\mu_{C_{E_0}}(B)\,\dd\mu_0(X)$ with $v:=\operatorname{Re}\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X$ real. At a bond mark $b\in E(Y_0)$ ($b\notin\mathsf P$): integrate over $B_b$ first; by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-5} with $\alpha=0$ the mark $\mathbf 1_{S_b}$ is replaced by $\varpi_b$, leaving $\int e^{-\langle B,v\rangle}e^{(\alpha_G/2)\sum_{b'\in\mathsf P}h(B_{b'})^2}\,\dd\mu_C\leq\int e^{-\langle B,v\rangle}e^{(\alpha_G/2)\|B\|^2}\,\dd\mu_C$, which Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment}(ii) pays with $e^{N_1(Z)}$ together with the $X$-integral. At a bond mark $b\in\mathsf P$: on $S_b$, $|B_b|_\sigma\geq r-|m_b|_\sigma\geq3r/4$, so $\mathbf 1_{S_b}(B_b)\leq\mathbf 1_{S_b}(B_b)e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{(\alpha_G/2)h(B_b)^2}$, the reserve of $b$ is kept, and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-5} with $\alpha=\alpha_G$ (the conditional Gaussian in $B_b$ with the tilt and the factor $e^{(\alpha_G/2)h(B_b)^2}$ on $b$) replaces the mark by $\varpi_b$ with all factors $e^{(\alpha_G/2)h(B_{b'})^2}$, $b'\in\mathsf P$, in place, so the bad-bond payment $e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}|\mathsf P|}$ is complete and the Gaussian payment is as before. Hence a bond-marked term is bounded by $\varpi_b$ times the majorant \eqref{eq:c5-5-3}. At a host mark $Y$, the smooth part is a constant, $2\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}$ or $N_hC_pg_0\bar s^2(1+\bar\omega)e^{\bar\omega}\sum_{E(Y)}h(m_b)$, in place of the host's majorant factor $\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\geq\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa(B_h+4)}$; the quotient (constant)$/(\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)})$ is at most (constant)$\cdot e^{\kappa(B_h+4)}/\omega_*$ ($d_0(Y)\leq B_h+4$, $e^{1/2}\geq1$), which is $\mathfrak m_Y$ without its factor $e^{1/8}$, reserved for the dummy comparison below; the cutoff part gives, for each $b\in E(Y)$, a bond mark $\mathbf 1_{S_b}$ ($b\notin\mathsf P$), paid by $\varpi_b$ as above, with the host's factor $\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}$ in place of $\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$, a quotient $\leq\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}e^{\kappa(B_h+4)}/\omega_*\leq e\bar\omega e^{\kappa(B_h+4)}/\omega_*\leq e^{-1/8}/N_{\mathrm{host}}\leq1/N_{\mathrm{host}}$ by \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(iii); the hosts $Y\in\mathcal D$ with $b\in E(Y)$ number at most $N_{\mathrm{host}}$ (Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-2}), so these contribute at most $\varpi_b$ per bond $b$, which with the bond's own mark $\varpi_b$ gives $\mathfrak m_b=2\varpi_b$; the rest of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2} (weakening per cube, Gaussian payment) is unchanged. Summing over the marks gives the first assertion with $\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z)$ (every mark bond lies in $E(Y_0)\cup\mathsf P\subseteq E(Z)$, every marked host in $\mathcal D$ lies in $Z_0'\subseteq Z$). For the dummy comparison with a mark, repeat the positive host comparison of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(c) with the marked factor: at the bond's own mark (the differenced factor is $\chi^t_b$ or $1-\chi^t_b$) the hostwise comparison is unchanged (the undifferenced hosts have $v_{\mathrm{ordinary}}=e^{A_Y}-1$ at either $t$, as above) and the mark $\varpi_b$ multiplies the whole; at a bond mark generated by the cutoff part of a host $Y\in\mathcal D$, the host $Y$ is active through its ordinary label with its factor replaced by $\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}$, so the sum over $Y$'s label subsets containing the ordinary label is $\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}(1+u_*)^{n_Y}\leq\bar\omega e^{\bar\omega}e^{1/8}$, whose quotient by $Y$'s majorant factor $\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$ is at most $e^{9/8}\bar\omega e^{\kappa(B_h+4)}/\omega_*\leq1/N_{\mathrm{host}}$ by \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(iii) (this is what the factor $e^{9/8}$ in (iii) is for), and the at most $N_{\mathrm{host}}$ such hosts again contribute at most $\varpi_b$ per bond, so $\mathfrak m_b=2\varpi_b$ suffices in the dummy comparison as well; at a host mark $Y$ the ordinary weight $v_{\mathrm{ordinary}}$ of $Y$ is replaced by the mark's constant, and $\sum_{\varnothing\neq S}\prod_{\ell'\in S}v_{\ell'}\leq(\text{constant})\cdot(1+u_*)^{n_Y}\leq(\text{constant})\cdot e^{1/8}$ ($n_Yu_*\leq\omega_*/8\leq1/16<1/8$), so the host's contribution is $\leq\mathfrak m_Y\cdot\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$, the factor $e^{1/8}$ being the one in $\mathfrak m_Y$; the remaining sums give $m(Z)$ as in Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(c), and dropping the constraint that the mark lies in the selection only enlarges the sum. (b) $k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z)=\sum_{(\mathcal D,\mathsf P)}\mathcal A^{f,t}_{(\mathcal D,\mathsf P,Z)}[1]$ is the $\mathsf J=\varnothing$, $F=1$ case of (a), summed over the selections, which yields $\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z)m(Z)$ by the same seed/enlargement sums; the cluster bound is the telescoping of the product $\prod_ik^{f,t}_\Lambda(D_i)$ with $|k^{f,t}_\Lambda(D_i)|\leq m(D_i)$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3}) and the tree-graph inequality for $|\varphi^T(\mathfrak c)|$.
\end{proof}

The mark weights are small where it matters: $\mathfrak m_b$ is of order $r^{d_G}$ times the decay from $b$ to $J_1(\Lambda)$; a host carrying an exterior input lies within the stencil range of $J(\Lambda)$ (its near primitive has an input in $J(\Lambda)$ and an input in $D(\Lambda)$), and its mark $\mathfrak m_Y\leq C_{\mathrm{bd}}g_0(1+3r)^3$ with $C_{\mathrm{bd}}:=2e\cdot e^{\kappa(B_h+4)}e^{1/8}C_0/\omega_*$ is small in the coupling ($g_0(1+r)^3\to0$) though not by a threshold; a host without exterior input has $\mathfrak m_Y\leq C'_{\mathrm{bd}}g_0(1+3r)^2\sum_{E(Y)}h(m_b)$ with $C'_{\mathrm{bd}}:=2e\cdot N_hC_pe^{\kappa(B_h+4)}e^{1/8}/\omega_*$ ($(1+\bar\omega)e^{\bar\omega}\leq2e$ for $\bar\omega\leq1$), which carries the decay of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-1}.

\begin{lemma}[the finite-difference bound for closed contractions]\label{lem:c5-5-7z}
Let $\pi$ be the irreducible unitary representation with $\chi=\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}\pi$ ($\pi$ also denotes the Lie-algebra representation, extended complex-linearly to $\mathfrak g_{\mathbb C}=\mathfrak g\oplus i\mathfrak g$, and $\pi$ denotes as well the holomorphic extension to the complexified group, with $\pi(e^{iX})=e^{i\pi(X)}$ for $X\in\mathfrak g_{\mathbb C}$), $h_{\mathbb C}(x+iy)^2:=h(x)^2+h(y)^2$ the Hermitian extension of $h$ to $\mathfrak g_{\mathbb C}$, $C_\pi:=\sup_{0\neq x\in\mathfrak g}\|\pi(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}/h(x)$, so that $\|\pi(x+iy)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_\pi(h(x)+h(y))\leq\sqrt2C_\pi h_{\mathbb C}(x+iy)$, $m_*:=1+\max_n|\mathcal F_{r_n}|$ (one more than the largest free-input set of a traversal link; $\mathcal F_r\subseteq\mathcal E_c\cap E_*$ for the row $c$ of $r$, so $m_*$ depends on $L$ only), $N_{\mathrm{tr}}:=\max_b\#\{\text{traversals }n\text{ of }C\, : \,b\in\mathcal F_{r_n}\}$ ($b$ lies in the two-block set $\mathcal E_c$ of boundedly many rows $c$, and a traversal with input $b$ is the free bond $b$ itself or the designated bond $b_0(c)$ of such a row, each traversed once by the square, so $N_{\mathrm{tr}}$ depends on $L$ only). For a row $c$ within $2R_{\mathrm c}+2L$ of $I$, Lemmas~\ref{lem:charts} and~\ref{lem:row-locality} give the phase $q_U(B)|_{\mathcal E_c}$ as an analytic function $\Phi_c$ of the row phase $A=g_0\mathsf C_UB|_{\mathcal E_c}$ on a complex neighbourhood of the closed ball $\|A\|\leq\vartheta_*/2$ (the chart statements of Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-primitive-bounds}: the row solution and the phase are analytic there), with values in $\mathfrak g_{\mathbb C}^{\mathcal E_c}$ and $h_{\mathbb C}(\Phi_c(A)(b))\leq Q_*$ for every $b\in\mathcal E_c$ on that ball, $Q_*$ a constant depending on $L$ and the group only; put $C_\Pi:=e^{\sqrt2C_\pi Q_*}$ (so $\|\pi(e^{i\Phi_c(A)(b)})\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|e^{i\pi(\Phi_c(A)(b))}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq e^{\|\pi(\Phi_c(A)(b))\|}\leq e^{\sqrt2C_\pi h_{\mathbb C}(\Phi_c(A)(b))}\leq C_\Pi$ on the ball) and $C_{\mathrm{link}}:=\max(C_\pi,4m_*C_\Pi C_{\mathsf C}/\vartheta_*)$, $C'_\chi:=2N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}$; all depend on $L$, the group and the character only. Under threshold item 3 of Appendix~\ref{app:thresholds} ($4C_{\mathsf C}g_0(1+2r)\leq\vartheta_*/2$), on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ the row $c$ of every traversal $n$ of $C$ has its free-input set $\mathcal F_{r_n}\subseteq D(\Lambda)$, and the row phase $A=g_0\mathsf C_Ua|_{\mathcal E_c}$ depends on $a|_{\mathcal F_{r_n}}$ only (Lemma~\ref{lem:row-locality}), so the exterior variable $A^{\Lambda,\Psi}$ (supported on $J(\Lambda)$, disjoint from $D(\Lambda)$) does not enter a traversal link at all; for every real $a$ in the enlarged box $\{|a_b|_\sigma\leq2r,\ b\in D(\Lambda)\}$ the input of the row lies in the row polydisc $\mathcal U_c=\{|B(b)|<2+4r\}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-primitive-bounds} (every input bond has $|\cdot|_\sigma\leq2r<2+4r$), and the chart statements give $\|A\|\leq2C_{\mathsf C}g_0(1+2r)\leq\vartheta_*/4$ for the row phase. At the reference data (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b)) the exterior variable is zero and the same holds. ``The enlarged box'' below means this.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
    \item (Link derivatives.) For a traversal link $L_n(a):=U_\xi(a)(r_n)=e^{iq_U(\Psi_\xi(a+A_{\mathrm e}))(r_n)}U_1(r_n)$ (\eqref{eq:c-reconstructed-field}; it depends on $a$ through $a|_{\mathcal F_{r_n}}$ only, Lemma~\ref{lem:row-locality}), every real $a$ in the enlarged box, every $k\leq m_*$ and every bonds $b_1,\dots,b_k\in\mathcal F_{r_n}$ (repetitions allowed) with $h$-unit directions $e_1,\dots,e_k\in\mathfrak g$: $\|\partial_{e_1,b_1}\cdots\partial_{e_k,b_k}\pi(L_n)(a)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0)^k$. Consequently $\|\partial_s\pi(L_n(a+sm))\|\leq C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\sum_{b\in\mathcal F_{r_n}}h(m_b)$ and $\|\pi(L_n(a+m))-\pi(L_n(a))\|\leq C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\sum_{b\in\mathcal F_{r_n}}h(m_b)$ whenever the segment from $a$ to $a+m$ lies in the enlarged box.
    \item (Mixed finite differences, with fixed insertions.) Let $g(a)=\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(X_1(a)\cdots X_p(a))$, where each factor $X_i(a)$ is either a traversal link $\pi(L_{n_i}(a))$ of $C$ or its inverse $\pi(L_{n_i}(a))^{-1}=\pi(L_{n_i}(a))^*$ (the orientation-reversed traversals of the Wilson word; at real points its real-direction derivatives are the adjoints of those of $\pi(L_{n_i})$, with the same norms, so (a) applies to it), with every traversal $n$ of $C$ occurring at most once among the link factors (as in the contour word, its inverse and their sub-words), or a fixed matrix $N_i\in\operatorname{End}(V_\pi)$ not depending on $a$, and put $\|N\|:=\prod_{i\, : \,X_i\text{ fixed}}\|N_i\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ ($\|N\|=1$ when every fixed factor is unitary; the closed contractions $\chi(L_{n_1}(a)\cdots L_{n_j}(a)h_0)$, $h_0\in G$, with $h_0$ anywhere in the word, and the words with a fixed unitary insertion such as $\pi(P)^{-1}\pi(h)$ for a word $P$ of links at a fixed argument, are of this kind with $\|N\|=1$). Let $\mathsf J$ be a finite set of bonds of $D(\Lambda)$, $a_0$ real with $|a_{0,b}|_\sigma\leq r/4$ for all $b$ (the base point), and $B$ with $|B_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4$ for $b\in\mathsf J$. Then $\Delta_{\mathsf J}g(B):=\sum_{K\subseteq\mathsf J}(-1)^{|\mathsf J\setminus K|}g(\mathbf 1_KB+a_0)$ satisfies
    \begin{equation*}
        |\Delta_{\mathsf J}g(B)|\leq(N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\cdot5r/4)^{|\mathsf J|}\|N\|\leq u_t^{|\mathsf J|}\|N\|\;,\qquad|\Delta_{\mathsf J}[\partial_{e,b}g](B)|\leq(N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\cdot5r/4)^{|\mathsf J|}N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\|N\|\leq u_t^{|\mathsf J|}(C'_\chi/2)g_0\|N\|
    \end{equation*}
    for every bond $b$ of $D(\Lambda)$ and $h$-unit $e$ ($u_t=C'_\chi g_0(1+2r)=2N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0(1+2r)\geq N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\cdot5r/4$). Both hold at the reference data ($U=\id$, Lemma~\ref{lem:charts}) with the same constants.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) Write $\pi(L_n(a))=\Pi_n(g_0\mathsf C_Ua|_{\mathcal E_c})\pi(U_1(r_n))$ (on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$, where no exterior input enters the row, as in the preamble) with $\Pi_n(A):=\pi(e^{i\Phi_c(A)(b_0(c))})=e^{i\pi(\Phi_c(A)(b_0(c)))}$ for the designated bond $r_n=b_0(c)$ (the component of the row phase at the designated bond), analytic on the neighbourhood of the closed ball $\|A\|\leq\vartheta_*/2$ (composition of the analytic $\Phi_c$ with the entire exponential of the linear map $\pi$ on $\mathfrak g_{\mathbb C}$) with $\|\Pi_n\|\leq C_\Pi$ there by the bound of the statement; for a free bond $r_n$, $q_U(B)(r_n)=g_0B(r_n)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:row-locality}), so $\pi(L_n(a))=e^{ig_0\pi(a_{r_n})}\pi(U_1(r_n))$ depends on $a_{r_n}$ alone, and its $k$-fold derivative at a real point is bounded by $(C_\pi g_0)^k$ directly (Duhamel's formula: the $k$-th derivative of $e^{ig_0\pi(a)}$ in the $h$-unit directions $e_1,\dots,e_k$ is a sum over the $k!$ orderings of the $k$ insertions $ig_0\pi(e_i)$, each of norm $\leq C_\pi g_0$, between unitary factors, integrated over a simplex of volume $1/k!$). The directional derivative $\partial_{e,b}$ of $a\mapsto\Pi_n(g_0\mathsf C_Ua)$ is $g_0D\Pi_n(g_0\mathsf C_Ua)[\mathsf C_Ue_b]$ with $\|\mathsf C_Ue_b\|\leq C_{\mathsf C}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-uniform-chart}(i); $e_b$ the vector $e$ in the slot $b$, of Hilbert norm 1), and the $k$-fold mixed derivative is $g_0^kD^k\Pi_n(A)[\mathsf C_Ue_{b_1},\dots,\mathsf C_Ue_{b_k}]$. At a real $a$ in the enlarged box, $\|A\|\leq\vartheta_*/4$, so the ball of radius $\vartheta_*/4$ about $A$ lies in the closed ball of radius $\vartheta_*/2$; Cauchy's estimate for the analytic $\Pi_n$ on that ball bounds the homogeneous polynomial $D^k\Pi_n(A)[h,\dots,h]/k!$ by $C_\Pi(4\|h\|/\vartheta_*)^k$, and polarisation bounds the symmetric $k$-linear form $D^k\Pi_n(A)/k!$ by $k^k/k!$ times the polynomial's norm; hence $\|D^k\Pi_n(A)[h_1,\dots,h_k]\|\leq k^kC_\Pi(4/\vartheta_*)^k\prod\|h_i\|\leq(m_*C_\Pi4/\vartheta_*)^k\prod\|h_i\|$ for $k\leq m_*$ ($C_\Pi\geq1$), giving $\|\partial_{e_1,b_1}\cdots\partial_{e_k,b_k}\pi(L_n)\|\leq(4m_*C_\Pi C_{\mathsf C}g_0/\vartheta_*)^k\leq(C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0)^k$; $\pi(U_1(r_n))$ is a constant unitary. The derivative and difference bounds are the chain rule and the mean value inequality along the segment, $\partial_s\pi(L_n(a+sm))=\sum_{b\in\mathcal F_{r_n}}h(m_b)\partial_{m_b/h(m_b),b}\pi(L_n)(a+sm)$, each term of norm $\leq h(m_b)C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0$ by the case $k=1$. (b) Write $q:=|\mathsf J|$ and $B^s:=(s_bB_b)_{b\in\mathsf J}+a_0$ for $s\in[0,1]^{\mathsf J}$. For each $b\in\mathsf J$, the difference in the label $b$ alone is $G(1)-G(0)=\int_0^1\partial_{s_b}G(s_b)\,\dd s_b$ for the function $G$ of $s_b$ at the other labels fixed, and iterating over the labels (the signs $(-1)^{|\mathsf J\setminus K|}$ are exactly the iterated differences) gives $\Delta_{\mathsf J}g(B)=\int_{[0,1]^{\mathsf J}}\partial_{s_{b_1}}\cdots\partial_{s_{b_q}}g(B^s)\,\dd s$ with $\partial_{s_b}g(B^s)=\langle B_b,\nabla_bg\rangle(B^s)=h(B_b)\partial_{B_b/h(B_b),b}g(B^s)$, so $|\Delta_{\mathsf J}g(B)|\leq\prod_{b\in\mathsf J}h(B_b)\cdot\sup_s|\partial^{\mathsf J}g(B^s)|$, where $\partial^{\mathsf J}$ is the mixed derivative in the $q$ directions $B_b/h(B_b)$ at the labels $b\in\mathsf J$; every $B^s$ is real and in the enlarged box ($|s_bB_b+a_{0,b}|_\sigma\leq5r/4+r/4\leq2r$). Product rule inside the normalised trace: the fixed factors $N_i$ do not depend on $a$, and a link $L_{n_i}$ depends on $a_b$ only if $b\in\mathcal F_{r_{n_i}}$, so $\partial^{\mathsf J}g=\sum_{\text{assignments}}\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(X_1^{(\mathsf J_1)}\cdots X_p^{(\mathsf J_p)})$, where $X_i^{(\mathsf J_i)}:=\partial^{\mathsf J_i}\pi(L_{n_i})$ for a link factor and $X_i^{(\varnothing)}:=N_i$ for a fixed factor, the sum over the assignments $\mathsf J=\bigsqcup_i\mathsf J_i$ of each label $b$ to one of the link factors with $b\in\mathcal F_{r_{n_i}}$, of which there are at most $N_{\mathrm{tr}}^q$; $|\operatorname{tr}(\prod M_i)|\leq\dim(\pi)\prod\|M_i\|_{\mathrm{op}}$, the undifferentiated links are unitary at real points, the fixed factors contribute $\prod\|N_i\|=\|N\|$, and each differentiated link has $|\mathsf J_i|\leq|\mathcal F_{r_{n_i}}|\leq m_*-1$ directions, so by (a) $\sup|\partial^{\mathsf J}g|\leq(N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0)^q\|N\|$. With $h(B_b)\leq|B_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4$ ($h\leq|\cdot|_\sigma$, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-1}) this gives $|\Delta_{\mathsf J}g(B)|\leq(N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\cdot5r/4)^q\|N\|\leq(N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0(1+2r))^q\|N\|\leq u_t^q\|N\|$, since $C'_\chi=2N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}\geq N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}$. For $\partial_{e,b}g$ the same argument applies to the mixed derivative $\partial^{\mathsf J}\partial_{e,b}g$ of order $q+1$ (the extra direction at the label $b$, possibly repeating a label of $\mathsf J$; each link then carries at most $m_*$ directions, within (a)): $|\Delta_{\mathsf J}[\partial_{e,b}g](B)|\leq(5r/4)^q(N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0)^{q+1}\|N\|\leq u_t^q\cdot N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\|N\|=u_t^q(C'_\chi/2)g_0\|N\|$. At the reference data Lemma~\ref{lem:charts} holds for $U=\id$ with the same $\vartheta_*$, $C_{\mathsf C}$, $Q_*$, so (a) and (b) hold there verbatim.
\end{proof}

Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z} replaces the finite-difference bound of \draftref{stage 4, \S4.1} (whose argument it follows: product rule inside the closed character, Cauchy estimates on the row chart, bounded traversal multiplicity) by a proof from Lemmas~\ref{lem:charts} and~\ref{lem:c2-uniform-chart} at the carrier $D(\Lambda)$; the parity gain of \draftref{stage 4, \S4.1} is not used.

\begin{lemma}[the defect difference as a sum of marked connected terms]\label{lem:c5-5-7}
Let $\pi$ be the irreducible unitary representation with $\chi=\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}\pi$, acting on $V_\pi$. For a group $W$ and $t\in\{0,1\}$ let $T^t_W(B)$ be the ordered product of the reconstructed links along the consecutive traversals of $\mathcal H(W)$ at the young variable $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal A(W)}B+tm$ (links off $\Omega_1$ equal to their background value), $P^t_W:=T^t_W(0)$ (young variable $tm$ throughout), and
\begin{equation*}
    \Upsilon^t_W:=w^t_\Lambda(W)\int\pi\bigl(T^t_W(B)(P^t_W)^{-1}\bigr)\,\dd\Bigl(\bigotimes_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\hat\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}\Bigr)(B)-a^\Lambda_W\cdot\id_{V_\pi}\in\operatorname{End}(V_\pi)\;,
\end{equation*}
so that $\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\Upsilon^t_W\pi(h))=c^t_W(h)-a^\Lambda_W\chi(h)$ with $c^t_W(h):=w^t_\Lambda(W)\int\chi(T^t_W(B)(P^t_W)^{-1}h)\,\dd\bigotimes\hat\nu^{f,t}_\Lambda$ (\draftref{stage 4, \S6.1} at the young variable $tm$).
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
    \item (Burnside.) There is $K_\chi$ depending on $\pi$ only with $\|\Upsilon\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq K_\chi\sup_{h\in G}|\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\Upsilon\pi(h))|$ for every $\Upsilon\in\operatorname{End}(V_\pi)$.
    \item For a compatible family $\mathfrak B=\{W_1,\dots,W_n\}$ with short intervals in cyclic order, $R^t_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)=\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\Upsilon^t_{W_1}U^t_1\Upsilon^t_{W_2}U^t_2\cdots\Upsilon^t_{W_n}U^t_n)$, where $U^t_j:=\pi(P^t_{W_j}G^t_j)$ with $G^t_j$ the ordered product of the links of the traversals strictly between $\mathcal H(W_j)$ and $\mathcal H(W_{j+1})$ (cyclically; $G^t_j=\id$ if there are none) at the young variable $tm$: the $t$-background word of the interval $\mathcal H(W_j)$ followed by the gap to the next interval (for a whole-contour group, $n=1$, $G^t_1=\id$ and $U^t_1=\pi(P^t_W)$).
    \item Hence
    \begin{gather}
        R^1_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)-R^0_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)=\sum_{j=1}^n\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\Upsilon^1_{W_1}U^1_1\cdots(\Upsilon^1_{W_j}-\Upsilon^0_{W_j})U^0_j\cdots\Upsilon^0_{W_n}U^0_n\bigr)+\sum_j\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\cdots\Upsilon^1_{W_j}(U^1_j-U^0_j)\Upsilon^0_{W_{j+1}}\cdots\bigr)\;,\notag\\
        |R^1_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)-R^0_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)|\leq K_\chi^{|\mathfrak B|}\Bigl[\sum_{W_0\in\mathfrak B}\mathfrak d^{\mathrm{diff}}_{W_0}\prod_{W\neq W_0}\bar{\mathfrak d}_W+C'_\chi g_0\Bigl(\sum_{b\in I_C}h(m_b)\Bigr)\prod_{W\in\mathfrak B}\bar{\mathfrak d}_W\Bigr]\;,\label{eq:c5-5-7}
    \end{gather}
    with $\bar{\mathfrak d}_W:=\max_{t\in\{0,1\}}\sup_h|c^t_W(h)-a^\Lambda_W\chi(h)|$ and $\mathfrak d^{\mathrm{diff}}_W:=\sup_h|c^1_W(h)-c^0_W(h)|$.
    \item (Marks in $\mathfrak d^{\mathrm{diff}}_W$.) With $M^t_\Lambda(W):=(1/k(W)!)\prod_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\rho_tm(Z)\prod_j|\Phi|^+_\Lambda(\mathfrak c_j)$ (the positive group majorant with root weight $\rho_tm(Z)$, the same for $t=0$ and $t=1$ since $\rho_t$ does not depend on $t$; write $M_\Lambda(W)$ for it; it is the $M(W)$ of \draftref{attempt, \S18.3} with $\rho=\rho_t$),
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-8}
        \mathfrak d^{\mathrm{diff}}_W\leq M_\Lambda(W)\Bigl[\sum_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z)+\sum_j\sum_{D\in\mathfrak c_j}\mathfrak M_\Lambda(D)+C'_\chi g_0\sum_{b\in I_{\mathcal H(W)}}h(m_b)\Bigr]\;,\qquad I_{\mathcal H(W)}:=\text{free inputs of the traversals of }\mathcal H(W),
    \end{equation}
    where the last term is the source-shift mark (the root measures contribute their $\prod_Z\rho_tm(Z)$ through the finite-difference factor $u_t^{|\mathsf J|}$ exactly as in $M_\Lambda(W)$; the shift adds the factor $C'_\chi g_0\sum h(m_b)$).
    \item (Bounds for the undifferenced slots.) $\bar{\mathfrak d}_W\leq M_\Lambda(W)+|a^\Lambda_W|=:\bar{\mathfrak b}_W$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) $\Upsilon\mapsto(h\mapsto\operatorname{tr}(\Upsilon\pi(h)))$ is linear and injective on $\operatorname{End}(V_\pi)$ ($\pi(G)$ spans $\operatorname{End}(V_\pi)$ for an irreducible representation, Burnside's theorem), so the sup over $h$ is a norm on the finite-dimensional $\operatorname{End}(V_\pi)$, equivalent to the operator norm; $K_\chi$ is the equivalence constant. (b) is the factorisation of \draftref{stage 4, \S6.1} at the young variable $tm$: a traversal in $\mathcal H(W_j)$ has no contact with a root of another group, so its link uses $W_j$'s root variables (and $tm$); a traversal outside every interval has its link at $tm$; in the expansion of the product, a slot $W_j$ taken from the $\int\pi(T^t_{W_j}(P^t_{W_j})^{-1})$-term contributes $\pi(T^t_{W_j}(B))\pi(G^t_j)$ (the links of $\mathcal H(W_j)$ at the root variables, then the gap at $tm$) and a slot taken from $-a^\Lambda_{W_j}\id$ contributes $-a^\Lambda_{W_j}\pi(P^t_{W_j}G^t_j)$ (the links of $\mathcal H(W_j)$ and the gap at $tm$), so the normalised trace of the ordered product is $f_\xi(\mathbf 1_{\mathcal A(\mathcal S)}B+tm)$ for the subfamily $\mathcal S$ of slots of the first kind, times $\prod_{W\notin\mathcal S}(-a^\Lambda_W)$; expanding $\prod(X_W-a_W\id)$ over subsets reproduces the signed sum $R^t_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(c) by multilinearity of the trace, and the Fubini evaluation of the $\int\pi(\dots)$-slots against the product measure is the one in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:schur}. (c) Telescoping of the ordered product in its $2n$ factors; then $|\operatorname{tr}(X_1\cdots X_{2n})|\leq\dim(\pi)\prod\|X_i\|_{\mathrm{op}}$, $\|U^t_j\|=1$, $\|\Upsilon^t_W\|\leq K_\chi\bar{\mathfrak d}_W$, $\|\Upsilon^1_W-\Upsilon^0_W\|\leq K_\chi\mathfrak d^{\mathrm{diff}}_W$ by (a), and $\|U^1_j-U^0_j\|\leq\sum_{\text{traversals in }\mathcal H(W_j)\text{ and in the gap after it}}\|\pi(\text{link at }m)-\pi(\text{link at }0)\|\leq C'_\chi g_0\sum_{b\in I_C}h(m_b)$ (the links are unitary and the word is their ordered product, so the difference is at most the sum of the link differences; $\|\pi(L_n(m))-\pi(L_n(0))\|\leq C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\sum_{b\in\mathcal F_{r_n}}h(m_b)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}(a), the segment $sm$ lying in the box of radius $r/4$; over $j$ the intervals and gaps partition the traversals of $C$, each traversal's inputs lie in $I_C$, and a bond is an input of at most $N_{\mathrm{tr}}$ traversals, so the total is at most $N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\sum_{b\in I_C}h(m_b)\leq C'_\chi g_0\sum_{b\in I_C}h(m_b)$). (d) $c^1_W-c^0_W$ is telescoped over its $t$-dependent slots: the $k(W)$ cluster weights $\Phi^{v,t}_\Lambda(\mathfrak c_j)$, the $|\mathcal A(W)|$ root measures $\hat\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}$ (through their split selections $\ell_Z$, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(a)), and the source word $g_t(B):=\chi(T^t_W(B)(P^t_W)^{-1}h)$. Cluster slot: $|\Phi^{v,1}-\Phi^{v,0}|(\mathfrak c_j)\leq\sum_{D\in\mathfrak c_j}\mathfrak M_\Lambda(D)|\Phi|^+(\mathfrak c_j)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-6}(b)), the other clusters at $|\Phi^{v,t}|\leq|\Phi|^+$, and the root integral $|\int\Delta_{\mathsf J}g_t\,\dd\bigotimes\Lambda^{f,t}_{\ell_Z}|\leq\prod_Zu_t^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\|\Lambda^{f,t}_{\ell_Z}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}$ summed with Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(c) and $(u_t/u_*)^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\leq\rho_t$ gives $\prod_Z\rho_tm(Z)$. Here $\sup|\Delta_{\mathsf J}g_t|\leq u_t^{|\mathsf J|}$ is the first assertion of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}(b) for $g_t(B)=\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\pi(T_W(\mathbf 1_KB+tm))N_t)$ with the fixed unitary factor $N_t:=\pi(P^t_W)^{-1}\pi(h)$ ($\|N_t\|=1$: $P^t_W$ is the word of $\mathcal H(W)$ at the fixed argument $tm$ and $h$ a fixed group element) at the base point $a_0=tm$ ($|tm_b|_\sigma\leq r/4$) with the increments $\mathbf 1_KB$, $|B_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4$ on the support of $\nu^{f,t}$. Root slot: one root $Z_0$ carries $\Lambda^{f,1}_\ell-\Lambda^{f,0}_\ell$ and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-6}(a) gives $\sum_\ell u_*^{|\mathsf J|}\|\Lambda^1_\ell-\Lambda^0_\ell\|\leq\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z_0)m(Z_0)$, with the same $u_t^{|\mathsf J|}$ from the finite difference, so the root pays $\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z_0)\rho_tm(Z_0)$. Source slot: all measures and weights at one $t$, and the insertion is $\Delta_{\mathsf J}[g_1-g_0](B)=\int_0^1\Delta_{\mathsf J}[\partial_sg_s](B)\,\dd s$ with $g_s(a):=\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\pi(T_W(a))\pi(P^s_W)^{-1}\pi(h))$, evaluated in the $K$-term of $\Delta_{\mathsf J}$ at $a=\mathbf 1_KB+sm$, $T_W(a)$ the word of $\mathcal H(W)$ at the young variable $a$ and $P^s_W=T_W(sm)$ (the de-younged bonds are constants, so only the inputs in $I_{\mathcal H(W)}\cap D(\Lambda)$ vary; $\partial_s$ below is the derivative of $s\mapsto g_s(\mathbf 1_KB+sm)$ in each $K$-term). Two things depend on $s$: the argument $\mathbf 1_KB+sm$ of the link word and the fixed factor $N_s:=\pi(P^s_W)^{-1}\pi(h)$, whose links sit at the argument $sm$ and not at $\mathbf 1_KB+sm$. Hence $\partial_sg_s=\sum_{b\in I_{\mathcal H(W)}\cap D(\Lambda)}h(m_b)\partial_{m_b/h(m_b),b}\tilde g_s+\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\pi(T_W(\mathbf 1_KB+sm))N'_s)$, with $\tilde g_s(a):=\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\pi(T_W(a))N_s)$ (fixed unitary insertion, $\|N_s\|=1$) and $N'_s:=\partial_s[\pi(P^s_W)^{-1}]\pi(h)=-\pi(P^s_W)^{-1}(\partial_s\pi(P^s_W))\pi(P^s_W)^{-1}\pi(h)$, a fixed non-unitary insertion with $\|N'_s\|\leq\|\partial_s\pi(P^s_W)\|\leq\sum_{n\in\mathcal H(W)}\|\partial_s\pi(L_n(sm))\|\leq\sum_{n\in\mathcal H(W)}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\sum_{b\in\mathcal F_{r_n}}h(m_b)\leq N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\sum_{b\in I_{\mathcal H(W)}}h(m_b)$ (product rule for the word of unitaries; Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}(a) along the segment $sm$, which lies in the box of radius $r/4$; each bond is an input of at most $N_{\mathrm{tr}}$ traversals). The mixed difference of order $|\mathsf J|$ of the first sum is bounded by the second assertion of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}(b) (base point $sm$, increments $\mathbf 1_KB$, $\|N_s\|=1$): $\leq u_t^{|\mathsf J|}(C'_\chi/2)g_0\sum_{b\in I_{\mathcal H(W)}}h(m_b)$; that of the second term by the first assertion with the insertion $N'_s$: $\leq u_t^{|\mathsf J|}\|N'_s\|\leq u_t^{|\mathsf J|}N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\sum_{b\in I_{\mathcal H(W)}}h(m_b)=u_t^{|\mathsf J|}(C'_\chi/2)g_0\sum_{b\in I_{\mathcal H(W)}}h(m_b)$. Together $\sup|\Delta_{\mathsf J}[\partial_sg_s]|\leq u_t^{|\mathsf J|}C'_\chi g_0\sum_{b\in I_{\mathcal H(W)}}h(m_b)$: the factor 2 in $C'_\chi=2N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}$ pays the two $s$-dependent words. This gives \eqref{eq:c5-5-8}; the three sums are over the marks of the three kinds. (e) $|c^t_W(h)|\leq M^t_\Lambda(W)$ by the same root and cluster bounds (no mark), and $|a^\Lambda_W\chi(h)|\leq|a^\Lambda_W|$.
\end{proof}

Nothing in Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7} uses a parity (odd-root) cancellation: the translated cutoffs $\chi^t_b$ are not even in $B$ for $t>0$, so the reversal argument of \draftref{stage 4, \S4.1} is unavailable and only the first-order gain $\rho_t=u_t/u_*$ per root is taken; this is why $\rho_t=Cg_0(1+2r)$ rather than $Cg_0^2(1+r)^4$ appears. Section~\ref{sec:c5-5R} recovers the second order.

\begin{lemma}[pins for the $t$-family]\label{lem:c5-5-8}
Let $F(W)$ be the footprint of $W$ (the $LM$-cubes of its roots, cluster constituents and hull stencils, \draftref{attempt, \S19.1}), $\sigma(W):=s(W)=n(W)+|\mathcal H(W)|/(LM)$ its size (\draftref{attempt, \S18.3}, \draftref{stage 4, \S6.2}; $n(W)$ counts the cubes of the roots and cluster constituents with tuple multiplicity, so $\sigma(W)$ is at least the number of domain occurrences in $W$), $\nu:=\eta$ the reserve constant of \draftref{attempt, \S18.3} and \draftref{stage 4, \S6.2}, and $C_F:=A_E$ the envelope constant of \draftref{attempt, \S19.3} ($|E(W)|\leq A_E\sigma(W)$). (a) Under \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(iv) the positive summation of \draftref{stage 4, \S6.2} and \draftref{attempt, \S18.3, \S20.1} applies to $M_\Lambda(W)$ (it uses only the root weights $\rho\,m(Z)$ with $\rho=\rho_t\leq\rho_{\mathrm{pin}}$, the positive incompatibility-tree weights $|\Phi|^+_\Lambda=M_v$, and the field-independent domain/cluster/interval geometry, none of which involves the carrier beyond the cubes): with $C_{\mathrm{pin}}$ the constant $C$ of the display of \draftref{stage 4, \S6.2},
\begin{equation*}
    \sup_\Delta\sum_{W\, : \,\Delta\in F(W)}e^{4\nu\sigma(W)}M_\Lambda(W)+\sup_n\sum_{W\, : \,n\in\mathcal H(W)}e^{4\nu\sigma(W)}M_\Lambda(W)\leq C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t\;.
\end{equation*}
Consequently, with $\bar\varepsilon_t:=2K_\chi C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t$,
\begin{equation*}
    \sup_\Delta\sum_{W\, : \,\Delta\in F(W)}e^{2\nu\sigma(W)}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}_W\leq\bar\varepsilon_t\;,\qquad\sup_n\sum_{W\, : \,n\in\mathcal H(W)}e^{2\nu\sigma(W)}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}_W\leq\bar\varepsilon_t\;,\qquad\sum_WK_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}_W\leq n_C\bar\varepsilon_t\;,
\end{equation*}
the last because every group has a contact with the contour (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(a)) and so meets $\sum_n$. The same statements hold for the reference gas at the carrier $I$ (the reference data of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}, activities $a_W$) with the same constants: the proof uses only the lemmas of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} and Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} at the carrier, which hold at $I$. (b) (Decay from the footprint to the contour.) If $\Delta\in F(W)$ then $\sigma(W)\geq\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)/(C_FLM)$: the envelope $E(W)\supseteq F(W)\cup C(\mathcal H(W))$ is connected under maximum-norm adjacency of $LM$-cubes and has at most $A_E\sigma(W)$ cubes (\draftref{attempt, \S19.3}), so its $\ell^\infty$ diameter in fine units is at most $A_ELM\sigma(W)$, and it contains $\Delta$ and a cube meeting the contour. Hence for every cube $\Delta$,
\begin{equation*}
    \sum_{W\, : \,\Delta\in F(W)}e^{\nu\sigma(W)}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}_W\leq\bar\varepsilon_te^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)/(C_FLM)}\;,\qquad\sum_{W\, : \,\Delta\in F(W)}\sigma(W)K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}_W\leq\nu^{-1}\bar\varepsilon_te^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)/(C_FLM)}\;.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) The first display is the cited summation with $\rho_t$ for the root weights; the second follows from $\bar{\mathfrak b}_W\leq2M_\Lambda(W)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d) and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7}(e)) and $e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\leq e^{\nu\sigma(W)/16}\leq e^{2\nu\sigma(W)}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d)). (b) $e^{2\nu\sigma(W)}\geq e^{\nu\sigma(W)}e^{\nu\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)/(C_FLM)}$ by the geometric fact, and $\sigma\leq\nu^{-1}e^{\nu\sigma}$ ($x\leq e^x$).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[the source-containing connected boundary terms]\label{prop:c5-5-9}
In the square/torus geometry of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0}, under the hypotheses of Lemmas~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}--\ref{lem:c5-4-4}, \eqref{eq:c5-4-T}, \eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp} and \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}, on every labelled term with final region $\Lambda$, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$,
\begin{gather}
    |\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]|\leq|S^\circ_\Lambda|e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}\bigl[C_1g_0r\,\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)+C_2\bar\varepsilon_tr^{d_G}\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)\bigr]\;,\label{eq:c5-5-9}\\
    \mathfrak S_1(\Lambda):=\sum_{e\in J_1(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu_H\operatorname{dist}(e,I_C)/2}\;,\qquad\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda):=\sum_{e\in J(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}\;,\qquad\mu':=\min\bigl(\mu_H/4,\,\nu/(2C_FLM)\bigr)\;,\notag
\end{gather}
with $C_1:=C_m\vartheta N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)(C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}+(1+N_{\mathrm{tr}})C'_\chi)$ and $C_2:=\nu^{-1}C_{\mathrm{dist}}[4C_\varpi N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)+N_{\mathrm{host}}e^{\nu R_{\mathrm{st}}/(C_FLM)}(64C_{\mathrm{bd}}+16C'_{\mathrm{bd}}C_m\vartheta N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2))]$, $C_{\mathrm{dist}}:=e^{7\nu/C_F}$, $R_{\mathrm{st}}:=4M$ ($\geq4L+3M$, since $M\geq8LM_2\geq8L^2\geq4L$ by \eqref{eq:c-standing-conditions}), constants depending on $L$, the group, $M$, $\kappa$, $\omega_*$, $\nu$, $\vartheta$, the character and the stencil ranges only, in the square/torus geometry of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0}; $g_0r\to0$ and $\bar\varepsilon_t=2K_\chi C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t\to0$ as $g_0\to0$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Divide \eqref{eq:c5-5-5} by $S^\circ_\Lambda$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d)): $\mathcal B^\Lambda/S^\circ_\Lambda=[f_\xi(m)-f_\xi(0)]+\sum_{\mathfrak B\neq\varnothing}q^\circ_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)[R^1_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)-R^0_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)]$. The first term is Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-4}: $\leq C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}C_m\vartheta N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)g_0r\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)$. For the second, insert \eqref{eq:c5-5-7} and $|q^\circ_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)|\leq\prod_We^{\theta^\circ(W)}$. The background-word part: $C'_\chi g_0(\sum_{I_C}h(m_b))\sum_{\mathfrak B}\prod_{W\in\mathfrak B}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak d}_W\leq C'_\chi g_0(\sum_{I_C}h(m_b))\prod_W(1+K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}_W)\leq C'_\chi g_0(\sum_{I_C}h(m_b))e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$ (families are sets of distinct groups; Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(a)), and $\sum_{I_C}h(m_b)\leq C_m\vartheta N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)r\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)$ by \eqref{eq:c5-5-2}: together with the first term this is $C_m\vartheta N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)(C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}+C'_\chi)g_0r\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$, the first part of the $C_1$ term (the second part is (ii) below). The marked part: $\sum_{\mathfrak B}\sum_{W_0\in\mathfrak B}\prod_We^{\theta^\circ(W)}K_\chi\mathfrak d^{\mathrm{diff}}_{W_0}\prod_{W\neq W_0}K_\chi\bar{\mathfrak d}_W\leq e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}\sum_{W_0}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W_0)}\mathfrak d^{\mathrm{diff}}_{W_0}$ (sum first over $\mathfrak B\setminus\{W_0\}$, as above). Insert \eqref{eq:c5-5-8}. (i) Root and cluster marks. Write $\mathfrak M_\Lambda(\Delta):=\sum_{b\in E(\Delta)}\mathfrak m_b+\sum_{Y\text{ anchored at }\Delta}\mathfrak m_Y$ for an $LM$-cube $\Delta$ (a host is anchored at the $LM$-cube containing its lowest $M$-cube; a bond meeting two cubes is counted in both), so that $\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z)\leq\sum_{\Delta\subseteq Z}\mathfrak M_\Lambda(\Delta)$ for every domain $Z$. The occurrences of a cube $\Delta$ among the roots and cluster constituents of $W_0$ number $\operatorname{mult}_{W_0}(\Delta)\leq$ (number of domain occurrences in $W_0$) $\leq\sigma(W_0)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}), so $\sum_{Z\in\mathcal A(W_0)}\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z)+\sum_j\sum_{D\in\mathfrak c_j}\mathfrak M_\Lambda(D)\leq\sum_{\Delta\in F(W_0)}\operatorname{mult}_{W_0}(\Delta)\mathfrak M_\Lambda(\Delta)\leq\sigma(W_0)\sum_{\Delta\in F(W_0)}\mathfrak M_\Lambda(\Delta)$, and $\sum_{W_0}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W_0)}M_\Lambda(W_0)\sigma(W_0)\sum_{\Delta\in F(W_0)}\mathfrak M_\Lambda(\Delta)\leq\sum_\Delta\mathfrak M_\Lambda(\Delta)\sum_{W_0\, : \,\Delta\in F(W_0)}\sigma(W_0)K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ}\bar{\mathfrak b}_{W_0}\leq\nu^{-1}\bar\varepsilon_t\sum_\Delta\mathfrak M_\Lambda(\Delta)e^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)/(C_FLM)}$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(b). Convert cube distances to bond and host distances: for $b\in E(\Delta)$, $\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)\geq\operatorname{dist}(b,C)-LM-1$, and for $Y$ anchored at $\Delta$, $\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)\geq\operatorname{dist}(Y,C)-4M-LM$ (a host has side at most $4M$), so $e^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)/(C_FLM)}\leq C_{\mathrm{dist}}e^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(b,C)/(C_FLM)}$, resp. $\leq C_{\mathrm{dist}}e^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(Y,C)/(C_FLM)}$, with $C_{\mathrm{dist}}:=e^{7\nu/C_F}$ ($(2LM+4M+1)/(LM)\leq7$). Now the three kinds of $\mathfrak m$-weights. Bond marks: a bond lies in $E(\Delta)$ for at most two cubes $\Delta$ (no site lies on a cube face), so $\sum_\Delta\sum_{b\in E(\Delta)}\mathfrak m_be^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(\Delta,C)/(C_FLM)}\leq2C_{\mathrm{dist}}\sum_{b\in D(\Lambda)}\mathfrak m_be^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(b,C)/(C_FLM)}$, and $\sum_{b\in D(\Lambda)}\mathfrak m_be^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(b,C)/(C_FLM)}\leq2C_\varpi r^{d_G}\sum_b\sum_{e\in J_1}e^{-\mu_Hd(b,e)}e^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(b,C)/(C_FLM)}\leq2C_\varpi r^{d_G}N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)\sum_{e\in J_1(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}$ (so the bond term of $C_2$ is $4C_\varpi N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)$ after the factor $C_{\mathrm{dist}}$ is taken out) by the splitting in the proof of \eqref{eq:c5-5-2} with $\mu'\leq\min(\mu_H/2,\nu/(C_FLM))/2$. Host marks with an exterior input $e\in J(\Lambda)$: the primitive's stencil has $\ell^\infty$ diameter at most $4L$ and contains its input $e$, and its host lies in the $M$-cubes meeting the stencil with one further $M$-layer (\eqref{eq:part1-potential-host}), so every point of the host is within $4L+3M\leq4M=R_{\mathrm{st}}$ of $e$ ($M\geq8L^2\geq4L$), $\operatorname{dist}(Y,C)\geq\operatorname{dist}(e,C)-R_{\mathrm{st}}$, and there are at most $N_{\mathrm{host}}$ such hosts per $e$ (Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-2}); their total is $\leq N_{\mathrm{host}}e^{\nu R_{\mathrm{st}}/(C_FLM)}C_{\mathrm{bd}}g_0(1+3r)^3\sum_{e\in J(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}$ ($\nu/(C_FLM)\geq\mu'$). Host marks without exterior input: $\sum_Y\mathfrak m_Ye^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(Y,C)/(C_FLM)}\leq C'_{\mathrm{bd}}g_0(1+3r)^2\sum_{b\in D(\Lambda)}h(m_b)\cdot\#\{Y\in\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)\, : \,b\in E(Y)\}\cdot e^{\nu R_{\mathrm{st}}/(C_FLM)}e^{-\nu\operatorname{dist}(b,C)/(C_FLM)}\leq C'_{\mathrm{bd}}N_{\mathrm{host}}e^{\nu R_{\mathrm{st}}/(C_FLM)}g_0(1+3r)^2C_m\vartheta rN_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)\sum_{e\in J_1}e^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}$ by \eqref{eq:c5-5-2} ($\operatorname{dist}(Y,C)\geq\operatorname{dist}(b,C)-4M=\operatorname{dist}(b,C)-R_{\mathrm{st}}$ for $b\in E(Y)$, a host having side at most $4M$; at most $N_{\mathrm{host}}$ hosts contain $b$). (ii) Source-shift marks: $\sum_{W_0}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ}M_\Lambda(W_0)C'_\chi g_0\sum_{b\in I_{\mathcal H(W_0)}}h(m_b)\leq C'_\chi g_0\sum_{b\in I_C}h(m_b)\sum_{n\in n(b)}\sum_{W_0\, : \,n\in\mathcal H(W_0)}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ}\bar{\mathfrak b}_{W_0}\leq N_{\mathrm{tr}}\bar\varepsilon_tC'_\chi g_0\sum_{b\in I_C}h(m_b)$ (the first step is an inequality: a bond $b\in I_{\mathcal H(W_0)}$ is an input of at least one traversal $n\in\mathcal H(W_0)\cap n(b)$, so every term on the left occurs at least once on the right, possibly several times when $b$ is an input of several traversals of $\mathcal H(W_0)$) $\leq N_{\mathrm{tr}}C'_\chi g_0C_m\vartheta N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)r\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)$ ($n(b)$ the set of traversals with input $b$, $|n(b)|\leq N_{\mathrm{tr}}$, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}; the contour pin of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(a) for each $n\in n(b)$; $\bar\varepsilon_t\leq1$ by \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(v); then \eqref{eq:c5-5-2}); this is the second part of the $C_1$ term, $C_1=C_m\vartheta N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)(C_{\mathrm{resp},\chi}+(1+N_{\mathrm{tr}})C'_\chi)$. Collecting, with $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}\leq e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$, $g_0(1+3r)^3\leq64r^{d_G}$ and $(1+3r)^2r\leq16r^{d_G}$ for $r\geq1$, $d_G\geq3$, gives \eqref{eq:c5-5-9} with $C_2=\nu^{-1}C_{\mathrm{dist}}[4C_\varpi N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2)+N_{\mathrm{host}}e^{\nu R_{\mathrm{st}}/(C_FLM)}(64C_{\mathrm{bd}}+16C'_{\mathrm{bd}}C_m\vartheta N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu_H/2))]$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[the exterior sum on the far event]\label{cor:c5-5-10}
$J(\Lambda)=J\cup(I\setminus D(\Lambda))$ with $J=E_{\Omega,*}\setminus I\subseteq E(\Lambda_1^c)$ (a bond not in $I$ has both endpoints off $\Lambda_1$, by the bond convention of Section~\ref{sec:c5-0}). On the far event $N_b^c$ of \draftref{attempt, \S20.2} ($\operatorname{dist}(\Gamma_C,E(\Lambda_1^c))>b$, $b=\lfloor LMR_1/16\rfloor$) every $e\in J$ has $\operatorname{dist}(e,C)\geq b-1$ in the convention of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0} (the distance for $N_b$ may be measured between bond sites rather than to the vertex set of $C$, which changes a distance by at most 1), and
\begin{equation*}
    \sum_{e\in J}e^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}\leq\sum_{s\geq b-1}\#\{e\, : \,\operatorname{dist}(e,C)\leq s\}e^{-\mu's}\leq4n_C\sum_{s\geq b-1}(2s+1)^4e^{-\mu's}\leq n_CN_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu'/2)e^{-\mu'(b-1)/2}
\end{equation*}
(at most $4(2s+1)^4$ bonds within $\ell^\infty$ distance $s$ of each of the $n_C$ vertices of $C$). Hence on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}\cap N_b^c$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-10}
    |\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]|\leq|S^\circ_\Lambda|e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}\bigl(C_1'g_0r+C_2\bar\varepsilon_tr^{d_G}\bigr)\bigl(n_CN_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu'/2)e^{-\mu'(b-1)/2}+\mathfrak D_\Lambda\bigr)\;,\qquad\mathfrak D_\Lambda:=\sum_{e\in I\setminus D(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}\leq|I\setminus D(\Lambda)|\;,
\end{equation}
using $\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)\leq e^{\mu_H(2L+1)/2}\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)$ ($\operatorname{dist}(e,I_C)\geq\operatorname{dist}(e,C)-(2L+1)$, $\mu_H/2\geq\mu'$), with $C_1':=C_1e^{\mu_H(2L+1)/2}$. The first summand is volume-independent and, since $b=\lfloor\ell_{\mathrm{mac}}/16\rfloor$ with $\ell_{\mathrm{mac}}=LMR_1$ growing as a fixed positive power of $\log g_1^{-2}$ (the scale integer $R_1$; $g_1\geq g_0/(1+\beta_0)$, so $\log g_1^{-2}\geq\log g_0^{-2}-2\log(1+\beta_0)$), beats every polynomial in $r$: $r^{d_G}e^{-\mu'(b-1)/2}\leq e^{-\mu'(b-1)/4}$ as soon as $b-1\geq(4d_G/\mu')\log r$, which holds for $g_0$ small, since $b-1\geq\ell_{\mathrm{mac}}/16-2$ grows as a power of $\log g_0^{-2}$ while $\log r=p_0\log\log g_0^{-2}+\log A_1$. The second summand $\mathfrak D_\Lambda$ is the term's de-younged bonds weighted by their distance to the contour; it is a quantity of the labelled term, bounded by the number $|I\setminus D(\Lambda)|$ of de-younged bonds, and its average over the labels is the bookkeeping of Section~\ref{sec:c5-6} (Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a)--(b): a de-younged bond $z$ of $D^{(0)}\setminus D^{\mathrm{fin}}$ is not itself marked; it is attributed to a marked bond $e$ (a collar-test failure $M_e$) with $x_e$ within $102\ell+1$ of an endpoint of $z$, and Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a) pays the marked bonds attributed to $z$ by $\sum_ep^\vartheta_e\leq N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta$, which is the term $N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta$ of \eqref{eq:c5-3-coverage-average}; the round-0 bonds $D\setminus D^{(0)}$ carry no marks and are paid by $p_z^{(0)}\leq q$, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}(b)).
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[scope of this subsection]\label{rem:c5-5-11}
The marked terms are the source-containing connected boundary terms: each is a closed contraction (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7}(b)) connected to $J(\Lambda)$ through its mark, a translated cutoff (bond), a potential with an exterior input or with the shift (host), or the shift of the source word, and each mark pays its distance to the contour by the footprint pin (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(b)) or by the decay of $m$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-1}); the perimeter factor $S^\circ_\Lambda$ is extracted before absolute values. What this subsection does not contain: (1) the identification of $S^\circ_\Lambda$ with $\kappa_Ce^{E^\circ_\Lambda}$ at the retreated carrier, which is Section~\ref{sec:c5-7} through Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-square-extraction} with $D=D(\Lambda)$; the reference data at $D(\Lambda)$ differ from those at $I$ not only in the groups whose roots or clusters meet $I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ but also in $H^\circ$ (the principal restriction to $D(\Lambda)$), the eligible labels $\mathsf L_Z$, the host set $\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$, the seed sets $E_0$ and their covariances $C_{E_0}$, so the activities of the groups near $I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ change as well, and no bound on the ratio $S^\circ_\Lambda/S^\circ_{\xi,C}$ is asserted; (2) the history average of $\mathfrak D_\Lambda$, Section~\ref{sec:c5-6}; (3) the complementary events: $(A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}})^c$ is paid by Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a) in terms of $q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta$, and $N_b$ is the far-history exclusion of \draftref{stage 4, \S2}, whose probability is paid there; (4) the inputs listed in Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0} are used as stated, and $\bar\varepsilon_t=2K_\chi C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t$ is the pin bound of those sections with $\rho_t$ for the root weights; (5) the labels enter \eqref{eq:c5-5-9}--\eqref{eq:c5-5-10} additively through $\mathfrak D_\Lambda$ and $\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)$ and multiplicatively through $|S^\circ_\Lambda|$ ($\Lambda=\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$ is label-dependent); the multiplicative dependence is divided out in Section~\ref{sec:c5-6}, which bounds $|\mathcal B^\Lambda|/|S^\circ_\Lambda|$ and uses $|S^\circ_\Lambda|\leq1$; (6) the square/torus geometry of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0} is a standing hypothesis of $C_{\mathrm{pin}}$, $A_F$, $A_E$ and the hull bounds. The factor $e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$ in \eqref{eq:c5-5-9} is the cost of summing over the undifferenced groups of a family, $\prod_W(1+K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}_W)\leq e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$; at fixed $n_C$ it is $1+O(n_Cg_0r)$, but in the square geometry it is a perimeter rate $2L\bar\varepsilon_t$ per coarse segment ($2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t$ over the $4n$ segments, $n_C=4nL$) with $\bar\varepsilon_t=O(g_0r)$, first order, whereas $\kappa_C=e^{-\delta mP(C)}z_c^4$ has the rate $\delta m$ built from one-mark components of weight $\tau^2$ and the tolerated error $E^\circ$ has the rate $C\tau^2$ per segment, $\tau=C[g_0(1+r)^3+e^{-cr^2}]$; for $n_C\gtrsim(\log g_0^{-2})^{2p_0}/(g_0r)$ the bound \eqref{eq:c5-5-10} would no longer put the boundary term below $\kappa_C$. Section~\ref{sec:c5-5R} brings the rate to second order, $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}$ with $\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}=O(g_0^2(1+r)^4)=O(\tau^2)$, the order accepted for $E^\circ$; a bound uniform in $n_C$ would need the undifferenced slots resummed against the reference exclusions, which is not done.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Second-order bounds for the undifferenced slots}
\label{sec:c5-5R}

Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5-9} carries the family-sum factor $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$ (written $e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$ in \eqref{eq:c5-5-9}), produced by $\prod_W(1+K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak d}_W)\leq\exp(\sum_WK_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak d}_W)$ with the first-order bound $\bar{\mathfrak d}_W\leq2M_\Lambda(W)$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7}(e), whose root weight $\rho_tm(Z)$ has $\rho_t=u_t/u_*=O(g_0r)$. This subsection proves the second-order bound $\bar{\mathfrak d}_W\leq2M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W)$, root weight $\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}m(Z)$ with $\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}=O(g_0^2(1+r)^4)$, for the undifferenced slots at $t\in\{0,1\}$ and for the reference activities, root by root inside the selected integrals, by three mechanisms: ($\alpha$) a root with at least two selected labels carries $(u_t/u_*)^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\leq\rho_t^2$ from the finite-difference bound as it stands; ($\beta$) a root with a single selected label whose split selection contains an ordinary host ($|\mathsf J_Z|=1$, $\mathcal D_Z\neq\varnothing$; a root with two or more labels is in ($\alpha$) whatever its hosts) carries the factor $2\omega_t/\omega_*$ of its own Mayer factors, through a complex parameter on the selected ordinary primitives, at every $t$ and without parity; ($\gamma$) a single-label root without ordinary host ($\mathcal D_Z=\varnothing$, $|\mathsf J_Z|=1$) has an even measure at $t=0$ and at the reference data, so the term of the source linear in its label integrates to zero and the remainder carries one more factor $u_t$; at $t=1$ its measure is the $t=0$ measure plus marked differences of its translated cutoffs, and each mark is paid by the large-field reserve (each mark costs $e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}$ against the majorant with the reserve $\frac9{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$, and the whole marked difference of the measure costs $2C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}$, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-2}(b)), not by $\varpi_b$, so the marks of the undifferenced slots carry neither exterior decay nor label dependence, and the exponent of the family sum stays a constant times $n_C$. The differenced slots ($\mathfrak d^{\mathrm{diff}}_W$, \eqref{eq:c5-5-8}) and the marked part of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5-9} are unchanged. The results are Lemmas~\ref{lem:c5-5R-1}--\ref{lem:c5-5R-3} and Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5R-4}, which restates \eqref{eq:c5-5-9}, \eqref{eq:c5-5-10}, \eqref{eq:c5-6-3}, \eqref{eq:c5-6-4} with $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}$ in place of $e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$.

Thresholds. In addition to the thresholds of Appendix~\ref{app:thresholds}, \eqref{eq:c5-4-T}, \eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp}, \eqref{eq:c5-5-T} and $\omega_*\leq1/2$ assume
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5R-T}
    \begin{aligned}
        &\text{(i) }\tfrac9{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}\geq\max\bigl(160(\kappa+\log(8M^4+8)+1),\,64\log(1/\eta_*)\bigr)\quad\text{(\eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp} for $a_{\mathrm{bad}}/8$)};\qquad\text{(ii) }\rho_t+u_t/2+2\omega_t/\omega_*+2C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}\leq1;\\
        &\text{(iii) }2C_M\leq(1+2r)^3,\quad\log g_0^{-2}\geq1,\quad(9c_n\alpha_G/64)A_1^2\log g_0^{-2}\geq1,
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
where $C_M:=8(4M+1)^4\geq\max_b|E(Y_b)|$ (a probe host is a box of $M$-cubes of side at most 4 cubes, \eqref{eq:c-probe-host}, so it has at most $(4M+1)^4$ sites, each an endpoint of eight bonds). Lemmas~\ref{lem:c5-5R-1}--\ref{lem:c5-5R-3} and the bounds of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5R-4} use (i)--(ii) only; (iii) is used only for the explicit form of the rate (the display $\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}\leq C_{\mathrm{ph}}g_0^2(1+2r)^4$ below and the rate bound of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5R-4}). All three are conditions on $g_0$ alone of the kind of \eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp} and \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}: $a_{\mathrm{bad}}=c_n\alpha_Gr^2/4\to\infty$, $r\to\infty$ and $g_0(1+r)^3\to0$ as $g_0\to0$, with thresholds depending on $L$, $M$, the group, the character, $\kappa$, $\omega_*$, $\alpha_G$, $\eta_*$, $A_1$ and $p_0$. Put
\begin{equation*}
    \rho_{\mathrm{ph}}:=\rho_t\bigl[\rho_t+u_t/2+2\omega_t/\omega_*+2C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}\bigr]\;,\qquad\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}:=2K_\chi C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}\;,\qquad M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W):=\frac1{k(W)!}\prod_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}m(Z)\prod_j|\Phi|^+_\Lambda(\mathfrak c_j)\;,
\end{equation*}
so that $\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}\leq\rho_t\leq\rho_{\mathrm{pin}}$ by \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}(ii) and \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(iv), $M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W)\leq M_\Lambda(W)$, and, with $\rho_t=C'_\chi g_0(1+2r)/u_*$, $u_t=C'_\chi g_0(1+2r)$, $\omega_t=C_0g_0(1+2r)^3$ and $a_{\mathrm{bad}}=c_n\alpha_Gr^2/4$,
\begin{equation*}
    \rho_{\mathrm{ph}}\leq C_{\mathrm{ph}}g_0^2(1+2r)^4\;,\qquad C_{\mathrm{ph}}:=(C'_\chi/u_*)(C'_\chi/u_*+C'_\chi/2+2C_0/\omega_*+1)\;,\qquad\text{hence}\qquad\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}\leq32K_\chi C_{\mathrm{pin}}C_{\mathrm{ph}}g_0^2(1+r)^4
\end{equation*}
under \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}(iii): the last bracket term needs $2C_Me^{-9c_n\alpha_Gr^2/128}\leq g_0(1+2r)^3$, which holds once $2C_M\leq(1+2r)^3$ and $e^{-9c_n\alpha_Gr^2/128}\leq g_0$, the latter because $r=A_1(\log g_0^{-2})^{p_0}$ with $p_0\geq1$ and $\log g_0^{-2}\geq1$ gives, with $c:=9c_n\alpha_G/128$, $e^{-cr^2}\leq e^{-cA_1^2(\log g_0^{-2})^2}=g_0^{2cA_1^2\log g_0^{-2}}\leq g_0$ as soon as $2cA_1^2\log g_0^{-2}\geq1$, which is the third condition of \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}(iii). The first three bracket terms are at most their coefficients times $g_0(1+2r)^3$ without any condition. In $\tau=C[g_0(1+r)^3+e^{-cr^2}]$ (\eqref{eq:marginal-fixed-forest-bound}), $\tau^2\geq C^2g_0^2(1+r)^6\geq C^2g_0^2(1+r)^4$, so $\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}\leq(32K_\chi C_{\mathrm{pin}}C_{\mathrm{ph}}/C^2)\tau^2$: the family-sum rate becomes second order, of the order of $E^\circ$.

\subsubsection*{Higher derivatives and the two-term Taylor remainder}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:c5-5R-1}
(a) Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}(a) holds for every $k\leq2m_*$ with $2C_{\mathrm{link}}$ in place of $C_{\mathrm{link}}$: $\|\partial_{e_1,b_1}\cdots\partial_{e_k,b_k}\pi(L_n)(a)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(2C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0)^k$ for real $a$ in the enlarged box, $b_i\in\mathcal F_{r_n}$ (repetitions allowed), $h$-unit $e_i$. (b) Let $g$ be as in Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}(b) (link factors, each traversal at most once, fixed insertions $N$), $a_0$ real with $|a_{0,b}|_\sigma\leq r/4$, $\mathsf J$ a finite set of bonds of $D(\Lambda)$, and $\varphi(B):=g(B+a_0)$ for $B\in\mathfrak g^{\mathsf J}$ with $|B_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4$ ($B$ extended by zero off $\mathsf J$). For $b\in\mathsf J$ define, on $C^2$ functions $\Psi$ of $B$ ($D_b$ makes sense on every function; $L_b$ and $R_b$ need two derivatives, and all three are applied to the analytic $\varphi$ only), $(D_b\Psi)(B):=\Psi(B)-\Psi(B|_{B_b\to0})$, $(L_b\Psi)(B):=\langle B_b,\nabla_b\Psi(B|_{B_b\to0})\rangle=h(B_b)\partial_{B_b/h(B_b),b}\Psi(B|_{B_b\to0})$, $(R_b\Psi)(B):=\int_0^1(1-s)\partial^2_s[\Psi(B|_{B_b\to sB_b})]\,\dd s$; operators at distinct bonds commute, and $D_b=L_b+R_b$ on $C^2$ functions. Then $\Delta_{\mathsf J}g(B)=(\prod_{b\in\mathsf J}D_b\varphi)(B)$, and for every partition $\mathsf J=\mathsf J'\sqcup\{b_1,\dots,b_p\}$ ($p\geq0$)
\begin{equation*}
    \Bigl|\Bigl(\prod_{b\in\mathsf J'}D_b\prod_{i=1}^pR_{b_i}\varphi\Bigr)(B)\Bigr|\leq2^{-p}u_t^{|\mathsf J'|+2p}\|N\|\;,\qquad\prod_{b\in\mathsf J}D_b\varphi-\prod_{b\in\mathsf J'}D_b\prod_{i=1}^pR_{b_i}\varphi=\sum_{i=1}^p\prod_{b\in\mathsf J'}D_b\Bigl(\prod_{i'<i}D_{b_{i'}}\Bigr)L_{b_i}\Bigl(\prod_{i'>i}R_{b_{i'}}\Bigr)\varphi\;,
\end{equation*}
where the $i$-th term of the sum is, at fixed values of the coordinates other than $B_{b_i}$, a linear function of $B_{b_i}$: $B_{b_i}\mapsto\langle B_{b_i},c_i(B|_{\mathsf J\setminus b_i})\rangle$. Both (a) and (b) hold at the reference data ($U=\id$, base point $a_0=0$, the link factors $\pi(L_n)$ at the flat phase) with the same constants, as Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z} does: the proofs use only the charts and the Cauchy estimates of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}, which hold there.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}(a) with $m_*$ replaced by $2m_*$: Cauchy's estimate and polarisation give $\|D^k\Pi_n(A)[h_1,\dots,h_k]\|\leq k^kC_\Pi(4/\vartheta_*)^k\prod\|h_i\|$ for every $k$, and $k^k\leq(2m_*)^k$ for $k\leq2m_*$, so $\|D^k\Pi_n(A)[h_1,\dots,h_k]\|\leq(2m_*)^kC_\Pi(4/\vartheta_*)^k\prod\|h_i\|$ and $\|\partial_{e_1,b_1}\cdots\partial_{e_k,b_k}\pi(L_n)\|\leq(8m_*C_\Pi C_{\mathsf C}g_0/\vartheta_*)^k\leq(2C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0)^k$; for a free bond the Duhamel bound $(C_\pi g_0)^k\leq(2C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0)^k$ holds for every $k$. (b) $D_b=L_b+R_b$ is Taylor's formula with integral remainder for $s\mapsto\Psi(B|_{B_b\to sB_b})$ on $[0,1]$. Expanding $\prod_{b\in\mathsf J}D_b$ (value minus value with the $b$-th coordinate zeroed) gives $\sum_{K\subseteq\mathsf J}(-1)^{|\mathsf J\setminus K|}\varphi(\mathbf 1_KB)=\Delta_{\mathsf J}g(B)$. The telescoping identity $\prod_i(L_{b_i}+R_{b_i})-\prod_iR_{b_i}=\sum_i(\prod_{i'<i}(L+R)_{b_{i'}})L_{b_i}(\prod_{i'>i}R_{b_{i'}})$ with $(L+R)_b=D_b$, and the commuting factor $\prod_{\mathsf J'}D_b$, give the second display. $L_{b_i}\Psi$ is linear in $B_{b_i}$ with a coefficient not depending on $B_{b_i}$; the operators at bonds $b\neq b_i$ are linear and act on the other coordinates, so they preserve this form. The bound: $D_b\Psi(B)=\int_0^1\partial_{s_b}\Psi(B|_{B_b\to s_bB_b})\,\dd s_b$ with $\partial_{s_b}=h(B_b)\partial_{B_b/h(B_b),b}$, and $R_{b_i}$ contributes $\int_0^1(1-s_i)\,\dd s_i\cdot h(B_{b_i})^2\partial^2_{e_i,b_i}$, $e_i:=B_{b_i}/h(B_{b_i})$; iterating, $\prod_{\mathsf J'}D_b\prod_iR_{b_i}\varphi(B)$ is an integral over $[0,1]^{|\mathsf J'|+p}$ with weights $\prod_i(1-s_i)$ of $\prod_{\mathsf J'}h(B_b)\prod_ih(B_{b_i})^2\cdot(\partial^{\mathsf J'}\partial^2_{e_1,b_1}\cdots\partial^2_{e_p,b_p}g)(a_0+B^s)$, every evaluation point real and in the enlarged box ($|s_bB_b+a_{0,b}|_\sigma\leq3r/2\leq2r$). The mixed derivative of order $Q:=|\mathsf J'|+2p$ is bounded as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7z}(b): product rule inside the normalised trace, at most $N_{\mathrm{tr}}^Q$ assignments of the $Q$ directions to link factors whose input set contains the direction's bond, fixed factors contributing $\|N\|$, undifferentiated links unitary, and each differentiated link carrying at most two directions per bond of its input set, hence at most $2|\mathcal F_{r_{n_i}}|\leq2m_*-2$ directions, bounded by (a); so $\sup|\partial^{\dots}g|\leq(2N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0)^Q\|N\|$. With $h(B_b)\leq|B_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4$, $\int_0^1(1-s)\,\dd s=1/2$ and $(5r/4)\cdot2N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0\leq2N_{\mathrm{tr}}C_{\mathrm{link}}g_0(1+2r)=u_t$, the bound is $2^{-p}u_t^Q\|N\|$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{Evenness, marks paid by the large-field reserve, and the Cauchy parameter}
For a split selection $\ell=(\mathcal D,\mathsf J,\mathsf P,Z)$ for the carrier $D(\Lambda)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(a)) and $x\in\{1/8,1\}$ put
\begin{equation*}
    \mathfrak M^{(x)}(\ell):=e^{-(\kappa_1-1)N_1(Z\setminus Z_0')+N_1(Z)-x\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}|\mathsf P|}\prod_{Y\in\mathcal D}(e^{A_Y}-1)\;,\qquad A_Y:=(\omega_*/2)e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\;,
\end{equation*}
the right side of \eqref{eq:c5-5-3} with the host factor $e^{A_Y}-1\leq\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$ (as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(c)) and the reserve exponent multiplied by $x$; $\mathfrak M^{(1)}\leq\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}$. ($\mathfrak M^{(x)}(\ell)$ is a majorant of a single selection and is not the mark weight $\mathfrak M_\Lambda(Z)$ of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5}.)

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:c5-5R-2}
Let $\ell$ be a split selection for $D(\Lambda)$, $\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}$ its measure on $\mathfrak g^{E_0}$ ($t\in[0,1]$) and $\Lambda^{f,\circ}_{\Lambda;\ell}$ the reference measure (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b)); the hypotheses are those of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
    \item (Evenness.) If $\mathcal D=\varnothing$ then $\Lambda^{f,0}_{\Lambda;\ell}$ and $\Lambda^{f,\circ}_{\Lambda;\ell}$ are invariant under $B\mapsto-B$ on $\mathfrak g^{E_0}$: $\int F(-B)\,\dd\Lambda(B)=\int F(B)\,\dd\Lambda(B)$ for bounded Borel $F$. In particular $\int\Psi\,\dd\Lambda=0$ for every bounded Borel $\Psi$ with $\Psi(-B)=-\Psi(B)$, e.g. $\Psi(B)=\langle B_b,c\rangle\mathbf 1\{|B_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4\}$ for a bond $b\in E(Y_0)$ and a fixed $c\in\mathfrak g$ (the truncation is even and equals 1 on the support of the measure, where $|B_b|_\sigma<r$ by the cutoff $\chi^0_b$; the untruncated linear function is not bounded).
    \item (Marked difference, large-field payment.) If $\mathcal D=\varnothing$ and $|\mathsf J|=1$, then under \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}(i), for bounded Borel $F$, $|\int F\,\dd(\Lambda^{f,1}_{\Lambda;\ell}-\Lambda^{f,0}_{\Lambda;\ell})|\leq\sup|F|\cdot2C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell)$.
    \item (Cauchy parameter.) For $\zeta\in\mathbb C$ let $\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}(\zeta)$ be defined by the display of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1} for the enlarged data with each Mayer factor $\mathbf W_t(Y,B)e^{t_Y\mathbf W_t(Y,B)}$, $Y\in\mathcal D$, replaced by $\zeta\mathbf W_t(Y,B)e^{t_Y\zeta\mathbf W_t(Y,B)}$ (every near primitive assigned to a host of $\mathcal D$ multiplied by $\zeta$; density, cutoffs, probes unchanged), so that $\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}(1)=\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}$; put $R_\Upsilon:=\omega_*/(2\omega_t)\geq1$ (\eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(ii)). For bounded Borel $F$ the function $\zeta\mapsto\int F\,\dd\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}(\zeta)$ is entire; it vanishes at $\zeta=0$ if $\mathcal D\neq\varnothing$; and $|\int F\,\dd\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}(\zeta)|\leq\sup|F|\cdot\mathfrak M^{(1)}(\ell)$ for $|\zeta|\leq R_\Upsilon$. Consequently $\|\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\mathfrak M^{(1)}(\ell)$, and if $\mathcal D\neq\varnothing$ then $|\int F\,\dd\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}|\leq(2\omega_t/\omega_*)\cdot\sup_{|\zeta|\leq R_\Upsilon}|\int F\,\dd\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}(\zeta)|\leq(2\omega_t/\omega_*)\sup|F|\mathfrak M^{(1)}(\ell)$. The same holds at the reference data.
    \item (Weakened dummy comparison.) Under \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}(i), for every connected final domain $Z$ for $D(\Lambda)$ and $x\in\{1/8,1\}$, $\sum_{\ell\, : \,Z(\ell)=Z}u_*^{|\mathsf J|}\mathfrak M^{(x)}(\ell)\leq m(Z)$, the sum over all split selections with final domain $Z$ and $Z_0\neq\varnothing$ ($\mathsf J=\varnothing$ included).
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
(a) With $\mathcal D=\varnothing$ the display of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1} for the enlarged data has, inside the $s$- and $\sigma$-integrations (which do not involve $(B,X)$), the integrand $\prod_{b\in E(Y_0)}\chi^0_b(B)\prod_{b\in\mathsf P}(1-\chi^0_b(B))F(B)$ against the density \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-density} $e^{-\frac12\langle\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X,C_{E_0}\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X\rangle}e^{-\langle B,\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X\rangle}\,\dd\mu_{C_{E_0}}(B)\,\dd\mu_0(X)$. Substitute $(B,X)\to(-B,-X)$: $\mu_{C_{E_0}}$ and $\mu_0$ are centred Gaussians, hence invariant; the quadratic form in $X$ and the bilinear form $\langle B,\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X\rangle$ are invariant under the joint sign change; $\chi^0_b(-B)=\mathbf 1\{|-B_b|_\sigma<r\}=\chi^0_b(B)$ and likewise $1-\chi^0_b$ ($|\cdot|_\sigma$ is a norm); there is no Mayer factor. Hence $\int F(-B)\,\dd\Lambda^0=\int F\,\dd\Lambda^0$. At the reference data the display is the same with $H^\circ$, $m^\circ=0$ and the cutoffs $\chi_b(a)=\mathbf 1\{|a_b|_\sigma<r\}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b)). For odd $\Psi$, $\int\Psi\,\dd\Lambda=\int\Psi(-B)\,\dd\Lambda=-\int\Psi\,\dd\Lambda$. (b) With $\mathcal D=\varnothing$ and $\mathsf J=\{b_0\}$, the $t$-dependent factors of the display are $\chi^t_b$, $b\in E(Y_0)=E(Y_{b_0})$ (at most $C_M$ bonds), and $1-\chi^t_b$, $b\in\mathsf P$ (proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-6}(a)); order them and telescope, $\prod a_k-\prod c_k=\sum_k(\prod_{i<k}a_i)(a_k-c_k)(\prod_{i>k}c_i)$, $a$ the $t=1$ and $c$ the $t=0$ factors: at most $C_M+|\mathsf P|$ terms, each with exactly one differenced factor (the mark at a bond $b$), of modulus $|\chi^1_b-\chi^0_b|\leq\mathbf 1_{S_b}(B_b)\leq\mathbf 1\{|B_b|_\sigma\geq r-|m_b|_\sigma\}\leq\mathbf 1\{|B_b|_\sigma\geq3r/4\}\leq e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{(\alpha_G/2)h(B_b)^2}$ (\eqref{eq:c5-4-1} and the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2}). Bound the modulus of each term factor by factor as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2}: undifferenced cutoffs $\leq1$; undifferenced bad factors $1-\chi^t_{b'}\leq e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{(\alpha_G/2)h(B_{b'})^2}$; the mark as displayed; $|F|\leq\sup|F|$; the product of the exponential factors is $e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}n}e^{(\alpha_G/2)\|B\|^2}$ with $n:=|\mathsf P|+\mathbf 1\{b\notin\mathsf P\}\geq\max(|\mathsf P|,1)$, and the density's modulus against $e^{(\alpha_G/2)\|B\|^2}$ integrates to at most $e^{N_1(Z)}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment}(ii) for $H$), the weakening giving $e^{-(\kappa_1-1)N_1(Z\setminus Z_0')}$. Since $\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}n\geq\frac9{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}|\mathsf P|+\frac{63}{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}\cdot\max(|\mathsf P|,1)$, each term is at most $\sup|F|\cdot e^{-\frac{63}{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}\max(|\mathsf P|,1)}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell)$, and the number of terms is at most $C_M+|\mathsf P|\leq C_M(1+|\mathsf P|)$. For $|\mathsf P|=0$ this gives $C_Me^{-\frac{63}{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}$; for $|\mathsf P|=k\geq1$, $C_M(1+k)e^{-\frac{63}{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}k}=C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}\cdot(1+k)e^{-\frac{63k-36}{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}\leq C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}\cdot(1+k)e^{-\frac{27}{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}k}\leq2C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}$, because $63k-36\geq27k$ for $k\geq1$ and $(1+k)e^{-ck}\leq2e^{-c}\leq2$ for $c:=\frac{27}{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}\geq1$ (implied by \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}(i)). (c) For fixed $(X,B,s,\sigma,(t_Y))$ the integrand is $\prod_{Y\in\mathcal D}\zeta\mathbf W_te^{t_Y\zeta\mathbf W_t}$ times a $\zeta$-independent factor, entire in $\zeta$; $\mathbf W_t(Y,B)$ vanishes off the translated local box of $Y$ and has $|\mathbf W_t(Y,B)|\leq\omega_te^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}\leq\omega_t$ on it (Section~\ref{sec:c5-4-2}, \eqref{eq:c5-4-4}), so on $|\zeta|\leq R$ the integrand's modulus is at most $(R\omega_te^{R\omega_t})^{|\mathcal D|}\sup|F|$ times the density's modulus, which is integrable (Lemma~\ref{lem:gaussian-payment}(ii)); the $s$-, $\sigma$-, $t_Y$-integrals are over compact sets. Dominated convergence gives continuity in $\zeta$ and, with Morera's theorem (or differentiation under the integral sign), analyticity on every disc: the function is entire. At $\zeta=0$ every Mayer factor vanishes and $\mathcal D\neq\varnothing$, so the integral is zero. For $|\zeta|\leq R_\Upsilon$, integrate $t_Y$ first: $\int_0^1\zeta\mathbf W_te^{t_Y\zeta\mathbf W_t}\,\dd t_Y=e^{\zeta\mathbf W_t}-1$, of modulus $\leq e^{|\zeta\mathbf W_t|}-1\leq e^{R_\Upsilon\omega_te^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}}-1=e^{A_Y}-1$ on the support of $\Theta^t_{E(Y)}$; the other factors are bounded as in the proofs of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2} and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(b) (cutoffs $\leq1$, bad bonds by $e^{-\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}e^{(\alpha_G/2)h^2}$, Gaussian payment $e^{N_1(Z)}$, weakening), giving $\sup|F|\mathfrak M^{(1)}(\ell)$. The TV bound is the case $\zeta=1$ (for $\mathcal D=\varnothing$ there is no $\zeta$, and the bound is the factor-by-factor bound directly). Schwarz's lemma for $\Phi(\zeta):=\int F\,\dd\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell}(\zeta)$ on $|\zeta|\leq R_\Upsilon$ with $\Phi(0)=0$ and $|\Phi|\leq M:=\sup|F|\mathfrak M^{(1)}(\ell)$ gives $|\Phi(\zeta)|\leq M|\zeta|/R_\Upsilon$, and $\zeta=1$ lies in the disc since $R_\Upsilon\geq1$. At the reference data \eqref{eq:c5-4-4} holds by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c) for these data (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b)). (d) The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(c): the hostwise comparison bounds the sum over the nonempty label subsets of a host of the weights $e^{A_Y}-1$ (ordinary) and $u_*$ (probe) by $\omega_*e^{1/2}e^{-\kappa d_0(Y)}$, so $\sum_\ell u_*^{|\mathsf J|}\mathfrak M^{(x)}(\ell)$ is at most the sum over the ordinary selections $\mathfrak s=(\mathcal D,\mathsf P,Z)$ of the majorant \eqref{eq:c5-4-5} with $x\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ in place of $\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$; the seed, enlargement and final-domain sums of Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} bound this by $m(Z)$ exactly as in Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3}, whose proof applies to every number $\geq1$ in the role of $a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ that satisfies \eqref{eq:native-source-bad-budget}, which $\frac9{128}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ does by \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}(i) (and $\frac9{16}a_{\mathrm{bad}}$ by \eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp}).
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{The second-order group bound}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:c5-5R-3}
Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}, \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T} and the setting of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0} (on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$), for every group $W$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(a)), every $h\in G$ and $t\in\{0,1\}$: $|c^t_W(h)|\leq M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W)$ and $|a^\Lambda_W|\leq M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W)$. Hence $\bar{\mathfrak d}_W\leq2M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W)=:\bar{\mathfrak b}^{\mathrm{ph}}_W$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7}(e) improved), and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8} holds with $(M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda,\rho_{\mathrm{ph}},\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}})$ in place of $(M_\Lambda,\rho_t,\bar\varepsilon_t)$:
\begin{equation*}
    \sup_\Delta\sum_{W\, : \,\Delta\in F(W)}e^{2\nu\sigma(W)}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}^{\mathrm{ph}}_W\leq\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}\;,\qquad\sup_n\sum_{W\, : \,n\in\mathcal H(W)}e^{2\nu\sigma(W)}K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}^{\mathrm{ph}}_W\leq\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}\;,\qquad\sum_WK_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}^{\mathrm{ph}}_W\leq n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}\;,
\end{equation*}
and (b) of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8} likewise with $\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}$. The differenced bound \eqref{eq:c5-5-8}, its majorant $M_\Lambda(W)$ and $\theta^\circ(W)\leq\nu\sigma(W)/16$ are unchanged.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix $W$, $h$ and $t\in\{0,1\}$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(a),(d) and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(a), $c^t_W(h)=w^t_\Lambda(W)\sum_{(\ell_Z)_{Z\in\mathcal A(W)}\, : \,\mathsf J_Z\neq\varnothing}\int\Delta_{\mathsf J}g_t\,\dd\bigotimes_Z\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell_Z}$, with $g_t(a)=\dim(\pi)^{-1}\operatorname{tr}(\pi(T_W(a))N_t)$, $N_t:=\pi(P^t_W)^{-1}\pi(h)$ fixed unitary, base point $a_0=tm$, $\mathsf J=\bigsqcup_Z\mathsf J_Z$, and $|w^t_\Lambda(W)|\leq(1/k(W)!)\prod_j|\Phi|^+_\Lambda(\mathfrak c_j)$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3} and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-6}(b), as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d)). The integrand $\Delta_{\mathsf J}g_t$ depends on $B$ through the labels $B_{\mathsf J}$ only, and the labels of the root $Z$ are its $\mathsf J_Z\subseteq E_0(\ell_Z)$. Fix a tuple $(\ell_Z)$ and sort its roots into three classes: ($\alpha$) $|\mathsf J_Z|\geq2$; ($\beta$) $|\mathsf J_Z|=1$ and $\mathcal D_Z\neq\varnothing$; ($\gamma$) $|\mathsf J_Z|=1$ and $\mathcal D_Z=\varnothing$, with single label $b_Z$.

Step 1 (splitting the class-($\gamma$) measures; $t=1$ only). For each class-($\gamma$) root write $\Lambda^{f,1}_{\Lambda;\ell_Z}=\Lambda^{f,0}_{\Lambda;\ell_Z}+\delta\Lambda_{\ell_Z}$ and expand the product measure: $\bigotimes_Z\Lambda^{f,1}_{\ell_Z}=\sum_{\mathcal S\subseteq(\gamma)}[\bigotimes_{Z\in\mathcal S}\delta\Lambda_{\ell_Z}]\otimes[\bigotimes_{Z\in(\gamma)\setminus\mathcal S}\Lambda^{f,0}_{\ell_Z}]\otimes[\bigotimes_{Z\notin(\gamma)}\Lambda^{f,1}_{\ell_Z}]$. At $t=0$ only $\mathcal S=\varnothing$ occurs. Fix $\mathcal S$; the roots of $(\gamma)\setminus\mathcal S$ are the parity roots, $Z_1,\dots,Z_p$ with labels $b_1,\dots,b_p$, and $\mathsf J':=\mathsf J\setminus\{b_1,\dots,b_p\}$.

Step 2 (parity). By Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-1}(b) with $\varphi_t:=g_t(\cdot+tm)$ ($|tm_b|_\sigma\leq r/4$ by \eqref{eq:c5-4-1}; $|B_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4$ on the supports of the measures, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-2}(a); every function of the labels below, $\varphi_t$, the $L$- and $R$-terms, $g^{(p)}$, is multiplied by the even truncation $\prod_{b\in\mathsf J}\mathbf 1\{|B_b|_\sigma\leq5r/4\}$, which equals 1 on the supports of the measures and makes it bounded Borel on $\mathfrak g^{E_0}$, so that Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-2} applies), $\Delta_{\mathsf J}g_t=g^{(p)}+\sum_{i=1}^p(\text{term linear in }B_{b_i})$, $g^{(p)}:=\prod_{\mathsf J'}D_b\prod_iR_{b_i}\varphi_t$. The $i$-th linear term depends on the variables of the root $Z_i$ through $B_{b_i}$ alone ($\mathsf J_{Z_i}=\{b_i\}$) and is odd under $B_{E_0(Z_i)}\to-B_{E_0(Z_i)}$ at fixed values of the other roots' variables; the measures are finite and the integrand is bounded Borel, so by Fubini and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-2}(a) ($\Lambda^{f,0}_{\ell_{Z_i}}$ is even, $\mathcal D_{Z_i}=\varnothing$) its integral against the product measure of Step 1 vanishes.
