Hence $\int\Delta_{\mathsf J}g_t\,\dd(\text{product for }\mathcal S)=\int g^{(p)}\,\dd(\text{product for }\mathcal S)$, and $\sup|g^{(p)}|\leq2^{-p}u_t^{|\mathsf J'|+2p}$ ($\|N_t\|=1$).

Step 3 (Cauchy parameter). Let $\Phi((\zeta_Z)_{Z\in(\beta)}):=\int g^{(p)}\,\dd[\bigotimes_{Z\in(\beta)}\Lambda^{f,t}_{\Lambda;\ell_Z}(\zeta_Z)\otimes(\text{the other factors of the product for }\mathcal S)]$. $\Phi$ is analytic in each $\zeta_Z$ separately on the open polydisc $\{|\zeta_Z|<R_\Upsilon\}$: at fixed values of the other roots' variables, the integral over the variables of the root $Z$ is, for every bounded Borel integrand, entire in $\zeta_Z$ with the uniform bound $\sup|\text{integrand}|\cdot\mathfrak M^{(1)}(\ell_Z)$ on $|\zeta_Z|\leq R_\Upsilon$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-2}(c), a single-root statement), and this uniform bound carries analyticity through the integrals over the other roots (finite measures, Fubini; dominated convergence and Morera's theorem for the outer integral). Analyticity on the open polydisc with a bound on the closed one is all Schwarz's lemma below needs. $\Phi$ vanishes whenever some $\zeta_Z=0$, and on the polydisc $\{|\zeta_Z|\leq R_\Upsilon\ \forall Z\in(\beta)\}$ satisfies $|\Phi|\leq M_\Phi:=\sup|g^{(p)}|\cdot\prod_{Z\in(\beta)}\mathfrak M^{(1)}(\ell_Z)\cdot\prod_{Z\in\mathcal S}\|\delta\Lambda_{\ell_Z}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\cdot\prod_{Z\in(\alpha)\cup((\gamma)\setminus\mathcal S)}\|\Lambda_{\ell_Z}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}$ (the TV norms of the $t=0$ measures of the parity roots, of the $t=1$ measures of class ($\alpha$)). Schwarz's lemma one variable at a time, from the inside out: write $(\beta)=\{Z_1,\dots,Z_q\}$; for each fixed $(\zeta_{Z_1},\dots,\zeta_{Z_{q-1}})$ in the polydisc, $\zeta_{Z_q}\mapsto\Phi(\zeta_{Z_1},\dots,\zeta_{Z_{q-1}},\zeta_{Z_q})$ vanishes at 0 and is bounded by $M_\Phi$ on $|\zeta_{Z_q}|\leq R_\Upsilon$, so $|\Phi(\zeta_{Z_1},\dots,\zeta_{Z_{q-1}},1)|\leq M_\Phi/R_\Upsilon$ on the polydisc of the remaining $q-1$ variables; the function $(\zeta_{Z_1},\dots,\zeta_{Z_{q-1}})\mapsto\Phi(\dots,1)$ is again entire in each variable, vanishes when any variable is 0 and is bounded by $M_\Phi/R_\Upsilon$, so the same step in $\zeta_{Z_{q-1}}$ gives $M_\Phi/R_\Upsilon^2$ at $\zeta_{Z_{q-1}}=1$, and after $q$ steps $|\Phi(1,\dots,1)|\leq R_\Upsilon^{-q}M_\Phi=(2\omega_t/\omega_*)^{|(\beta)|}M_\Phi$.

Step 4 (collecting). With $\|\Lambda_{\ell_Z}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq\mathfrak M^{(1)}(\ell_Z)\leq\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-2}(c)) and $\|\delta\Lambda_{\ell_Z}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\leq2C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-2}(b)), the summand for $((\ell_Z),\mathcal S)$ is at most
\begin{equation*}
    \prod_{Z\in(\alpha)}u_t^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)\cdot\prod_{Z\in(\beta)}u_t(2\omega_t/\omega_*)\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)\cdot\prod_{Z\in(\gamma)\setminus\mathcal S}(u_t^2/2)\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)\cdot\prod_{Z\in\mathcal S}u_t\cdot2C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)\;.
\end{equation*}
Summing over $\mathcal S\subseteq(\gamma)$, a class-($\gamma$) root contributes $[u_t^2/2+2C_Mu_te^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}]\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)=\rho_t[u_t/2+2C_Me^{-\frac9{32}a_{\mathrm{bad}}}]u_*^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)\leq\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}u_*^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)$; a class-($\beta$) root $\rho_t(2\omega_t/\omega_*)u_*^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)\leq\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}u_*^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)$; a class-($\alpha$) root $\rho_t^{|\mathsf J_Z|}u_*^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)\leq\rho_t^2u_*^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)\leq\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}u_*^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)$ ($\rho_t\leq1$ by \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(i)). Summing over the tuples, $\sum_{(\ell_Z)}\prod_Z\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}u_*^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)=\prod_Z[\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}\sum_{\ell_Z\, : \,Z(\ell_Z)=Z}u_*^{|\mathsf J_Z|}\mathfrak M^{(1/8)}(\ell_Z)]\leq\prod_Z\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}m(Z)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-2}(d) (the sum over all split selections dominates the sum over those with $\mathsf J_Z\neq\varnothing$). Multiplying by $|w^t_\Lambda(W)|$ gives $|c^t_W(h)|\leq M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W)$.

The reference activity $a^\Lambda_W=w^\circ_\Lambda(W)\int g^\circ_W\,\dd\bigotimes_Z\hat\nu^{f,\circ}_{\Lambda,Z}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b),(d)) is treated by the same four steps at the reference data, where Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-2} holds (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b)): base point 0, increments $|B_b|_\sigma\leq r\leq5r/4$, $\mathcal S=\varnothing$ throughout (the reference cutoffs are even, so every class-($\gamma$) root is a parity root), $g^\circ_W$ a closed contraction with $\|N\|=1$, and $|w^\circ_\Lambda(W)|\leq(1/k(W)!)\prod_j|\Phi|^+_\Lambda(\mathfrak c_j)$.

Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7}(e) improved: $\bar{\mathfrak d}_W=\max_t\sup_h|c^t_W(h)-a^\Lambda_W\chi(h)|\leq2M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W)$. The pins: the first display of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(a) is the positive summation with a root weight $\rho\,m(Z)$, and the inherited summation bound (\draftref{stage 4, \S6.2}, \draftref{attempt, \S18.3}) holds in the form ``sum $\leq C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho$ for every $\rho\leq\rho_{\mathrm{pin}}$'' (its smallness conditions on $\rho$ are monotone, so every $\rho\leq\rho_{\mathrm{pin}}$ satisfies them, and the bound is linear in $\rho$ at each such $\rho$; monotonicity alone would only give $C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t$); applied at $\rho=\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}\leq\rho_{\mathrm{pin}}$ it gives $C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}$. Equivalently, $M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W)=(\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}/\rho_t)^{|\mathcal A(W)|}M_\Lambda(W)$ with $|\mathcal A(W)|\geq1$, so the first-order pin at $\rho_t$ gives $C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t(\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}/\rho_t)=C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}$ as well; the second display uses $\bar{\mathfrak b}_W\leq2M_\Lambda(W)$ and $e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\leq e^{2\nu\sigma(W)}$, now with $\bar{\mathfrak b}^{\mathrm{ph}}_W=2M^{\mathrm{ph}}_\Lambda(W)$ and the unchanged $\theta^\circ(W)\leq\nu\sigma(W)/16$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d), which uses the first-order $M_\Lambda$, $|a^\Lambda_W|\leq M_\Lambda(W)$ and \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}(iv), all still in force); Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(b) is the geometric fact applied to the new pin.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{The repaired bounds}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:c5-5R-4}
Under the hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5-9} and \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}, on every labelled term with final region $\Lambda$, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-5-9R}
    |\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]|\leq|S^\circ_\Lambda|e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}\bigl[C_1g_0r\,\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)+C_2\bar\varepsilon_tr^{d_G}\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)\bigr]\;,
\end{equation}
with $C_1$, $C_2$, $\mu'$, $\mathfrak S_1$, $\mathfrak S_2$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5-9}; on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}\cap N_b^c$, \eqref{eq:c5-5-10} holds with $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}$ in place of $e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$; and \eqref{eq:c5-6-3}, \eqref{eq:c5-6-4} of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-6-4} and its closing form hold with $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}$ in place of $e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$. In the square geometry of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0} ($n_C=4nL$, $4n$ coarse segments) the family-sum factor has, under \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}(iii), the perimeter rate $L\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}\leq32LK_\chi C_{\mathrm{pin}}C_{\mathrm{ph}}g_0^2(1+r)^4$ per coarse segment, second order in $g_0(1+r)^2$, of the order $\tau^2$ of $E^\circ$; the first-order constant $\bar\varepsilon_t$ survives only as the prefactor $C_2\bar\varepsilon_t$ of $\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)$ (through Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-5-10} it multiplies $n_CN_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu'/2)e^{-\mu'(b-1)/2}+\mathfrak D_\Lambda$) and no longer appears in an exponent.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5-9} with one change. In \eqref{eq:c5-5-7} the undifferenced slots carry $\bar{\mathfrak d}_W$, bounded by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-3} instead of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-7}(e); so the two family sums $\prod_W(1+K_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak d}_W)$, in the background-word part, and over $\mathfrak B\setminus\{W_0\}$ in the marked part, are at most $\exp(\sum_WK_\chi e^{\theta^\circ(W)}\bar{\mathfrak b}^{\mathrm{ph}}_W)\leq e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}$ (the contour pin of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5R-3} summed over the $n_C$ traversals, every group having a contact with the contour). The differenced slot $\mathfrak d^{\mathrm{diff}}_{W_0}$ keeps \eqref{eq:c5-5-8} with the first-order $M_\Lambda(W_0)$ and the pins of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8} with $\bar\varepsilon_t$, so (i) and (ii) of that proof and the constants $C_1$, $C_2$ are unchanged, and the closing step $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}\leq e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$ is dropped. Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-5-10} and Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-6-4} use \eqref{eq:c5-5-9} only through its right side. The rate: $n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}=4n\cdot L\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}$, and $\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}\leq32K_\chi C_{\mathrm{pin}}C_{\mathrm{ph}}g_0^2(1+r)^4$ by the display above under \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T}(iii).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[scope of this subsection]\label{rem:c5-5R-5}
The mechanisms (parity of the kernel, the parameter $\zeta$ on the ordinary primitives) are those of \draftref{stage 4, \S4.1}, re-proved here from the display of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-1}, the factor bounds of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2} and Schwarz's lemma, and not imported. The differenced slots and the marked part keep the first-order prefactor $\bar\varepsilon_t$ (\eqref{eq:c5-5-8}), harmless because it multiplies the exterior sum and not $n_C$. The family-sum factor remains $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}\geq1$ and is not uniform in $n_C$: like $e^{E^\circ}$ (\eqref{eq:regional-square-periodic-comparison}), it grows with the perimeter at a second-order rate; a form linear in $n_C$ would need the resummation of the undifferenced slots against the reference exclusions, not done. The inherited inputs of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0} (the pin sums for every $\rho\leq\rho_{\mathrm{pin}}$, the host comparison, the reference-gas expansion, the tree-graph inequality, the square geometry) remain inherited. The quantity $\rho_{\mathrm{ph}}$ of this subsection is not the quantity of the same name in \draftref{attempt, \S20.1} (built from different constants); both are $O(g_0^2(1+r)^4)$, and nothing of that section is imported.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The labelled history sum}
\label{sec:c5-6}

The history sum of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} is the sum over the branch data $\xi=(\Omega_1,R_1,V,V_0,A_{\mathrm e})$ inside the outer operation $\mathcal O$, each term carrying the weight $\mathsf w_\xi=e^{c_\xi}I_{\xi;1}=\int_{\mathfrak g^I}\rho_\xi\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}$ and the conditional Wilson expectation $F_\xi(1)=\mathcal J^a_\xi[f_\xi]/\mathcal J^a_\xi[1]$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:collar}, \eqref{eq:c-collar-identity}), and the leading term, the defects and the exterior response are read off \eqref{eq:c-one-step-branch-identity}. After Section~\ref{sec:c5-3} every history term is the finite sum of its labelled terms ($\mathrm{lab}=(B^{(0)},B^{(1)},\dots)$, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(a)), with $\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}=1$ pointwise (Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(c)); a labelled term has the final region $\Lambda:=\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}(\mathrm{lab})$, carrier $D(\Lambda)$, exterior $J(\Lambda)=J\cup(I\setminus D(\Lambda))$, and the objects of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5} and Sections~\ref{sec:c5-4}--\ref{sec:c5-5R}. Write $\mathcal J^{a,\mathrm{lab}}_\xi[f]:=\int_{\mathfrak g^I}\rho_\xi\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}f\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}$ for bounded Borel $f$ on $\mathfrak g^I$; then $\mathcal J^a_\xi[f]=\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathcal J^{a,\mathrm{lab}}_\xi[f]$ (Tonelli for $f\geq0$, then by linearity), and the extra resummation index is $\mathrm{lab}$.

\begin{lemma}[the labelled branch identity]\label{lem:c5-6-1}
On every history and every labelled term, with the convention that $e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}I^\Lambda_1F^\Lambda(1)$ and $e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}I^\Lambda_1$ denote the integrals $\int_{\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}}\rho_\xi(a,a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)})f_\xi(a)\,\dd a$ and $\int_{\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}}\rho_\xi(a,a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)})\,\dd a$ over the retreated fibre (both vanish where the fibre is empty, i.e. where $|a_{\mathrm{old},b}|_\sigma\geq r$ for some $b\in I\setminus D(\Lambda)$, since $\rho_\xi$ carries the box factor $\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D_\xi}$; $c^\Lambda_\xi$ and $F^\Lambda(1)$ themselves are used only where the fibre is nonempty),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-6-1}
    \mathcal J^{a,\mathrm{lab}}_\xi[f_\xi]=\int_{\mathfrak g^{I\setminus D(\Lambda)}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}I^\Lambda_1F^\Lambda(1)\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)}\;,\qquad\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi=\int_{\mathfrak g^{I\setminus D(\Lambda)}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}I^\Lambda_1\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)}\;,
\end{equation}
and, on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ (an event depending on the labels through the region only), on the support of $\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}$ (which carries the collar condition $|A_{\mathrm e}|_\sigma<\vartheta r$ on $J_{\mathrm{col}}(\Lambda)$ required by Section~\ref{sec:c5-4} and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2}; every use of \eqref{eq:c5-6-2} below is multiplied by $\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}$), where the fibre is nonempty, under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-2} and \eqref{eq:c5-5-T} (through Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}, whose absolute convergence and reference gas rest on the inputs listed in Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0}),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-6-2}
    F^\Lambda(1)=S^\circ_\Lambda W_\chi(C;U_1)+\sum_{\varnothing\neq\mathfrak B\text{ compatible}}S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda R^0_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)+\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]\;,
\end{equation}
the retreated carrier's version of \eqref{eq:c-one-step-branch-identity}, with $S^\circ_\Lambda$, $S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda$, $R^0_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3} and $\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-4}, bounded by Propositions~\ref{prop:c5-5-9} and~\ref{prop:c5-5R-4}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-5}, pointwise $e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}K^\Lambda_{\xi;1}(a)=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(a)|\det D\Psi_\xi(A_{\mathrm e})|\exp\mathscr E_\xi(\Psi_\xi(a+A^\Lambda_{\mathrm e}))=\rho_\xi(a_{\mathrm{old}})$ at $a_{\mathrm{old}}=(a,a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)})$ (the identity $\rho_\xi=e^{c_\xi}K_{\xi;1}$ of \draftref{stage 3, \S7.2}, the same density in the two splits), and $f_\xi$, $\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}$ are functions of the same variables ($\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}$ of $A_{\mathrm e}$ and $a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)}$ only); integrate over $a\in\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}$ first (Fubini; $\rho_\xi\geq0$, $f_\xi$ and $\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}$ bounded): $\int K^\Lambda_{\xi;1}f_\xi\,\dd a=I^\Lambda_1F^\Lambda(1)$ by the definition of $\nu^\Lambda_1$ in Section~\ref{sec:c5-4} where the fibre is nonempty (both sides vanish otherwise), and with $f=1$ the weight. For \eqref{eq:c5-6-2}: $F^\Lambda(1)=F^\Lambda(0)+\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-4}), $F^\Lambda(0)=S^\circ_\Lambda f_\xi(0)+\sum_{\mathfrak B}S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda R^0_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(c) at $t=0$), and $f_\xi(0)=W_\chi(C;U_1)$ on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$: at $a=0$ the free vector is ($0$ on $D(\Lambda)$, $A^{\Lambda,\Psi}$ on $J(\Lambda)$), every contour bond is a bond of $\Omega_1$ and its free inputs lie in $D(\Lambda)$, so the argument of Lemma~\ref{lem:covered} with $I$ replaced by $D(\Lambda)$ gives $U_\xi(0)=U_1$ on the contour (the de-younged bonds, though nonzero, are not inputs of the contour on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$).
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{The species and their locality}
The species of the labelled term are the final domains $Z$ for the carrier $D(\Lambda)$ with their activities $k^{f,t}_\Lambda(Z)$ and probe measures $\nu^{f,t}_{\Lambda,Z}$ (Lemmas~\ref{lem:c5-4-1}, \ref{lem:c5-5-2}), the groups $W$ with their weights $w^t_\Lambda(W)$ and reference activities $a^\Lambda_W$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}), and the marked-polymer objects built from them (the pins of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}). They depend on the labels through $D(\Lambda)$ only. Let $\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}$ be the set of $LM$-cubes meeting a bond of $I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ (no site lies on a cube face, so a bond meets exactly the one or two $LM$-cubes containing its endpoints, and $|\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}|\leq2|I\setminus D(\Lambda)|$).

\begin{lemma}[locality of the species]\label{lem:c5-6-2}
Let $Z$ be a union of $LM$-cubes containing no cube of $\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}$, equivalently: every bond of $I$ with an endpoint in $Z$ lies in $D(\Lambda)$. Then for every selection and split selection with final domain $Z$, the selected integrals at the carrier $D(\Lambda)$ and at the carrier $I$ coincide at $t=0$: $k^{f,0}_\Lambda(Z)=k^f_\xi(Z)$, $\nu^{f,0}_{\Lambda,Z}=\nu^f_{\xi,Z}$, and likewise for the reference data ($k^{f,\circ}_\Lambda(Z)=k^{f,\circ}_\xi(Z)$, $\hat\nu^{f,\circ}_{\Lambda,Z}=\hat\nu^{f,\circ}_{\xi,Z}$). Consequently, for a group $W$ none of whose roots and cluster constituents contains a cube of $\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}$, $w^0_\Lambda(W)=w_\xi(W)$, $a^\Lambda_W=a_W$, and on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ the pairings $\mathfrak Q^0_\Lambda(\mathcal A(\mathcal S);f_\xi)=\mathfrak Q_\xi(\mathcal A(\mathcal S);f_\xi)$ for families $\mathcal S$ of such groups, so $A^0_\Lambda(\mathcal S)=A_\xi(\mathcal S)$ and $R^0_\Lambda(\mathcal S)=R_\xi(\mathcal S)$. The species that differ between the two carriers are those with a root or constituent containing a cube of $\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix a selection $\mathfrak s=(\mathcal D,\mathsf P,Z)$ (or a split selection) at the carrier $I$ with final domain $Z$; it is a selection at the carrier $D(\Lambda)$ and conversely: the hosts $Y\in\mathcal D$ lie in $Z$ ($Y\subseteq Z_0\subseteq Z_0'\subseteq Z$), their primitives have free-input sets in $Y$, whose young part lies in $E(Y)=\{b\in\text{carrier}\, : \,b\cap Y\neq\varnothing\}$, the same set for both carriers by hypothesis, so a primitive assigned to $Y$ is near relative to $I$ exactly when it is near relative to $D(\Lambda)$, and $\mathbf D_0(\Lambda)$-hosts inside $Z$ are the $\mathbf D_0$-hosts inside $Z$ (probe hosts $Y_b\subseteq Z$ have $b\in E(Y_b)$, the same set); the bad bonds $\mathsf P$ meet $Z$ (their incident cubes are in $Z_0\subseteq Z$, Definition~\ref{def:selection}), so $\mathsf P\subseteq D(\Lambda)\setminus E(Y_0)$ and $\mathsf P\subseteq I\setminus E(Y_0)$ are the same condition; the seed, $E_0$, $Z_0'$ are formed from $\mathcal D$, $\mathsf P$ alone. The integrand of \eqref{eq:c-selected-integral} at $t=0$: the potentials $\mathbf W_0(Y,B)=\sum_{\mathfrak p\, : \,Y_{\mathfrak p}=Y}\mathfrak p(B)$ (exterior input zero in both splits) and the cutoffs are the same functions of $B|_{E_0}$; $C_{E_0}=(\iota^*_{E_0}H\iota_{E_0})^{-1}$ involves the entries of $H$ between bonds of $E_0$, which are in both carriers; $\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)=\iota^*_{E_0}H\Pi_{\text{carrier}\setminus E_0}S_{Z_0}(\sigma)$ involves, for a row $e\in E_0$, the entries $H_{eb'}$ with $d(e_-,b'_-)\leq R$, so $b'\subset\operatorname{int}D\subset Z$ for the component $D$ of $e$ (proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:component-blocks}), and the walk series $S_{Z_0}(\sigma)_{b'b}$ with $\sigma_\Delta=0$ off $Z$, whose nonvanishing walks have $D(\omega)\subset Z$ (every cube meeting $D(\omega)$ is in $Z$, by the weight \eqref{eq:fixed-kernel-walk-weakening}); the blocks $(\mathsf A_H^r)_{b_0b_1}$, $(\mathsf P_H)_{b_jb_{j+1}}$ of a walk are entries of powers of $\id-H/s_H$ restricted to the carrier, and each depends only on the carrier's $H$-coefficients within distance $(n+1)R$ of its first bond, which lie in $D(\omega)\subset Z$ by the interior margin of $D_b$ (Section~\ref{sec:fixed-first-kernel}: $D_b$ contains the bonds and coefficient stencils of every block starting at $b$ with an interior margin); hence every bond entering $S_{Z_0}(\sigma)$ and $\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)$ is a bond of $I$ meeting $Z$, in $D(\Lambda)$ by hypothesis, and every coefficient is an entry of $H$ between two such bonds, the same for both carriers (principal restrictions of the one Hessian $H_\xi$), so the two carriers give the same $\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)$ and the same $C_{E_0}$; the $X$-integral is over $\mathfrak g^{\text{carrier}}$ with the standard Gaussian, but $\Gamma_{E_0}(\sigma)X$ involves $X$ only on bonds of $Z$ (the columns of $\Gamma$ outside $\bigsqcup J_D$ vanish, Lemma~\ref{lem:component-blocks}), so the remaining coordinates integrate to one in both cases. The Dirac tensors of a split selection are the same. Hence $\mathcal A^{f,0}_{\Lambda;\mathfrak s}[F]=\mathcal A^f_{\xi;\mathfrak s}[F]$ for every $F$, giving the activities and measures; the reference case is the same argument with $H^\circ$. The group statements follow: $w^0_\Lambda(W)$ is a product of activities of constituents, $a^\Lambda_W$ an integral of the reference source word (the same function, the reference links depending on the root variables only) against the same measures, and $\mathfrak Q$ involves $f_\xi(\mathsf e_{\mathcal A}B)$, which on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ depends on $B|_{I_C}$ with $I_C\subseteq D(\Lambda)$ and is the same function for both carriers.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[the reference gas at the retreated carrier]\label{lem:c5-6-3}
$S^\circ_\Lambda=S^\circ_{\xi,C}\cdot\exp(\eta_{\mathrm{lab}})$ with
\begin{equation*}
    |\eta_{\mathrm{lab}}|\leq\sum_{\Gamma\text{ containing a group }W\text{ with }F(W)\cap\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}\neq\varnothing}\bigl(|\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma)|+|\Phi^\circ_\xi(\Gamma)|\bigr)\leq\sum_{\Delta\in\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}}\sum_{\Gamma\text{ hits }\Delta}\bigl(|\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma)|+|\Phi^\circ_\xi(\Gamma)|\bigr)\leq2|I\setminus D(\Lambda)|\cdot\varepsilon^\circ_g\;,
\end{equation*}
where $\varepsilon^\circ_g:=\sup_\Delta\sum_{\Gamma\text{ hits }\Delta}(|\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma)|+|\Phi^\circ_\xi(\Gamma)|)\leq2C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t$: a cluster $\Gamma$ hits the cube $\Delta$ when one of its groups $W$ has $\Delta\in F(W)$; the rooted bound of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d), $\sum_{\Gamma\ni W}|\Phi^\circ(\Gamma)|\leq|a_W|e^{13\nu\sigma(W)/8}$, gives $\sum_{\Gamma\text{ hits }\Delta}|\Phi^\circ(\Gamma)|\leq\sum_{W\, : \,\Delta\in F(W)}|a_W|e^{13\nu\sigma(W)/8}\leq\sum_{W\, : \,\Delta\in F(W)}e^{4\nu\sigma(W)}M_\Lambda(W)\leq C_{\mathrm{pin}}\rho_t$ by $|a_W|\leq M_\Lambda(W)$ and the first display of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(a), at the carrier $D(\Lambda)$ and, by the closing remarks of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d) and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}(a), at the carrier $I$. Moreover $|S^\circ_\Lambda|\leq1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$\log S^\circ_\Lambda-\log S^\circ_{\xi,C}=\sum_\Gamma[\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma)-\Phi^\circ_\xi(\Gamma)]$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(d) at both carriers; the reference group gas at $I$ is that of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}, with the same Kotecký--Preiss expansion); a cluster all of whose groups have roots and constituents containing no cube of $\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}$ has $\Phi^\circ_\Lambda(\Gamma)=\Phi^\circ_\xi(\Gamma)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-2} (the Ursell coefficient depends on the incompatibility graph only, the group activities coincide), and the groups present for one carrier and not the other contain a cube of $\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}$ as well (a root or constituent met by a de-younged bond). $|S^\circ_\Lambda|\leq1$: $S^\circ_\Lambda=\int f^\circ K^\circ_\Lambda\,\dd a/I^\circ_\Lambda$ with $K^\circ_\Lambda>0$ on the box (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c) for the reference data) and $|f^\circ|\leq1$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{The accounting}
Three quantities acquire the index $\mathrm{lab}$: the history weight ($\mathsf w_\xi=\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi$, \eqref{eq:c5-6-1}), the exterior-response/uncovered term $E_{\mathrm{bdry}}$, and the defect term $E_{\mathrm{def}}$. Define
\begin{equation*}
    E^{\mathrm{lab}}_{\mathrm{bdry}}:=\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O\Bigl[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\int_{\mathfrak g^{I\setminus D(\Lambda)}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}I^\Lambda_1\bigl(\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}+F^\Lambda(1)\mathbf 1_{(A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}})^c}\bigr)\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}\Bigr](V)\,\dd V\;,
\end{equation*}
the labelled version of the $E_{\mathrm{bdry}}$ of Appendix~\ref{app:exterior-chessboard} (the exterior response on covered terms, the whole conditional expectation on uncovered terms, $|F^\Lambda(1)|\leq1$; the integrands are read with the convention of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-1}, as $\int_{\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}}\rho_\xi(\dots)\,\dd a$, so they are defined and vanish where the fibre is empty), and $E^{\mathrm{lab}}_{\mathrm{def}}$ the same with $\sum_{\mathfrak B}S^{\circ[\mathfrak B]}_\Lambda R^0_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}$ in place of the bracket; the leading term is $\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\int\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{c^\Lambda}I^\Lambda_1S^\circ_\Lambda\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}\cdot W_\chi(C;U_1)]\,\dd V$. By \eqref{eq:c5-6-1}--\eqref{eq:c5-6-2} these three sum to the labelled Wilson numerator, exactly.

\begin{proposition}[the boundary accounting with labels]\label{prop:c5-6-4}
On compatible even fine periods exceeding $200\ell$, under the hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5-9}, for $g_0$ below the thresholds of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-4}, of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-2-6} ($g_*(\vartheta)$) and of \eqref{eq:c5-2-3}, with $q$, $q_\vartheta$, $N_{\mathrm{ret}}$, $C_L$ of Sections~\ref{sec:c5-2}--\ref{sec:c5-3} and $\bar\varepsilon_t$, $C_1$, $C_2$, $\mu'$ of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5} (the labelled integrands $S^\circ_\Lambda$, $F^\Lambda(t)$, $\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]$ are Borel in the branch data: the labels take finitely many values, and on each labelled term these are ratios and differences of the Borel integrals of the measurability paragraph of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction}):
\begin{gather}
    \mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O\Bigl[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\int_{\mathfrak g^{I\setminus D(\Lambda)}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}I^\Lambda_1\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}\frac{|\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]|}{|S^\circ_\Lambda|}\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}\Bigr]\dd V\leq e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}\bigl(C_1g_0r+C_2\bar\varepsilon_tr^{d_G}\bigr)\bigl(1+e^{\mu_H(2L+1)/2}\bigr)N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu')\,n_C(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)\;,\label{eq:c5-6-3}\\
    \mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O\Bigl[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi\mathbf 1_{(A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}})^c}\Bigr]\dd V\leq C_Ln_C(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)\quad\text{(\eqref{eq:c5-3-coverage-average})},\notag
\end{gather}
hence, with $|S^\circ_\Lambda|\leq1$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-3}),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-6-4}
    |E^{\mathrm{lab}}_{\mathrm{bdry}}|\leq n_C(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)\Bigl[e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}\bigl(C_1g_0r+C_2\bar\varepsilon_tr^{d_G}\bigr)\bigl(1+e^{\mu_H(2L+1)/2}\bigr)N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu')+C_L\Bigr]\;,
\end{equation}
and for $g_0$ below a further threshold depending on $L$, $M$, $M_2$, the group, the source constants, $\vartheta$ and the character, $r^{d_G}N_{\mathrm{ret}}(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)\leq e^{-c_q\vartheta^2\pi_*^2/2}$, so that $|E^{\mathrm{lab}}_{\mathrm{bdry}}|\leq Cn_Ce^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}e^{-c_q\vartheta^2\pi_*^2/2}$: the form $|E_{\mathrm{bdry}}|\leq Cn_C(1+g_0r)e^{-c_*\pi_*^2}$ of Appendix~\ref{app:exterior-chessboard} with the exponent $c_q\vartheta^2/2$ in place of $c_*$ and the family-sum factor $e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}$ of \eqref{eq:c5-5-9} (it is not a history weight; Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5R-4} replaces it by $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}$).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\eqref{eq:c5-6-3}: on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$, \eqref{eq:c5-5-9} gives $|\mathcal B^\Lambda|/|S^\circ_\Lambda|\leq e^{2n_C\bar\varepsilon_t}[C_1g_0r\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)+C_2\bar\varepsilon_tr^{d_G}\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)]$ with $\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)\leq e^{\mu_H(2L+1)/2}\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-5-10}), and $\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)=\sum_{e\in J(\Lambda)}e^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}\leq\sum_ee^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}\mathbf 1\{e\in E(B^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi)\}$, $B^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi:=(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})^c$, because a bond not in $D(\Lambda)$ has no endpoint in $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$ (Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a)). The prefactor is a constant (independent of the history, the labels and the fields: \eqref{eq:c5-5-9} is uniform in the exterior data within the term's support), and $\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)$, $S^\circ_\Lambda$ and $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ depend on the labels through $\Lambda$ only, so the integral over $a_{\mathrm{old}}|_{I\setminus D(\Lambda)}$ of $\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}I^\Lambda_1\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}|\mathcal B^\Lambda|/|S^\circ_\Lambda|$ is at most (prefactor)$\cdot\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)\cdot\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}$. By \eqref{eq:c5-3-mark-inequality}, $\mathbf 1\{e\in E(B^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi)\}\leq\mathbf 1\{e\in E(\mathfrak B_0)\}+\sum_{e'\, : \,x_{e'}\text{ within }102\ell+1\text{ of an endpoint of }e}M_{e'}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(c)--(d)); multiply by $\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi\geq0$, sum over the labels and histories, apply $\mathcal O$, divide by $\mathcal Z_0$ (Tonelli, finitely many labels per history): the first term gives $p_e^{(0)}\leq q$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}(b)), the second $\sum_{e'}p^\vartheta_{e'}\leq N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(d), Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-2-6}). Then $\sum_ee^{-\mu'\operatorname{dist}(e,C)}\leq n_CN_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu')$ (at most $4n_C(2s+1)^4$ bonds at $\ell^\infty$ distance $\leq s$ from the contour's initial sites, as in Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-5-10}). The second display is \eqref{eq:c5-3-coverage-average}. \eqref{eq:c5-6-4} adds the two with $|\mathcal B^\Lambda|\leq|S^\circ_\Lambda|\cdot(\dots)$ and $|F^\Lambda(1)|\leq1$. The comparison: $\log(r^{d_G}N_{\mathrm{ret}})\leq d_G(\log A_1+p_0\log\log g_0^{-2})+C+4\mathfrak r\log(2\log g_0^{-2})$ by \eqref{eq:c5-3-1} and $r=A_1(\log g_0^{-2})^{p_0}$, while $\log(1/q_\vartheta)\geq c_q\vartheta^2A_0^22^{-2p_0}(\log g_0^{-2})^{2p_0}$ by \eqref{eq:c5-3-2}, and $q\leq q_\vartheta$ (proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-4}: $\theta_\vartheta\leq\theta$ for $\vartheta\leq1$, and $z_P$, $z_G$ are decreasing in $\theta$ at fixed $n_W$; alternatively, \eqref{eq:c5-2-3} at $\vartheta=1$ gives $q\leq e^{-c_q\pi_*^2}\leq e^{-c_q\vartheta^2\pi_*^2}$ for $g_0<g_*(1)$), so $q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta\leq(1+N_{\mathrm{ret}})q_\vartheta$; every logarithm is dominated for small $g_0$, leaving $e^{-c_q\vartheta^2\pi_*^2/2}$. The polynomial prefactor $r^{d_G}$ is thus paid by the same log comparison ($\log r=O(\log\log g_0^{-2})$); it is a prefactor, not the cost in the exponent growing with $r$ that the comparison of Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4} set aside.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[scope of this subsection]\label{rem:c5-6-5}
The locality of the species at $t=0$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-2}) means that every activity, probe measure, group weight and reference activity of a labelled term at $t=0$, and on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ every defect $R^0_\Lambda(\mathfrak B)$, coincides with that of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} unless its domains meet the de-younged bonds, so the extra resummation index touches only the species whose domains contain a cube of $\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}$ (such domains may extend far from $I\setminus D(\Lambda)$; what is local is the marking, not the domain); the $t>0$ species, which enter $\mathcal B^\Lambda$ only, depend on $m$ and the exterior data and are bounded, not identified, by Section~\ref{sec:c5-5}. Every term of \eqref{eq:c5-6-3} is a one-point witness probability times a lattice sum, uniform in the volume. Not contained here: (1) Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-3} gives $S^\circ_\Lambda=S^\circ_{\xi,C}e^{\eta_{\mathrm{lab}}}$ with $\eta_{\mathrm{lab}}$ marked by the de-younged bonds, but $|I\setminus D(\Lambda)|$ counts the round-0 bonds $D\setminus D^{(0)}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}; zero when $\Lambda_1$ is filled in the side-$100\ell$ sense) and up to $N_{\mathrm{ret}}$ bonds per failed collar test, numbers controlled in probability only ($p_z^{(0)}\leq q$, $p_e^\vartheta\leq q_\vartheta$ per bond), so $e^{\eta_{\mathrm{lab}}}-1$ is not small on every history, and averaging it over the labels would need exponential moments of $|I\setminus D(\Lambda)|$ under the history measure, which Sections~\ref{sec:c5-2}--\ref{sec:c5-3} do not supply (they give one-point probabilities only); the leading coefficient is therefore identified at $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$ directly (Section~\ref{sec:c5-7}) rather than compared to the carrier $I$. (2) $E^{\mathrm{lab}}_{\mathrm{def}}$: the per-branch defect bound of \draftref{stage 4} at the carrier $D(\Lambda)$, i.e. that draft read with $I\to D(\Lambda)$, $S^\circ\to S^\circ_\Lambda$, $R_\xi\to R^0_\Lambda$, whose inputs hold at $D(\Lambda)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}, Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-4-3}, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b) and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-8}, and whose species are those of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} away from $\mathcal N_{\mathrm{lab}}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-2}); the reading itself is not carried out here. (3) The marked-polymer induction of Section~\ref{sec:marginal-perimeter} (the pinned recursion \eqref{eq:marginal-pinned-recursion}) is unchanged in form; its species are the groups at the carrier of the term, and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-2} is what makes the induction's inputs label-independent away from the de-younged bonds. (4) Everything in this subsection inherits the status of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5} and of its inputs listed in Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Identification of the labelled leading coefficient}
\label{sec:c5-7}

The labelled leading coefficient $S^\circ_\Lambda$ of \eqref{eq:c5-6-2} is identified with $\kappa_Ce^{E^\circ_\Lambda}$, $\kappa_C:=e^{-\delta mP(C)}z_c^4$ (the $e^{-\delta m^\sigma P(C_n)}(z^\sigma_c(g_0,\pi/2))^4$ of \eqref{eq:extracted-loop-scalar} in the cutoff prescription $\sigma$ fixed throughout), on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$, with a bounded $E^\circ_\Lambda$, and the bounds \eqref{eq:c5-6-3}, \eqref{eq:c5-6-4} (in the form of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5R-4}) and the leading term of \eqref{eq:c5-6-1}--\eqref{eq:c5-6-2} are restated relative to $\kappa_C$. The objects: $S^\circ_\Lambda=\sum_{\mathfrak B}\prod_{W\in\mathfrak B}a^\Lambda_W=\int f^\circ K^\circ_\Lambda\,\dd a/I^\circ_\Lambda$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b), Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-3}), the reference data at $D(\Lambda)$ being $H^\circ=(\mathsf H^{\Omega_1}_{\id})_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}$, exterior variable zero on $J(\Lambda)$, $m^\circ=\ell^\circ=0$, $f^\circ(a)=W_\chi(C;e^{iq_{\id}(a)})$, so that, by the definition of $K^\Lambda_{\xi;t}$ (Section~\ref{sec:c5-4}) at $t=0$ and these data,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-7-1}
    K^\circ_\Lambda(a)=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}(a)\exp\Bigl[-\tfrac12\langle a,H^\circ a\rangle+\sum_{\mathfrak p\text{ near rel. }D(\Lambda)}\mathfrak p(a)\Bigr]\;,\quad a\in\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}\text{ (exterior variable zero)},\qquad I^\circ_\Lambda=\int K^\circ_\Lambda\,\dd a\;,\qquad S^\circ_\Lambda=\frac{\int f^\circ K^\circ_\Lambda\,\dd a}{I^\circ_\Lambda}\;,
\end{equation}
the near primitives being those of all three kinds (action, Haar, constraint Jacobian) whose free-input set meets $D(\Lambda)$. Inputs, used at their stated status: (I1) the regional square scalar \eqref{eq:regional-square-scalar}, $S^\circ_{D,C_n,T}=\int\Theta^\sigma_D(B)e^{P_{D,T}(B)}\chi(\operatorname{Hol}_{U_{g_0,T}(i_{D,T}B)}C_n)\,\dd\mu_{C_{D,T}}(B)/\int\Theta^\sigma_D(B)e^{P_{D,T}(B)}\,\dd\mu_{C_{D,T}}(B)$, $C_{D,T}=(i^*_{D,T}H_Ti_{D,T})^{-1}$, defined for every set $D$ of free bonds such that every free-input stencil of the contour lies in $D$ (covered), with the original primitive labels retained including those crossing the boundary of $D$; (I2) Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-square-extraction}: under coverage, $K_\mu\geq3(n+2)$, the collar conditions \eqref{eq:regional-collar-sizes} ($M\geq8LM_2$, $LMR_1>4R_0+4L^2M_2R_1+8$, $R_0=4(1+R_*+R_H+L)$, enforceable by choosing $M$ with $LM-4L^2M_2>4R_0+8$ before the coupling) and the common small-coupling thresholds of the flat and regional expansions, $S^\circ_{D,C_n,T}=\kappa_Ce^{E^\circ_{D,C_n,T}}$ with $|E^\circ_{D,C_n,T}|\leq C\tau^2[\sum_{j=0}^{4n-1}e^{-cd_j}+4ne^{-c\iota_T}+(n+1)e^{-cn}]$ (\eqref{eq:regional-square-error}), $d_j=\max(0,d_T(K_j,D^c)-R_0)$, $\iota_T=\max(0,N_{\min}/4-R_0)$, $K_j$ the union of the free-input stencils of the fine traversals of coarse segment $j$, complements in the torus free carrier, $d_T$ the periodic fine-bond metric of Section~\ref{sec:regional-identification}, $C$, $c$ independent of $D$, $n$ and the periods; (I3) the identification paragraph of Section~\ref{sec:regional-identification} preceding \eqref{eq:regional-collar-sizes} and \eqref{eq:regional-kernel-identification}: the comparison holds for every $D_-\subseteq D\subseteq D_+$ ($D_\pm$ the free bonds with both/at least one endpoint in $\Lambda_1$), assigning the complementary free variables to the exterior integral, with its facts at identity data: every stencil meeting $D\subseteq D_+$ stays in the regular region and the Hessian on $D$ is $H_{D,T}=i^*_{D,T}H_Ti_{D,T}$ including the diagonal terms of crossing plaquettes; the constraint linearisation gives the flat phase \eqref{eq:flat-phase} so that the reconstructed links at identity data are $e^{iq_{\id}}$; at zero exterior free field the conditional cutoff is $\Theta^\sigma_D$ with $r=\delta_0/g_0$; the primitives of the conditional reference scalar are those of \eqref{eq:regional-periodic-weight} with the terms whose free support misses $D$ vanishing at $i_{D,T}B$; (I4) Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-3}(iv) ($\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D^\Lambda}=\Theta_{D(\Lambda)}$ on the term's support), Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b) (the reference data at $D(\Lambda)$), $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-1} (every contour bond in $\Omega_1$ with its free-input set $\mathcal F_r\subseteq D(\Lambda)$), Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a) (a bond not in $D(\Lambda)$ has no endpoint in $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$, so lies in $E(B^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi)$, $B^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi:=(\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}})^c$), Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-6-4} and Section~\ref{sec:c5-5R}. Thresholds, in addition to items 1--5 of Appendix~\ref{app:thresholds} (with the collar item 4), \eqref{eq:c5-4-T}, \eqref{eq:c5-4-Tp}, \eqref{eq:c5-5-T}, \eqref{eq:c5-5R-T} and the period hypothesis of Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-1}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-7-T}
    \begin{aligned}
        &\text{(i) the collar conditions \eqref{eq:regional-collar-sizes}: $M\geq8LM_2$ (already in the order of choice of Appendix~\ref{app:thresholds}) and $LMR_1>4R_0+4L^2M_2R_1+8$ with $R_0=4(1+R_*+R_H+L)$}\\
        &\quad\text{(a condition on $M$ before the coupling, of the kind of threshold item 1, which has $R_{\mathrm c}$ in place of $R_0$; both are imposed), with aligned nested partitions and all periods}\\
        &\quad\text{divisible by $LMR_1$ (\eqref{eq:c-standing-conditions}) and the contour a coarse translate of the counterclockwise $(e_1,e_2)$ square of side $nL$ (Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0});}\\
        &\text{(ii) the common small-coupling thresholds of the flat and regional expansions ($g_0C_{\mathsf C}(2+4r)<\vartheta_*/2$, $g_0(2+4r)<a_L$, and the polymer-load inequalities}\\
        &\quad\text{of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-square-extraction}), conditions on $g_0$ alone;}\\
        &\text{(iii) $K_\mu\geq3(n+2)$ for every coarse period (Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0}), and, for the $n$-uniform form of Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-7-2}(c) only, $4ne^{-c\iota_T}\leq1$ (a condition on the torus: $N_{\min}\geq4R_0+(4/c)\log(4n)$).}
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}

\subsubsection*{The identification at the retreated carrier}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:c5-7-1}
Under \eqref{eq:c5-7-T}, on every labelled term with final region $\Lambda$, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs, on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$: $S^\circ_\Lambda=S^\circ_{D(\Lambda),C,T}$, the regional square scalar (I1) at $D=D(\Lambda)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Both sides are ratios of two integrals over $\mathfrak g^{D(\Lambda)}$ with the same denominator structure; it suffices to identify the four factors of the integrands, numerator and denominator alike, the Gaussian normaliser of $\mu_{C_{D(\Lambda),T}}$ being common to numerator and denominator on the side of (I1) and absent on both on the side of \eqref{eq:c5-7-1}. First, $D(\Lambda)$ qualifies: $D(\Lambda)\subseteq I=D_+(\Lambda_1)$ (the carrier of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} consists of all free bonds with an endpoint in $\Lambda_1$, the bond convention of Section~\ref{sec:c5-0}; the retreat only removes bonds), so every stencil meeting $D(\Lambda)$ stays in the regular region and every bond of $D(\Lambda)$ has an endpoint in a good cube of $\Lambda_1$, which is all the facts (I3) use (the lower inclusion $D_-\subseteq D$ of the comparison statement is not used in the proofs of these facts, which are stated for $D\subseteq D_+$ with the collar); the de-younged bonds $I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ are complementary free variables assigned to the exterior integral, which is what the zero exterior variable on $J(\Lambda)$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b) does; and $D(\Lambda)$ is covered on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$, since every contour bond lies in $\Omega_1$ with $\mathcal F_r\subseteq D(\Lambda)$, and $\mathcal F_r$ contains the coverage stencil $\Lambda_r$ of Section~\ref{sec:remaining-physical-comparison} ($\Lambda_b=\{b\}$ for a free bond, $\Lambda_{b_0(c)}=\mathcal S_c\cap E_*$ for a designated bond; $\mathcal F_r\subseteq\mathcal E_c\cap E_*$ for the row $c$ of $r$, Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0}; what (I2) needs is $\Lambda_r\subseteq D(\Lambda)$, so the inclusion used is $\Lambda_r\subseteq\mathcal F_r$, Remark~\ref{rem:c5-7-5}(2)). (F1) Precision: $H^\circ=(\mathsf H^{\Omega_1}_{\id})_{D(\Lambda)D(\Lambda)}$ is the Hessian at identity of the regular-region action (CRE's constrained quadratic form, $\mathsf H^{\Omega_1}_{U_1}=\mathsf C^*\Delta_1\mathsf C$, Lemma~\ref{lem:primitive-sum}) restricted to $D(\Lambda)\times D(\Lambda)$; Lemma~\ref{lem:c2-reference} states that the entries of $H^\circ$ with a label in $I$ are $f_{bb'}(0)$, the entries of the flat Hessian of \eqref{eq:flat-covariance} on the branch's torus, so the principal restriction to $D(\Lambda)\subseteq I$ is the flat torus precision on $D(\Lambda)$ including the diagonal contributions of the plaquettes crossing the boundary of $D(\Lambda)$, the same statement as (I3) fact 1 at $D=D(\Lambda)$, which holds because every stencil meeting $D(\Lambda)$ lies in the regular region, where the action at identity is the flat quadratic form; the flat Hessian of Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} and $H_T$ are the same operator \eqref{eq:flat-covariance} on the same torus. So $\exp(-\frac12\langle a,H^\circ a\rangle)\,\dd a$ and $\dd\mu_{C_{D(\Lambda),T}}(B)$ agree up to the common normaliser, with $a=B$. (F2) Cutoff: at the reference data the cutoff of $K^\circ_\Lambda$ is the product of the bond tests $\prod_{b\in D(\Lambda)}\mathbf 1\{|a_b|_\sigma<r\}$ by the reference-data convention (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}(b), the same cutoffs, the reference data not being a branch; Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c) for these data, $K^\circ_\Lambda>0$ on the open box, proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-3}; the $K^\circ_{\xi;0}=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D_I}\exp(\dots)$ of Appendix~\ref{app:background-estimates} on the open box at the carrier $I$); on a branch the corresponding statement is Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-3}(iv), which is not what is used here. $\Theta^\sigma_D$ is the same product of strict bond tests at the same threshold $r=\delta_0/g_0$ in the same norm $|\cdot|_\sigma$ (I3). (F3) Primitives: $\sum_{\mathfrak p\text{ near rel. }D(\Lambda)}\mathfrak p(a)$ at zero exterior variable is the sum of the local primitives of Section~\ref{sec:source-phase-primitives} at identity background whose free-input set meets $D(\Lambda)$, evaluated at $a=i_{D(\Lambda),T}B$; $P_{D(\Lambda),T}(i_{D(\Lambda),T}B)$ is the sum of the primitives of \eqref{eq:regional-periodic-weight} with labels meeting $D(\Lambda)$ (crossing labels retained), the others vanishing at $i_{D(\Lambda),T}B$. The two sums agree term by term once the primitive functions agree, which is the definition \eqref{eq:c-potential-definition} (the local terms of the action, Haar and constraint Jacobian at identity background, the same expansion as that of Section~\ref{sec:marginal-perimeter}; the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-square-extraction} asserts this agreement for its own conditional kernel); the far primitives contribute their value at zero exterior variable, a constant common to numerator and denominator of \eqref{eq:c5-7-1}, so whether they are counted is immaterial. (F4) Wilson factor: $f^\circ(a)=W_\chi(C;e^{iq_{\id}(a)})$ is $\chi$ of the holonomy along $C$ of the links $e^{iq_{\id}(a)_r}$; by (I3) fact 2 the reconstructed links at identity data are $U_{g_0,T}(i_{D(\Lambda),T}B)=e^{iq_{\id}}$ evaluated at the same free field, and on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ the link of each contour bond $r$ depends on the free field through $\mathcal F_r\subseteq D(\Lambda)$ only, so the exterior variable (zero) does not enter and $f^\circ(a)=\chi(\operatorname{Hol}_{U_{g_0,T}(i_{D(\Lambda),T}B)}C)$. There is no fifth factor: at $t=0$ and $m^\circ=\ell^\circ=0$ the kernel $K^\Lambda_{\xi;0}$ has no linear term and no shift, and facts 3 and 5 of (I3) (boundary change, cross term) concern the identification of the conditional CRE scalar with $S^\circ_{D,C,T}$, which is the statement of Section~\ref{sec:regional-identification} itself and is not needed to compare the two explicit integrals \eqref{eq:c5-7-1} and (I1). Numerator and denominator agree factor by factor, so the ratios agree.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:c5-7-2}
Under \eqref{eq:c5-7-T}, on every labelled term, on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$, with $E^\circ_\Lambda:=E^\circ_{D(\Lambda),C,T}$ (the error of (I2) at $D=D(\Lambda)$) and $d_j(\Lambda):=\max(0,d_T(K_j,D(\Lambda)^c)-R_0)$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
    \item
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-7-2}
        S^\circ_\Lambda=\kappa_Ce^{E^\circ_\Lambda}\;,\qquad|E^\circ_\Lambda|\leq C\tau^2\Bigl[\sum_{j=0}^{4n-1}e^{-cd_j(\Lambda)}+4ne^{-c\iota_T}+(n+1)e^{-cn}\Bigr]\leq C\tau^2(9n+1)\;,
    \end{equation}
    $E^\circ_\Lambda$ depends on the labels through $D(\Lambda)$ only (a simple function on the finitely many labels of a history), $\kappa_C\neq0$ (finite perimeter exponent, $z_c$ the exponential of a convergent sum, Section~\ref{sec:remaining-physical-comparison}; also $S^\circ_\Lambda\neq0$ by (I2)), and $|\kappa_C|e^{-C\tau^2(9n+1)}\leq|S^\circ_\Lambda|\leq|\kappa_C|e^{C\tau^2(9n+1)}$ (so $S^\circ_\Lambda\neq0$ on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$, consistent with $|S^\circ_\Lambda|\leq1$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-6-3});
    \item the leading term of \eqref{eq:c5-6-1}--\eqref{eq:c5-6-2} is $\kappa_C\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{E^\circ_\Lambda}\int_{\mathfrak g^{I\setminus D(\Lambda)}}\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}I^\Lambda_1\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}W_\chi(C;U_1)\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}$ ($e^{E^\circ_\Lambda}$ and $\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}$ depend on the labels only and leave the $a_{\mathrm{old}}$-integral), and on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$, by \eqref{eq:c5-5-9R},
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-7-3}
        |\kappa_C|^{-1}|\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]|\leq e^{|E^\circ_\Lambda|}e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}\bigl[C_1g_0r\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)+C_2\bar\varepsilon_tr^{d_G}\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)\bigr]\leq e^{C\tau^2(9n+1)}e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}\bigl[C_1g_0r\mathfrak S_1(\Lambda)+C_2\bar\varepsilon_tr^{d_G}\mathfrak S_2(\Lambda)\bigr]\;;
    \end{equation}
    \item (refined split) $|E^\circ_\Lambda|\leq C\tau^2(2+1/c)+C\tau^2\sum_je^{-cd_j(\Lambda)}$ under $4ne^{-c\iota_T}\leq1$ (\eqref{eq:c5-7-T}(iii); for $n\geq1$ this is $N_{\min}\geq4R_0+(4/c)\log(4n)$), since $(n+1)e^{-cn}\leq1+\sup_{x\geq0}xe^{-cx}=1+1/(ec)\leq1+1/c$: the only part of the error that grows with $n$ at fixed $\tau$ is the boundary sum $\sum_je^{-cd_j(\Lambda)}\leq4n$, each of whose terms is at most 1 and exponentially small in $d_T(K_j,D(\Lambda)^c)-R_0$ (the sum is zero only when $D(\Lambda)^c=\varnothing$, by the convention $e^{-\infty}=0$); it is the quantity averaged in Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-7-3}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
(a) Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-7-1} and (I2) at $D=D(\Lambda)$, whose hypotheses are \eqref{eq:c5-7-T} and coverage ($A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$); the crude bound uses $e^{-cd_j}\leq1$, $e^{-c\iota_T}\leq1$ and $(n+1)e^{-cn}\leq n+1$ (as in the constant $B_C$ following \eqref{eq:retained-source-correction}); $D(\Lambda)$ is a function of the labels. (b) The leading term of \eqref{eq:c5-6-2} is $S^\circ_\Lambda W_\chi(C;U_1)$ integrated as in \eqref{eq:c5-6-1}; substitute (a). The bound: \eqref{eq:c5-5-9R} is $|\mathcal B^\Lambda|\leq|S^\circ_\Lambda|e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}[\dots]$ and $|S^\circ_\Lambda|=|\kappa_C|e^{\operatorname{Re}E^\circ_\Lambda}\leq|\kappa_C|e^{|E^\circ_\Lambda|}$. (c) The stated elementary inequality.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection*{The label average of the boundary distances}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:c5-7-3}
In the setting of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-6-4} (square geometry of Section~\ref{sec:c5-5-0}, compatible even fine periods exceeding $200\ell$, the hypotheses of Sections~\ref{sec:c5-2}--\ref{sec:c5-3}), for every $c>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:c5-7-4}
    \mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O\Bigl[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}\sum_{j=0}^{4n-1}e^{-cd_j(\Lambda)}\Bigr]\dd V\leq e^{c(R_0+\varrho_K)}N_{\mathrm{lat}}(c)\,n_C(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)\;,\qquad\varrho_K:=\max_j\max_{k\in K_j}\min_{r\in C_j}d_T(k,r)\leq2L+1\;,
\end{equation}
with $N_{\mathrm{lat}}(c)=\sum_{s\geq0}4(2s+1)^4e^{-cs}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-1-2}, $C_j$ the contour bonds of coarse segment $j$, $q$ and $q_\vartheta$ of \eqref{eq:c5-2-3} and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-2-4}, $N_{\mathrm{ret}}$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}: uniform in the volume, and of the same form as \eqref{eq:c5-6-3}, a lattice sum times one-point witness probabilities.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Pointwise on a term: $d_j(\Lambda)\geq d_T(K_j,D(\Lambda)^c)-R_0$, so $e^{-cd_j(\Lambda)}\leq e^{cR_0}e^{-cd_T(K_j,D(\Lambda)^c)}\leq e^{cR_0}\sum_{b\notin D(\Lambda)}e^{-cd_T(K_j,b)}$ (the maximum over $b\in D(\Lambda)^c$ of $e^{-cd_T(K_j,b)}$ is at most the sum; if $D(\Lambda)^c=\varnothing$ the left side is zero by the convention $e^{-\infty}=0$). For $k\in K_j$ and $b$, the triangle inequality for $d_T$ gives $d_T(k,b)\geq\min_{r\in C_j}d_T(r,b)-\varrho_K$, hence $e^{-cd_T(K_j,b)}\leq e^{c\varrho_K}\max_{r\in C_j}e^{-cd_T(r,b)}\leq e^{c\varrho_K}\sum_{r\in C_j}e^{-cd_T(r,b)}$, and $\sum_j\sum_{r\in C_j}=\sum_{r\in C}$ because the segments partition the contour bonds. A bond $b\notin D(\Lambda)$ has no endpoint in $\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$ (for $b\in I\setminus D(\Lambda)$ by Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a); for a free bond $b\notin I$ because $I=D_+(\Lambda_1)$ is the set of all free bonds with an endpoint in $\Lambda_1\supseteq\Lambda_1^{\mathrm{fin}}$, the bond convention of Section~\ref{sec:c5-0}; the equality, not only $D(\Lambda)\subseteq I$, is used here), so $\mathbf 1\{b\notin D(\Lambda)\}\leq\mathbf 1\{b\in E(B^{\mathrm{fin}}_\xi)\}\leq\mathbf 1\{b\in E(\mathfrak B_0)\}+\sum_{e'\, : \,x_{e'}\text{ within }102\ell+1\text{ of an endpoint of }b}M_{e'}$ (\eqref{eq:c5-3-mark-inequality}, Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(c)--(d)). Altogether $\sum_je^{-cd_j(\Lambda)}\leq e^{c(R_0+\varrho_K)}\sum_{r\in C}\sum_be^{-cd_T(r,b)}[\mathbf 1\{b\in E(\mathfrak B_0)\}+\sum_{e'\text{ near }b}M_{e'}]$, every term nonnegative and label-independent except through the marks. Multiply by $\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}\leq\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi$, sum over the labels and histories, apply $\mathcal O$ and divide by $\mathcal Z_0$ (Tonelli; finitely many labels per history; $\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi=\mathsf w_\xi$, Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(c)): for each fixed $b$ the first term gives $p^{(0)}_b\leq q$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-0}(b)) and the second $\sum_{e'\text{ near }b}p^\vartheta_{e'}\leq N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-3-1}(d), Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-2-6}), exactly as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-6-4}. Finally $\sum_be^{-cd_T(r,b)}\leq\sum_{s\geq0}4(2s+1)^4e^{-cs}=N_{\mathrm{lat}}(c)$ for each contour bond $r$ ($d_T(r,b)\geq\|x_r-x_b\|_\infty$, and at most $4(2s+1)^4$ bonds have initial site within $\ell^\infty$ distance $s$ of $x_r$), and $\sum_{r\in C}1=n_C$. The bound on $\varrho_K$: for a free contour bond $r$, $\Lambda_r=\{r\}$ and $d_T(r,r)=0$; for a designated $r=b_0(c)$, the stencil bonds and $r$ itself have both endpoints in the two endpoint blocks of $c$ ($\Lambda_{b_0(c)}=\mathcal S_c\cap E_*$ with $\mathcal S_c$ the bonds with both endpoints in the two endpoint blocks of $c$, Section~\ref{sec:remaining-physical-comparison}; $r=b_0(c)$ is a bond of the row $c$), two adjacent blocks of side $L$, any two sites of which are within $\ell^\infty$ distance $2L-1$ of each other, so $d_T(k,r)\leq2L-1+1=2L\leq2L+1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[bounds relative to $\kappa_C$]\label{cor:c5-7-4}
Under \eqref{eq:c5-7-T} and the hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5R-4} and Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-7-3}:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
    \item (crude form) $\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\int\mathbf 1_{\mathrm{lab}}e^{c^\Lambda_\xi}I^\Lambda_1\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}|\kappa_C|^{-1}|\mathcal B^\Lambda_\xi[f_\xi]|\,\dd a_{\mathrm{old}}]\,\dd V\leq e^{C\tau^2(9n+1)}\times$(the right side of \eqref{eq:c5-6-3} with $e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}$) $=e^{C\tau^2(9n+1)}e^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}(C_1g_0r+C_2\bar\varepsilon_tr^{d_G})(1+e^{\mu_H(2L+1)/2})N_{\mathrm{lat}}(\mu')n_C(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)$, by \eqref{eq:c5-7-3} and the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-6-4} with $|\mathcal B^\Lambda|/|S^\circ_\Lambda|$ replaced by $|\kappa_C|^{-1}|\mathcal B^\Lambda|\leq e^{C\tau^2(9n+1)}|\mathcal B^\Lambda|/|S^\circ_\Lambda|$; by the comparison of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-6-4} ($r^{d_G}N_{\mathrm{ret}}(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)\leq e^{-c_q\vartheta^2\pi_*^2/2}$ for $g_0$ below a further threshold; $N_{\mathrm{ret}}$ and $r^{d_G}$ themselves diverge as $g_0\to0$) this is at most $Cn_Ce^{n_C\bar\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ph}}}e^{C\tau^2(9n+1)}e^{-c_q\vartheta^2\pi_*^2/2}$, and $e^{C\tau^2(9n+1)}$ is the factor carried in $B_C=|\kappa_C|\exp(C\tau^2(9n+1))$ following \eqref{eq:retained-source-correction}.
    \item (first moment of the leading error) $\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}|E^\circ_\Lambda|]\,\dd V\leq C\tau^2(2+1/c)+C\tau^2e^{c(R_0+\varrho_K)}N_{\mathrm{lat}}(c)n_C(q+N_{\mathrm{ret}}q_\vartheta)$ under \eqref{eq:c5-7-T}(iii), by Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-7-2}(c), Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-7-3} and the normalisation $\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O[\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi\mathbf 1_{A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}}]\,\dd V\leq\mathcal Z_0^{-1}\int\mathcal O[\mathsf w_\xi]\,\dd V=1$ ($\sum_{\mathrm{lab}}\mathsf w^{\mathrm{lab}}_\xi=\mathsf w_\xi$, Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(c); $\mathcal Z_0=\int\mathcal O[\mathsf w_\xi]\,\dd V$, Section~\ref{sec:c5-6}): the labelled leading error has, in label average, a part $O(\tau^2)$ uniform in $n$ and a part linear in $n_C$ paid by the one-point witness probabilities (small for $g_0$ below the threshold of the comparison of Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-6-4}, at fixed $n_C$); the first moment is not uniform in $n$. Not obtained: a bound on the average of $e^{E^\circ_\Lambda}-1$ or of $e^{|E^\circ_\Lambda|}$ uniform in $n$, which would need exponential moments of the boundary sum under the history measure, the same limitation as for $e^{\eta_{\mathrm{lab}}}$ in Remark~\ref{rem:c5-6-5}(1); the crude (a) is what is available for the exponential.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{remark}[scope of this subsection]\label{rem:c5-7-5}
The identity $S^\circ_\Lambda=S^\circ_{D(\Lambda),C,T}$ on $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-7-1}) is a factorwise comparison of the two explicit integrals (precision, cutoff, primitives, Wilson factor) using the facts (I3) at $D=D(\Lambda)\subseteq D_+(\Lambda_1)$; the identification deferred in the closing remark of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} (after Lemma~\ref{lem:covered}) is, at the retreated carrier, reduced to these four factor identities. (1) Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-square-extraction} and the facts of the identification paragraph are used at their stated status; in particular (F3) rests on the sum-level agreement of the exponent of Section~\ref{sec:sourced-effective-interaction} (CRE (1.14)--(1.16), Lemma~\ref{lem:primitive-sum}) with $P_{D,T}$ at identity data, which the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:regional-square-extraction} asserts for its own kernel. (2) The inclusion $\Lambda_r\subseteq\mathcal F_r$ (the coverage stencil of Section~\ref{sec:remaining-physical-comparison} inside the free-input set of the traversal link), which is what makes $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ imply the coverage of (I2), follows from $\mathcal F_{b_0(c)}=\mathcal E_c\cap E_*$ (proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:covered}) and $\Lambda_{b_0(c)}=\mathcal S_c\cap E_*$, i.e. from $\mathcal S_c\subseteq\mathcal E_c$ (the bonds with both endpoints in the two endpoint blocks of $c$ among the bonds of the two-block set $\mathcal E_c$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:two-block}); if it failed, $A^{\mathrm{fin}}_{\mathrm{cov}}$ would have to be intersected with $\{\Lambda_r\subseteq D(\Lambda)\text{ for every contour bond }r\}$, with the same probabilistic bounds (Section~\ref{sec:c5-3-4}(a) uses only that a stencil bond lies within $2L+1$ of its contour bond) and with Lemmas~\ref{lem:c5-5-3}, \ref{lem:c5-6-1} and Proposition~\ref{prop:c5-5R-4} still using $\mathcal F_r\subseteq D(\Lambda)$. (3) The comparison statement of Section~\ref{sec:regional-identification} is for $D_-\subseteq D\subseteq D_+$; $D(\Lambda)\subseteq D_+$ but $D_-\subseteq D(\Lambda)$ fails whenever a bond with both endpoints in $\Lambda_1$ is de-younged; Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-7-1} uses only $D(\Lambda)\subseteq D_+$ with the collar, which is what the proofs of the facts use. (4) Uniformity in $n$ of the exponential $e^{E^\circ_\Lambda}$ in label average is not obtained (Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-7-4}(b)). (5) $E^{\mathrm{lab}}_{\mathrm{def}}$ (Remark~\ref{rem:c5-6-5}(2)) is untouched; \eqref{eq:retained-leading-source} already keeps $e^{E^\circ_\xi}$ inside the history expectation as a label-dependent factor, which is the form Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-7-2}(b) gives at the retreated carrier. (6) \eqref{eq:c5-7-T}(i) adds the $R_0$-collar condition to the $R_{\mathrm c}$-condition of threshold item 1; both are conditions on $M$ before the coupling and are compatible (choose $M$ after both); whether $R_0\leq R_{\mathrm c}$ or conversely is not needed. (7) Further inputs used as stated: the reference-data cutoff convention behind (F2); $I=D_+(\Lambda_1)$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:c5-7-3}; the geometry of $\Lambda_{b_0(c)}$ behind $\varrho_K$; the square orientation and $M\geq8LM_2$ of \eqref{eq:c5-7-T}(i); the normalisation $\mathcal Z_0=\int\mathcal O[\mathsf w_\xi]\,\dd V$ in Corollary~\ref{cor:c5-7-4}(b); \eqref{eq:regional-periodic-weight}, \eqref{eq:flat-phase}, \eqref{eq:perimeter-literal-local-terms}, \eqref{eq:marginal-fixed-forest-bound} ($\tau$), Lemma~\ref{lem:two-block} ($\mathcal E_c$), \eqref{eq:regular-stencil-extension-support}, and Lemmas~\ref{lem:c5-1-2} and~\ref{lem:c5-4-0}(c).
\end{remark}
